## Summary

Heroes (Tor and Roberta), NPCs and enemies all run on one character runtime. Each active character owns a fixed runtime record, a body object with three skeleton blocks (main, sub-part and face), a placement record that holds its world position and transform, an animation instance, and two pools of numbers (health and a second pool used for magic). Each tick the runtime runs the type-specific update (hero, NPC or enemy behaviour), updates the pose and world transform, advances the animation clock, and probes the ground. Destroying a character resets its record and leaves it in its pool for reuse. Move checks can refuse a movement request. Separation from the world is a different step: every tick, inside each character's motion-record driver, the character is tested against the primitives and trigger objects of its grid cell and against nearby moving objects, and the contact response pushes it out (Behaviors 11 and 14A; SPEC-0018). The follow-camera eye pass (SPEC-0027) also pushes its point out of movable-block box records with a 1.25 radius; it is separate from character motion-record contacts.

Alongside the body, a second motion-record system keeps its own world position, step vector and displacement. Placing a motion record sets its vertical velocity to 1.0 (Behavior 13). Each tick a motion record also samples its position into a history ring (Behavior 14). For the hero, the displayed height follows the world height with a lag (SPEC-0033, Behavior 7). The hero travels over the ground by the root motion of the clip it plays: each tick the root step of its animation is turned by the facing basis and added to the motion record's position (Behavior 14C). Townsfolk, enemies and bosses travel the same way, by the root step of the clip they play, with a row speed added for flying rows (Behavior 14D). The world-wall contacts run inside the motion-record driver by the owner's class: the heroes test frame records and cell lists A and B, region characters cell list B and their navmesh borders, the petpet its own frame-record step and cell list A (Behavior 14A; SPEC-0018). Some owners also keep two or four tracked points and solve a heading from them (Behavior 8b); the callers of that dispatch are not confirmed.

Behaviour that differs by kind is named in the hooks described below: hero attacks are in SPEC-0036, locomotion and the hero's action states in SPEC-0033, enemy thinking in SPEC-0029, animation playback in SPEC-0005 and the ground probe in SPEC-0018. The shared runtime has no jump, swim or climb rule of its own. Its motion-record routines add gravity to each record's force every tick, hold grounded records on the ground with friction, and integrate velocity and position (Behavior 14A, items 5 to 7); in the air the vertical velocity changes by 32 G / 30 per tick for the record's gravity gain G, equal to a continuous gravity of 128 G units per second squared at 60 ticks per second. The hero's locomotion has a targeted leap, a timed hop and an airborne landing branch, all described in SPEC-0033 (Behaviors 9, 9A and 10); they are not part of the shared runtime. With this spec the clean side can hold, update and retire characters in the same order and with the same pools, and it knows which parts of the original's character code are still unknown.

## Where it lives on the disc

- The runtime has no on-disc layout of its own. Each character starts from a character record of the level's type-36 chunk (tag 3, spawn kind 3; SPEC-0006). Its skeleton indices and model-set index are in SPEC-0005 and SPEC-0006. Enemy type and variant are read from the same record and mapped to a monster row (SPEC-0029, Layout 4).
- Boot-program data words read by the runtime (High for every value below; "f32" is an IEEE single):
  - 0x31E890, f32 1.0e9. The runtime stores its negation as the "no ground" height when a character is created. It is also the starting distance of the nearest-candidate search (Behavior 20). Value: 1.0e9.
  - 0x2EB1C4, a 32-bit word that gates root accumulation (see Behavior 10). Value: 0 in the boot image.
  - 0x2DD8AC, f32 2.6, and 0x2DD8B0, f32 0.6. They cap the held-item scale (Behavior 10, item 5). Value: 2.6 and 0.6 as f32.
  - 0x2EB2C0, f32 0.75: the height step limit of the move checks (Behavior 11). Value: 0.75.
  - 0x2EB2C4, f32 0.25: the fixed point-vs-box margin used by the character/world move check in Behavior 11. Value: 0.25.
  - 0x2DB400, f32 1.25: the push radius of the follow camera's eye collision pass (SPEC-0027; the pass that SPEC-0018 calls the contact pass). Every reader of this word is in the camera family; no character step reads it (Behavior 11). Value: 1.25.
  - 0x2DCB3C, f32 1.0, and 0x2DCB40, f32 5.0: the radius and length of the cylinder that the beam (Water/Bubble) attack family tests along its beam (Behavior 12A). Value: 1.0 and 5.0.
  - 0x2DA60C, f32 -0.1: the per-tick gravity of the ball records. The ball step of SPEC-0010 (Bouncing balls) adds it to the vertical velocity each tick. Placement does not write it (Behavior 13). Value: f32 -0.1.
  - 0x2DA600, f32 1.0: the starting vertical velocity written when a ball record is placed (Behavior 13), and written back on a bounce (SPEC-0010). Value: f32 1.0.
  - 0x2EC850, a 32-bit mode word: selects the tracked-point routine of Behavior 8b. The disc value is 1. No writer is known. The dispatcher that reads it has no caller, so the word has no effect in the shipped build (High for the absence of a caller; Medium overall).
  - 0x2EC860, f32 0.10: the per-call rate cap of the look-at controller (Behavior 23A). Value: 0.10.
  - 0x2EC864, f32 -1.57, and 0x2EC868, f32 1.57: the yaw limits of the look-at controller (Behavior 23A). Value: f32 -1.57 and 1.57.
  - 0x2EC86C, f32 -0.35, and 0x2EC870, f32 0.35: the pitch limits of the look-at controller (Behavior 23A). Value: f32 -0.35 and 0.35.
  - 0x2DD6C0, f32 20.0: the in-range distance of the hero's candidate lock (Behavior 20). Value: 20.0.
  - 0x2DD69C, f32 4.0, and 0x2DD6A0, f32 -4.0: a facing range pair read beside the candidate facing (Behavior 20). Their exact roles are open. Value: 4.0 and -4.0.
  - 0x2DD698, f32 0.25: a turn offset in the same facing region (SPEC-0033, Where it lives). It is not a facing word: the hero's head look-at step lowers its look blend by this amount (Behavior 20, facing words). Value: 0.25.
  - 0x2DD87C, f32 0.2: the factor on the horizontal distance to the hero in the Can Fly 1 goal height (Behavior 21). Value: 0.2.
  - 0x2DD880, f32 2.0: the height a Can Fly 1 goal is kept above the ground (Behavior 21). Value: 2.0.
  - 0x2DD58C, f32 0.25: the per-tick blend rate toward a direction, used by the lock facing (Behavior 20) and the stick step (SPEC-0033). Value: 0.25.
- 0x2DD86C, f32 0.1: the blend weight that the two movement routines pass to the steering rule on most of their calls (Behavior 8a). Value: 0.1.
- 0x2DD854, f32 0.99: the progress gate of hero state 51 (Behavior 15.3). Every other progress test passes its own constant to the progress helper (0.99 in most callers, 0.95 in some; Behavior 22B), so no other gate uses this word. The face-override revert of Behavior 3D does not read it: it uses a fixed program constant of the same value. Value: 0.99.
- Tick rate: the boot image holds f32 60.0 at 0x30C598. It agrees with the 60 ticks per second of SPEC-0007. This is a different word from the run-time tick multiplier named in SPEC-0030 (which is not on the disc). The tick length 1/60 s is taken from SPEC-0007; the clean side may read the word as a cross-check.
- Palette data (Behavior 26; High): seven colour presets are program data, one block every 0x30 bytes starting at 0x316D78 (blocks 0 to 6; preset p is block p − 1). Each block holds 15 RGB triples (45 bytes), then three zero bytes. In all seven blocks triple 0 is zero (black) and triple 14 is white. The colour override set of Behavior 26, item 5, is program data at 0x316CA0: three words per colour word, where −1 keeps the record's byte. The colour table at 0x32CE44 (14 entries of 4 bytes; the 64 bytes from 0x32CE40 are zero-filled at load) and the player-index word at 0x2D759C belong to a debug tuner that never runs in play (Behavior 26, item 7). The clean side takes the preset and override values from the disc at those addresses, not from this spec.
- 0x320F48: six 32-bit joint indices, 6, 7, 8, 11, 14 and 17: the neck joints that the Plague Serpent's neck look turns (Behavior 23A; High).
- Physics block at 0x3A1A70 (High for the values). Six fields are written once at start-up as fixed program values, so the block is not stored in the file image and the clean side takes these values from this spec. The same start-up step zeroes the 16 bytes at 0x3A1A60 (High):
  - +0x00: 30.0, the inverse of the time scale. No routine reads it through the block (Medium-High).
  - +0x04: 1/30 (0.0333...), the time scale k of the motion-record integrator (Behavior 14A, items 5 to 7). A per-step scale, not a tick rate: the update that uses it runs every tick (Behavior 14). Also used by the hero's per-record wall contact for its predicted point (SPEC-0018).
  - +0x08: 0 (not read).
  - +0x0C: 32.0, gravity g (Behavior 14A, item 5). In the air a record's vertical velocity changes by 32 G / 30 per tick (G the record's gravity gain), equal to a continuous gravity of 128 G units per second squared at 60 ticks per second (Behavior 14A, item 7; High).
  - +0x10: 80.0, not read by any routine (unused; Medium-High).
  - +0x14: 0.03, the friction coefficient of the base motion-record class only (Behavior 14A, item 5c). Character records (heroes, NPCs, enemies) use 0.6, a fixed program constant (Medium-High).
- Run-time words (not in the file image). The following are written while the game runs and are not stored in the boot image. The clean side must not read them from disc; it must take their values from this spec or from the rule that sets them:
  - 0x368FA0: the frame-rate word (60, a count of ticks per second). 0x368FA8: the tick-length word (1/60 s). 0x368FA4: the half-rate word (30 divided by the frame rate, 0.5). All three lie outside the file image (zero-filled at load) and are written at start-up from the boot rate table at 0x30C598 (SPEC-0007, Per-tick words; High). The clean side takes their values from SPEC-0007. The 1/30 at 0x3A1A74 is the physics block field +0x04 above, also written at start-up.
  - 0x3932E0: the reference vector R, the constant up vector (0, 1, 0, 0), one of five constant vectors written once at boot by the start-up table walk (SPEC-0007). It is not in the file image. The gravity step adds minus weight times gravity times gain times this vector to the record's force, which pulls a record down (Behavior 14A, item 5). The driver's probe point adds 0.8 times it to a point moved 2.0 along the record's facing (Behavior 14A, item 1). The look-at controller (Behavior 23A) also reads it as the reference for the pitch sign and as the neck turn axis.
  - 0x3932C0: the zero vector (0, 0, 0, 0), another of the five boot-written constant vectors. The animation player constructor copies it into the root step and root accumulator of its four playback records (Behavior 25, Defaults).
- Fixed program constants (not data words, so the clean side takes them from this spec): the beam length of 20.0 units (Behavior 17), the rock travel limit of 250.0 units (Behavior 17) and the piece push of 0.25 that a breakable contact hands to the break (Behavior 16). No data word is known for them (Medium). The 0.25 piece push is not the 0.25 character radius word at 0x2EB2C4.
- The vector-unit program behind the held-item draw of Behavior 12 is the renderer's lit model draw (SPEC-0046); no character collision program exists. The boot image holds exactly two vector-unit microprograms: the large renderer program of the first unit (every draw, SPEC-0046) and a short skinned-lit-vertex program for the second unit's micro mode (never selected on disc, SPEC-0046 Open question 73). Neither one does collision, push or movement work; the "vector unit" arithmetic of the movement code (SPEC-0033, SPEC-0018, SPEC-0036) is ordinary main-processor coprocessor code and is fully described in words and math in those specs. No level module or disc resource carries microcode (High).
- Steering constants (Behavior 8a): the 0.3 blend weight of the goal-move routine's final path, the 0.1 blend weight of the strafe modes and the 0.9 dot threshold are fixed program constants. The 0.1 strafe weight is a separate constant that has the same value as the boot word above. The reference direction of the strafe modes is a run-time vector set at program start; it is not on disc.
- Fixed program constants (not data words, so this spec is their source): target pick radius 60 units with at most 10 candidates; spread values 5.0 and 3.0 and the off-axis dot threshold 0.85; the reach margin 1.5; the reaction draw range 1 to 100; the recovery windows 0.5 to 0.75 s and 2 to 4 s (Behavior 15.1); the hero ledge-guard constants (the drop limit 2.0, the look-ahead 3.0 and the raised top 10.0; Behavior 19 and SPEC-0018) and the edge margin 0.75 of the ledge hand-off; and the facing range of 4.0 units for the nearest candidate (Behavior 20, which reads the same value as the data word at 0x2DD69C). Random draws come from the game's shared random source, whose state is not on disc. Chain-body tuning values (Behavior 25) and the contact-slot gates (Behavior 24) are fixed program constants too. The tracked-point constants of Behavior 8b (0.02, 0.15, 0.025, 0.0015, the count limit 20 and the angle limit 1.7) are fixed program constants too.

## Layout

### Runtime fields of one character (names are descriptive)

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Kind | int | Hero (Tor or Roberta), NPC or enemy/creature. Heroes share one hook table with two per-kind update paths. |
| State | int | Current state of the character's own state machine. The previous state is kept when the state changes. For heroes the state is the action state of SPEC-0033 (Behavior 15). Known state numbers are described in Behavior 15. |
| Position | vec3 (+ w) | World position, held in the placement record. Many state routines write it directly. |
| NPC mode word | int | NPC only. Selects the NPC mode routine that the per-tick update runs (Behavior 3B). |
| Applied and pending priority | int, int | Priority of the applied state, and priority of the pending request. A hold word delays a pending request (Behavior 15A). |
| Facing | vec3 | Forward direction of the body object's basis (see Behavior 8). |
| Ground height | float | Height from the last ground probe. "No ground" value is the negated 1.0e9 word. |
| HP, max HP | int | Health and its cap. |
| MP, max MP | int | Second pool (magic for heroes) and its cap. |
| Status timers | 14 signed ints | One per status (names in SPEC-0036, Status effects). Active when at least 1. Off value is -1. |
| Target index | int, -1 = none | Index into the region's character list. |
| Lock index | int, -1 = none | Hero only: the candidate chosen by the lock (Behavior 20). |
| Animation set | handle, short index | Animation set bound to the character and its current animation index. The previous index and the animation slot are also kept. |
| Animation request ids | 3 ints at +0x3B0, +0x3B4, +0x3B8 | Main id (+0x3B0), second main id (+0x3B4; -1 when unset) and face override id (+0x3B8; -1 when unset). They are table ids, read every tick by the animation hookup (Behavior 3D). High. |
| Play-rate factors | f32 at +0x3D4, f32 at +0x8B8 | Character speed scale (+0x3D4; 1.0 for the heroes) and per-tick speed scale P (+0x8B8; Behavior 3A). The clip play rate is R = (+0x3D4) × (+0x8B8). High. |
| Root vector | vec4 block | Root displacement of the current animation, copied from the animation instance (Behavior 10). |
| Attachment handles | 2 | Two handles to attached effects or sounds, released on teardown. |
| Emote queues | two queues of four entries each | Body queue at +0x8D0 and face queue at +0x904. Each entry is 12 bytes; each queue has its count at queue +0x30. The current-emote words are at +0x938 (body) and +0x93C (face) (Behavior 22). |
| Melee hit list | 10 signed ints at +0x400 | Ids of the targets already hit by the current attack; -1 is an empty slot (Behaviors 5 and 12A). |
| Grid cell pointer | pointer at +0x174 | The grid cell record under the character's motion record, refreshed on every record tick (Behavior 14). Its cell list B holds the world trigger objects that the hero's breakable walk uses (Behavior 16). |
| Pending contact shapes | count at +0x2F8, ten entries of 0x70 bytes from +0x300 | Contact shapes queued for the character mover (Behavior 14A). |
| Footprint patch (movable-block records, +0x9C) | int | The ground-probe patch chosen at block placement. Every placement writes it and every disc block has a non-zero value, so the block's footprint step always runs (SPEC-0048, Push start, step 2; SPEC-0033, Behavior 15A, step 5). The list test and height step are in Behavior 11. |
| Light context | block | The character's four light slots (SPEC-0046, Character light slots), filled by the light walk of Behavior 24 and passed to every lit draw of the character, including the held-item draw of Behavior 12. |
| Active word | int | Nonzero while the character's record in its region's character list is active (Behavior 11). |
| Motion record | record | Second motion system: world position, transform, step vector, accumulated displacement, target vector, motion vector, blocking normals, history ring and countdown (Behaviors 13 and 14). Every hero and every region character (townsfolk, enemies and bosses) has one, of the same record kind (Behavior 14D). |
| Start pose | vec4, vec4 | Hero only. Start position at hero +0x3E0 and start facing at hero +0x3F0. Stored by placements and read only by the respawn at the start pose (Behavior 1C). |
| Home point | 16 bytes at non-hero +0x3E0 | Every non-hero character keeps a home point, copied from its placement record by the placement hook; level-script moves do not change it. |
| Displayed height | float | Hero only. Drawn vertical height that follows the world height with a lag (SPEC-0033, Behavior 7). |
| Motion-record physics fields | record words, runtime | +0x10 ground normal n (written by the probe). +0x24 ground gap: position y minus the ground height under it (character records); the base record class also subtracts the radius +0x08. +0x28 penetration tolerance: -0.1 for character records (set by the record reset), 0 for the base class. +0x40 unit direction of the velocity. +0x50 position. +0x60 velocity (units per 1/30 s step). +0x70 force accumulator, cleared by every integrator step. +0x110 position at the start of the tick (P0). +0x144 no-gravity flag and +0x148 no-snap flag (per tick; the hero dispatcher clears both every tick before the hero state routine). +0x14C clear-on-ground flag. +0x150 floor-contact flag (the gap getter returns 0 while it is set). +0x160 last motion (position change of the last tick). +0x188 gravity gain G (1.0 after reset). +0x200 and +0x220 take-off point and direction (hero air states). +0x280 lock word (Behavior 14E). +0x2F0 and +0x2F4 boost flag and boost height (SPEC-0033). Behavior 14A. High. |
| Grab cooldown | s32 at hero +0x9CC | Hero only. Ticks left before the ladder and climb-panel grabs may pass again; counted down once per hero update (SPEC-0033, Behavior 20). High. |
| Body size (motion record) | f32 at record +0x04, +0x08, +0x0C | Weight W (+0x04; the weight that divides the force in Behavior 14A items 5 to 7), radius r (+0x08) and height H (+0x0C) of the body cylinder. Written once by the placement (Behavior 13) and kept by every re-placement; no other writer is known. Both heroes: W 5.5, r 1.175, H 3.5 (fixed program constants). The same cylinder serves as Roberta's collision body for walls, pushes and pickups, and as the target of enemy projectiles; her melee hurt shape is a separate single bone capsule (Tor has nine; SPEC-0036, Hero body cylinder in the enemy hit tests), and the hero hittable query fails for her during her leap and its landing (attack sub-states 8 and 13), as it does for either hero in states 17, 25, 53, 54, 57, 59 and 60 (SPEC-0036, Hittable predicate). Her camera focus height is this H, as Tor's (SPEC-0027). Region characters take them from their monster row (row of type plus variant, SPEC-0029): +0x118 weight, +0x28 radius, +0x2C height. 143 of the 259 rows (townsfolk, named NPCs and several enemies and bosses) hold the hero values; examples of others as (r, H, W): Spyder 1.0, 1.0, 2.0; Minion Brute 4.0, 7.0, 5.5; Gnarfas 2.5, 7.0, 5.5; Juppie Monster 3.0, 10.0, 20.0; Crokabek 1.0, 1.5, 1.0. High. |

### Player-slot runtime layout (runtime, not on disc)

Confidence: High for the backing tables, strides, and known binding paths. Each player slot (indices 0 to 2) has three per-slot records, each placed at a table base plus the slot index times a stride. The boot initializer assigns run-time base pointers to fixed backing arrays in the boot image; the bases are not runtime-allocated. The character record points to each one through a field:

| Per-slot record | Boot-image backing base | Stride per slot index | Pointer field in the character record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Locomotion sub-object (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 9 and 10) | 0x32E880 | 0x1BA0 (7072 bytes) | +0x170 |
| Motion record (second motion system) | 0x333C50 | 0x760 (1888 bytes) | +0x16C |
| Locomotion block (control block of SPEC-0033) | 0x333B60 | 0x50 (80 bytes) | +0x7CC |

During boot initialization, before the main loop, the initializer assigns these three addresses to separate base-pointer globals. The hero-record table has a separate base at 0x335270 and stride 0x1680, with two records. The slot-binding step uses the same index for all three per-slot arrays only when the index is below 3. Its known construction paths are: hero selection invokes the hero constructor, which invokes the shared slot-binding step; region-character creation invokes the region-character constructor, which also invokes the shared step. Region-character ids are 100 or greater, so they skip the player-array binding branch; their locomotion sub-object instead comes from the owning block's per-slot pool. These are the known boot-image call paths, not an exhaustive survey of runtime-loaded modules.

**Motion record and locomotion sub-object are separate (High).** The character's motion record is the one named by the pointer at character +0x16C, not +0x170. In the motion record, +0x80 holds its class pointer and +0x90 its owner (the character). The locomotion sub-object named by +0x170 is a different record: it is the hero's animation object. Its animation player starts at +0x30, so the player's gate word is the sub-object's +0x40 (it gates both the clip play and the root-step travel of Behavior 14C), and the player's layer 0 holds the clip pointer at +0x70, the clip speed at +0x90 and the root step at +0xA0 (16 bytes, four floats; Behavior 14C). The word at +0xFA0 must be nonzero for the record tick to run (Behavior 14). The motion record has no reader of its own +0xA0 field in that chain.

**Animation player layers (High).** The animation player is the character's animation sub-object + 0x30, and every playback record offset of SPEC-0005 is relative to the player, not to the sub-object. The player holds four playback records of 0x60 bytes: main layer 0 at player +0x40, main layer 1 at +0xA0, the face record at +0x100 and the sub-part record at +0x160. A main clip plays on the layer named by its header byte 0x28 (0 on every clip on disc). Fields of one layer: +0x00 clip pointer, +0x08 mirror flag, +0x0C weight, +0x10 fade step, +0x14 fade count, +0x15 fade mode, +0x18 cursor, +0x1C fraction, +0x20 speed, +0x30 root step, +0x40 root accumulator. There is no separate stick target: the field at sub-object +0xA0 is layer 0's root step.

Attaching a slot (slot index below 3) sets its state to 1 and resets five-float rows of the slot up to offset 0x8C8. The strides are runtime layout facts: a clean implementation needs them only if it keeps the original field offsets.

**Second player actor and selector (High for the selector and slot bindings; Medium for the separate global).** The second player actor record sits 0x1680 bytes (5760) after the first in one run-time table, and two read sites agree on that offset. The selector sets the selected-player pointer to the table base plus the slot index times 0x1680, for slot indices 0 and 1 only; any other index leaves the pointer unchanged. There are only two hero records, so no slot-2 selection path exists (High). The hero setup binds locomotion sub-objects from a separate fixed three-entry array with stride 0x1BA0, motion records from another three-entry array with stride 0x760, and control blocks from a third three-entry array with stride 0x50. These arrays have independent bases; the slot index is shared across them. Region characters use a different path that allocates a 0x1BA0 locomotion sub-object on demand and stores its pointer in the owning character block's per-slot pointer array.

### Pools and caps

| Pool or limit | Count or cap | Confidence and note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Player-side character slots | 3 (indices 0 to 2) of animation sub-object, motion record and locomotion block; 2 hero records | High. The boot program builds three of each per-slot record but only two hero records (0x1680 apart), one for each hero index (+0xF80: 0 Tor, 1 Roberta). Only the two heroes attach slots 0 and 1; region characters attach with ids of 100 or more and get their own sub-objects. Slot 2 is a spare that is built and torn down but never attached (High for the attach callers; Medium-High that nothing else uses it). |
| Hero records | Constructed in a pool loop; one record per slot | Medium. Timing of construction relative to level load not confirmed. |
| NPC and enemy records | Up to 15 character objects per character block; at most 6 bound at once by default | High (SPEC-0006, Character activation). A block holds up to 40 kind-3 records and owns min(records, 15) character objects; its active cap is 6, 15 after a slot load without keep, and SetMaxActiveAI sets it up to 15. Character ids are the block's id base (100, 101, 200, 1000, 2000, or a slot base) plus the index, so they never collide with the hero ids 0 and 1. |
| Animation instance pool | Groups of 32 instances, ticked by the object loop when their flag is set | Medium (read of the object pool). |
| Motion records (second motion system) | Heroes bind records from a fixed three-entry backing array (only two hero records are selected); region-character creation allocates one 0x760-byte record per character from the owning character block allocator, at most 15 per block | High for the known hero and region-character paths and per-block limit. System-wide count/head owner, any global capacity and generic record allocation remain unestablished. |
| Attachments per character | 2 | High. |
| Status timers | 14 | High. |
| Skeleton blocks per character | 1 main, 1 sub-part and 1 face, each with per-bone state arrays | Medium. Index -1 means "none". |
| Model sets per region | Up to 8 (index 0 to 7) | High (SPEC-0005, SPEC-0006). Index above 7 is an error in the original. |
| Skeleton indices per family | Up to 8 per family (index 0 to 7); -1 = none | High. Negative main-family values: -1 none; -5 the Roberta main set; -6 the Tor main set; -7 the shared townsfolk set (body 526 with header 492; SPEC-0005, Body 526). A global set is bound in place of a regional main family; the record's own sub-part and face families are bound as usual. |
| Bones per skeleton | Disc maximum 74 (SPEC-0005) | No conflict: 74 is the largest skeleton on the disc and 83 is the most joints the pose composition accepts (SPEC-0005). Every disc skeleton fits under the cap (High). |
| Melee hit list | 10 target ids per character | High (Behavior 12A). The beam and bubble target lists of SPEC-0036 ("Beam and bubble lists") are two further 10-slot lists with timed slots (Behavior 27); they are not the melee hit list. |
| Pending contact shapes | 10 per character | Medium-High (Behavior 14A). |
| Held-item draw records | One 112-byte record per hero slot index in each of two equipment draw lists | High (Behavior 12). |
| Hero candidate list | One list of 15 records, walked each tick | High (Behavior 20). |
| Id table (character object) | 323 entries of 32-bit values in a list at the character's +0x62C (0x142 + 1 slots); searched and cleared by three routines | Medium. The entries have no known visible effect. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): keep a 323-entry list, empty at creation, searched and cleared as the three routines do. |
| Character slot table | Capacity is the count supplied to SlotAllocate; each created record is 0xB90 (2960 bytes) apart | High that no fixed 40-slot limit is present in the code read. The separate per-slot object list holds up to 40 entries. |

### Character slot table (shared with SPEC-0030)

Confidence: Medium for the stride and the field offsets; High that capacity is set by SlotAllocate and has no fixed 40-slot limit in the code read. The character side reads the same table that SPEC-0030 section 4 describes for level slots. The main record at +0x58E8 is separate from the created-slot table; its semantic name is developer intent and not encoded anywhere (it is searched first, then the table records).

| Field (owner object) | Meaning | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| +0x6C | Number of created records (next slot index) | High |
| +0x74 | Pointer to the records; capacity is set at +0x70; stride 0xB90 (2960 bytes) | High |
| +0x80 | Pointer to one region number per record (-1 = empty) | High |
| +0x58E8 | A main record, searched before the table records | Low-Medium |

- **Lookup by region number (Medium).** Given a key, the search checks the main record and then each table record in order. A record matches when it is active (its active word is nonzero) and its region number equals the key. The result is the index of the first match. A negative key gives -1; a key with no match gives -2.
- **Capacity and per-slot object list (High).** The table capacity is the count passed to SlotAllocate; the code read applies no fixed upper bound to that count. The 40-entry limit applies to the object list inside each slot record, not to the number of world slots. Each slot can also spawn at most 15 active character objects (SPEC-0030, section 4).
- **Id split (Medium).** An id in the 16-byte descriptor entries (byte 0 is the key, byte 1 the value) is split into a block number (the id divided by 50, rounded down) and an offset (the id modulo 50). The spacing of 50 matches the id base per slot in SPEC-0030 (id base plus 50 times the slot number plus 1). That link is an inference.
- **Walk of 167 entries (Low).** One routine walks 167 entries (0xA7) and passes each nonnegative value to a helper.
- **Packed animation-table entry word (High; SPEC-0030, slot-record +0x384).** Each indexed entry contains two 32-bit words after the table count; its first word is a 32-bit value. Bits 0-7 hold a byte, bits 8-10 a 3-bit field, bits 11-19 a 9-bit field, bits 20-30 an 11-bit field, and bit 31 a flag tested as the sign bit. The entry belongs to a kind-2 animation table referenced by the slot record animation-table array, not to the slot record character-pointer array at +0x44. A sample first word with bits 0, 8, 11, 20 and 31 set is read as one 32-bit word (synthetic width check).

## Behavior

### 1. Creation from a spawn record

1. The spawn gives a character record (Where it lives). For an enemy, the record's type and variant select the monster row (SPEC-0029). That row supplies the starting HP and MP, the default behaviour class and the timers.
2. The runtime initialises the common fields of the record. State is set to the idle-like state (1 in the base constructor). Target is -1. Ground height is set to the negated 1.0e9 word (the "no ground" value). Attachment handles and the animation references are cleared. Speed scalar is 1.0.
3. The body object is spawned for the slot: slot index, body object, placement record and locomotion block are linked. Hero-kind records additionally set the hero's tuning and reset its attack state (SPEC-0036).
4. The three skeleton blocks are built from the character record's indices (SPEC-0005, SPEC-0006). Each block gets one bone-state array per bone, filled with defaults (including unit and half values). The main block is required for a normal character; if its index is -1 the main block is simply not attached (the build treats -1 as "none"; indices -5, -6 and -7 select global objects instead). The build still succeeds: the sub and face blocks attach by their own indices, and the initial-pose pass that follows is skipped when the main block is empty. Several shipped character records carry -1 here (Medium-High). What such a character draws then comes from its remaining blocks. Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) by the slot bind code: a block is attached only when its index is at or above 0 and below the region slot's count for that family (main, sub-part, face; index at or above the count: no attach); a region slot holds at most 8 model sets and 8 skeletons of each kind (an index of 8 or more prints an error such as "Too many ... skeletons" and is not attached; the model-set error is "Too many character meshes in this character region!" and the bind goes on); -5, -6 and -7 use the game object's global skeleton words (the skeleton's pool is temporarily set to the slot's fix-up handle or pool during the attach). The clip-set index word of the player is the record's value at +0x8C and the main, sub-part and face clip sets are the record's values at +0x98, +0x9A and +0x9C (SPEC-0006 rows). A model-set index at or above 8 (the arrays hold 8; on disc the model-set indices are 0 to 7) is reported and the model is not registered.
5. A first channel is started on the main family with weight 0.5 (group 0): the root matrix is the identity, the layered clip start passes blend 0.5 and zero for every other argument, then one pose snapshot is taken and the pose-ready flag is set (High, confirmed 2026-11-09). The pose is then computed once; this step is skipped when the main block is empty. The weapon-trail-ready flag and the +0x1964 trail-request word are then cleared.
6. Kind-specific spawn finishes the record: heroes set kind and tuning; NPCs and enemies clear their action references to none and their target to -1.

**Base state 42, the parked state (High).** For the shared NPC kind (townsfolk, enemies and bosses) the constructor sets base state 42, and the reset method (class slot +0x64) also sets 42 through set-state. Every bind of a region record to the object (streamed bind, forced bind, and spawn by key) then sets base state 1 before the first frame, so right after a bind the previous-state word (+0x7FC) holds 42. No data word feeds this starting state; it is the constant 1 in all three bind paths.

**Set-state rule (High).** The set-state method copies the current state to the previous-state word (+0x7FC) only when the new state differs from it; the new state is stored either way.

### 1A. Generic object init and slot setup (Low-Medium)

This is a second init path for generic objects that have a radius and a class. It is not confirmed whether characters use it too. Steps:

1. The 96-byte init record is copied into the object. The meanings of its fields are open (Open questions). A second node routine of the same kind reads a 48-byte record instead, with the radius at the same object offset (Open question 163).
2. The object's radius is set from the record into its radius field at object offset 0x140. That field is a runtime field, not on disc.
3. The object's class init is called, as in Creation (step 2 of Behavior 1).
4. Slot variant: a wrapper takes a free slot, runs steps 1 to 3, and then runs two pool steps with a spacing of 2.0 between them. The spacing is a code constant of 2.0 (Medium). The role of the pool steps and the unit of the spacing are not described (Open question 163).

**Ownership boundary (High for the pool facts; unresolved for the generic records).** The dormant world object-separation pool is a separate runtime structure: it has capacity 2, uses 0x160-byte records, and stores each record's radius at +0x140. Its world constructor prepares these slots; the radius is not enough to identify the 96-byte init record in step 1. A second world pool uses 0xE0-byte slots, so it cannot itself be the owner of an inline field at +0x140. The known paths do not establish which authored object classes provide the 96-byte or 48-byte record, whether either record is copied from a node, or whether the 2.0 spacing belongs to either record's layout. Keep those relationships open (Open questions 156 and 163).

### 1B. Player selector and default spawn (Medium; High for the spawn vector and the default)

Used when a player slot is chosen. Steps:

1. Slot indices 0 and 1 set the selected-player pointer to the table base plus the slot index times 0x1680. Other indices leave it unchanged; slot 2 has no hero record (Pools and caps).
2. Spawn vector: the four 32-bit values at world-context offset 0x57D0. They hold the world's spawn vector written at load (Behavior 1C, World spawn vector), normally entry record 0 of the current world with its height probed. The code also has a default, x = 2429.0, y = 0.0, z = 2295.0 and w = 1.0 (a code constant, not a disc data word; the first component is 2429.0, not 3045.0), meant for an absent world context. The presence test checks the field's address rather than the world pointer, so it always passes and the default is never used in practice (High).
3. Direction: the pointer stored at world-context offset 0x57E0 leads to a 16-byte vector record. At load it is set to entry 0's facing when the world has entries and is null otherwise. When the pointer is not null, that record gives the initial direction. When it is null, the default (1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0) is used (High).
4. The selected player is initialised with the character init routine, using the spawn vector and direction and the three motion parameters 5.5, 1.175 and 3.5: the motion record's weight, radius and height (Layout, body size; High). The same three values are the defaults of a fresh monster row (confirmed 2026-11-09, High; defaults listed under Behavior 26 addition "Monster row defaults"). The init then stores the spawn vector and direction as the hero's start pose (Behavior 1C).
5. If the second actor pointer is not null, the same init is run on it with the same vector and direction.
6. The locomotion sub-object (the character's +0x170 pointer, plus 0x30) is then passed to two locomotion setup routines, described in SPEC-0033.

### 1C. Hero placement: start pose, respawn, moves, new game and teleport (High unless marked)

Notation: H(x, z, c) is the ground height at (x, z) probed downward from the ceiling height c (SPEC-0018). normxz(v) is v with its y component set to 0, then normalised. The focus hero is the active hero; the partner is the other hero.

**Start pose.** Each hero record keeps a start position (16 bytes at hero +0x3E0) and a start facing (16 bytes at hero +0x3F0). The placement slot of the hero class only stores these two vectors; it moves nothing. Writers: the character init at spawn (Behavior 1B, step 4), SetHeroStartPos (SPEC-0030 row 281), the placement after a world load (SPEC-0030 section 6), every teleport landing (entry 0 of the target world, below), the interior entry and exit jobs (SPEC-0030 rows 278 and 279), the scene-start apply of a loaded game (saved position and facing), timer F (SPEC-0055 section 8A) and the seamless world switch (the partner gets entry 0). The only reader is the respawn at the start pose.

**Respawn at the start pose** (one routine; option: heal). With Ps the start position, Fs the start facing and U = (0, 1, 0):

1. The hero's motion is re-initialised at Ps and Fs, keeping its three motion parameters.
2. Camera: eye E = Ps - 10·Fs + 2.5·U; side = normalize(U × Fs); the camera pose is set to E (SPEC-0027).
3. If the hero's field at +0x148 is nonzero and the global second-object pointer is non-null, copy the hero's world id to that object and move it to the hero's start pose. Hero initialization sets +0x148 to 1; the generic character constructor sets it to 0. The field is the player-controlled flag: it also gates the stick input of the hero update (Behavior 3A, step 3d) and the deadly-ground and hurting-surface rules (SPEC-0055). The second object is the live petpet, the second focus character (SPEC-0047), so the respawn takes the petpet to the hero's world and start pose when one is out (High). The word “partner” in this spec names that object.
4. State: death mode 1 gives state 59; death mode 2 gives state 60; any other mode gives state 1 with animation 0, unless the hero is already in state 59. The death mode is then set to 0 (SPEC-0055).
5. The character speed scale at hero +0x3D4 (a factor of the clip play rate R; Layout and Behavior 3D) is set to 1.0. No routine of the boot program or of the level modules writes another value, so it stays 1.0 for every character (High).
6. With the heal option: health to its maximum and three bar refills.
7. The grab-point cooldown at +0xA04 is set to the 60 Hz frame-rate value, 60. A positive value is decremented by one on each selected-hero update, without underflow. Grab-point acquisition and the state-28 exit to state 48 require this value to be below 1; the hang/leap end, reset and search-end paths also reload it to 60. At the normal selected-hero cadence this blocks those actions for 60 updates, about one second. It is distinct from the shield timer at +0x1574 (High for the field rules; Medium for elapsed-time interpretation when the hero is inactive). Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High, from the code): the per-world hero reset (lock candidates, tables, ledge, climb and wall words cleared, targets -1, movable-block index -1, the 0x29-byte table cleared) is the routine that restarts this cooldown at one second of ticks (60); the separate per-hero initial reset (hero kind 0: target range 12.0, secondary mode 1; kind 1: range 25.0, secondary mode 2; shot range 8.0, shot speed scale 0.5, voice cue word 6, tap seed drawn from the two boot words times the time scale, the three item slots cleared when their item is missing or empty) sets it to 0, not 60.

Callers: the new game (with heal), the placement after a world load, the scene-start apply and timer F (all three without heal).

**Move to position and facing.** The position is set (w = 1); the facing basis (forward, side, up) is rebuilt from the facing, with a fixed fallback basis for a near-vertical facing; the velocities are zeroed; the ground cache is cleared and the ground re-attached; the previous position is set to the new position. The position-only form keeps the basis. Both are immediate. Teleports, the interior jobs, MoveHero and the entity moves use them; the exact list of writes, what they leave alone and the difference from the full reset used by the respawn (step 1 above) are in Behavior 13 (Placement reset of a motion record).

**World spawn vector (at load).** When a world loads with entries: S = entry 0 position and the spawn facing = entry 0 facing (SPEC-0006). Then h = H(S.x, S.z, S.y + 8.0). If h < -1e-7, S.y keeps the record's y; otherwise S.y = h. With no entries the spawn stays at its reset value (0, 0, 0, 1) and the facing is none, so the default direction of Behavior 1B applies.

**New game.** The default load puts pack 691 into the transition slot and pack 909 into the exterior slot; both loads finish before the next step, and 909 becomes current. The conditional skip tests bits 0, 2, 5 and 6 of a singleton runtime-object word, not controller input; that word initializes to zero and no later writer appears in the checked boot paths, so the skip is unreachable through normal controls in this build (SPEC-0006, “New-game load mask”). Both heroes are then spawned (Behavior 1B) at the current world's spawn vector with its facing, which also becomes their start pose. The camera is reset and the focus hero respawns at its start pose with the heal. In this build the heroes start at entry 0 of pack 909 (test vector 99). No Init of packs 909, 691 or 678 moves the hero or stores a start pose; the first-play branch of 909's Update (progress flag 707) plays movies 88 and 1 and runs act start 1 without moving the hero.

**Teleport landing** of character C onto teleport plane k of target world W (options: fade, reverse). The plane gives a reference point P, a through axis F (the plane normal), a width axis D, a height axis Q ((0, 1, 0) for upright doors), a width extent w and a height extent h (SPEC-0006).

1. If k < 0 or k >= W's teleport plane count, the landing is refused: nothing moves and the caller gets a failure. The plane is flat when |F.y| > 0.85.
2. If C is the focus hero, W becomes current; with the fade option a fade of length 10 (unit open) runs around the move. C's action state is reset.
3. Facing input G comes from the plane's facing vector F; reverse negates the value passed onward. The pose setter passes only the low 64 bits (two 32-bit lanes) of this vector into the facing-basis rebuild, so the plane's fourth lane is excluded. The exact xyz mapping under the platform's vector calling convention remains uncertain (High for fourth-lane exclusion; see Open questions).
4. Position. Flat plane: L = P + h·Q + w·D, then L.y = L.y - 5.0, with no probe. Upright plane: L = P + 0.5·w·D + 5.0·normxz(F - Q), then L.y = H(L.x, L.z, L.y + 6.0).
5. If C is the focus hero, W is the interior slot and W has entries: L and G are replaced by W's entry 0.
6. C is moved to (L, G) at once. If W has entries, C's start pose becomes W's entry 0.
7. Focus hero only: the partner takes the same world id, the same start pose and the same (L, G), and the camera eye is set to the hero position - 5 × the hero forward.

**Probe starts.** The world spawn vector probes from y + 8.0; upright teleport landings, the plane landings of the interior jobs and every enter-interior landing from y + 6.0 (leave-interior entry landings are not probed); MoveHero from y + 4.0 (SPEC-0030 row 156). All three offsets are fixed program constants; the 6.0 is not a run-time word.

### 2. Teardown (reset, not free)

1. Release the character's handle and its owner link (if any).
2. Release both attachment handles.
3. Clear every status timer to -1 and clear the hit list.
4. Stop any playing music or effect tied to the character and remove it from the event list.
5. The record is not freed. It stays in its pool and the next spawn re-initialises every field in Creation. Field values not written by creation are not guaranteed to be zero.
6. Release sets base state 42 unless the character is dead. The dead test of the shared NPC kind (townsfolk, enemies and bosses) is true when the base state is 16, or when the AI mode word (+0xA34) is 16 and the counter at +0xD14 is above 0 (High; the 16 of the second arm is the AI mode word, not an animation kind). A slot release first writes the character's current position and facing back into the region's character record it was bound from and clears that record's cell cache, unless the AI mode word is 7; then it sets 42 when not dead and deactivates the character. When a whole world is released, every bound character of that world is deactivated without writing its position back (High). The region update sets any object that exists and is not dead in an inactive slot to 42 on every tick, and the streaming pass sets any kept character that it finds in 42 back to base state 1 on every tick (High). The dead test is true from the tick on which the death hook writes base state 16, so a level script that asks later in that same tick (the script step follows the character updates, SPEC-0007) already sees the character dead; it does not wait for the death countdown. The countdown is a separate matter: the dead-state update decrements it only on ticks on which the corpse clip passes the 0.95 progress test, and that test is false for a corpse request whose action has no entry in the character's table (the progress test looks the action up without the clip-0 fallback of the request), so such a character keeps its countdown at the starting value, never sinks and is never released as dead (the Plague Serpent, whose table lacks action 58; SPEC-0016, Open question 28).

### 2A. Character table reset (level path)

Confidence: Medium-High. The reset runs during character-record initialization in the level lifecycle. The matching release branch destroys the existing records and does not itself call this reset.
1. Clears the sixteen hero slot words.
2. Clears the forty character slot words.
3. For each character slot in use, calls the class clear method (Behavior 2, item 3). Slots not in use are not called.
4. Resets the hero sub-objects.

This is the table-wide form of Teardown; the records are kept and reused as in Behavior 2. In the fixed level-lifecycle path, the release branch destroys the two character records; the initialization branch later constructs them and reaches this reset. The reset is therefore after teardown when both lifecycle branches run in sequence, but the call sites do not establish that every reset is immediately preceded by teardown. World-character pool creation also reaches the same record setup. The owner is SPEC-0040 or SPEC-0026; it is not in SPEC-0033.

**Fixed pool resets (Medium).** Fourteen small routines each reset one fixed pool. Each calls the reset method of every element of its pool, stepping through the pool in strides of 28 bytes (0x1C). The pool positions are fixed in the program's data and are not listed here.

### 3. Per-tick update order

Each tick (1/60 s, SPEC-0007) the runtime runs these steps for each active character, in this order:

1. **No collision sweep**: the runtime does not sweep the root displacement through any collision volume. The 64-byte frame record of Behavior 12 belongs to the hero's held-item draw, which runs in the frame's second character pass (attachments and item draws), and it neither moves nor blocks the character (High).
2. **Motion dispatch**: choose between the predicted-path motion object and the other motion path.
3. **Class update**: the type-specific update runs. For heroes this is the hero update (SPEC-0033 and SPEC-0036; the order of its steps is in Behavior 3A); the hero's state dispatch and candidate lock (Behavior 20) run inside it. For non-hero characters, townsfolk, common enemies and bosses share one NPC update. In this order (High) it: returns at once when the character has no body object; syncs the colour block; marks the motion record dirty; refreshes the attachment marker; updates the clip-driven effects with the character's element (the monster row's alignment, or the attack alignment of the selected attack slot when one is selected); ticks the hit shield and the countdown meter; resets the per-tick speed scale P to 1.0; applies the status factors (Behavior 3E); clears the motion record's no-gravity and no-snap words, the animation sub-object word at +0x3E0, the attack-active latch and the deflect flag; raises the swing-trail request on the model when the attack-window query is true; runs the base-state switch; sets the no-gravity word again when the character has gravity-free motion; re-times the playing clip; runs the clip cue step; and finishes with the ghost fade tint, the attachment point refresh and the pad blend. In the default base-state arm (and after the state-specific work in states 17 and 31), the class arbiter steps the character's behavior coroutine inside this update. The global behavior schedulers run after the region-character, hero and petpet passes, but class coroutines have auto-run disabled and are skipped by those scheduler calls.
   1. P (+0x8B8) is set to 1.0.
   2. The status-effect tick runs (Behavior 3E).
   3. It switches on the base state (+0x144) and runs that state's NPC-specific work. The default arm steps the class arbiter; states 17 and 31 run their state work and then reach the arbiter. The arbiter is not run in states 16, 25, 33, 36, 42, 53 or 55.
   4. After the switch, the playing clip is re-timed from R = (+0x3D4) × P (the exact rule is in Behavior 3E), and the remaining tail steps run. Base state 42 returns before this re-time (and before everything after the switch; its only work is clearing the motion record's mover flag). Class-coroutine writes to P and animation requests therefore precede this re-time and the animation pass. The re-time and the cue step are the same two calls that close the hero update (Behavior 3A, step 6).
4. **Animation tick**: for each active slot, the player handles clip end or wrap, samples the clip at the cursor value for this tick, and then adds the clip speed to the cursor (SPEC-0005 Playback 2–4; High). A newly requested or re-timed clip is prepared in step 3 before this tick. The character animation object runs this slot update before the shared skeleton pose pass (High).
5. **Pose and world transform**: the shared skeleton pose pass reads three pointers to joint-record lists and writes three matching arrays of world-transform matrices. From the pose-pass argument base, the input pointers are at +0x240, +0x244 and +0x248 (main, sub-part, face); each input record is 0x20 bytes. The output pointers are at +0x24C, +0x250 and +0x254 in the same family order; each output matrix is 0x40 bytes. The pass handles the three families in order. SPEC-0005, Families and Posing a skeleton, defines family membership, index mapping and parent-first composition. The pass is used by skinned owners, including effects, and is not hero-specific (High for the pointer and record roles; Medium for one face-index boundary). The body basis and world transform are then written from the placement record. After the pose is built, the animation player applies a pending look request (Behavior 23A) and then the secondary step chosen by its mode word (Behavior 25; High).
6. **Ground probe**: the surface under the feet is probed (Behavior 9).
7. **Events**: time-window events from the current animation fire (SPEC-0005, playback section).

The clip is sampled once per active slot before its cursor advances. The skeleton pose pass runs after that sample, so the pose built for this tick reflects the cursor value from the start of the tick; the advanced cursor supplies the next tick. Example: cursor 2.25 and speed 1/6 frame per tick produces a sample at key 2 with fraction 0.25, then leaves the cursor at 2.4167 for the next tick.

**Loop-level order (High).** The region character loop visits each slot of the region in order:
- An active slot (+0xB04 = 1) first sets its character's zone flag (+0x18C) from the zone test of its zone record (1 when it has none). When the flag is set and the hold word +0xE3C is 0, three calls run in order: (1) the class update hook (step 3 above; the NPC update steps its class coroutine in the base-state arms that permit it); (2) the motion-record tick (Behavior 14): grid cell refresh, ground step with the moving-object contacts, stick target, record pre-step (edge guard, deadly ground, ground constraint), gravity, the driver with the contact walks, integrator; (3) the animation pass (step 4 above; start any newly requested clip, evaluate, store layer 0's root step for the next tick), with the zone flag passed in. Then the slot's post-step runs. A character in an inactive zone is not updated at all.
- An inactive slot whose character is alive is sent to base state 42 when it is not there already.

The world update runs the region characters first (the current world's own region, then its slot regions, then the second world's regions), then the selected hero (update hook of Behavior 3A, motion-record tick, animation pass), then the active petpet (update hook, motion-record tick, animation pass; SPEC-0047), and then advances the two global behavior schedulers (High). A hit that a region character lands on the hero in its update hook is applied at once, before the hero's own update of the same tick (SPEC-0007, When a hit on a hero takes effect; High). These schedulers run coroutines selected by their run flags; the class coroutines have auto-run disabled and the scheduler calls pass a zero mask, so they are not stepped there. The other hero slot is not updated at all: the hero branch of the world update skips any hero slot that is not the selected hero or whose ready word (+0xFA0 of the locomotion sub-object, Behavior 10 item 11) is 0, and the ready word of a parked hero is cleared when it is swapped out (Resolved; High). The hero branch visits both hero records in index order and, for a record that has its model object, its locomotion sub-object, a nonzero ready word and is the selected hero, runs in order: the update hook, the motion-record tick (the object at record +0x16C), the model tick and the draw submit. The item pickup test of step 3h is called from nowhere but that update hook, so a parked hero collects no item; regeneration, status countdowns and the warnings are steps of the same hook (High). The ready word is cleared by one helper (called for the outgoing hero of a swap, for both heroes at an act start and at a save load) and set to 1 by the three placement routines (spawn, post-load placement, incoming hero of a swap). Resolved: steps 4 and 5 of the per-tick list (clip tick, and the pose pass with the body basis copied from the motion record) run inside the third call, the animation-object update, which is itself gated by the ready word (High). The ground probe of step 6 is the one that opens the motion-record tick (Behavior 14), and the events of step 7 follow the pose pass (Medium). Steps 1 and 2 (no sweep, motion dispatch) only concern the rendering-side root group and have no gameplay effect (Medium-Low). Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): run steps 4, 5 and 7 in the animation pass and skip steps 1 and 2.

Character contacts (SPEC-0018) therefore run inside the motion-record tick of each character that has a motion record: after its class update hook and before its skeleton pose pass (Behavior 14A). The follow-camera eye pass (SPEC-0027) also tests movable-block box records with a 1.25-radius push-out; it is separate from this character motion-record loop.

The motion-record update of Behavior 14 runs once every tick for characters that have a motion record (High; no every-other-tick gate), in call (2) of the loop above. For the heroes the order within a tick is known (High): (1) the hero update (Behavior 3A); (2) the motion-record step (Behavior 14, cadence), which moves the hero by the root step stored on the previous tick (Behavior 14C); (3) the hero's animation object: it starts the requested clips, evaluates the playing clip and writes the root step used on the next tick. Non-hero characters run the same three steps in the same order (NPC update, record step, animation pass; Behavior 14D); the class arbiter is part of the NPC update. The animation slot tick and sample precede the shared skeleton pose pass of call (3), which builds the final joint matrices. The world-wall contacts run inside the driver of the motion-record tick, by the branch of the owner's class (Behavior 14A, item 1).

### 3A. Hero update hook: order within one tick

Confidence: High for the order of the steps below; Medium for their contents. The rules inside each step belong to the specs named.

The hero's update hook is the class update of step 3 above. Within one tick it runs these steps in this order:

1. **Pre-steps** (High for the order, which is fixed by the call sequence; the role of each is as marked). In this order:
   - a. The body-push flag of the hero's motion record (record +0x284) is set to 1 (High for the write and the only reader; Medium for the wording of the role). The character-versus-character body push (Behavior 11) moves a body only while this flag is nonzero and the body is not in state 16 (dead), and the pushing body must not be in state 33 or 53. The flag is 1 after creation and reset; code that holds, throws or otherwise takes over a body (the hero's grab start, some enemy attack and missile routines) writes 0 for the body concerned, and this step restores it to 1 on every hero tick.
   - b. Sound-slot upkeep: the sound blocks of the weapon, shield and power slots are compared with the wanted blocks, then queued or unloaded (SPEC-0031, Sound slots; Medium).
   - c. HUD equip icon indices (High, read from code; this replaces the earlier reading "item-slot update for the equipped weapon and shield models", which was wrong). The routine reads item slots 1 and 2 of the hero's id and computes two icon indices that it writes into the HUD sprites; it updates no item slot and no model. Weapon icon = the slot-1 item id minus 0x62, or minus 0x67 when the id is 0x6F or more; shield icon = the slot-2 item id minus 0x6A when that result is 0 to 4, otherwise -1 (SPEC-0021).
   - d. The hero's attack-active word (hero +0x1578) is stored: it is 1 when event bit 0x1 is set on the current frame of any of the hero's up to four playing animation slots, and 0 otherwise (SPEC-0036, Attack window; High). Its readers use the hero class getter (slot +0x18C), among them step 3a, the locomotion routine and the attack routines of SPEC-0036.
   - e. Ground marker (hero class slot +0x2F4; Medium). When the hero has a ground record and a class test (slot +0x278) is false, the hero's ground marker, a disc object, is placed at the hero's x and z, 0.02 above the ground height read from the record, tilted to the ground normal. Otherwise the marker is hidden.
   - f. The word at +0x1570 is set to 0 and the per-tick speed scale P to 1.0 (High).
   - g. The guard timer (+0x1574) and the grab-point cooldown (+0xA04) each go down by 1 while above 0 (High).
   - h. Status pass (SPEC-0036, Status timers; High for the call and the order). Each of the 14 status timers above 0 goes down by 1, and a timer not above 0 refreshes its status display entry. Then the per-status work runs: status 0 requests state 1 and sets P to 0; status 2 is the periodic hit; statuses 3 and 5 scale P (0.75 while the hero is moving or attacking, and 1.85 while moving); statuses 12 and 13 are the regeneration ticks of +2 on each tick whose lowered status-12 timer is a multiple of 60 (status 13 reads the status-12 timer, not its own; SPEC-0036, Regeneration). Every effect reads the timers after this step's decrement, so a status of D seconds acts on 60 D - 1 ticks (Behavior 7).
   - i. Outfit sync (hero class slot +0x2B4): when the hero's variant index changes, the outfit parts are loaded (SPEC-0021; Medium).
   - j. The four-button mash detector (SPEC-0033, Behavior 9B): each of four face buttons keeps a toggle count (at most 3) and an age; a toggle resets the age and raises the count, an age of 15 ticks clears the count, and a count of 3 inside the age window is a mash (Medium).
   - k. Petpet and base regeneration: the periodic health and magic gains, including the live petpet's passive gains (SPEC-0036, Regeneration; SPEC-0047, Passive bonuses), each added to the pool (health capped at its maximum) and followed by the bar refill (High for the call order). Gates (High, read from code): the petpet passive health and magic gains apply whatever the other conditions are. The base health trickle is added only when the poison status (status 2) is not active AND the hero is not dead by the class dead test AND health is above 0; the base magic trickle is added only when poison is not active (no health test). The second pool is clamped at its maximum.
2. **Low-health and low-magic warnings** (High): two independent checks on the active hero's own values. If its health, as a float, is below 0.15 times its maximum health, cue 157 plays and the HUD change timer is re-armed to 240 (SPEC-0023). Then, independently, if its second pool is below 0.15 times the pool maximum, cue 158 (the magic-pool warning, not a second hero's health warning) plays and the same timer is re-armed. A maximum of 0 gives no warning. Example: health 22 of 150 plays cue 157 (22 is below 22.5); 23 of 150 plays nothing; pool 13 of 90 plays cue 158. The rule is also in SPEC-0036 (Low-health warning).
3. **Input, look, cue and scan steps** (High for the order; roles as marked). In this order:
   - a. Streaming step (SPEC-0006, Pending step; High): the grid-cell value under the hero, the byte at +0x18 of the hero's ground record, or -1 without a record, is handed to the step that loads the neighbouring pack. Just before that streaming call, when the hero's index is 0 (Tor) and the attack-window query (the class test of slot +0x18C of step 1d) is true, the update raises the swing-trail request on the character model: one flag word of the model (model +0x1964) is set to 1. The model's own tick consumes the request, clears it, and keeps the held-weapon trail going (SPEC-0034, Held-weapon and other trails). The test is on the hero index, not on the pad word; no other routine sets this flag for a hero (High for the condition; Medium that Roberta's trail comes from a different path, since no other writer of the flag exists in the hero code).
   - b. Music intensity: a smoothed level, raised by the number of enemies in the aggro state (class state 19) and by low health, picks the music layer by its whole part (SPEC-0009, Intensity inputs; Medium).
   - c. One empty routine.
   - d. Locomotion input: the stick is turned into the movement tier, the state and the rate multiplier (SPEC-0033, Behaviors 3 and 4; High). It runs only for a hero with a pad (+0x148 nonzero). Its pad step also writes 0 to hero +0xF6C every tick (meaning unknown; no reader identified here).
   - e. Right-stick aim mode (SPEC-0036, Right-stick aim mode; High).
   - f. Footstep cue chooser (SPEC-0031, hero footsteps; High). It is called every tick. There is no attack cue chooser (SPEC-0036).
   - g. Head look target choice: the look point 5.0 ahead of the head joint (joint 6), taken from the held target when there is one (Behavior 20 and Behavior 23A; Medium-High, structure raised 2026-11-09). Corrected 2026-11-09 (High): the "4.0 above or below" applies only in action state 0x29 (clips 0x5B and 0x5A up, 0x5F and 0x5E down, left stick up or down) and state 0x15 (clips 0x38 up, 0x2D down); other states look sideways (±5.0 along the right vector in states 0x15, 0x1E and 0x17), steer along the heading (when the two class tests answer true), or use a held target, a list-B object (kinds 1 and 3, dot above 0.5, range word +0xF84, +1.5 height) or the target's head point. The full rule is in Behavior 23A, "Hero target choice".
   - h. Pickup test and collect (SPEC-0021, Pickup; High). For ordinary items the test fails in action states 25, 33, 51 and 55 (Medium).
   - i. Two scans of nearby characters, each refreshed every tick (the first record of a list stays pinned as the primary entry for up to 90 ticks before the whole list is re-sorted from index 0; distance-sorted with a +1.0 swap margin; the candidate rule uses horizontal distance and horizontal direction, so height never matters) with an alignment limit of 0.5: the hero's target list at +0xA70 (range 20.0 for Tor and 100.0 for Roberta; SPEC-0053, Target list) and a second list of the same layout at +0xC20 with its count at +0xDBC (range 20.0; Medium).
4. **Late counters and flags** (High for the order). In this order: the grab cooldown at +0x9CC goes down by 1 while above 0 (SPEC-0033, Behavior 20); the motion record's no-gravity word (+0x144) and no-snap word (+0x148) and the sub-object word at +0x3E0 are set to 0, so a state routine sets them again on every tick on which it needs them; one empty routine; the six hero shot slots are advanced (SPEC-0036, Hero shot slots).
5. **Action-state switch**: the hero's action state selects one routine for the tick. The state routes are in Behavior 15.3; the staged-special routine is in SPEC-0036.
6. **Tail** (High for the order). In this order: the class end hook (slot +0x1E4, called with -1); the quick-item selector, which runs only while the game-globals flag D and the game-state code are both 0 (SPEC-0021, quick items) and otherwise clears its panel flag; the application of the clip rate R to the playing clip (Clip speed, below); the clip cue step, which is gated by the pose-ready word and by the character's main table being bound, and which compares the previous and current layer-0 cursor with the cue start frame of the current main id (SPEC-0005, Cue start frame: when the previous cursor is below the frame and the cursor has reached it, the first class slot, +0x2DC, runs; the previous cursor then takes the cursor) and then, whatever the comparison gave, calls the second class slot (+0x2E4, the keep-alive refresh) every tick; when the current id's cue id is the none value the comparison is skipped and only the second slot runs; the candidate list of Behavior 20; and last one empty routine (High).

Test vector (order inside one tick, synthetic): guard timer 1, grab-point cooldown 1 and grab cooldown 1 at the start of the update. After step 1g the guard timer and the grab-point cooldown are 0, and a state routine in step 5 sees both as 0. The grab cooldown (step 4) is 0 before step 5 as well. A status timer of 3 on status 12 and a health of 20 of 100 at the start: step 1h lowers the timer to 2, step 2 plays no warning (20 is not below 15).

The hook that runs this update is the hero kind's update record (Behavior 4A).

**Clip speed within the hero update (High).** At the start of every hero update the hero's per-tick speed scale P is set to 1.0. The status effects and the stick tilt factor then multiply it (SPEC-0033 and SPEC-0036), and the stance routine requests the walk or run animation id. At the end of the update the playing clip's speed is set to R × 0.5 ÷ S (SPEC-0005, Playback 1a), with R = (character speed scale at +0x3D4, 1.0 for the heroes) × P (the per-tick speed scale at +0x8B8) and S the clip's speed byte. This is done on every tick, so a change of tilt or status changes the travel speed on the next tick. The re-time touches exactly two playback records, and only when the pose is ready: the layer that the clip of the current main id names (clip header layer byte), and, when the character has a sub-part skeleton, the sub-part layer (layer 3), each with its own clip's speed byte. The second-main-id layer and the face layer are not re-timed; they keep the speed they were started with. If the main id's clip index is outside the bound table, nothing is written. The re-time is applied to the layer of that clip even when a different clip is still playing there.

### 3B. NPC mode word and villager pairs

Confidence: High for the setter's requested-mode input, known mode dispatches, eligibility list, pair radius, timers, empty townsfolk modes and reset; Medium for the design role of mode 1 and any unindexed entry path (Open question 168).

Each NPC has a mode word, an integer that its per-tick update reads to choose one mode routine. The mode setter accepts a requested mode and applies case-specific entry setup; its mode values are interpreted by the active behavior class (Open question 168). The NPC reset sets the mode word to 0, the social gate word to 0 and the townsfolk flag (Behavior 3C) to 0 (High). Mode values and what this spec says about them:

- **3: villager pair.** Described below.
- **5 and 6: wander and path patrol.** These are active townsfolk modes; their detailed routines are not described in this spec. The common-enemy variants are in SPEC-0038.
- **1, 2, 4, 7, 8 and 9.** In the townsfolk class (behaviour class 6) these modes have no body: the character does nothing in them and keeps its playing animation (High). The common-enemy class (class 17) has its own bodies for these modes; they are in SPEC-0038.

**Mode entry setup (common-enemy mode setter; High for the stores, Medium for the names).** The setter takes the requested mode, runs the case below, then (unless the case replaced the mode or returned) saves the old value to the previous-mode word (+0xA38) when the new value differs, stores the new value in the mode word (+0xA34) and runs the mode timer update. The gait set is the animation id for each locomotion mode (SPEC-0029, flyer redirect), the flyer set is the replacement id list used when the row's Can Fly value is not 0. Cases:
- Mode 0: stores 0 when the row's Display in Default field (+0x82) is nonzero; otherwise the spawn row's +0x84 field, when nonzero, turns the request into mode 23.
- Mode 1: clears +0xAC0; gait id 20 for the first locomotion mode (the others unset); flyer id 154 when Can Fly is not 0.
- Mode 2: when +0x82 is 0 and the word +0xE60 is 0, the request becomes mode 21; otherwise a countdown +0xA70 is set to a random whole number drawn between the row's +0x60 and +0x5C values, and the hero's position is copied to +0xA80.
- Mode 3: clears +0xA90.
- Modes 5, 7 and 8: gait ids 2 (first mode) and 21 (third mode), flyer id 98 when Can Fly is not 0; mode 8 also clears the ambush word +0xBC0. Mode 6: gait ids 4 and 21, flyer id 99. Mode 9: gait ids 11 and 21, flyer id 100.
- Mode 10: plays animation 73, or 163 when either flight test of Behavior 21 passes; when that animation is missing the mode becomes 0.
- Mode 14: clears +0xC04. Mode 16: plays animation 58, or 153 when either flight test passes. Mode 18: plays animation 0.
- Mode 19 (agro): starts the row's entry effect when its effect byte (+0xB7) is not 255, runs the mode 0 case, clears +0xAB8, +0xB18, +0xB88 and the four words below +0xB9C, draws the word +0xB8C as a random number between the row's +0x78 and +0x74 values, and registers the character with the attack manager (SPEC-0038, Behavior 45).
- Any other request stores the mode with no setup. Test vector: a request for mode 5 on a Can Fly 0 row stores 5, saves the old mode, sets gait ids 2 and 21 and no flyer ids; a request for mode 2 on a row with +0x82 = 0 and +0xE60 = 0 stores 21 instead.

**Villager pair (mode 3).** Two villagers that are eligible find each other and talk or wait. On disc no pair ever forms (step 2), so the rules below never run in the shipped game; the clean side keeps them for completeness.

1. **Eligibility.** A villager is eligible when its social gate word is 0, its current animation id is one of 0, 1, 2, 8, 9, 10, 11, 59, 60, 107 and 112 to 116 (it must be playing one of these ids), and its mode is not 3 (High). The script calls EnableVillagerSocialize and DisableVillagerSocialize set the gate to 0 and 1.
2. **Search.** The search runs only in modes 5 and 6. Each tick the searcher's two pair counters, one of which is the cooldown, drop by 1. When the cooldown is below 1, the searcher walks the character list of its region in order and takes the first other character that is eligible, is on the same nav mesh index and lies at a horizontal distance below the pair radius. The search repeats every tick until it matches. The pair radius is the searcher's monster-row word +0x44 (SPEC-0029), which is 0.0 on every class-6 row, so no distance passes the strict test and pairs never form on disc (High).
3. **Pairing.** On a match, each villager stores the other's character id as its partner, and the search sets mode 3 for both through the mode setter, with role 0 (High).
4. **Approach.** Each tick, each villager walks toward its partner's current position. While the distance is 6.0 or more, the approach continues, and the partner's position is taken again each tick.
5. **Talk or wait.** When the distance is below 6.0, a random choice of 0 or 1 decides which villager takes role 1 and which role 2 (both gesture; see the gestures paragraph below). The approach ends for both.
6. **Talk and wait timers.** Entering talk or wait draws a timer as a whole number from 5k to 12k ticks, where k is the frame-rate word (60; Where it lives, run-time words), so 300 to 720 ticks (5 to 12 s) (High). When the timer ends, both villagers go to the reset sub-mode.
7. **Reset and cooldown.** The reset draws a cooldown as a whole number from 10k to 30k ticks, 600 to 1800 ticks (10 to 30 s), then the villager may search again (step 2) (High).

The pairing does not follow the hero. It ends with the talk and wait timers and the cooldown.

**Villager gestures (mode 3; High for the ids, Medium for the unreachable entries).** The draw happens when a role word is assigned (at pairing and at each turn change), not every tick: the villager draws a uniform whole number from 0 to 15 and requests the gesture id at that position of the list for its role word (1 or 2), then keeps playing that stored id. Both roles gesture. Role 1 is the turn-driver: when its gesture finishes, the two villagers swap roles (the old role 2 becomes role 1) and both draw again. Role 2 plays its single gesture and, when that finishes, clears the stored id and returns to the idle clip until the next swap (Medium-High). A tick with a stored id of 0 (no gesture) simply waits for the timer. Any other role word draws nothing and replays the stored gesture id. The first assignment after pairing is a coin flip (0 or 1) for which villager starts as role 1; a swap can only happen once the role-1 gesture has finished. The talk timer (step 6) is not restarted by a swap. This corrects the earlier statement that a new gesture is drawn on every tick of the conversation, which changes what a player sees: each villager performs a gesture, finishes it, and the pair take turns, instead of changing gesture every frame. This is the picker that SPEC-0005 calls the random idle picker.

- Role 1 list (positions 0 to 15): 99, 85, 51, 30, 50, 29, 84, 89, 90, 26, 100, 43, 35, 81, 87, 49.
- Role 2 list (positions 0 to 15): 23, 64, 61, 47, 27, 88, 41, 81, 87, 79, 32, 38, 25, 31, 82, 96.
- The role-2 list has four more positions, 16 to 19 (ids 28, 40, 62 and 95), which the 0 to 15 draw cannot reach (Medium: this rests on the draw including both ends, as the five-value fidget draw of Behavior 3C does).
- If the drawn id has no clip in the character's main table, the stored gesture id is reset to 0 and id 0 plays. For the townsfolk tables (main-family value -7) every id of both lists has a clip; the 30 distinct ids give 46 different body 526 clips over the four table variants.

**No follow, escort or teleport rules.** No NPC follows the hero, fights beside it or escorts it. No rule in the character runtime or in the NPC modes sets an NPC's walk target to the hero's position. The villager pair is the only rule where one character's walk target is another character's live position. No rule teleports a character that is far from its target or from the hero. Positions change through steering and walking (Behaviors 8a and 14), through scripted moves (SPEC-0030, MoveEntity) and through placement (Behavior 13). An NPC walks by the root motion of the walk clip it plays; the townsfolk walk speed is 5.00 units/s (Behavior 14D).

### 3C. NPC idle, fidget and walk animation ids

Confidence: High for the ids, the draw ranges, the flying test, the townsfolk step of the idle pick, the townsfolk flag and the townsfolk walk slots; Medium for the names walk, run and fast run.
