Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) by the idle-pick routine read in full: idle 0 normally and 97 (0x61) while the "busy A" class test (the flying test) is true; the agro mode (hex 0x13 = 19, written as "13" in one reading note) gives 1, or 141 (0x8D) in the flying / Can Fly 2 case; townsfolk give 0; otherwise a repeat timer at +0x8C8 (120 to 600 ticks) and one of the five idle variants 115 to 119 (0x73 to 0x77), or 142 (0x8E) in the flying case, chosen by a draw of 0 to 4 when the timer runs out and kept while the variant clip is still playing.

1. **Idle pick.** The idle picker returns one animation id per call. The switch between the ground and the flying idle is the flying test (High): a character is flying when its monster row's Can Fly value (SPEC-0029) is 1 and its grounded countdown (+0xBF4) is not positive. The steps run in this order, and the first one that applies gives the result:
   1. **Mode 19 (agro).** While the NPC mode word is 19, the result is 141 when the character is flying or its row's Can Fly value is 2, and 1 otherwise. The picker returns this id without first looking it up in the main table.
   2. **Townsfolk (High).** Otherwise, when the character's townsfolk flag is set, the result is 0. Nothing else happens: no fidget, no change to the hold timer, no random draw and no flying idle.
   3. **Base idle and fidget.** Otherwise the base idle id is 0, or 97 while flying. While the base idle plays, the hold timer drops by 1 per call. When it reaches 0, a fidget id is drawn uniform from 115 to 119 (five values, both ends included); while flying, the fidget id is 142 instead. The fidget id is checked against the character's main table first: if it has an entry, it is the result; if it has none, the hold timer is redrawn as a whole number of ticks uniform from 120 to 600 and the result is the base idle. While a fidget (115 to 119, or 142) plays and its clip is below progress 0.99 (Behavior 22B), the result is that fidget. After it, or while any other id plays, the hold timer is redrawn in 120 to 600 ticks and the result is the base idle.

   **Townsfolk flag (High).** Word +0xC54 of the character. It is the same word as the talker flag of SPEC-0022 and the townsfolk flag of SPEC-0056. It is 1 exactly when the character's default behaviour class is 6: the NPC reset clears it, the default class request at bind sets it (item 2), and script class requests never change it, so it is constant while the character is bound. On disc it is set for 666 placements: all 592 on the shared townsfolk body bank (main-family value -7) and 74 others.
   **Id miss (High).** A requested id that has no entry in the main table does not leave the old clip playing: the lookup gives clip index 0, so main clip 0 of the bound set starts (clip index 0, not id 0), unless clip 0 already plays on that layer (Behavior 3D). The same holds for the sub-part and face families. Only the picks that check the table first (the fidget pick above and the villager gestures of Behavior 3B) avoid this.
2. **Walk slots.** The walk routines (walk to a point, path walk and wander; SPEC-0056) request the walk id of the character's movement class: class 0 gives id 1, class 1 id 6, class 2 id 5, and classes 3 and 4 id 3. A speed code of 6 sets the class-0 walk id to 8. **Townsfolk walk slots (High).** A character whose townsfolk flag is set takes its walk slots from a speed code: code 5 (wander) gives walk id 1 and code 6 (patrol) gives walk id 8. The default class request sets the townsfolk flag to 1 exactly when the character's default behaviour class is 6 (the townsfolk class), and the NPC reset clears it (Behavior 3B). In the walk routines the movement class is the character's move type, which the script call SetAI_MovementType sets (0 walk, 1 run, 2 fast run; default 0). Movement types 3 and 4 therefore play the idle clip and do not travel (High).
3. **Fixed-animation modes.** Mode 10 plays id 65 (sit), and modes 11 and 12 play ids 97 and 98 (lying) (SPEC-0056, Modes).
4. **Townsfolk (main-family value -7; SPEC-0005, Body 526).**
   - Id 0 plays the standing idle (body 526 clip 48). Townsfolk never fly, so the flying base idle 97 does not apply to them; on body 526 id 97 is a lying loop, and the lying ids 97 and 98 come only from modes 11 and 12 (item 3).
   - The tables give ids 115 to 119 clips 51, 52, 102, 123 and 126 (in table variant B, ids 118 and 119 have none), and clips 51 and 52 are copies of clip 48. Every main-family -7 character is a class-6 townsfolk, so the idle picker never requests ids 115 to 119 for them (item 1, step 2). Clips 123 and 126 are named only by ids 118 and 119, and clip 102 only by ids 117 and 102, so they play only if a level script requests those ids (whether any does is open). A townsfolk in an idle state shows clip 48 (id 0). The wander and patrol pause fidgets (ids 2 and 11; SPEC-0056) also name clip 48, so their request starts nothing (Behavior 3D).
   - Ids 141 and 142 have no clip in any variant. The picker never reaches 142 for a townsfolk (item 1, step 2). Id 141 is reached only through mode 19 (item 1, step 1), and no class-6 row has Can Fly 1 or 2, so for a townsfolk that step gives id 1, not 141. A request for 141 would start main clip 0 of body 526 (not the standing idle, clip 48), because the id has no table entry.
   - The walk slots give: class 0 a walk cycle, class 1 a run cycle, class 2 a fast run, and classes 3 and 4 the idle clip (no leg motion). The enemy-style walk and run ids 2 and 3 also give the idle clip.
   - No body 526 clip moves bone 0 horizontally, but travel does not come from bone 0 (for the heroes either): it comes from the per-key root records of table A (Behaviors 14C and 14D; SPEC-0005). Body 526 has table-A root travel in the forward (third) lane: walk clips 104 and 107 z = 512 per key, walk clip 108 z = 175, carry walk 110 z = 128, run clips 105 and 111 z = 1382 with a small sideways sway in x, fast run 106 z = 1975; the idle clips have none. So the townsfolk travel by root motion like the heroes; their speeds are in Behavior 14D (High).

### 3D. Animation request ids and the per-tick hookup

Confidence: High for the ids, R, the start order and the face-override revert; High for the emote routing (Behavior 22).

Clips are not started on the animation player directly. Code requests them by table id through three ids kept on the character (Layout, animation request ids): the main id, the second main id (-1 when unset) and the face override id (-1 when unset). The request store (the "Animation select by index" hook of Behavior 4) writes all three, with one condition: the main id is stored only when it differs from the current one, and only then (when the main table is bound) it resets the previous-cursor value of the cue step to −1.0 and loads the cue start frame of the new id (Behavior 3A, step 6); the second id and the face override id are stored on every call, even when equal. A repeated request for the same main id therefore changes nothing but those two. The face-id setter writes only the face override id. Each id is resolved through the character's main, sub-part or face table (SPEC-0005); an id with no entry resolves to clip index 0 (Behavior 3C, id miss).

Each tick, before the animation player's own step (SPEC-0005, Playback), the hookup runs once for the character's animation sub-object:

1. **Play rate.** R = character speed scale (+0x3D4) × per-tick speed scale (+0x8B8). Clips started in this tick get the speed R × 0.5 ÷ S (S > 0) or R × 0.5 (S = 0), with S the clip's speed byte.
2. **Mirror flags.** Main: the mirror bit of the main table entry of the main id. Sub-part: the sub-part table entry of the second main id. Face: the face table entry of the face override id (SPEC-0005, mirrored clips).
3. **Forced start.** When the sub-object's force-start word (+0x1B00) is nonzero, the main, sub-part and face clips of the request start at once with no already-playing test (the face clip is that of the face override id when it is set, else of the main id), and the word is cleared. Nothing else runs this tick.
4. **Main request** (otherwise). If the main clip's layer already holds that clip index with the same mirror flag, nothing starts. Otherwise the main clip starts, and the sub-part clip of the main id and the face clip (override id when set, else main id) start with it.
5. **Second main id.** When it is 0 or more, its main clip starts on the layer its clip names, with the same already-playing test. With -1 nothing starts.
6. **Face override.** When the face override id is set, its face clip starts unless the face record already holds that clip with the same mirror flag. Then, if the face record holds the override's face clip and its progress is 0.99 or more (a fixed program constant), the override is set to -1 and the face clip of the main id starts at once (the base face clip).

So a face override plays to 99 percent of its clip and then reverts to the face clip of the current main id without any other request. Every start fades in from the last displayed pose (SPEC-0005, clip start).

### 3E. Non-hero speed scale and clip speed

Confidence: High for the order, the NPC-local class arbiter and the resulting same-tick re-time of the current clip; Medium-High that new clips requested by an active class coroutine start at that tick rate.

At the start of each NPC update (Behavior 3, step 3) the per-tick speed scale P (+0x8B8) is set to 1.0. The shared status tick then checks the active status timers: an active stun (status 0) sets P to 0 and requests AI mode 4; slow multiplies P by 0.5 and speed multiplies it by 2.0, the last two while the character is in a moving state (SPEC-0036). The status apply accepts townsfolk characters. A stun would set mode 4 through the shared mode word, but no source applies a status to a townsfolk except the mode 12 self-poison (SPEC-0056, Open question 26). Then, in every base state except 42, the playing clip's speed is set to R × 0.5 ÷ S (SPEC-0005, Playback 1a) with R = (character speed scale +0x3D4, 1.0) × P. **Rule for the time-scale word (High; matched code).** R is always the product of the two words, in that one place, for both the re-time and for every clip start (a start gives the new layer the speed R × 0.5 ÷ S, or R × 0.5 when S is 0). The word at +0x3D4 is written only by the three character resets, which store 1.0; no controller, status or boss routine writes any other value to it, so in the shipped game it is always 1.0 and the whole variable part of R is P (+0x8B8). Routines that "set the character's time scale" (the direct store and the multiply, which only touch +0x8B8) therefore change the per-tick clip rate in proportion: P = 0.5 halves the cursor step and the root step of that tick, P = 2.0 doubles both. Another character's rate can be copied from this product (the Cloud Beast's companion stores its owner's product as its own P each time the boss controller runs). Test vector: S = 1, +0x3D4 = 1.0, P = 0.75 gives a cursor step of 0.375 key per tick; with S = 3 the step is 0.125. Base state 42 is the parked state (creation, reset, release and inactive slots; Behavior 1). Every bind sets base state 1, so the townsfolk and all other bound region characters are in state 1 or in a state their AI sets, and their playing clip is re-timed on every update. Only a character parked in 42 keeps its start speed; the NPC update returns early for it. In play the only bound characters in 42 are the Gelert Assassin's waiting mirror images (SPEC-0013). New clips are started only in the animation pass (Behavior 3D), with the R of that tick, which comes after this update. This one rule covers every non-hero character, bosses included; which controllers change P before the rewrite, and the test vectors, are in the re-time rule table of SPEC-0005 (Playback 1b). In particular the Black Knight and the Werelupe King store 1.0 into P every update, which overwrites the stun and slow factors, while the Darkest Faerie controllers never write P.

So the status factors work: a slowed walker plays its clip at half speed and travels half as far per tick. Class-coroutine routines also write P (the attack wind-up, attack and recovery speeds of SPEC-0038, the boss phase speeds of SPEC-0019, the speed word of SPEC-0020, the Black Knight speed of SPEC-0012). The class arbiter steps these routines inside the NPC update, after P is reset and status factors are applied but before the playing clip is re-timed and before the animation pass (Behavior 3). A P write therefore can affect the current clip's re-time and the speed used when a requested clip starts in the same tick; the following NPC update resets P to 1.0 and reapplies status factors before the next re-time. Status factors applied during the NPC update still affect playback. The listed writers all run inside the class-coroutine pass, and the order of a write against an animation request in the same pass has no effect, since a clip starts only in the animation pass with the final P of the tick. Test vector: a wind-up routine requests clip A and then writes P = 0.5 in one tick; the animation pass starts clip A at the rate for P = 0.5; the next tick P is 1.0 again before the routine runs.

### 4. Method hooks (kind-specific behaviour)

Each character kind has a table of hooks. The hooks named below are the roles that could be confirmed. Slot numbers and table addresses are not part of the spec, except the four hero class slots named after the table below. Hero and NPC kinds override most of the base hooks.

| Hook role | Called when | Behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Set state | A new state is requested | If the new state differs, the old state is kept and the new state is written. The hero version adds special cases for some states (exit hooks, forced animation). |
| Update | Every tick, step 3 | Runs the kind-specific update. |
| Spawn | Creation, step 3 | Initialises the kind-specific fields (see Creation). |
| Teardown | Teardown | Releases attachments, clears statuses (see Teardown). |
| Take hit | An attacker damages the character | Receives the damage as an integer, the attacker's identity and flags. The hero version starts a hit reaction. |
| Damage applied | After any damage, whether HP fell or was already at zero | Notification only. |
| Heal applied | HP rises | Notification only. |
| Max HP changed | Max HP is set | Notification only. |
| Death | HP reaches zero | Heroes run the death gate and then the default death (SPEC-0036, Death). Non-hero kinds (the NPC table): unless the row's reanimation word is nonzero or its behaviour class is 1, 11 or 32, the hook writes base state 16, which stops the character's class coroutine from the next tick (SPEC-0015 for the Juppie). |
| MP spent, MP gained, MP max changed, MP reached zero | Second pool changes | Notifications for the second pool. |
| On-hit callback | An attack resolves against a target | Adds the target to the attacker's hit list. |
| Can be hit | Hit scan (Behavior 5), shots and charm walks | The hittable predicate of Behavior 12A: whether a target is a candidate this tick. |
| Defence | Damage formula | Returns the target's defence value. Hero defence is zero for both heroes (SPEC-0036, Damage; High). |
| Outgoing multiplier | Damage formula | 1.0, times 0.75 while the curse status is active, times 1.5 while the strength status is active (SPEC-0036). Confidence High. |
| Melee damage | Damage formula | Computes damage from the attack power, weapon stat and factors in SPEC-0036. |
| Play animation by id | Scripts and kind logic | Starts a clip by its table index. |
| Action-lock predicate | Before actions are allowed | False in a fixed set of states (death, a few hit and special states); otherwise true only while a lock counter is zero. The exact state list is not given here; the clean side should treat the set as Medium confidence. For the hero's busy test, see Behavior 15.2 (it is the IsHeroOccupied test of Behavior 18); the action-lock predicate is a different predicate. |
| Animation sound-cue start | The layer-0 cursor crosses the cue start frame stored in the current animation entry (SPEC-0005, Word B) | The base/shared table does nothing. The hero table starts its animation sound cue; the region-character table starts the cue from the character sound block. The shipped hero animation tables contain no cue requests, so the hero override has no cue to start from those entries. Confidence High for the known tables. |
| Animation sound-cue refresh | After the cue-start test on each active animation update | The base/shared table and hero table do nothing. The region-character table refreshes the stored cue only when it still matches the current animation entry and is marked keep-alive (SPEC-0031). Confidence High for the known tables. |
| Status query and clear | A status is queried or cleared | Reports whether one status is active; when it is, sets that status's timer to the off value (-1). Confidence High. |
| Animation select by index | A script or kind logic selects an animation index | The request store of Behavior 3D. Stores the index as the main id (+0x3B0) and its two arguments as the second main id (+0x3B4) and the face override id (+0x3B8). When the index changes, it sets a per-animation value to -1.0 and, if an animation set is bound, reads a time from it; it also clears one related value. Confidence High for the stores, Medium for the role. |
| Attachment release | Teardown, per handle | Releases one effect or sound handle. |

The base kind's hook table has 94 slots. Of these, 30 do nothing (no-ops), 42 return a fixed value or answer a state or flag query, 19 have real behaviour (state change, update, spawn, teardown, action-lock, status query, damage multiplier, animation select, handle registration, reset, world helpers and similar), and 3 are not or only partly described. Confidence High for the shapes, Medium for the role names. The hero and NPC tables override most of these slots.

**Hero class slots (Confidence: High for the four roles; Medium-High for the hit-point field offset).** Four slots of the hero class are named here by their byte offset in the class's method table. They are not object fields. Two of the numbers (0x154 and 0xBC) also appear as object offsets, and those object fields are different things.

- **+0x14, set state** (hero, new state). This is the state setter of the table above.
- **+0x3C, death hook** (hero, flag 1, flag 2). The death gate runs only when the third argument (flag 2) is nonzero: damage passes 1 and a script kill passes 0. See SPEC-0036 (Death) and SPEC-0055 (hero death and respawn).
- **+0x154, is-dead test.** Returns 1 when the hero's state is 16 (the death state). This slot is not the hit-point value. The hero's hit-point data field is at the same numeric offset of the hero object (SPEC-0036, Hit points), so the slot and the field must not be merged.
- **+0xBC, take hit** (hero, damage, attacker, flags). This is the take-hit role of the table above. The object field at offset 0xBC used by the sample ring (Behavior 14B) is a different field.

### 4A. Hook table records and the tables that use them

Confidence: High for the record layout and the counts; Medium for the roles of the hero and base tables and of the subsystem hook lists; Low for the owner of the actor class.

Record layout (High). A hook table is a run of 8-byte records. Each record holds a 16-bit object offset in its first two bytes and the function entry in its last four bytes. The two bytes between them are not described. The offset is the sub-object the method runs on: the caller adds it to the object's address to find that sub-object (Medium; inferred from its use).

Tables and kinds:

- **Hero kind table**: 94 records (High). The hero constructor installs it, and the hero constructor runs when the hero records are built in the pool setup (Medium-High). Its update record is the hero update of Behavior 3A (High). Hero locomotion and input routines are among its records (Medium-High).
- **Base character kind table**: 94 records (High). A setter called from the hero constructor installs it (Medium). The slot roles are given in Behavior 4.
- **Actor class = the motion-record class** (High): the 15-record table at 0x3167A0. Its constructor sets the record's class pointer at record offset 0x80 and initialises the sub-object at record offset 0xA0. The hero pool setup (two hero motion records) and the character loader (one 0x760-byte motion record per loaded character) build it in place, so every character's motion record (Layout, Player-slot runtime layout) has this class. Record 0 is the destructor, which switches the class pointer back to the base class at 0x322108. Its integrator is the motion-record integrator of Behavior 14A, item 6. The hero update reads a different class pointer, at object offset 0x164 (Medium).
- **Subsystem hook lists** (stride 4): these are not class tables. The subsystem init runner (SPEC-0026 game flow) calls each entry with the owner. A non-zero owner means init, a zero owner means teardown, and the runner passes a mode value of 0xFFFF (Medium-High). The effect group of SPEC-0034 is registered in these lists.

The kind tables of NPCs and enemies, other than the base table, are not identified (Open questions).

### 5. Melee hit scan (summary)

While a hero's attack is active (attack window bit 0x1 of the current frame, Behavior 12A), every tick the hero visits each entry of the global hittable list in order. A character whose id is already in the hero's 10-slot hit list is skipped. Otherwise the resolver runs (the hittable predicate, then the weapon test against the target's bone shapes (flat-ended finite cylinders, not capsules; the "capsule" wording in this spec is a naming slip, confirmed 2026-11-09), Behavior 12A); a nonzero result adds the target to the hit list and calls the on-hit callback, so one target is hit at most once per attack until the list is cleared. The resolver's damage and magic rules are in SPEC-0036. Confirmed by a full reading of the reach test and the resolver (High): the reach test stores the two weapon-tip points in the attacker (+0x390, +0x3A0) and clears 16 bytes at the target model's +0x18C0 on every call that passes the 65.0 distance gate; the resolver counts a swing as landed on a block, on positive damage or on a non-zero absorbed word (+0x8C0, read even when the reach test missed); the caller registers the target in the hit list only on a landed return. The hero's own walk over the list at +0x174 is a different walk: that list is cell list B of the hero's grid cell and holds world breakables, not characters (Behavior 16).

### 6. Health and the second pool

**Health (HP)**

- Damage d (integer, 0 or more) is applied as follows. If d is less than HP, HP becomes HP minus d. Otherwise HP becomes 0 and the death hook runs with its two flags set. The damage-applied notification runs after either branch, including when d is 0.
- Heal h raises HP by h and clamps HP to max HP; the heal-applied notification runs. Fill sets HP to max HP and runs the heal-applied notification.
- Set HP to v (High): if v is above max HP, the fill runs. Otherwise HP becomes v with no lower clamp; the heal-applied notification runs first when v is above the old HP, the damage-applied notification when v is below it, and none when v equals it.
- Death sequence (heroes): the hero's death hook first runs the death gate. A registered script death callback can skip the default death entirely. Otherwise the default death runs: the hero enters the death state, clears its hit list and clears all status timers, and if either hero fails the alive test, the party-wipe cue (sound event 3) plays. Full rules, including the repeated-death case, are in SPEC-0036 (Death). Game over follows a death through timer E when the partner cannot take over, and can also be set by a script (SPEC-0055, sections 4 and 8A). A death on deadly ground with a respawn entry index respawns the hero at that entry after more than 1.5 s, through the respawn at the start pose (Behavior 1C; SPEC-0055, section 8A); no area or scene setup runs after a death, so nothing heals the heroes on that path (SPEC-0055, section 2A).
- Regeneration (Resolved; High): a pool below T = int(max × 0.10) + 1 gains toward T and never above it. The rates are 2 HP per second and 1 MP per second, which on the 60 Hz tick come out as +1 HP on every tick whose pass counter is a multiple of 29 and +1 MP on every tick whose pass counter is a multiple of 59 (the counter is the global main-loop count, not counted from the moment the pool fell; SPEC-0036, Regeneration). Only the selected hero's update runs this step, so the parked hero never regenerates. The gates are exactly these: health regenerates when HP is above 0, the poison status (status 2) is not active and the hero is not in the dead base state (16); magic regenerates when the poison status is not active, with no HP test. Every other status (stun, curse, slow, strength, speed, the immunities, the two regeneration statuses) leaves the trickle running. Earlier wording that tied the gate to "any status active" was wrong: the status test is called with the poison index, so the question of an inverted "any status" test does not arise. The passive gain of a live petpet (SPEC-0047) is added to the trickle gain and applied to the pool whatever the gates say. Test vector: Roberta, max HP 120, HP 10, poison inactive, slow active: T = 13, gains on counters 0, 29 and 58, so HP is 13 after counter 58 and stays there; with poison active no gain falls at all; Tor, max MP 90, MP 0, no status: T = 10 and MP reaches 10 after counter 531.

**Second pool (MP)**

- Spend c: if c is less than MP, MP becomes MP minus c. Otherwise MP becomes 0 and the MP-empty hook runs. The MP-spent notification runs after either branch.
- Set v: a value above max MP fills the pool (MP = max MP, MP-gained notification). Otherwise MP becomes v with no lower clamp; the MP-gained notification runs when v is above the old MP, the MP-spent notification when it is below, and none when equal.
- Add a: MP rises by a and is capped at max MP; the MP-gained notification runs. Fill sets MP to max MP and runs the MP-gained notification.
- Set max to m: both max MP and MP become m. The MP-max-changed notification runs only when m differs from the old max.
- A spend happens only when an attack lands (SPEC-0036).

**HUD re-arm (High).** For the heroes, the seven value notifications of Behavior 4 (damage applied, heal applied, max HP changed, MP spent, MP gained, MP max changed and MP reached zero) are hero value-change hooks. Each one re-arms the HUD change-detector timer to 240 (SPEC-0023, section 4). So every accessor that changes a hero's value (set, deduct, add, fill, set pool, deduct pool, add pool, fill pool, set pool max) re-arms the timer. A damage that takes HP to 0 runs the death hook and then the damage-applied notification, so it re-arms too. A set to the unchanged value, and a set pool max with an unchanged max, call no notification and do not re-arm.

### 7. Status timers

- There are 14 timers, one per status (names and effects in SPEC-0036).
- A status is active when its timer is at least 1. A value of 0 or below, including -1, is inactive.
- Clearing all statuses sets every timer to -1. Death and teardown both clear them.
- How statuses are started, their default durations and their per-tick effects are specified in SPEC-0036 (hero combat). This spec only fixes the shared rule: active while the timer is at least 1, cleared to -1 on death.
- Tick rule (High; heroes and every region character). An apply writes the duration in whole seconds times the frame-rate word (60), so 5 s is 300 and 120 s is 7200; nothing is rounded. Each update of the character then runs one status step before its other work: every timer of 1 or more goes down by 1, and a timer of 0 or below is left as it is and runs the display's expire check. All per-status effects of that update read the lowered timers. So a status of D seconds acts on exactly 60 D - 1 updates, and a periodic effect that fires when the lowered timer is a multiple of N (poison 240, regeneration 60) fires on the values 60 D - N, 60 D - 2N, ..., N: 29 poison hits in 120 s, 59 regeneration gains in 60 s, never on the apply value and never on 0.
- First effect tick. For the hero, whose update runs after the region characters, the projectile pool and the attack manager (SPEC-0007, Order of work inside one tick), a status applied by an enemy strike or a pool shot acts on the apply tick itself; one applied by a level script or by an item used in the hero's update tail first acts on the next tick. For a region character, a status sent by the hero (the power step in the hero update, the Mote objects after the hero's animation pass) first acts in that character's next update, on the next tick.
- Expired versus cleared. A timer that runs out by counting stays at 0; a clear (one status or all of them, death, the swap of SPEC-0053, step 9) writes -1. Both are inactive, but a test of the form "timer is a multiple of 60" is true at 0 and false at -1, which matters for the magic-regeneration quirk of SPEC-0036 (status 13 reads the status-12 timer).
- Who counts. Only the selected hero's update runs the hero step, so a parked hero's timers do not count down; they are cleared when it next comes in (SPEC-0053). A region character's timers count only on ticks on which its update runs its status step.
- Test vectors (synthetic): stun applied by a class-17 strike on tick 500: the timer is 300 on the strike and 299 after the hero's step of tick 500; the hero is stunned on ticks 500 to 798 and free on 799, where the timer reads 0. The same stun applied by a script on tick 500: stunned on 501 to 799. Poison applied by a strike on tick 1000: hits on 1239, 1479, ..., 7959 (29), the timer reads 0 after the step of 8199.

### 8. Facing and turning

**Character facing: how to read it (High).** A placed character's facing is a direction vector in world axes (y up), stored as three f32 lanes x, y, z at +0x10 of its kind-3 record (SPEC-0006, "Character placement: position and facing decode"; SPEC-0056, "Facing test vectors"). Heading in degrees = atan2(x, z) reduced to 0 to 360, from +Z toward +X (+Z 0, +X 90, −Z 180, −X 270). At bind the motion record's three basis rows are filled from it: forward row B3 (+0x2B0) = the unit vector f; side row B1 (+0x290) = normalise((0, 1, 0) × f) = (f.z, 0, −f.x) over √(f.x² + f.z²), or (1, 0, 0) when that length is below 0.01; up row B2 (+0x2A0) = f × B1. All three rows have w = 0. The steering facing (locomotion block +0x40) gets the same vector with y set to 0, normalised; every later tick rebuilds B1 to B3 from that flat vector. The model's forward direction is B3, and a forward root step moves the body along +B3. Example: facing (1, 0, 0) is yaw 90, with B3 = (1, 0, 0), B1 = (0, 0, −1), B2 = (0, 1, 0). Port faults that make every character share one heading are listed in SPEC-0056, "Why every townsperson faces the same way".

- Facing is the forward direction of the body object's basis. The basis is written in step 4 (from the placement) and again by the ground probe in step 6 (from the surface normal).
- Locomotion writes the direction each tick (SPEC-0033). The hero's facing turn in SPEC-0033 has a limit set by a run-time word (SPEC-0033, Behavior 2); that limit belongs to the hero's locomotion, not to the shared runtime. The shared character runtime has no turn-rate limit, yaw smoothing or acceleration. A new facing takes effect in the same tick. The clean side must not add a turn rate to the base update. The only blend of facing in the movement routines is the steering rule of Behavior 8a, a fixed-weight blend that is not a rate cap.
- Non-hero characters (High): on each motion-record tick the body facing takes the steering heading at once (the character has no pad word, so the hero's blended turn of SPEC-0033, Behavior 2 never applies to it). Any smoothing comes from the weights that the steering routines pass when they set the heading (Behavior 8a and the aim rule below). The facing is kept instead of the heading in base states 16, 33, 51 and 55.
- Confirmed and extended 2026-11-09 (High; read in full from the basis builder and the turn-choice routine): (a) the three basis rows are NOT written while the owner is in base state 53 (0x35); (b) the facing basis builder takes forward = normalise(facing), up = (0, 1, 0, 0), side = normalise(up × forward), replaced by (1, 0, 0) when that cross product is shorter than 0.01, and up' = forward × side, all w lanes 0; (c) the facing the body takes each tick: the heading flattened (y = 0) and normalised and the body forward flattened and normalised; the body's own facing is kept in states 16, 33, 55 and 51 and when the moving flag is clear (and none of the immediate cases applied); the heading is taken at once in the states 25, 37, 28, 38, 41, 19, 13, 30, 18, 50, 52 and 27, when the strafe-family class query answers true and the owner is not in state 20, and when the owner's pad slot word (+0x148) is 0; state 21 takes the heading only when the class query answers 4 or 5 and the moving flag is set, otherwise the body facing. With |speed value| (locomotion block +0xC) above 0.01: c = max(dot(heading, body), turn limit), result = body × c + heading × √|1 − c²|, where the turn limit is the float RETURNED by the owner's class method (+0x15C), not a stored word (the hero's 0.95 is that method's value). With the speed value at most 0.01 the eased rule applies: d = the dot (above 1 gives 1; above about 0 gives 1 − (1 − d)², below about zero d unchanged; the zero reference is cos(π/2)), t = 1 − 0.003 X and floor = 1 − 0.3 X with X the tick length word, e = t − 3.3333 (t − d')² raised to the floor when d' < t, c = e, result = body × c + heading × √(1 − c²). (SPEC-0033 Behavior 2 holds the hero reading; the formulas are the same routine.)
- Aim rule (High for the rule; Medium-High overall). The turn call that AI, state and conversation routines make with a weight w (the class method that SPEC-0033, SPEC-0036 and SPEC-0038 write as a "rate") turns the stored facing c toward a unit direction t: v = w t + (1 − w) c; when dot(c, t) is below −0.99 (nearly opposite), 0.1 times the motion record's side axis is added to v; the facing becomes v divided by its length. It does not change the play rate. A weight of 1.0 snaps; smaller weights are partial blends, made once per call by the routine that wants the turn (for example 0.3 per tick for an NPC in a conversation and 0.1 for the hero; Behavior 22).
- The previously reported boss-only heading cap is resolved as a Cloud Beast lightning effect distance update, not a character-facing rule (High for the owner and rule). It takes the normalized direction from an effect origin to a target point, uses its fourth lane as the target distance, and updates the effect's current distance. When target distance is above current distance, it rises by at most 500.0 per tick; when target distance is at or below current distance, it snaps down immediately. Cloud Beast lightning initializes this per-object step from the lightning tuning word 500.0; this is the same per-update beam-growth rule specified in SPEC-0010, Lightning. This effect update does not turn the boss or change shared character facing.
- Lock facing (Behavior 20) is a hero routine with its own blend of 0.25 per tick. It is not a shared-runtime rule either. The tracked-point heading solve (Behavior 8b) is a separate routine and is not the shared rule either.
- A timed walk state advances its motion-record position by −(0, 1, 0, 0) × 1/180 per eligible tick, using the constant world-up vector (SPEC-0007). The resulting movement is downward along world Y; the update is gated by the state’s timer and class predicate (High; Open question 14).

### 8a. Locomotion steering (shared by two movement routines)

Confidence: High for the mode rule, the blend, the strafe reference side and the body-forward test (raised 2026-11-09 after the steering routine was read in full); Medium for the caller weights; Low for the stored spare word.

Two generic movement routines turn the character toward its move direction through one shared steering rule. The goal-move routine follows a goal point. The path-segment routine moves between the points of a path record, the point being chosen by a small index. Boss-owned routines in SPEC-0012, SPEC-0013, SPEC-0014 and SPEC-0019 call the generic goal-move routine, which reaches this helper on its active movement path. This does not establish that every such routine or state reaches it on every tick, and it does not make the boss-only heading step of Behavior 8 the same algorithm. Each call passes a unit move direction d and a blend weight w. The rule reads the character's locomotion mode m (a number kept in its movement block) and sets a target direction t:

- Modes 3, 4 and 8 (backward): t = -d. w is the caller's weight.
- Modes 5 and 9 (strafe, one side): t = normalise(up × d), where up is the constant world-up vector (0, 1, 0, 0) at 0x3932E0 (SPEC-0007). Let f be the motion record's forward basis B3 (Behavior 14C, stored at +0x2B0); this is the vector used for the dot test. If dot(f, t) is at most 0.9, w = 0.1. If it is above 0.9, w = 1.0 and the turn snaps.
- Modes 6 and 10 (strafe, other side): t = -normalise(up × d), the direction opposite modes 5 and 9, with the same weight rule.
- Any other mode: t = d. w is the caller's weight.

The new facing is the normalised blend v = w * t + (1 - w) * c, where c is the character's stored facing vector (the direction block that the movement block keeps, not the body forward). The stored facing becomes v divided by its length. Because the blend is a fixed fraction of the remaining difference each tick and the result is normalised, the angle change per tick is not constant. This is not a rate cap.
Flyer mode requests (High). When Can Fly is 1 or 2 and the grounded countdown is below 1, requests 0, 1 or 2 become mode 7; requests 3 or 4 become mode 8; request 5 becomes mode 9; request 6 becomes mode 10. Requests 7 or higher are stored as requested. If either flight condition fails, every request is stored unchanged. These destinations use the steering forms above: 7 forward, 8 backward and 9/10 opposite strafes. Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High; raised from Medium-High for the whole of 8a): the mode word is stored at +0xA3C; the flight test reads Can Fly at row +0x94 and the grounded countdown at +0xBF4; the steered heading is written to the locomotion block (+0x7CC) by the heading setter; the direction used for modes 5, 9, 6 and 10 is flattened (y = 0) and normalised; the weight is 0.1 raised to 1.0 when dot(steered, the CHARACTER'S OWN body forward) is above 0.9 (the body-forward query returns the forward of whatever character is passed, not the hero's); modes 0, 1, 2, 7 and any above 10 keep d with the caller's weight; the new facing is normalise((1 − w) × current heading + w × steered), with the current heading read from the locomotion block (+0x40).

Caller weights (High for the known branches). In the goal-move routine, when the current navigation-mesh lookup returns no triangle, it steers directly toward the fallback target with weight 0.1 (the boot word). With a valid mesh route, route-edge movement steers toward its computed edge point with the same 0.1 weight; after the current and target triangles match and the arrival/block gates do not fire, the settled branch steers directly toward the goal with weight 0.3 (fixed constant). A missing target triangle plays the idle choice and does not steer. The path-segment route-step routine passes the boot word 0.1 at its steering call. These are weight-selection conditions; they do not establish a constant turn rate.

Edge cases and open points for this rule are in the Edge cases section and Open questions 76 to 82.

### 8b. Tracked points and heading solve (steering objects)

Confidence: High for the mode dispatch rule and the disc value of its word; High that the settle family is dormant in the shipped build (the dispatcher has no caller, and the four-point ease has no caller); Medium overall for the dormancy. High for the two-point settle, the mode-0 solve and the four-point ease (all three read in full 2026-11-09; see the corrections under rules 2, 3 and 6); Medium for the heading solve; Medium-Low for the heading axis. The rules below are kept as a reference of what the routines do.

Layout. A steering object keeps its tracked points in a block attached to the object (object offset +0x240). The block is a table of 0x20-byte slots. A point is 16 bytes (four floats) and point n is stored at slot offset n times 0x20. Slots 8, 9, 0x10 and 0x11 hold four points; slots 0x13 and 0x14 hold two points. Other object fields used here: saved copies of the two points at +0x2A4 and +0x2C4; the second latch at +0x38C; the settled flag at +0x398; the mode-0 settle flag at +0x3A4; the first-tick flag of the four-point ease at +0x394; the ease count at +0x3E0; the ease sign word at +0x3D4; the turn-hold flag at +0xF3C; the heading quaternion output at +0xC0 (16 bytes). (Medium-High for the offsets; Medium for the sizes of the saved copies.)

Dispatch (High for the rule; dormant). Each call to the dispatcher reads the global mode word and runs one routine:
- 0: the mode-0 routine (rule 3).
- 1: the two-point settle with smoothing (rule 2). This is the disc value of the mode word.
- any other value: a short initialiser (rule 4).

Dormant (High for the absence of a caller; Medium overall). The dispatcher has no caller in the boot program, and no code pointer refers to it. Rules 2, 3 and 4 are reached only through the dispatcher, so none of them runs in the shipped build. No function-pointer table, method table, stored address, split address load or host export table names the dispatcher, and no level module calls it, so no data route reaches it either (High). Rule 6 has no caller and is dormant for the same reason. Rules 2, 3, 4 and 6 are kept below as reference text only. Rule 5 is not reached through the dispatcher and is not marked dormant by this finding.

Rule 2 (dormant). Two-point settle and smoothing (mode 1). Each tick, each of the two tracked points is compared with its saved copy. A point is moving when any axis differs from its saved copy by more than 0.02. A moving point moves its saved copy 15 percent of the way toward the current point (saved = saved + 0.15 x (current - saved)). A still point copies the current point into the saved copy. The updated saved copy of the first point is written to slot 0x13 and that of the second point to slot 0x14. If neither point is moving, the settled flag is set. Corrected 2026-11-09 (High): when the settled flag (+0x398) is already set on entry, the routine sets the second latch (+0x38C) to 1 and returns at once, with no comparison, no copy and no slot write; the latch is not "set instead" after the usual per-point work. The two points are handled one after the other (the first point's slot is written before the second is looked at); "moving" is any of dx, dy, dz beyond ±0.02 (strict); a moving point's saved copy is a four-lane lerp 0.15 of the way to the current point. The 0.02 test, the 0.15 smoothing and the slot order 0x13 then 0x14 are confirmed (High). (The per-axis test order has no effect.)

Rule 3 (dormant). Mode 0, two-point settle and angle. When the second latch is set, the routine clears the latch, the settled flag and the mode-0 flag, and copies both current points into the saved copies (a restart). The routine applies the same 0.02 test and 0.15 smoothing to its own settle flag. CORRECTED 2026-11-09 (High; the earlier text "writes no tracked-point slot" and "an angle limited to 1.7 radians" is withdrawn): this routine is a two-bone reach solve and it writes pose slots 0x13 and 0x14 on every call. Steps: if the second latch is set, clear the latch, the settled flag and the mode-0 flag (+0x3A4) and reload the two current points from the table (slots 0x13 and 0x14 quads at +0x260 and +0x280); set the table quads at +0x260, +0x280 and +0x2A0 to (0, 0, 0, 1) and refill the joint matrices; transform a fixed target point (1169.79, 76.2762, 1842.38; three constants in the boot image) into the root frame (inverse of the root axes, relative to the root position at +0x290); bone lengths a = |table +0x2D0|, b = |table +0x2B0|, c = |table +0x290|; shoulder = joint 0x13's position, elbow = joint 0x14's position; d = the shoulder-to-target distance clamped to 0.99 × (a + b + c); by the law of cosines (cos A = (p² + q² − r²) ÷ 2pq) the elbow angle = π − arccos(cos(c, a + b, d)) and the shoulder yaw = −arccos(cos(d, c, a + b)); the shoulder rotation = the quaternion that turns the joint-0x13-to-0x14 direction onto the target direction, times a yaw of −(shoulder angle), converted to Euler angles; if the target direction's y is below −0.1 and the horizontal dot of the arm and target directions is at most 0 and the Euler z is negative, z += π; z is clamped to at most 1.7 and x to [−1, 1] (the 1.7 limit is on this third Euler angle only), and the rotation is rebuilt from the Euler angles; the elbow rotation = a pure rotation about y by the elbow angle. Each of the two new rotations is compared with its stored state (+0x2A4 and +0x2C4) with the 0.02 test; while the flag +0x3A4 is 0 and the state is moving, state = lerp(state, new, 0.15), otherwise state = new; the states are written to slots 0x13 and 0x14. If neither moved, +0x3A4 is set to 1 and from then on the state snaps to the new value. (A dead comparison chain against 2.3 is left over from an empty branch.) This rule is dormant, so the angle's use and the order of the restart against the settle test have no effect on play. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): leave rule 3 unimplemented. (Medium-High for the settle; raised to High 2026-11-09 for the settle and the solve; the 1.7 limit belongs to one Euler angle, see above.)

Rule 4 (dormant). Initialiser (other mode values). It stores the two points and writes the first point to slot 0x13. (Medium-Low; no other use is known.)

Rule 5. Heading solve. Given a direction d and the object's forward vector f, the turn angle is the arc-cosine of their clamped dot product, with its sign taken from a cross-product test of the two. If the turn-hold flag is nonzero, the turn is zero. Otherwise the heading is stored as a quaternion whose four components are the cosine and the sine of half the turn angle, with the sine on the up axis (the component order and the axis are Medium-Low). The turn is the full angle of the call; this is not a rate cap. An orientation step reads two tracked-point slots and then calls this solve (Medium).

Rule 6 (dormant: no caller). Four-point ease (High after the 2026-11-09 read; earlier Low-Medium). CORRECTED 2026-11-09: the first-tick flag (+0x394) is tested the other way round from the earlier text. The routine first clears +0x3A0. When the flag is NONZERO (first tick of a cycle) the four saved state vectors (+0x34C, +0x36C, +0x30C, +0x32C) are copied from the table quads at +0x100, +0x120, +0x200 and +0x220, the ease count (+0x3E0) is set to −1 and the flag is cleared. Otherwise the four table quads are set to identity, the joint matrices are refilled, and for each mirrored pair (joints 8 and 9 with states +0x34C and +0x36C; joints 0x10 and 0x11 with states +0x30C and +0x32C) the targets are COMPUTED (the earlier "source of the targets is not confirmed" is resolved): the desired direction is (±0.7, −1, −0.8), or (±0.3, 0.8, 0) when the ease sign word (+0x3D4) is negative (the sign follows the side of the pair); the pose direction is the unit vector from joint 8's translation to joint 9's (joints 0x10 to 0x11 for the second pair); the first state = lerp(state, (rotation turning the pose direction onto the desired direction) × identity, rate); the second state = lerp(state, a yaw of 0, −1.57 (mirrored pair +1.57) about up, 0.025), or the identity yaw when the sign word is negative, and then the count rises by 1 per pair (so up to 2 per tick) to a cap of 20. The rate is 0.025 while the ease count is −1, otherwise the count times 0.0015. There is no 0.02 test. Finally the four states are written to slots 8, 9, 0x10 and 0x11.

Relation to SPEC-0013. Rule 2 is the two-point settle that SPEC-0013 (section 4.14) describes for the Gelert family, and its constants match. In the shipped build no Gelert state reaches rule 2, because its dispatcher has no caller, so SPEC-0013 section 4.14 is a reference only. Rule 3 has the same constants but does not report to a slot, and rule 6 has no settle test.

Edge cases for 8b:
- The settle family (rules 2, 3, 4 and 6) is dormant in the shipped build, so the cases below for those rules do not occur in play. They describe the routines only.
- A mode value other than 0 or 1 runs the initialiser (rule 4); it is not an error.
- A point whose difference is exactly 0.02 on every axis is still; the test is "more than 0.02".
- With the turn-hold flag set, the heading solve leaves the heading unchanged.
- A zero-length direction or blend: no guard is known (Open question 79 applies in the same way).

Test vectors for 8b:
62. Settle, moving point (dormant rule; synthetic): saved (0, 0, 0), current (0.03, 0, 0). The difference 0.03 is above 0.02, so the point is moving and its saved copy becomes (0.0045, 0, 0).
63. Settle, still point (dormant rule; synthetic): saved (1, 2, 3), current (1.01, 2, 3). The difference 0.01 is not above 0.02, so the point is still and its saved copy becomes (1.01, 2, 3). If both points are still on a tick, the settled flag is set; on a later still tick with the settled flag set, the second latch is set.
64. Heading (synthetic): direction (1, 0, 0), forward (0, 0, 1). The dot product is 0, so the turn angle is 1.5708 rad (pi over 2). The half-angle cosine and sine are both 0.7071 in magnitude. With the turn-hold flag set, the heading is unchanged.
65. Mode-0 angle limit (synthetic; corrected 2026-11-09): the limit applies to the third Euler angle of the shoulder rotation only: a computed z of 2.0 rad is limited to 1.7 rad, and an x of 1.4 is limited to 1.0; the dormant routine otherwise writes slots 0x13 and 0x14 as described under rule 3.
66. Mode dispatch (synthetic): mode word 0 runs rule 3, mode word 1 runs rule 2, and mode word 7 runs rule 4.

### 8c. Place a carried character: basis and position from a direction, an up vector and a point (High for the roles, Medium for the axis names)

This is the placement the carrier's carry step applies to the character it carries (SPEC-0005, Attach points and carrying): the grab-and-throw enemy's held hero (hero state 53) and the hero's carried Miamouse. Inputs: a direction D (the negated third axis of the attach frame), an up vector U (the second axis of the attach frame) and a point P (the attach frame's translation, plus the optional offset when the carrier's offset flag is set; the grab-and-throw enemy never sets it). It builds the carried character's orientation basis and writes it into two blocks: a frame block in the locomotion sub-object at offset 0x1880 onward, and a second copy at offset 0x290 onward of the same sub-object; it also sets the carried character's position. Steps:

1. Normalise the input direction.
2. Let d be the normalised direction (fourth lane 0) and test whether it is almost parallel to the reference up vector: the dot product is strictly above 0.9999 (the single-precision constant 0.9999, a fixed value in the code). If it is not, the second basis row is B2 = normalise(d × U) and the first is B1 = normalise(B2 × d). If it is, the cross product d × U would have no length, so the constant world-up vector a = (0, 1, 0, 0), the run-time block written at start-up (SPEC-0027), takes the place of U in the first axis: B1 = normalise(a × d) and B2 = normalise(d × B1). The third row is B3 = d. (High for the formulas and the threshold.)
3. The position is P minus 1.25 times B2 (fourth lane 1). The 1.25 is a fixed constant in the code; the word at 0x2DB400 is the follow camera's, so this offset is not that contact push word (Open question 161). The carried character's origin therefore sits 1.25 from the attach point, along the second basis row (High for the formula; Medium for which on-screen direction that row points in a given clip).
4. Write B1, B2, B3 to the two basis blocks (offsets 0x290, 0x2A0, 0x2B0 of the motion-record block and the same rows of the frame block at 0x1880 onward) and write the position to the carried character's motion record (position at +0x50) at once.
5. The carry step then clears the carrier's carry slot and offset flag, so the placement happens only on ticks on which the carrier set the slot again.

Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) by the carry basis routine: it normalises the direction, tests dot with the reference strictly above 0.9999 (the world up vector replaces the side), writes the rows to the model's block at +0x1880 and to the root matrix at +0x290, and the root position = position − 1.25 × row B2. A different direction-to-basis routine used by boss and enemy code (side = unit(direction × up), forward = direction, third row = −unit(side × direction), no near-parallel test and no 1.25 offset) is NOT this placement. Confidence: High for the placement (it replaces the earlier reading that this routine only builds an axis set), High for the 0.9999 threshold (strictly greater) and for the 1.25 being a fixed constant. Test vectors: direction (0, 1, 0) with reference up (0, 1, 0) has dot 1.0, above 0.9999, so the fallback applies; direction (0.01, 1, 0) normalised has dot 0.99995 and also takes the fallback; direction (0.02, 1, 0) normalised has dot 0.9998 and does not. Placement example: d = (0, 0, 1), U = (0, 1, 0), P = (10, 3, -4) is not parallel, so B2 = (-1, 0, 0), B1 = (0, 1, 0), B3 = (0, 0, 1) and the carried character's position is (10, 3, -4) - 1.25 x (-1, 0, 0) = (11.25, 3, -4).

### 9. Ground probe and surface basis

1. Probe from the feet, 8.0 units above (a fixed constant; the common feet probe, used by most call sites).
2. The probe uses the height-patch and cell rules of SPEC-0018. A result above -9.0e8 counts as a hit; -9.0e8 itself is a miss.
3. On a hit, the surface's type code is recorded on the character, and the basis is written from the surface (the surface normal orients the character).
4. On a miss, the character keeps its height and the default surface is used.
5. No gravity, fall, jump or landing is applied in this path. The hit result is the character's world height (SPEC-0018 says it is written to the character's y). For the hero, the drawn height then lags it (SPEC-0033, Behavior 7). For a region character placed by the ground placement routine, the probed height replaces the placement height only when the probe hits (result above minus 0.9e9) and the character's row Can Fly word is 0; otherwise the placement keeps the height it was given (Resolved, Open question 29; High).
6. Other probe start heights (High for the counts, Medium for the roles): in the hero ledge guard the call is made with the argument reference y + 10.0, and the probe adds its argument to the probe point's own y for its ceiling, so the ceiling read from the code is about twice the height plus 10, not feet + 10 (High, settled 2026-11-09 by two independent code readings: ceiling = probe y + reference y + 10 = 2 × reference y + 10 + forward.y × s, a behavior the rewrite must reproduce; open: an in-game check where y is negative, see Open questions; SPEC-0018); a start of 6.0 at one site, 5.0 at two sites and 2.0 at one site; and, for the motion-record ground follow only, a start 2.5 above the record's height. No probe starts 4.0 above a point (High).
7. **Surface basis blend (High for lane and branch behavior; Medium for basis naming).** Let slot 1 be the input surface vector. The helper runs only when its second float lane is strictly below 0.999 and the integer flag in slot 9 is zero. Equality at 0.999 skips the operation. When it runs, it forms and normalizes `0.9 × (slot 1 first lane embedded as an X-only vector) + 0.1 × (0, 1, 0, 0)`. Slot 0 is a consumed reference vector and is cleared; slot 1 remains the primary evolving frame vector, and slot 2 receives a derived perpendicular frame vector. When either gate fails, slots 0–2 are unchanged. The axes' domain labels and handedness are unknown.

### 10. Root motion

The stored pose-block root group is read by a per-tick helper path for the active hero and by a corresponding path for other active characters. In the examined routes, the canonical group is copied into a per-character request record and submitted through the lit model-draw path; this is a rendering consumer, not a character contact sweep or a world-position update (Open questions 1 and 3). Character travel comes from layer 0 root steps through the motion-record routes (Behaviors 14C and 14D).

1. **Accumulation.** The clip root record contributes to its accumulator on each sampled tick. When the looping end rule sets its wrap flag, the last key's root record is also added. The flag is set whenever the starting cursor is at least *F* − 1, including exactly *F* − 1 and the entire [*F* − 1, *F*) boundary-blend interval where the cursor is left unchanged; so this extra addition can occur before the cursor reaches *F*. The accumulator is cleared when a clip starts or changes and is not reset on wrap (SPEC-0005, Playback 3 and 5). Confidence: High for the flag timing/reset points; Medium-High for accumulation and the wrap addition. The delta-mode gate word and per-instance float writers remain open (Open question 15).
2. **Stored root.** The character keeps a root group in its pose block: a root vector, two neighbouring vectors and a tail vector. The writer of this group was not identified (Open question 1). In the examined active-hero and region-character routes, it is copied into a per-character request record and reaches a lit model-draw submission; this does not make it a contact sweep or a character position update (item 9; Open questions 1 and 3). The rendered object's identity and the group's full visual effect remain open. The five 64-byte blocks that SPEC-0005 once called root-sample blocks are per-weapon frames chosen by the damage bits, not root motion, so they are not its source (SPEC-0005, Weapon frame blocks; High). Character travel comes from layer 0 root steps (Behaviors 14C and 14D). Confidence: High that the group exists and is read each tick; Medium-High for this rendering consumer.
3. **Gate.** The per-tick root step does nothing unless the character's pose update has run in this tick (a flag equal to 1). Confidence: High.
4. **Canonical group.** The request receives the group in the order: second neighbour vector, first neighbour vector, the root with its sign inverted, and the tail vector. The sign inversion is applied before the group is copied into the per-character draw request. This establishes the submitted frame's input sign, but not the final visible effect of that sign in the rendered model. Confidence: Medium-High for the order and inversion.
5. **Not root motion: held-item scale.** The 1.6 and 2.6 scales belong to the hero's held-item draw (Behavior 12); no root step uses them. Before an item model is drawn in the hand, the three axis rows of its 4 by 4 frame (not the position row) are multiplied by s, where w is the width of the item model along x (the absolute difference of the x values of the two corner points of the model's bounds). The branch is chosen by the draw list, not by any root state: for the record of the first list s = 1.6 / w, with no cap (1.6 is a fixed program constant); for the record of the second list s = min(0.6, 2.6 / w) (2.6 and 0.6 are the boot data words of Where it lives). The first list's record is skipped when the hero's slot index is 1 (Behavior 12, Order and lists). So every hand-held item is drawn at a fixed width whatever the size of its model (High).
6. **Not root motion: held-item frame.** The 64-byte group that the held-item draw writes into each 112-byte record is the item's hand frame, taken from the hero's body (or the variant with one row negated for one inventory setting), and drawn with the character's lights (Behavior 12). It is not a root group and it does not move the character (High).
7. **Predicted path.** The motion object predicts up to 200 samples per tick, each the base point plus the sample index times the motion vector. No rotation is applied. Confidence: High.
8. **Facing.** The root group of this step is not rotated by the facing basis. Confidence: Medium-High (absence). The travel of the heroes and of all other characters is a separate path that does rotate its root step by the facing basis (Behaviors 14C and 14D; High).
9. **Position.** This root step does not write the character's world position. For the heroes the position store is the root-step travel of Behavior 14C, and for every other character the same travel through its motion record (Behavior 14D) (High). Confidence: High for the absence in this root step.
10. **Return value.** The held-item draw returns the sum of the draw counts of its item draws; its caller does not use it. Confidence: High.
11. **Pose-updated flag.** Resolved (Medium-High): the flag is the ready word of the locomotion sub-object (+0xFA0), not a word that is rewritten every tick. It is set to 1 once, straight after the first full pose build of a placement (the hero's spawn, the post-load placement, and the incoming hero of a hero swap) and cleared when the hero is parked (the outgoing hero of a swap, and the act-start and save-load resets). While it is 0 the animation-object update (the third call of the hero order), the root step of item 3 and the hero's record tick all do nothing, which is why the parked hero is never updated. For the selected hero it stays 1 from placement to the next swap, including ticks with a pending clip switch. (Code check, High: the only locomotion routine that writes 0 to this word is the stick-steer routine of SPEC-0033 "Path A", which nothing calls, so there is no live conflict.) Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): treat it as "this hero is placed and selected"; set it when the hero is placed or swapped in and clear it when swapped out.
12. **Accumulator reset points.** Resolved: the root accumulator is cleared when a clip starts or changes and is not reset on wrap. The looping wrap flag is set from cursor *F* − 1 onward, even when the cursor remains in [*F* − 1, *F*); on every flagged tick, the sampler also adds the last key's root record to the accumulator (SPEC-0005, Playback 3 and 5; High for flag timing/reset points, Medium-High for accumulation).

### 11. Move checks, character blocking and contact separation

The state-18 move-check callers pass the movable-block record as the mover. Its +0x68 points to its type-11 source entry, and that entry's +0x10 is the authored size/clearance used by these move probes; it is not a hero motion-record body radius (High for the caller and pointer relationship; Medium-High for the source-field role). The footprint probe uses half that size plus 0.1 in its ahead distance (confirmed 2026-11-09, High: the ahead point is position + direction × (step + size ÷ 2 + 0.1) with the size read at +0x10 of the block's source entry; with the guard flag at +0x9C set the ahead point must be over ground; the second point from the selected hero is at (step + 0.75) × 1.1 along the direction, passes when it is over ground, else the ground heights 8 units above at the character and at that point may differ by at most 0.75). The separate character/world variant uses the full size plus 1.5 to place its ahead point, then performs world-segment and actor-box checks when its move flag requests them. The point-vs-box test uses the distinct fixed 0.25 margin. For the footprint variant, the second point is built from the selected hero's current position (not the block's) as that position plus the direction times (distance + 0.75) times 1.1, and the fallback compares ground heights at the block position and at that second point against the 0.75 step limit (SPEC-0018, Move check; High).

**Footprint variant (used for ground-level moves).**

1. Ahead point: position + direction × (distance + 0.5 rb + 0.1).
2. For a movable block, the footprint word is its footprint patch (+0x9C; SPEC-0048, Push start, step 2). It is non-zero on every disc block, so steps 3 to 5 always run for blocks. Only a zero word would skip them, and that does not occur on disc.
3. The ahead point must pass the footprint list test (SPEC-0018, candidate list test, applied to x and z). Failing it blocks the move.
4. Second point: R + direction × (distance + 0.75) × 1.1, where R is the current position of the selected hero (the position in its motion record). If the footprint list test passes at the second point, the move is allowed at once.
5. Otherwise, take the ground heights with an 8.0-unit ceiling lift, first at the position of the moved object and then at the second point. R is the current motion-record position of the selected hero, and the second point is built from R. A difference above 0.75 blocks the move; otherwise it is allowed. Confidence: High for the probe positions and order; Medium-High for the surrounding rule. The step limit 0.75 is a boot data word (High, SPEC-0018). The factors 0.5, 0.1, 0.75 (in the second point) and 1.1 are code constants.

**State-18 movable-block variant (used against other blocks and the world).** The caller passes a move flag M. Zero means a real move; nonzero means a test that does not look at the world or other blocks.

1. Ahead point: position + direction × (rb + 1.5). Confidence: Low-Medium for the distance.
2. If the footprint patch is set (every disc block; SPEC-0048): if the ahead point passes the footprint list test, the height step below is skipped. If it fails, take the ground heights under the block and at the pull-back point P of SPEC-0033 Behavior 15A, step 5 (not at R). A difference above 0.75 blocks the move. (Unlike the footprint variant, a failed list test here does not block by itself.) Confidence: Medium.
3. Two points near the character along the direction form a segment.
4. If M is zero: the segment is tested against the world with the segment query of SPEC-0018. Any hit blocks the move. Confidence: Medium.
5. When M is zero, each other active movable-block record in the world list (count +0x580C, records at +0x5810 with 0xB0-byte stride; active word +0x74) is tested; the selected mover is skipped by identity. Its +0x68 source link leads to a type-11 entry whose +0x20 points to the block's authored kind-4 rectangle primitive (SPEC-0028, SPEC-0048). Before the point-vs-box test, the primitive center (+0x30) is written from the block position at +0x40 and then overwritten from +0x50, so the second position is used. The primitive half-extents (+0x24, +0x28, +0x2C) and axes (+0x40, +0x50, +0x60) remain from the authored type-11 box record; they are not scratch defaults or character-body dimensions. The test uses radius 0.25 (boot data word at 0x2EB2C4); a hit blocks the move. Confidence: High for the list, source pointer, center write order and shape-data source.
6. Otherwise the move is allowed.

**Blocking, not separation.** A refused move gives no separation. The move checks above only allow or block; no separation distance is computed in any of them.

**Separation by the character contacts (High for the lists and routing; Medium-High for the push).** Separation from the world runs once per tick inside the motion-record driver of each character (Behavior 14A, item 1), not in a separate pass. Which sets a character uses depends on the driver branch of its class (Behavior 14A, item 1): the heroes use all three below, region characters use only cell list B (with the navmesh confinement step), and the petpet uses its own frame-record step and cell list A. The grid cell under the character's motion record (Layout, grid cell pointer) gives three sets: the cell's frame records (per-record contact), cell list A (primitives, count at cell +0x00 and items from +0x04) and cell list B (second-list trigger objects of SPEC-0028 section 13, count at cell +0x10 and items from +0x14). Cell list A goes through the shape dispatcher of SPEC-0018 with filter 0: kind 1 plane-and-actor, kind 2 cone, kind 3 cylinder, kinds 4 and 7 a box push (shape value +0x2C above 0; it does nothing for a hero in state 21, 38 or 41) or a zero-radius contact (0 or below), and kind 8 plane-and-actor only for the player object. Cell list B goes through the contact walk (kinds 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7; never kind 8). In the ground step of the record tick, each nearby moving object's six primitive lists are walked through the same dispatcher in that object's frame (the moving-object step). On an overlap the contact response moves the character by depth times direction (SPEC-0018). The filter value 1 is used only by the script call CollideEntityWithTriggers (SPEC-0030); with filter 1 the dispatcher handles a primitive only when its class bit 3 (kinds 1, 2 and 3), bit 8 (kinds 4 and 7 with a value of 0 or below) or bit 5 (kinds 4 and 7 with a positive value) is set.

**The 1.25 pass is the camera's.** The pass that SPEC-0018 calls the contact pass (cell list A dispatch with kinds 1, 3, 4 and 7, the push radius 1.25 at 0x2DB400, the movable-block box list, the contact-normal latch and the saved position) is the follow camera's eye collision pass of the mode-0 camera update (SPEC-0027). It moves the camera eye, not a character (High). Character-to-character push (High for gates and side-selection mechanics; Medium for exact semantic labels): the subject is skipped in action states 33 and 53 and must pass the collidable getter; state 16 fails that getter, while other states require its word (+0x284) to be nonzero. In the two hero slots, candidates must be active, pass the same collidable getter, and fail the additional action-state predicate; the routine does not explicitly skip a candidate whose identity equals the subject. For region-character subjects, candidates must be active, differ from the subject and pass the collidable getter; this branch has no additional action-state predicate. Both branches require centre distance strictly below 20 units and overlap between vertical body cylinders (radius +0x08, height +0x0C). The region-character branch pushes the subject along the overlap direction. For a hero subject, the response normally targets the subject; it targets the candidate when the subject's touch latch (+0x238) is already set, or when the latch is clear and the candidate's class callback returns nonzero, or when the subject is in states 50, 52, 21 or 41. A nonzero candidate word (+0xC54) cancels the latch-based and callback-based selection together, but not the four-state override. When the candidate is selected the response direction is negated; otherwise the overlap direction is retained. The candidate's touch latch (+0x238) is set after every overlap, whichever body moved. The state-53 selection fallback exists in the body but cannot be reached because the outer gate excludes that state. The word +0xC54 is the townsfolk flag (Resolved, High): a townsfolk candidate always cancels the latch and callback selection, so a hero that overlaps a townsfolk is pushed itself, not the townsfolk, unless it is in state 50, 52, 21 or 41. The callback's design purpose is not encoded anywhere (its enemy rule is given below). The candidate list is the region character list only, so the heroes are not in the list. On the hero path a candidate must also be not hidden. The same contact response runs with the full overlap depth and, on the hero path, with the floor flag set. The touched flag is set on the pushed body. For the hero as subject, its earlier wall contacts and its own earlier pushes this tick also set the flag, so the first overlapping candidate pushes the hero (when no wall was touched first) and later ones are pushed instead. The ordinary enemy class callback is true only in attack mode 2, sub-state 2 with the attack slot's damage-special bit 0x08 set; the base character and hero tables return zero. The full rule is in SPEC-0033, Behavior 53. Confirmed by a full reading of the push routine (High): it is called from the motion tick after the touch latch and the floor flag have been cleared; the overlap test is a finite flat-ended cylinder test (centre, axis, radius and length of each body; corrected 2026-11-09, High: not capsule against capsule; with both bodies upright it takes the parallel branch, so the overlap needs the vertical offset of the centres at most the half-height sum and the horizontal distance at most the radius sum, all inclusive) that returns a depth and a direction (in the parallel branch the direction is the unit horizontal direction from the other body toward the subject and the depth is that distance minus the radius sum, at most 0), and the response is the slide response with the flags (1, 1, 0); the body centre is the reference point plus the up axis times half the height; a region-character subject pushes itself along the direction and then calls an empty debug hook with the record code 8 (no effect); a short counting loop in that branch (identity plus 1 below 3) never runs for an identity of 2 or more. The state numbers 33, 53, 50, 52, 21 and 41 above are 0x21, 0x35, 0x32, 0x34, 0x15 and 0x29. Reconfirmed 2026-11-09 against the readable form of the push routine (High; Medium-High parts of this paragraph raised to High): the subject is skipped in states 0x21 and 0x35 or when not collidable; for a hero (character id below 2) the candidates are the active characters of the game's character list (count at g_Game +0x148, list at +0x14C) whose record is non-null, collidable and not hidden, with body centres (reference point + 0.5 × height × up) less than 20.0 apart, tested with the flat-ended cylinder overlap using the other's radius and height and the subject's; on an overlap "the other moves" when the subject's touch latch is clear and the other's class method (+0x174) answers non-zero, or when the latch is already set; the selection is cancelled when the other is a townsfolk, but the state-based selections (hero states 0x32, 0x34, 0x15 and 0x29) are applied after that cancel and still win; the slide response is applied to the other body with the negated normal, or to the subject with the normal, with the flags (1, 1, 0), and the other's touch latch is set in both cases; for a character id of 2 or more every other collidable record within 20.0 that overlaps pushes THIS body along the normal, then an empty hook runs. The "subject in state 0x35" arm is dead because the routine returned earlier. The motion driver passes an extra position argument to the push routine and to the tunnel guard (Behavior 14A, item 8), neither of which reads it: both read the record's own fields.

Synthetic response vector: overlap direction n = (0, 1, 0). A region-character subject is displaced along n. For a hero subject with candidate-selection false, the subject is displaced along n; when a state override selects the candidate, the candidate is displaced along −n. The move checks above block such moves, and the radius separation of Behavior 11A is a separate routine.

**The character push rule: direction, depth and who can push the hero (from the code, not run in the original; High for the rule and its sign, Medium for the multi-tick result).** Participants: in the hero's pass every region character that is collidable (its collidable word is nonzero and it is not in the dead state 16), is not hidden by the hidden test, and whose centre is less than 20 from the hero's centre is a candidate. Centres are the origin plus half the body height along the up axis. No class or flag marks a body as a platform, and there is no class filter. The hero is skipped altogether in states 33 and 53 and when his own collidable word is zero. Both bodies are vertical cylinders (radius 1.175, height 3.5 for the hero and for a class 20 creature, so the half heights add to 3.5). The overlap test for parallel axes applies when the dot product of the two axes has an absolute value above 0.99. Let L be the horizontal distance between the axes and h the hero's feet height above the other body's origin. There is no contact when L is above rA + rB (2.35 for two bodies of 1.175) or when the axial offset between the centres exceeds 3.5 (h outside −3.5 to 3.5). Otherwise:
- Default: the push is horizontal. The normal is the unit vector from the hero's axis to the other body's axis, and the depth is L − (rA + rB), zero or negative.
- Vertical caps, only when the hero's axis is within 0.95 of straight up (its Y above 0.95): when the other body is above the hero and h is below −3.1 (hero head within 0.4 of its underside), and L is below the hero radius 1.175, the normal is (0, 1, 0) and the depth is 3.5 + h (under 0.4); when h is above 3.1 (feet within 0.4 below the other body's top) and L is below 1.175, the normal is (0, −1, 0) and the depth is 3.5 − h (under 0.4). For L of 1.175 or more, or h between −3.1 and 3.1, the horizontal result stands. The direction therefore depends on h and L only, never on velocity.
- Response: the hero's position is moved by the normal times the depth. Because the cap normal points toward the other body, a hero over a body's top is pulled down into it, not held up. The floor flag (+0x150) is set only when the normal's Y has an absolute value above 0.9, that is in a cap case; the flag makes the ground gap read 0 for that tick and the jump machine counts it as landed. The response exits with no change when the depth is 0.05 or less and the hero is not moving into the normal (velocity plus step along the normal not negative, force along it not negative). If the velocity (plus step) points into the normal, its part along the normal is removed. In a cap case the velocity and force are zeroed unless the vertical velocity is at least 0.1 and the speed at least 0.1.
- Who is moved: the hero by default; the other body instead (normal negated) in the cases listed above. An exact L of 0 gives a zero normal and no move (Low).
- Consequence: there is no stable standing on a body. With a cap, h between 3.1 and 3.5 and L under 1.175 pulls the hero down by up to 0.4 per tick with a floor-flag landing; once h is 3.1 or less the horizontal rule shoves him out to 2.35 from the axis; above 3.5 there is no contact and he passes over the top with no support. A standing jump (apex about 3.2 above take-off) with the other body's origin on the take-off ground has h above 3.1 for roughly ticks 20 to 27 of the jump when L is under 1.175.
- Test vectors (other body's origin (0, 0, 0), both radii 1.175, heights 3.5): hero feet (1.0, 0, 0): horizontal, normal (−1, 0, 0), depth −1.35, hero ends at x = 2.35. Feet (0.5, 3.3, 0): normal (0, −1, 0), depth 0.2, feet go to 3.1, floor flag set, velocity zeroed unless rising at 0.1 or more. Feet (0.5, 3.46, 0): depth 0.04, at most 0.05, so with no motion into the normal nothing changes and no flag is set. Feet (1.5, 3.3, 0): L is 1.5, horizontal, depth −0.85, hero to x = 2.35 at the same height. Feet (0.5, 3.0, 0): horizontal, depth −1.85, hero shoved to x = 2.35 and then falls. Other body's origin 3.3 above the hero's feet, L = 0.5: normal (0, 1, 0), depth 0.2, hero moves up 0.2, floor flag set. Feet 3.6 above the origin: no contact.
- Open: a class 20 creature queues two contact shapes into the hero during its sub-steps 1 and 2; where the hero's mover consumes them is not known.
