**A boss and the hero in the character push (High for who is moved and for the absence of any guard; High that the Ixi Chieftain answers no to the class check and that it is not hidden).** A boss is a region character, so its own push pass (driver branch 1) walks the region-character list only. The heroes are not in that list. A boss is therefore never displaced by the hero and never reacts to it in its own pass; its position, velocity and blocked flag are untouched by an overlap with the hero. The overlap is handled entirely in the hero's pass (driver branch 0), where the boss is one candidate.
- *Who is moved.* In the hero's pass the hero is the moved body unless one of the selecting conditions above holds. For a hero that stands (states 1, 48 and the landing that follows) beside a boss that is not in a charging attack with the damage-special bit, none holds: the hero's touch latch is clear (it is cleared at the start of the hero's driver and set only by an earlier wall contact or an earlier push of the same driver), the boss's class check answers no (the bosses do not run the common enemy's attack mode 2), and the hero's state is not 50, 52, 21 or 41. The hero is then displaced by the whole overlap (radius sum minus the centre distance, horizontally, along the line from the boss centre to the hero centre), with no strength factor, in one tick. The boss's touch latch is set after every overlap.
- *When the boss is moved instead.* If the hero is walking or turning (states 50 or 52), climbing (21 or 41) or has touched a wall earlier in the same driver, the boss's motion record is displaced instead, by the same full overlap along the line from the hero to the boss. That is a direct position change: it raises no blocked flag, so a scripted run does not end because of it. If the push left the boss off its mesh, the next tick's navmesh step restores it and raises the blocked flag then (Medium).
- *Tick order.* The world runs the region characters first (a boss: its update, then its motion tick, whose driver clears its touch latch at the start), then the hero (update, then its driver with the push). The latch the hero's push sets on the boss is therefore the one the boss's update reads on the next tick. A boss behavior that needs "body contact" reads it one tick after the overlap.
- *What does not keep a hero in place.* There is no edge guard for the heroes (the guard of Behavior 14E belongs to the region characters and the hero classes answer 0), no navmesh confinement for the heroes, and the push writes the hero's position directly with no ground or wall test. Push flags are not cleared by the knock: the hero's own update sets the collidable word to 1 at the start of every tick, and the knocked state (48) clears it only on a tick in which the hero's vertical speed is above 12.0 (the knock's 4.0 up does not reach that). A lying or landing hero is therefore pushed like a standing one. Only wall panels hold a hero: they resolve earlier in the same driver, set his touch latch, and so make the boss the moved body (a hero pinned against a wall pushes the boss back and the boss's run stays in place, a stuck run, not a stop).
- *Deflection (why a carried hero is released).* A hero carried ahead of a boss that runs straight at his centre slides sideways, because the push acts along the line between the centres. With R = the radius sum (boss 2.0 + hero 1.175 = 3.175), boss speed b per tick and a small lateral offset e0 between the boss's line and the hero, the angle t of the hero from the line follows tan(t / 2) = tan(t0 / 2) x exp(b x n / R) after n ticks of contact (t0 = e0 / R for small e0). The boss passes him when t reaches 90 degrees. With b = 20 / 60 per tick, the boss's travel after the first contact until it passes is about 16.3 units for e0 = 0.01, 11.5 for 0.05, 7.3 for 0.2, 4.6 for 0.5, 2.6 for 1.0 and 0.75 for 2.0. The same lateral offset decides the hero's final side: the hero ends on the side away from the boss's offset. With e0 = 0 exactly the hero is carried until the run ends.
- *Test vector (pack 864, mode 1, the Ixi Chieftain's run past against a standing hero).* Hero at the fight-start place (-839.08, 40.6, 2282.72); boss radius 2.0, hero radius 1.175, contact distance 3.175. The run past starts with the boss 5.998 east of the hero on a line 0.05 off his centre (boss at x = -833.082, z = 2282.77) and moves 0.3334 west per tick. Tick 8: boss x = -835.749, distance 3.33, no overlap. Tick 9: boss x = -836.082, distance 3.00, overlap 0.175: the hero moves to (-839.257, 2282.717) and the boss's touch latch is set. Tick 10: the boss's update sees the latch with the hero within 7.5 and not in state 48: hero velocity 3.0 away plus 4.0 up, state 48; the push still acts every tick (hero at x = -839.59). Tick 20: boss x = -839.749, hero (-842.919, 2282.591). Tick 30: boss x = -843.082, hero (-846.211, 2282.23). Tick 33: boss x = -844.082, hero (-847.168, 2282.023), past the west outline of the arena's ground (about x = -846.9 at that z, patch outlines have no blocking wall for a hero standing at ground level), so he leaves the ground and drops about 12.6 onto the deadly ground at height 28.0 (no respawn entry: game over). The run past does not end (the hero stays within 7.5 and the boss is not blocked) until the boss's own navmesh edge stops it. With e0 = 0.5 the hero is deflected to 2.7 south of the line and released after 4.6 units, and does not fall.
- Resolved (High): the Ixi Chieftain's class check answers no and its hidden test is false, so the hero's pass treats it as an ordinary candidate and only the hero is moved. The class check is true only when the common-enemy attack mode word (+0xAF8) and sub-state word (+0xB2C) are both 2 and the attack-slot bit 8 is set; only common-enemy code writes those words, and the Chieftain's controller never does, so both stay 0. The hidden test reads the monster row's Display in Default word, which is 1 on the Chieftain's row (the row with 300 health, radius 2.0 and height 5.0); with a nonzero value and the ambush word at its creation value of -1, the test is true only in AI mode 14 with the walking-home phase word at 1, a state the Chieftain never enters. The words 2 and 2 are written only by the common enemies' attack chooser and counter routines, which run only inside the common-enemy think loop that the Chieftain does not use; no shared hook (take-hit, death, status) and no script call sets class state 8 or 14 on it. The test has a second branch that compares the current animation with the hide animation (9 for the Chieftain's record, whose cue word and emerge selector are both 0). The Chieftain's strafe-close walk also uses animation 9, but that branch is reached only when the ambush word is 0, and it holds -1 from creation, so the Chieftain is never hidden while it walks.

### 11A. Object separation by radius (dormant; High)

This is not a character step. The routine belongs to the world's +0x29F0 object pool (SPEC-0028, section 15.1), whose add routine is never reached, so it never runs in play and a clean implementation may omit it. Its records are 0x160-byte pool records, not characters; the radius r is at record +0x140. The rule, for completeness:

1. Within one record's update, the record (self, radius r_s) is tested against records 0 and 1 of the pool, skipping itself and disabled records.
2. With centre distance d below r_s + r_o, the overlap is o = r_s + r_o − d, and u = unit(self − other).
3. Self moves by u × o × r_o / (r_s + r_o), and the other record by −u × o × r_s / (r_s + r_o). So the smaller object moves more.

The full per-record update (drift, ground lift, separation, leash) and the walk quirk of the pool are in SPEC-0028, section 15.1.

### 11B. Movable-block pair relation test (High for scan and test forms; selector call sites open)

A scan checks whether any other movable-block record passes one of four pair tests. The scan walks the region's world movable-block array, whose count is at region +0x580C and whose 0xB0-byte records start at +0x5810; it skips the record being tested. Its direct caller is the movable-block movement check. The helper's size r comes from the first record's +0x68 source pointer and the pointed type-11 entry's +0x10 authored size/clearance (SPEC-0040 OQ28). It is not the +0x140 object radius used by the separate, dormant radius-separation rule in Behavior 11A; that rule operates on 0x160-byte pool records, while this scan uses 0xB0-byte movable-block records. The 0.05 side margin and 0.5 depth slack apply to the authored clearance r. The four test forms use selector inputs in the range 0 to 3; the table that maps the push direction and the held face to a form, with the forward reach of r + 0.5 or 1.5 (r + 0.5), is in SPEC-0048 (Second clearance test).

For the one-sided side-band test with forward Z:

1. Offsets: dx = x(b) − x(a) and dz = z(b) − z(a), where x is the first and z the third component of the position vector at offset 0x40 of each record (High; Open question 165).
2. Depth test: dz is at least 0 and at most r + 0.5.
3. Side window, from the flag: flag 0 gives [dx, dx + r]; flag 1 gives [dx − r, dx]. This window must overlap the open interval (−(r − 0.05), r − 0.05).

The other forms are: (1) centered X side band with forward-Z depth, requiring |dx| < r − 0.05 and dz ≥ 0, with the upper dz limit selected as either r + 0.5 or 1.5(r + 0.5); and (2) axis-swapped forward-X with a one-sided Z interval, requiring dx ≥ 0, interval overlap with (−(r − 0.05), r − 0.05), and dx ≤ r + 0.5. The two side-selector values choose [dz, dz + r] or [dz − r, dz]. Record order, axis choice and side orientation depend on the direction and face combination, which SPEC-0048 lists in full (Second clearance test). Confirmed and refined 2026-11-09 (High for the three forms found; the size r is the float at +0x10 of the pointed block entry): the one-sided forward-Z test (the numbered list above) matches the one-sided routine read (dz ≥ 0, window [dx, dx + r] for flag 0 or [dx − r, dx] otherwise overlapping (−(r − 0.05), r − 0.05), dz ≤ r + 0.5); form (1) matches the centred-band routine (|dx| < r − 0.05, dz ≥ 0, dz ≤ r + 0.5, or 1.5 (r + 0.5) when the mode is 1). A THIRD routine exists that the earlier text did not list: the centred-band test with the axes swapped (forward X: dx ≥ 0, |dz| < r − 0.05, dx ≤ r + 0.5, or 1.5 (r + 0.5) when the mode is 1). The one-sided forward-X form (2) as written (a Z interval with forward X) was NOT found in this read; the forward-X one-sided twin of the one-sided forward-Z routine must be elsewhere or the swapped centred-band routine is what the "axis-swapped" wording describes (open; see Open questions 219).

The scan returns false on the first passing pair and true when no other record passes. The margins 0.05 and 0.5 are code constants.

### 12. Held-item draw records (High)

The 112-byte records and their draw step belong to the hero's held-item draw: the hand-held items are drawn as lit models with the character's own lights. Nothing in this behavior collides, moves or blocks a character, and nothing is written back to the character (High).

**Draw record.** Two equipment draw lists of the hero's inventory each hold one 112-byte record per hero slot index (0 or 1); the record used is list base + slot index × 112. Layout:

| Offset | Size | Field | Writer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | u32 | the item's model; zero when the record has no model | inventory |
| 16 to 79 | 64 | the item's world frame: a 4 by 4 matrix (three axis rows and a position row) | the held-item draw |
| 80 | u32 | active flag, set to 1 by the held-item draw | the held-item draw |
| 84 | u32 | draw mode word: a lookup result chosen per list, written by the held-item draw each pass | the held-item draw |
| 88 | u32 | draw list word: the shared draw list the model is bound to, written by the held-item draw each pass | the held-item draw |
| 96 | s32 | item count | inventory |

**Owned test.** A record is drawn only when its item count is above 0 and its model word is nonzero (the item-owned test of SPEC-0021). Otherwise nothing is written and the draw count is 0.

**Frame.** The frame is the hero's hand frame from its body (an alternative frame with one row negated is used for one inventory setting), with the item scale of Behavior 10, item 5 applied to the three axis rows.

**Draw.** With the active flag set, the draw binds the model to the draw list word and submits one lit model draw (SPEC-0046) with a colour scale of 1.0, the frame, the mode word and the character's light context (Layout; Behavior 24). It returns the draw count of that submission.

**Order and lists (High).** Each pass, for the focus hero: the first list's record is drawn unless the hero's slot index is 1; then the second list's record is drawn when the slot index is 0 or an inventory flag word is nonzero. The draw counts are summed and returned; the caller ignores the sum.

**Relation to SPEC-0018.** This behavior has no collision geometry. The box sweep of SPEC-0018 is a separate routine and is not called here.

**Attack volumes.** The attack volumes are finite cylinders and spheres tested against the target's hurt shapes (Behavior 12A).

### 12A. Hurt shapes, attack windows, weapon volumes and the hit list

Confidence: High for the layouts, the bit gates, the row fields, the hit list and the weapon-against-bone test (exact decision table in SPEC-0018); Medium-High for the roles; Medium for the lane mapping of the posed frames. The overlap test is the finite-cylinder test of SPEC-0018.

**Hurt shapes.** A character can be struck through one of two shapes:
- **Bone capsules** (melee weapons and shots). Each bone record of the character's skeleton bone table (SPEC-0005, flat type-18 chunk) has a base point at +0x30 (x, y, z, 1, bone-local), a length at +0x40 (f32) and a radius at +0x44 (f32). A bone with a length above 0 is a hurt capsule; a bone with length 0 or below is skipped. With P the bone's current world frame applied to the base point and Y the frame's local Y axis in world space, the capsule is a flat-ended finite cylinder of radius +0x44 and full length +0x40 with axis Y, running from P to P + length times Y (centre P + 0.5 times length times Y). In a weapon test it takes the second-shape role of SPEC-0018: a flat card facing the weapon, not a round solid. Bones are tried in index order; the first hit ends the test. On disc 1,355 bone tables hold 60,766 bone records, 5,093 of them with a length above 0; 579 tables have none, so those characters cannot be struck by melee or shots.
- **Body cylinder** (charm attacks and the class-0 world contact). A finite vertical cylinder with the motion record's radius (+0x08) and height (+0x0C). Charm attack walks centre it on the character position point, so its vertical extent is position.y ± height/2. The class-0 world contact instead runs it from the position up by the height (centre = position + half the height along up; SPEC-0018). This difference is intentional in the two callers (High).

**Attack windows.** Table B of a clip (SPEC-0005, one u32 per frame) carries the windows: bit 0x1 weapon 1, 0x2 weapon 2, 0x4 weapon 3, 0x8 weapon 4 and 0x10 weapon 5 active on that frame. The hero is attack-active when bit 0x1 is set on the current frame (refreshed at the start of each hero update); an enemy-kind character is attack-active when any of the bits 0x1 to 0x10 is set. The multi-frame and loop rules of the bit query are in SPEC-0005.

**Monster weapon volumes.** Weapon N (N = 1 to 5) is a finite cylinder whose radius is the monster row word at +4(N - 1) and whose full height is the row word at +0x14 + 4(N - 1); its centre is the anchor and its axis the Z row of attachment frame N - 1 (SPEC-0005 pose entries). The monster schema names these fields the other way round: the radius word is named "Weapon N Collision Length" and the height word "Weapon N Collision Radius" (a naming swap in the original). The schema value 40.0 is the upper clamp of these fields, not a default. On a tick, weapon N is tested only when its window bit 2^(N - 1) is set, against each bone capsule of the target with the static overlap test (no sweep); the first hit returns true. **The exact test (High; full decision table and test vectors in SPEC-0018, Finite cylinder tests).** The weapon is the first shape and is an exact solid round cylinder: radius = the row radius word, full height = the row height word, centre = the anchor, axis = the Z row of the frame as stored (not renormalised; its length is 0.90 to 1.0 for posed monsters, which moves grazing contacts by up to a tick). Each bone capsule is the second shape and acts as a flat card, not a round or boxed solid: the rectangle through the bone's axis that faces the weapon's centre, as long as the bone's hurt length and as wide as twice its hurt radius, centred at P + 0.5 times length times Y with Y the bone frame's second row as stored. A hit needs the card's edges to cross the weapon's surface in one of the patterns of SPEC-0018 (or, for a card wholly inside the weapon, a strictly smaller radius and length than the weapon); it is not a volume overlap, so a bone that is partly inside the weapon can still be missed and the box-corner reading (up to 41 percent more reach along diagonals) does not exist. The sideways orientation question is closed: the card turns to the weapon's centre and the weapon is round. A weapon of zero size is a point at the anchor and hits only when that point lies inside the bone's solid cylinder. Rows and disc values are in SPEC-0029. **Against the hero (High).** The target shape is the hero's own bone capsules, nothing else: Tor has nine (eight of length 0.367 and radius 0.367 on bones 0, 1, 2, 3, 24, 25, 27 and 30; one of length 0.823 and radius 0.431 on bone 4) and Roberta one (length 1.112, radius 0.436, bone 0), posed from the hero's bone frames of the previous tick (SPEC-0036, Hero body cylinder in the enemy hit tests). The anchor and axis of each weapon are rows of the attacker's attachment frame N - 1 as its last animation pass posed it: the anchor is the frame's translation row (offset 0x30 of the 4 x 4 block) and the axis its Z row (offset 0x20), the capsule axis is the second row of the bone's world frame, and the base point is transformed by the full frame including the translation row. There is no distance, facing, height, sight or hittable gate and no sweep. The step-by-step flow of the strike (guard, hit list, zero damage, effects) is SPEC-0038 Behavior 13.

**Beam volume.** The four-corner volume of the beam (Water/Bubble) attack family is a cylinder along the beam, radius 1.0 (0x2DCB3C) and length 5.0 (0x2DCB40), tested against the target's body cylinder. The other attack family volumes are in SPEC-0036 (Attack sweeps).


**Hit list.** Every character has a 10-entry list of target ids at +0x400; -1 is empty. A target already in the attacker's list is skipped. The list clears at character initialization and release. Both heroes' charge routines clear it on every sub-state transition; the hero attack-start reset and entry to action state 13 clear it; hero state 12 clears it on every update; and the class-17 agro attack-start path clears it (the outer-entry event 2 of SPEC-0038 Behavior 44, which every normal or counter choice reaches, and the re-entry of sequencer state 2 from state 3 for each further attack window; Behavior 13). The class-17 strike tests the list before the weapon test and adds the hero after the take-hit, and a blocked strike adds it twice. A target hit in one phase can therefore be hit again after a later phase transition. The remaining callers are the start of a hero air attack (the air slash or plunge start of Tor's jump states 7 to 11 and strafe-jump states 43 to 46, and Roberta's air shot from jump states 7 to 11, which goes to state 13), and the Werelupe King's swing and overhead attacks, each of which clears the list when the attack's animation is requested (SPEC-0019). Nothing else clears it. Confidence High for the listed paths and Medium-High for the air-attack start points.

**Hittable predicate** (the "Can be hit" hook of Behavior 4). A target is a candidate for the hero swing, the hero's shots and the charm walks only when the predicate passes:
- base rule: the state is not 16, 33, 36, 53 or 54, and the invulnerability flag at +0x7F0 is clear (SPEC-0030, SetEntityInvulnerable); confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) by the shared predicate: states 0x10, 0x21, 0x24, 0x35 and 0x36 are never hittable and every other state is hittable exactly when the invulnerable word is 0 (the predicate's jump table covers states 0x10 to 0x36, 39 entries);
- hero: in addition, the state is not 17, 25, 57, 59 or 60, the hero is not busy by the busy test (Behavior 18), and it is not in hero mode 1 with mode code 8 or 13;
- enemy-kind characters: not when the class state is 16, nor when any of the six status tests listed in SPEC-0036 (Hit detection, Hittable predicate) holds, otherwise the base rule.
A guarding target is still tested by the hero swing and by shots even when the predicate fails (it can block). The class-17 melee strike of SPEC-0038 does not call the predicate.

### 13. Placement reset of a motion record

Placing a motion record (spawn, teleport or script reset) is one operation with these effects. The effects below are the ones that occur in the placement path of ball records (SPEC-0010). Resolved (High for which path calls which routine; Medium-High for the play consequences): there are two placement operations, and only some paths make the writes listed here.

- **Full reset** (this list, items 1 to 8, plus a rebuild of the facing basis from the passed facing, storing the weight, radius and height words, zeroing the step accumulators and the height-follow state, and setting the drawn-position copy to the new position). It is used by the character's first placement (spawn and the placement from a placement record) and by the hero's respawn at the start pose (Behavior 1C), which passes the start position and start facing and the record's own three motion words. In the respawn the vectors stored at +0x120 and +0x2C0 are the zero displacement (0, 0, 0, 1). The respawn then also resets the hero's locomotion sub-object, sets the speed scale to 1.0 and the grab-point cooldown to 60, and moves the live petpet with the light move, as listed in Behavior 1C.
- **Light move** (Behavior 1C, "Move to position and facing" and its position-only form). It is used by every teleport landing, by MoveHero and by the entity moves of SPEC-0030 (for heroes and region characters), by the hero swap placement, by the partner and live petpet in the respawn, and by the interior enter and leave jobs. It does not make the full reset. Its writes, in order: the record position (w = 1); the movable-block index (+0x180) set to -1; the facing basis rebuilt from the given facing (facing form only, and skipped while the character is in base state 53); the locomotion sub-object's position copy (+0x13D0) set to the new position; velocity (+0x60) and force (+0x70) zeroed; then the same ground probe that opens every record tick (it refreshes the grid cell pointer at owner +0x174, saves the previous ground entry from +0x178 only when that one is non-null, finds the new ground entry, the ground normal at +0x10, the ground cache at +0x130 and +0x140, the navmesh height cache and the ground gap at +0x24 from the new position); and last the drawn-position copy at +0x2D0 set to the new position.
- **Not written by the light move:** the tick-start position P0 (+0x110), the last displacement (+0x120), the last motion (+0x160), the direction word (+0x40), the 60-tick history counter and ring, the countdown (+0x23C), the blocking-normal word (+0x280), the penetration tolerance (+0x28), the gravity gain (+0x188), the no-gravity, no-snap and clear-on-ground flags (+0x144, +0x148, +0x14C), the height-follow state (+0x2E0 to +0x2E8) and the three motion words. The next record tick rewrites P0, +0x120, +0x160, +0x40 and the countdown, so these only matter to a reader that runs between the move and that tick.
- **Region characters:** the navmesh state lives outside the motion record and is not touched by the move. On the next tick the confinement step locates the character again (starting at its old triangle) and, if the new point is on its mesh, stores it as the saved position. If the point is off the mesh and the lock value is 0, the character is put back at its previous saved position, so a script move to an off-mesh point is silently undone one tick later.
- **What goes wrong in play if a rewrite skips them.** Without the movable-block index reset, a hero that stood on or beside a block is still linked to it after the teleport and the block pass drags or pushes him. Without the velocity and force reset he keeps his old speed and falls or slides on arrival. Without the immediate probe, the hero update of the move tick (it runs before the record tick) reads the ground gap, normal and floor flag of the old spot and can start a fall or landing, or skip one, for a tick; the breakable and trigger lists of the old grid cell are also walked once more. Without the drawn-position write, the hero model glides vertically from the old height to the new one over several frames. Without the basis rebuild a door landing leaves him facing the old way. A rewrite that applies the full reset to every move would also clear the no-snap and clear-on-ground flags and the gravity gain, which the light move leaves alone.
- Test vector (landing point from the disc, pack 909 teleport plane 0, vector 100 of the test vectors; the hero's earlier state is synthetic): a hero standing at height 52 with velocity (3, -9, 0) is landed at x = -1887.209, z = 1173.177, with y probed down from the ceiling 45.960. Afterwards position y is the probed height, velocity is zero, the movable-block index is -1, the drawn-position copy equals the position, the ground gap is the gap measured at the new spot, and P0 still holds the old position until the next record tick.
- Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): implement the light move as the writes above; run the full reset only for first placement and the start-pose respawn.

1. The record's transform position is set to the record's world position.
2. The displacement words at record offsets +0x44 and +0x4C are zeroed (Medium). CORRECTED 2026-11-09 (High): the full reset of a character record (the one run by the first placement and the start-pose respawn) does NOT write +0x44, +0x4C (or +0x48, item 5); those writes belong to the owner class's own reset (called first, through the owner's class method) or to the ball initialiser only.
3. The passed vector is written to record fields +0x120 and +0x2C0. During the ordinary character tick, +0x120 is cleared and overwritten with the computed travel displacement; the hero and region-character root-travel path does not read the passed vector from +0x2C0. The role of +0x2C0 remains unknown. CORRECTED 2026-11-09 (High): the full reset always writes (0, 0, 0, 1) to +0x120 (not the passed vector) and writes the passed POSITION quad (the third argument, w forced to 1) to +0x2C0. The complete write list of the full reset of a character record is: the owner class reset first; the facing basis rebuilt from the passed facing (the three rows, skipped in base state 53); +0x2C0 = the position (w = 1); +0xE0, +0xE4 and +0x100 = 0 (the "three state counters"); +0xF0 = (0, 0, 0, 1); the +0xD0 quad's w = 2.0 and the byte at +0xB0 = 1; the flight placement (weight at +0x04 = the first float, radius at +0x08 = the second, position at +0x50 = the position with w = 1, +0x28 = 0, the ground gap through a virtual probe); +0x120 = (0, 0, 0, 1); the tick-start position +0x110 = the position; penetration tolerance +0x28 = −0.1; gravity gain +0x188 = 1.0; +0x144, +0x148 and +0x14C = 0; the position snapshot (drawn position +0x2D0 = the position and the height-follow state +0x2E0 to +0x2E8 = 0); height +0x0C = the third float; lock word +0x280 = 0; countdown +0x23C = 0. The callers are the ground placement from a spawn record, the pet placement, the root-motion sync and the unit-scale variant (which builds the facing (1, 0, 0, 0)).
4. In the known ball-slot initializer, the direction vector at record offset +0x30 is initialized from the owning character facing vector. The ball step uses it for candidate displacement and updates it during bounce response. No other placement caller is known.
5. The vertical velocity at record offset +0x48 is set to 1.0 (data word 0x2DA600, Where it lives). (Ball initialiser only; not part of the character full reset, 2026-11-09.)
6. In that same initializer, the fixed 1.0 write targets the +0x48 vertical-velocity field in item 5; no distinct second field is established by this path. The ball step decreases it by 0.1 each tick and restores it to 1.0 on its bounce branch (SPEC-0010).
7. Three state counters are zeroed.
8. The first blocking-normal entry's word and one further counter are zeroed.

Confidence: High for the writes, Medium for the meanings.

Body size (High). A character's motion-record placement also writes the record's weight (+0x04), radius (+0x08) and height (+0x0C) (Layout, body size). Later re-placements keep those three words.

The value is not a sentinel. It is the starting vertical velocity of the placed record. The ball step of SPEC-0010 adds the gravity word to this velocity each tick and then moves the position by it. The character ground-placement routine of SPEC-0018 does not directly read this vertical-velocity field: it probes above the character and selects its ground result using a separate signed setup value from the caller record. The ball's vertical velocity belongs to a separate world-pool record and is not an input to that character-placement rule. Confidence High for the distinct record owners and direct reads; no indirect virtual effect on placement was found. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): placement ignores the ball's velocity.

The placement reset clears the first blocking-normal entry. The consumer is the step-accumulation gate (SPEC-0033, step gate): it refuses a step when the dot product of the step with any listed blocking normal (the count is at +0x500, the normals at +0x280 with stride 0x20) is zero or less, unless the speed lane is above 0.01, the owner is in state 28 or the override flag at +0x510 is set (Medium; the sign convention of the normals is not pinned down). Clearing the first entry's word means that entry no longer blocks until a contact writes it again. The follow-camera contact-normal list is a separate camera field group (SPEC-0027).

#### World pool record set-up (not a character spawn step; High for the owner, Medium for the rise)

These defaults are not a character's motion record (High). They are the set-up of one 0x160-byte record of the world's +0x4260 pool, the last of the six world sub-tables that the world tick updates (SPEC-0026; SPEC-0028, section 15.1). That pool is never filled in play: the routine that takes a free record and runs this set-up has no caller in the boot program and no stored pointer to it (High). Characters never run these steps, so they do not affect the character placement above or Behavior 11. The clean side may leave the pool empty.

The set-up takes a 4 by 4 frame: three axis rows R0, R1 and R2 and a position P (Medium-High for the row roles). It writes:

1. The record's own reset method, then R0, R1 and R2 at +0x70, +0x80 and +0x90 and P at +0xA0 (the current frame), and the same four rows at +0x20, +0x30, +0x40 and +0x50 (the previous-frame copy). The word at +0x60 is set to 1.
2. A scalar at +0x154 set to 1.75. Nothing in the boot program reads it.
3. The word at +0x15C set to -1. Nothing in the boot program reads it; it is not the character lock index of Behavior 20.
4. The word at +0x150 and the bytes at +0x158 and +0x159 cleared.
5. A square patch of sizes a = 8.0 and b = 8.0 built on the frame: corner C0 = P - (a/2) R2 - (b/2) R0, C1 = C0 + a R2, C2 = C0 + b R0 and C3 = C0 - a R1. Three planes through three points each are stored: (C0, C2, C1) at +0xD0 (the face spanned by R0 and R2), (C0, C2, C3) at +0xE0 and (C0, C3, C1) at +0xF0. The sizes are kept at +0x100 (b) and +0x104 (a).

Per world tick, for each record in the pool (Medium): when the current world's script flag 0 is set and the second component of P is below -15.0 (boot data word 0x2EB348), that component goes up by 0.01 and the patch is rebuilt from the stored sizes. Then the current frame is copied to the previous-frame copy. The patch therefore rises 0.6 units per second until it reaches -15.0. It has no effect on play, since nothing fills the pool. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): omit this pool.

The 1.75, -1, 8.0 and 0.01 values are fixed program constants, not data words (High).

### 14. Per-tick motion sample (motion records)

Confidence: Medium for the order of the listed steps; Low-Medium for the formulas; High for the cadence. Each tick a motion record that has one runs:

1. A countdown decrements toward zero.
2. Every 60 ticks (1.0 s) the record's world position is stored in a 5-slot history ring (16 bytes per slot); the slot index is kept modulo 5. The index starts at 0 when the record is built and is never advanced, so every sample overwrites slot 0 and slots 1 to 4 keep their start values (High; reproduce it).
3. Tick bookkeeping (High). The record's last displacement (+0x120) is reset to the zero vector with w = 1, and the tick-start position (+0x110) takes the current position with w = 1. The record step then runs (facing, basis, root-step travel, pre-step, gravity, driver and integrator; the cadence list below). Afterwards the tick's actual movement is formed as the new position (w = 1) minus the tick-start position: the displacement copy (+0x2C0) takes the new position, the direction word (+0x40) takes the unit vector of that movement (its w lane carries the movement length), the last-motion vector (+0x160) takes the movement itself, and the position is stored back with w = 1. The last displacement therefore holds only the root-step displacement that the travel step stored during this tick (Behavior 14C), while the last-motion vector holds everything that moved the record, contacts and integration included.
4. For the hero only, the height follow of SPEC-0033 (Behavior 7) runs and the drawn copy of the position takes the displayed height, unless the owner is in state 53.
5. The record's lock word (+0x280) is cleared at the very end of the tick, after the height follow. Anything that sets it during the tick (the edge guard of Behavior 14E reads it) therefore acts within that tick only. Inside the record step, after the four hooks, a word at +0x100 is cleared and the pending offset (+0x270) is reset to the zero vector, and the alternate-friction flag (+0x2EC) is cleared before the hooks.

**Cadence and order for the hero (High for the cadence and the order; the whole tick is listed in SPEC-0007, Order of work inside one tick).** The record tick runs exactly once per tick (60 times per second, SPEC-0007). There is no parity, frame-skip or every-other-tick gate. For the selected hero (the other hero slot is not updated; Loop-level order), it runs when the motion record pointer (+0x16C), the locomotion sub-object pointer (+0x170) and the sub-object's word at +0xFA0 are all nonzero. Within one tick, in this order (High for the order):
1. Grid cell refresh: the owner's grid cell pointer (+0x174) is set to the grid cell record under the record's position (+0x50). The player uses the world's grid; another character uses its own world when one is set, else the current world, and the second world slot when the first gives no cell. The pointer is 0 when there is no cell.
2. Ground step: the ground follow (probe 2.5 above the record's height, Behavior 9) with the moving-object contacts. For each object of the world's moving-object list whose active byte (object +0x1B6) is set and whose primitive set (object +0x1D8) exists, and when the record lies within that object's radius (object +0xBC; the squared distance is compared with the squared radius), the record's position is taken into the object's local frame, the object's six primitive lists are walked through the shape dispatcher with filter 0, and the result is taken back to world space (SPEC-0028). The move into and out of the object's frame, the displacement and the single-sided write are spelled out in SPEC-0033, Behavior 43 (only this record moves; the object is never written; Medium).
3. The 60-tick counter and the history ring of item 2 above, and the countdown.
4. The facing update (SPEC-0033, Behavior 2), the basis rebuild and the stick target with the root-step displacement of Behavior 14C, in that order.
5. The record's own steps: a pre-step (the edge guard of Behavior 14E, the deadly-ground test of SPEC-0055, then the ground constraint of Behavior 14A item 5b), gravity (Behavior 14A, item 5, with friction 5c), the driver with the contact walks (Behavior 14A, item 1) and the integrator (Behavior 14A, item 6); then the record's per-tick flags are cleared. These four are methods of the record's class, called in this fixed order every record tick; the flag at record +0x2EC (Behavior 14A, item 3) is cleared just before them. The probe (gap and normal) runs at the start of the record tick, before the root travel of item 4; the root travel adds the clip root step to the position before the constraint (High). A second chain of the same kind without the driver exists but has no caller (High for the absence).

Each region character runs the same record tick, steps 1 to 5, from the region character loop (Behavior 3, loop-level order), between its NPC update and its animation pass; its facing update uses the non-hero rule of Behavior 8 and its step is that of Behavior 14D (High).

The physics block's 1/30 is a per-step scale inside these steps, not a cadence. For every active character the record tick runs after the class update hook and before the skeleton pose pass (Behavior 3, loop-level order); for the heroes it runs after the hero update and before the hero's animation object.

### 14A. Shared motion-record routines (not hero-specific)

Confidence: Medium for the structure; Low-Medium for the separate segment-step helper's probe details and owner; Low for the alternate friction branch's lane arithmetic; High for items 5 to 7 unless marked. These routines serve every character that has a motion record. The hero is one owner among them; the hero's own behaviours are in SPEC-0033.
1. Driver. A per-tick class method runs for each motion record, with the record's parent as owner. It branches on an owner query that the owner's class answers: 0 for the heroes, 1 for every region character (townsfolk, enemies and bosses), 2 for the live petpet (High). The wall rules of each branch are in SPEC-0018.
   - Branch 0 (heroes) clears the touched flag (+0x238), the object-wall flag (+0x240) and the floor flag (+0x150) and forms an item-search point P = reference point (the position plus the pending offset at +0x270, xyz) + 2.0 times the record's facing (+0x2B0, the forward axis B3 of Behavior 14C; the up axis is +0x2A0 and the side axis +0x290) + 0.8 times the reference vector R (Where it lives, 0x3932E0) (Medium-High, read from code: P starts from the reference point, not the raw position, and it is used only by the nearest-item search below, never by a wall test). It remembers the position before the pre-step as the tick-start point of the final tunnel guard. It then calls the hero owner pre-step. The hook always clears +0x9B4. When +0x9D8 is nonzero, or the hero state is 30, 35, 37, 38 or 47, it does nothing else. Otherwise it sets +0x7C8 to -1, calls the hero target setter with 0 (which clears +0x9D4), and clears +0xA14 and +0xA18. This hook runs before later contacts, which may set +0x9B4 again (OQ101; High). It then runs the contacts in this order: the grid cell's frame records, each through the hero's per-record wall contact and ledge guard (SPEC-0018; each gated by the mask word of the model-set entry it names); cell list A through the shape dispatcher with filter 0; cell list B through the contact walk of item 2; the movable-block walls, and then the type-10 object walls (corrected from code: the movable blocks run BEFORE the object walls; SPEC-0018 has the same order); a one-word step that writes -1 to the motion record's door-link word (+0x18C); the pending contact shapes (item 2a); the nearest-item search (replaces the earlier "navmesh height cache (+0x17C)", which was wrong, High from code: +0x178 is the nearest-item token and +0x17C the nearest-item distance, a float, 1e9 when none was found or when an interaction was started); an empty hook; the character push, whose radius is the record radius r; and last the tunnel guard, which receives the position from before the pre-step (High for the lists and their order). The nearest-item search runs only when the world's item pool exists: it asks for the item nearest to P (any kind, with the token stored at +0x178); when one is found with distance f, and the owner is the selected hero and f is below 8.0, the script manager is asked to start the item interaction (given the PREVIOUS tick's stored distance, the item's id, and the press-edge bit 7 of the pad's press half-word); if that call returns nonzero, or nothing was found, the stored distance becomes 1e9, otherwise it becomes f. **State 41 skips the hero's per-record frame response and ordinary type-10 world-wall loop. It retains cell lists A and B, the earlier inactive movable-block prepass, pending contact shapes and character-cylinder push.** The pack-909 lower-ladder target is in grid cell (21, 86), whose list A includes an ungated, class-0 kind-4 box. The box push returns at once for a hero in state 21, 38 or 41, so this box does not resist the mount, and the hero climbs there (High; SPEC-0033 Behavior 13). Outside those states, the hero's radius of 1.175 holds him about 1.07 from the ladder face at the mount spot, inside the grab window. The selected-hero tick order is established: state 41 adjusts the position, the motion-record driver refreshes the cell and runs list-A contacts, then integration follows. Within state 41, a missing ladder target resets the sub-mode, and flag bit 0x40 resets sub-modes other than 8 before the local sub-mode step. Resolved: bit 0x40 is the Cross press edge of the pad’s press-edge word (SPEC-0033, Behavior 13), written each tick by the pad read, so the interruption is the jump-off: it resets the sub-mode and sends the hero to state 7 (High). The hero re-enters state 41 through the ladder grab test (SPEC-0033, Behavior 22), as for a first mount (Medium). Recurring correction by the list-A box is therefore conditional on a valid target and the Cross edge staying clear. The user’s report that the original lets the hero climb the pack 909 lower ladder means the box push must not be modelled as stalling the mount; see the default in SPEC-0033. Side-axis coupling from the box is about 0.0021 per 0.1-unit approach/correction and its vertical axis is orthogonal to the ladder; those other two ladder axes do not remove the normal-axis discrepancy in this calculation. This remains a conditional result: it assumes the live collision reference follows the computed target and the listed box continues to be visited, and does not prove that the retail mount always stalls or that its full three-axis path reaches this neighborhood. The exact held type-5 frame needs a runtime check. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): the hero climbs the pack 909 lower ladder (the user's report), so do not model the box push as stalling the mount. Every hero primitive test in this branch uses one point, the motion record's position (the feet) plus the pending offset, with no body height added; the cylinder and sphere tests use the hero body of radius 1.175 and height 3.5 based at that point (SPEC-0018, Hero test points and radii).
   - Branch 1 (region characters) clears +0x238 and +0x150 and runs the owner pre-step (it stores -1 in the character's wall-type word +0x7C8), the navmesh confinement step (border edges of the current navmesh triangle act as walls, and a character found off the mesh is put back to its last on-mesh position; SPEC-0018 and SPEC-0056), the character push, cell list B through the contact walk of item 2 and one closing step, which is the movable-block contact pass (SPEC-0048; SPEC-0056). It runs no frame records, no cell list A walk, no object walls and no item search (High; order confirmed from code).
   - Branch 2 (petpet) clears +0x238 and +0x150 (like the other branches), runs no owner pre-step, runs the petpet's own frame-record step (SPEC-0018) and then cell list A through the dispatcher, with no cell list B walk. Corrected 2026-11-09 (High, from the code): the petpet's frame-record step is not the hero's: its cell walk requires the owner's grid cell (+0x174) to be non-null with a non-null entry array (+0xC) and an entry count (+8) above 0; the gate mask (the u16 at +0x42 of the entry's resolved model-set entry) is tested once, on the first entry only (0 passes, else it must share a bit with the world mask at +0x56B4), and then every entry's frame-record list (count +0xC, list +0x10, 0x14-byte entries) goes to the per-record step, or none does; the hero's walk (the same loop shape) tests the gate for every entry and calls the hero's wall contact. The per-record step works on a copy of the 0x60-byte record whose depth word (+0x44) is raised by 10.0, requires the hero-frame-record plane test (SPEC-0018) with mode 0 to hit, then probes the ground (reach 8.0) at the reference point (g0) and 2.0 ahead along the body forward (g1) and pushes (slide response with push 1, floor 1, no-compensation 0) when g1 <= −1,000,000, or |g0 − g1| > 0.5, or (|g0 − g1| <= 0.5 and the reference y + 0.25 is strictly below the edge height of the original, unraised record at the reference point). The earlier "bypass when the ground ahead is below g0 − 2.0" never acts; a rise ahead of more than 0.5 also pushes. The driver runs once every tick (Behavior 14, cadence). The per-tick sample of Behavior 14 runs in the record tick before the driver (Behavior 14, cadence). Branch 2 also runs no navmesh confinement and no character push; the petpet is not in the region-character list, so no character pushes it either (SPEC-0047, Petpet build guide G6).
   - **No-snap flag (+0x148) writers (High for the direct state-handler writes).** The hero tick clears the motion-record no-snap flag before dispatching the current action state; it is a per-tick request. Hero action states 21, 30, 41, 47, 56 and 58 set it in selected branches. State 21 sets it during nested phase 7 while action 59 is active and below 0.95 progress. State 30 sets it during pull-up sub-mode 4 while its current clip is below 0.95. State 41 sets it in phase 8 while action 135 is active and below 0.95. State 47 sets it in alignment phases 1 or 2 after its actor/ground checks pass, while action 134 is active and below 0.95. State 56 sets it during timed phases 1 and 2 after entering from a ground gap of at most 0.1. State 58 sets it in phase 2 while action 134 is active and below 0.95. For the action-progress branches, an inactive action is requested first and does not set the flag that tick. A region-character action update also clears its record flag each tick and sets it unconditionally in state 16. These are per-tick overrides; the state-specific gates are separate for each action. **[High for branch conditions; Medium for interpreting the shared progress predicate's polarity]**
2. Contact walk (cell list B). For each entry of the grid cell's list B whose breakable state word (+0x40; SPEC-0048) is 1 or 3 (intact), whose owner class predicate returns 0 and whose primitive reference (+0x60) is set, the primitive's kind selects a helper: kinds 2, 1 and 3 each have their own; kinds 4 and 7 choose between two helpers by the sign of the shape value (+0x2C): a positive value takes the box push, and a zero or negative value takes the zero-radius contact. The walk has no mask gate and no filter, and never handles kind 8. A store of three world words runs before the walk (SPEC-0018). Confirmed in code 2026-11-09 (High): the three words are the cell's shader, particle and spawner context words, fed to the cached-vector setter first; with no cell nothing happens; the owner predicate is the class-table slot 53 (zero means "not ignored"); for kinds 4 and 7 the box response runs when the +0x2C value is strictly above 0.0 (with a shared output word cleared at the start), else the zero-radius contact.
   - **Kind 2 cone helper (High for the path; High from 2026-11-09 for the cone equation, band, radius formula and the positive-depth rule; the contact normal is not the true cone normal: it is the vector (dh.x / k, 1, dh.z / k) normalised with dh the horizontal offset from the axis and k = R / H, so it tilts with the distance from the axis, and it is (0, 1, 0) when the horizontal distance is 0; SPEC-0018, Other collision helpers).** Inputs are the motion-contact context, the loaded cone primitive, and a zero mode value. It uses the character's reference position P and motion-record radius r, and tests them against the cone; it does not use the character's capsule height. The cone's vertical axis passes through base point B; H is its height and R its base radius. Let h = P.y − B.y and d be the horizontal distance from P to the cone axis. The vertical band is −r ≤ h ≤ H + r. Within that band, allowed radius is `rad = r + (H − (h + r)) × R / H`; penetration is `depth = rad − d`. A positive depth returns contact normal and depth. Class 0 applies the shared contact response; if class 0 is clear, class 3 calls the owner's contact callback; otherwise there is no hit effect. It has no owner pre-contact call, mask gate or extra event route. The list-B owner predicate does not reject kind 2: the hero override returns zero for this kind, and other character owners use the zero-return predicate. Example: H = 4, R = 2, r = 0.5, h = 1 gives rad = 1.75; d = 1 yields depth 0.75 (contact), while d = 2 yields depth −0.25 (no contact). At h = 4.8 the height gate rejects because H + r = 4.5. **Disc reachability:** the survey of all 192 area-pack archives found 2,030 type-29 second-list entries with a volume pointer; every one appears in a cell's list B, and their volume kinds are 1,915 kind-3 records and 115 kind-4 records. No shipped type-29 list-B entry has kind 2. This leaves the list-B kind-2 branch without a static shipped placement; the separate cell-list-A dispatcher still routes kind 2 to a cone test.
   - **Kind 1 sphere helper (High; partial answer to Open question 102).** The inputs are the motion-contact context, the loaded sphere primitive, and a zero mode value. Before dispatch, the list-B walk requires entry state 1 or 3, the owning character's predicate for that entry to return 0, and a non-null primitive reference. For the hero, the predicate runs the entry-kind landing-break test; a nonzero result breaks the entry and skips contact. Other character owners use a predicate that returns 0. The helper calls the owner's pre-contact method, then tests the motion record's vertical flat-ended cylinder (called a capsule elsewhere; it is not rounded at the ends) from its position against the sphere, by the exact rule of SPEC-0018, Finite cylinder tests. The cylinder uses the motion record's height and radius fields; the sphere uses its center and radius fields. On a hit, class 0 applies the shared contact response; otherwise class 3 calls the owner's contact callback; otherwise class 4 must be clear and class 9 set, with the owner equal to the selected hero, to raise the player-side event. On a miss, class 0 enables a second test after the owner pre-contact step and during the contact walk, before the record integrator. Let P be the current motion-record position, v its velocity, F its force accumulator, W its weight, k = 1/30, and D its stored displacement at +0x120. The retry point is P + (v + (F/W)k)k; when |D| > 0.1, add 0.5D. The retry changes only the capsule query center: it reuses the same capsule height, radius, vertical axis, and sphere fields as the first query. A hit there applies the shared response. The helper does not apply the primitive mask gate. This uses the same force/weight and time-scale terms documented for motion-record updates in SPEC-0018 and the shared integrator in this spec. For example, with P = (1, 2, 3), v = (3, 0, 0), F/W = (0, 0, 0), and D = (0, 0, 0.3), the retry point is (1.1, 2, 3.15); if |D| = 0.1, the half-displacement is not added. [High]
   - **Kind 3 cylinder helper (High; partial answer to Open question 102).** Its inputs are the motion-contact context, the loaded cylinder primitive, and a zero mode value. Before it is called, the list-B walk requires entry state 1 or 3, the owning character's class predicate for that entry to return 0, and a non-null primitive reference. The predicate is the same one the sphere helper uses: for the hero it is the entry-kind landing-break test, and every other character answers 0, so it never blocks a non-hero (High). Inside the helper, primitive class bits are checked in this order: class 1 exits; class 2 tests the indexed world-surface record and falls back to the class-0 cylinder contact only on a miss; classes 3, 8 and 6 select the body-cylinder overlap path; otherwise class 0 selects the record-sized cylinder path. The body cylinder has radius 1.0 and height 3.5, centered 1.75 above the character position. On overlap, class 3 calls the owner's contact callback and then applies the shared response if class 0 is also set; otherwise class 8 raises the player-side event with value 1, or class 6 raises it with the primitive's book value. The record-sized path first calls the owner's pre-contact method, compares against the motion record's own radius and height, then runs the contact effect and shared response on overlap. The world-surface record's role and detailed field semantics remain open. This helper itself does not apply the primitive mask gate.
   - **Kinds 4 and 7 contact helpers (Medium; partial answer to Open question 102).** The sign boundary is shape value +0x2C > 0 for the box-push helper; zero and negative values use the zero-radius helper. In the positive branch, class 2 is checked first and selects the indexed world-surface test; a nonzero result exits, while zero falls through to the shared box response. With class 2 clear, class 1 exits. If classes 5 and 7 are both clear, class 3 exits when set; when all three are clear, the shared box response runs directly. Otherwise class 8 exits when set, and with class 8 clear the current-position point/radius-versus-box test runs. On overlap, class 7 raises the player-side event; otherwise the owner contact callback runs. Class 0 additionally enables the shared box response after that route. In the zero/non-positive branch, class 1 plus entry type 500 or 501 selects the fixed-object branch; only when the owner's current target selects the designated object does it run the fixed-object overlap test and trigger on a hit. Other class-1 entries take the plane test and can raise the player-side trigger. With class 1 clear, the owner pre-step runs and the motion segment is tested. An immediate hit routes in this order: class 0 or 6 selects the contact event (the shared slide response with push 1 and floor flag 1; class 4 is a separate call, not this response); otherwise class 4 selects the owner event (one owner call that swaps the active hero slot; its meaning is unknown and no zero-radius rectangle on the disc has class 4); otherwise class 8 selects the owner callback. On a miss, the retry point is P + (v + (F/W)k)k, with k = 1/30; add half the stored displacement D at +0x120 only when |D| > 0.1. This is the same force/weight and time-scale calculation as the shared motion update in SPEC-0018. A retry hit checks classes 0 and 6 for the contact-event route (the slide response with push 0 and floor flag 1; the depth argument is forced to 0 when the unit velocity dotted with the plane normal is above 0), then class 4 for the owner event, then class 8 for the owner callback; class 1 is clear on this moving branch (a class 1 record never reaches it, so the earlier reading of a plane-trigger route after a retry hit is dead code). The plane test, the slide response and the disc census of flat rectangles are in SPEC-0018 (Zero-radius contact of a character). For the retry contact event, the dot product of the owner direction and primitive direction is tested: a strictly positive result sets the event float to zero, while zero or a negative result preserves the scalar output written by the successful segment query. An immediate segment hit passes that query scalar through unchanged. The retry uses the predicted position as query center. For example P=(1,2,3), v=(30,0,0), F/W=(0,0,0), D=(0,0,0.3) gives (2,2,3.15); at |D|=0.1, it gives (2,2,3). The hero list-B owner predicate chooses its landing-break test by the sign of +0x2C; a positive result breaks the entry and skips either helper. Other character owners' predicate returns 0. This per-tick contact walk is separate from the attack-volume rectangle sweeps of Behavior 16. [High for class ordering and candidate formula; see the basis for the remaining event-argument caveat]
2a. Pending contact shapes (Medium-High). Each character keeps a list of up to ten pending contact shapes in its own record: a count at +0x2F8 and entries of 0x70 bytes from +0x300. The hero mover consumes this list at the end of driver branch 0, after object-wall contacts, then resets the count. Tags 0 to 3 select, respectively: finite-cylinder overlap against the body; a sphere against the body cylinder (tag 1, specified below; R-0272); a one-face test using two stored extents; or a point-against-box test with the record radius as padding. Corrected and extended 2026-11-09 (High; the dispatcher was read in full): tag 0 (cylinder) passes point A, point B, extent 0 and a scalar; tag 1 (sphere) passes point A and a scalar; tag 2 passes the two stored extents (+0x60 and +0x64) and the three axis planes (+0x30, +0x40, +0x50) and NO point (the earlier "three stored axis extents" is withdrawn); tag 3 passes the three extents, point A and the three axis planes. The consumer runs the entries in order and then clears the count. The oriented-box append (used by Juppie's blocker) takes three extents e0, e1, e2, a centre and three axis vectors P, R, Q, refuses when the count is already 10, and stores: corner (point A, w = 1) = centre − 0.5 × e0 × P − 0.5 × e1 × R − 0.5 × e2 × Q; e0 at +0x60, e1 at +0x64, e2 at +0x6C; and each axis row with w = −dot3(axis, corner): P at +0x50, Q at +0x30, R at +0x40 (so extent e0 pairs with the axis at +0x50, e1 with +0x40 and e2 with +0x30); then count + 1. Tag 0 is tested by the same static finite-cylinder routine as the monster weapon strike (SPEC-0018, Finite cylinder tests; flat ends, no normalising of the stored axes). Tag 3 is not: it never calls that routine. It uses the shared point-against-box routine (the one the wall-box contacts also use): the character's position point is tested against the entry's box (three perpendicular axes with a stored extent each), every face padded by the record radius, and the response runs from that box result. The tag-3 axes are made perpendicular to each other when the entry is stored. A port needs the finite-cylinder routine for tag 0 only, and a point-in-box test (padded by the radius) for tag 3. Tag 1 (R-0272 Resolved; High, from the code): the entry's point A is the centre of a sphere whose radius is the stored scalar (4.0 for the Plague Serpent's mouth contact), tested against the mover's own body cylinder with the shared sphere-against-flat-ended-cylinder routine (SPEC-0018, Sphere against flat-ended cylinder). The body cylinder has its base at the mover's reference point (the position plus the pending offset at +0x270, which is zero for the hero; this is the feet, not the body centre), an upward axis, radius +0x08 (1.175 for a hero) and height +0x0C (3.5). The hero radius is therefore added to the sphere radius: with the centre's height between the feet and the top of the head (feet.y <= centre.y <= feet.y + 3.5) the test hits when the horizontal distance L from the centre to the hero's vertical axis is strictly below 1.175 + 4.0 = 5.175; the contact normal is the horizontal unit vector from the sphere centre toward the hero's axis and the depth is 5.175 - L, so the push is horizontal and moves the hero out to exactly 5.175 from the centre (a hero standing exactly on the axis, L = 0, has no defined direction). When the centre lies above the head or below the feet the rules of the SPEC-0018 routine apply instead, with a vertical or oblique normal and the depth from that routine; the call is not horizontal-only in that case. On a hit the slide response runs with the contact normal, the depth, push 1, floor 1 and no-compensation 0, and every response is the standard one: it exits with no change when the velocity plus this tick's stored displacement, and the force, are both not moving into the normal (dot with the normal at least 0) and the depth is at most 0.05; otherwise the position moves by normal x depth (fourth lane 1); when the velocity plus the displacement points into the normal (dot below 0), the velocity's whole normal component (velocity dot normal, of either sign) is removed from the velocity and, when the displacement is longer than 0.1, the displacement's normal component is taken back out of the position; when the force points into the normal (dot below 0) its normal component is removed; the contact-normal latch (+0x250) is set to the normal and the touched flag (+0x238) to 1; when the normal's vertical part is above 0.9 in size the floor flag (+0x150) is also set, and the velocity and force are zeroed if the vertical velocity is below 0.1 or the speed is below 0.1. There is no separate velocity clip beyond that. The touched flag set this way is what the grab test of SPEC-0029 Behavior 23 reads, and the flag is cleared again at the start of the hero's next movement update (item 1). The known direct writers are the Black Knight's enraged floor strike (tag 0); Juppie's slam and dazed blocker (tags 0 and 3); the Plague Serpent's bite/mesmerize path (tag 1); and PetPet's geometry/effect helper (tags 1 and 2). An append is refused once the list already contains ten entries. The per-tick mover consumes the list and resets its count. Indirect or unindexed writers are not excluded. (High for the direct callers and capacity; Medium-High for gameplay-phase labels; Medium for shape interpretation.)
3. Travel step. The owner update supplies the character's root step: the hero path is specified in item 14C and the region-character path in item 14D. The motion record transforms that step through its facing basis, applies ground projection when its gates pass, stores the displacement at +0x120 and adds it to position +0x50. The velocity/friction method is separate; it reads weight, force, velocity and the time factor, updates velocity, and does not write position or a history ring. Its alternate branch is selected by +0x2EC (items 5c and 6b); the alternate branch is the same friction step except that the vertical velocity component is restored to its value before the step (Resolved, item 103; High).
4. Segment probe. A probe helper from the owner, whose direction and length are scaled by fixed constants (0.5 and 0.25), is used by the attack family and by other modules. It is not part of the held-item draw of Behavior 12 (High).
5. Gravity and ground forces (character records: heroes, NPCs and enemies, and the records of one other creator, probably the projectile pool). Each tick the gravity step builds a = minus (W times g times G) times R, where W is the record weight (+0x04), g = 32 (physics block +0x0C), G the gravity gain (+0x188) and R = (0, 1, 0, 0), the up vector at 0x3932E0 (SPEC-0007), so a is downward. If the no-gravity flag (+0x144) is set, the Y part of a is 0. For the hero the flag lives one tick only: the hero update sets it to 0 (with the no-snap word +0x148) just before the action-state switch of every tick (Behavior 3A, step 4), and only a state routine that runs later in that same tick sets it again. So a hero that leaves a state that holds the flag (21, 35, 37, 41, 47, 58 and the rope states) by any route, a knock into state 48 included, falls under gravity from the first tick of the new state; nothing carries the flag across a state change (R-0134). Let c = gap + (vy + (Fy / W) times k) times k, where gap is +0x24, vy and Fy are the Y parts of the velocity (+0x60) and the force (+0x70), and k = 1/30 (physics block +0x04). If c is at most 0.1 (grounded): when the clear-on-ground flag (+0x14C) is set, the velocity and the force are cleared and a is 0; a is projected onto the ground plane, a = a minus dot(a, n) times n, with n the ground normal at +0x10; then friction (5c) runs. Finally F = F + a. The base record class adds gravity without a gain and counts as grounded when its gap is 0 or less.
5b. Ground constraint (part of the pre-step, so it runs before item 5). With the same c: if c is above the penetration tolerance (+0x28; -0.1 for character records), nothing happens. Otherwise F and v lose their components along n; if the remaining velocity has vy > 0, only the vertical lane of the velocity is set to zero (corrected from code, High: x, z and w of the velocity stay); then, if the no-snap flag (+0x148) is set, the routine ends here (the gap is not zeroed and the position's fourth lane is not rewritten); otherwise the position moves by minus n times (gap times the Y part of n) on all four lanes, its fourth lane is set to 1, and the gap becomes 0. On flat ground this puts the record exactly on the ground; on a sloped plane it moves the record to the nearest point of the plane. The base class compares the gap itself with its limit. Refined 2026-11-09 (High, base-class routine read in full): the base class constraint fires when the gap (+0x24) is at or below the tolerance (+0x28); then the velocity and the force lose their components along the normal (+0x10) and the position moves by −normal × (gap × normal.y) on all four lanes. It has NO "vy > 0" rule, NO no-snap test, does NOT set the position's fourth lane to 1 and does NOT zero the gap (those belong to the character variant above). The base class ground attach: height = the ground probe at (position x, position z, ceiling 1.0e9) with the normal stored at +0x10; the gap (+0x24) = the class's gap method applied to that height; for the base class that gap = (position.y − support height) − radius. Its ceiling is unbounded.

   Hero tick order around the constraint, and the numbers (from the code, not run in the original; High for the order, High for the numbers). In one hero motion-record tick: (a) the ground attach probes the ground at the position the tick starts with and stores the ground normal and the gap (feet y minus ground height; the probe ceiling is the feet height plus 2.5, and the highest candidate not above the ceiling wins); (b) facing; (c) the travel step of item 3, turned by the basis and projected onto the plane of the stored normal, only when the stored gap (taken as 0 while the floor flag +0x150 is set) is below 1.0, the no-gravity flag is clear and the hero state is not 21, 30 or 41; the position moves by the projected step; (d) the ground step: the constraint of this item, then gravity and friction (items 5 and 5c), then the driver contacts (item 1, including the box push and the pending shapes of item 2a), then the integrator (item 6). The constraint's gap is the one from (a): the ground is not probed again after the travel step, and the travel step is projected with the same probe's normal. With c = gap + (vy + (Fy / W) times k) times k and k = 1/30, the constraint fires when c is at most -0.1 and does nothing for c above it, so a hero 0 to 0.1 under the ground stays there. When it fires and the no-snap flag is clear, the position moves along the normal by the gap times the normal's Y part, a snap that is not vertical on a slope and that also lifts by any amount (a hero up to 2.5 under a ground entry whose height is inside the ceiling is lifted onto it). Grounded test of item 5: c at most 0.1. On a ramp of about 20.5 degrees, friction (0.6 times g times n.y times k, about 0.599 per tick) exceeds the down-slope pull (about 0.350 per tick), so the velocity stays 0 and only the projected travel step moves the hero (arithmetic High; equality to the last float digit Medium).
5c. Friction (grounded records only). Let u = v + (a_t + F) times k / W (a_t the projected a of item 5), L = |u.xyz|, and f = mu times dot(a, n) times k / W, with mu = 0.6 for character records (a fixed program constant) and 0.03 (physics block +0x14) for the base class. If L is below |f|, f becomes −L. The shared velocity-add step adds (f ux/L, f uy/L, f uz/L, f L) to the four velocity lanes: the xyz direction is normalized, while its W lane carries L. With G = 1 on flat ground this removes 0.64 from the xyz speed per tick (mu g G / 30) and never reverses it. For the character +0x2EC alternate branch, the helper receives the same four-lane increment, including W increment fL. The branch then repacks the helper-updated velocity as (x, y before the helper, z, w of the helper result), so the vertical velocity is unchanged by friction on this branch (Resolved; the lane order was re-checked in the instruction stream; High; Behavior 14A, items 3 and 6b, and item 103). The direction includes the vertical part of the velocity (quirk: a landing record loses 0.64 of its fall speed on its first grounded tick). Records rest on slopes up to atan(0.6) = 30.96 degrees and slide on steeper ones. Confirmed and refined 2026-11-09 (High; base-class routines read in full): the friction arithmetic (limit f = mu × dot(a, n) × k ÷ W, L < |f| gives −L, the direction normalised with the W lane carrying L) is confirmed; in the BASE class the gravity routine passes only a_t to the friction step (the force F is added to the force accumulator after the friction call), so there u = v + a_t × k ÷ W, not v + (a_t + F) × k ÷ W; the base-class gravity has no gain and no no-gravity flag, is grounded when the gap is 0 or less, and projects a −= (a · n) n; the friction step is a virtual method of the record (slot 7) which receives a_t and the projected scalar of slot 6. The shared velocity-add step used by friction adds the given quad to the velocity lane by lane (the velocity's W lane takes the L × f term).
6. Integrator (every record, every tick, after the driver). v = v + (F / W) times k; F = 0; p = p + v times k; the direction word +0x40 = unit(v). The force word is therefore cleared every tick, and W cancels from gravity. Refined 2026-11-09 (High for the base-class integrator, read in full; the character-class integrator was not re-read): the integrator stores the force as (0, 0, 0, 1) (not four zeros), stores the direction word as (unit(v) xyz, |v|) (the W lane carries the speed) and sets the position's fourth lane to 1 after the step.
6b. Moving platform (Medium). The owner's platform pointer (+0x190) is written only by the ground attach of SPEC-0018 (Ground attach of a character, The platform pointer): it names the placed object whose inline height patch is the ground entry under the character (or, for a hero, whose ledge or ladder record is held), is cleared and re-set every tick, and is zero for ground of the pack's top-level patches and for deadly ground. It also supplies the platform's per-update translation change (platform record +0x150, vertical part +0x154) that the carry uses. The platform pointer and the ground gap are produced only by the ground attach: a flat rectangle or a box top never writes them and gives only the floor flag (+0x150), which the landing logic reads (SPEC-0018, Zero-radius contact of a character; from the game code, not run in the original; High). A character that stands on a moving placed object is also moved sideways with it, by the carry of SPEC-0018 (x and z each clamped to 1.5 a tick, starting the tick after landing); this item only keeps the vertical contact (R-0205; High). When the pointer is set, the constraint works relative to the platform: with the platform's vertical displacement d in this tick, c' = gap + d + (vy + (Fy / W) times k - d / k) times k. If c' is 0.1 or more, nothing changes. If c' is between 0 and 0.1, vy = 1.05 times d / k. If c' is 0 or less, the velocity is replaced by (0, 0.95 times d / k, 0) (1.05 when d < 0), F loses its normal part, the alternate-friction flag +0x2EC is set and the record is moved onto the platform top. Additions from code (High): the ground gap is increased by d in every tick on which the pointer is set, even when the constraint then does nothing; the new velocity in the landing case has fourth lane 1; the position's fourth lane is set to 1 only in the landing case; the middle band stores vy = 1.05 s (s = d / k) for either sign of s; the factor in the landing case is 1.05 when s < 0 and 0.95 otherwise. Compute in the code's order (s = d / k first, then (v + (F / W) k) - s, then times k): the test vector below is a rounding borderline in single precision (c' is 0 up to rounding), so do not use it as an exact check. Test vector (shipped platform, pack 1382 object 27 sinking at 0.2217 units a tick; k = 1/30; a hero standing on it with gap 0, vy 0, no force): d = -0.2217, d / k = -6.65; c' = -0.2217 + (0 + 0 + 6.65) / 30 = 0.0000, which is 0 or less, so the velocity becomes (0, 1.05 times -6.65, 0) = (0, -6.98, 0), the alternate-friction flag is set and the hero is put back on the platform top: he sinks with it. For a hero standing on ground of the pack's top-level patches the pointer is zero and the ordinary constraint of item 5b applies. A second shipped platform is the pack 956 lift (object 9, an inline 10 by 10 grid of spacing 1.0 at origin (-706.91, -827.66); the lift track rises from about y 4.7 to 67.1): the module moves the hero only to the two lift locators and then the object's animation alone carries him, through this item, with no script carry (R-0259; High for the mechanism, Medium for its applying in the blocked scripted state: no code was found that disables the ground attach or this constraint for it). Details in SPEC-0043 (956, Lift).
7. Fall law (High). In the air with gain G, each tick: vy(n) = vy(n - 1) - 32 G / 30, then y(n) = y(n - 1) + vy(n) / 30. From rest the drop after K ticks is (32 G / 900) times K (K + 1) / 2 = 0.0177778 G K (K + 1). This equals a continuous gravity of 4 g G = 128 G units per second squared at 60 ticks per second (a speed in units per second is 2 v). The weight cancels. Gains in use: 1.0 (128 units per second squared; the default, standing, the launch tick and landings), 0.3 (38.4) and 0.35 (44.8). The hero's jumps and falls use this law with the gains set by the hero air states (SPEC-0033, Behavior 50).
8. Tunnel guard and other motion-record helpers (new 2026-11-09; High, read in full).
   - **Final tunnel guard** (the last step of driver branch 0). It runs only when a global enable word (0x2DD438) is non-zero, the owner is not in state 0x2F and the owner is in a grid cell that has entries. The segment runs from the tick-start position (the record's +0x110, not an argument) to the position, both lifted 0.5 in y. For each cell entry whose ground-entry mask (u16 at +0x42) is 0 or enabled in the current world's collision mask (+0x56B4), every item whose byte +0x4C is not 6 is tested with the segment-versus-box test. On the first hit: the position becomes the tick-start position (the whole quad) and, if the owner is in state 0x25 or 0x26, the touched flag (+0x238) is set to 1; the walk stops.
   - **Reset of the motion tuning** (the defaults every record gets at construction): pending offset quad +0x270 = (0, 0, 0, 1); penetration tolerance +0x28 = −0.1; gravity gain +0x188 = 1.0; +0x184 = 1.0e9; +0x18C = −1; collidable +0x284 = 1; no-gravity +0x144 = 0; no-snap +0x148 = 0; nearest-item token +0x178 = −1 and distance +0x17C = 1.0e9; nearest index +0x170 = −1 and distance +0x174 = 1.0e9; movable-block index +0x180 = −1; quad +0x1A0 = (0, 0, 0, 1); floor flag +0x150 = 0; +0x230, +0x234, touched +0x238 and object-wall +0x240 = 0; +0x260 = 1; boost flag +0x2F0 = 0; contact-shape count +0x2F8 = 0. The record constructor sets the class table, initialises a pose-and-flag member at +0xA0, runs two empty array loops (5 and 10 elements) and then this reset.
   - **Root-motion step of one record tick** (the body-basis update; runs only when the owner and its model instance exist and the model's player gate word is non-zero): the owner's class method (+0x194) supplies the step in body axes (x side, y up, z forward). It is zeroed to (0, 0, 0, 1) when dot(body forward, owner heading) < 0 unless the owner is in state 0x15, 0x17 or 0x29 (these keep the step), and always zeroed in state 0x35; it is also zeroed when, for a non-hero owner (character id at +0x14C not 0) whose class method (+0xE4) answers non-zero and a selected hero exists, the 3D distance from the selected hero's position to this record's position is at most (this record's radius + the hero record's radius). The world step = side × x + up × y + forward × z (rows +0x290, +0x2A0, +0x2B0), passed through the ground-normal-component removal, stored as the last displacement (+0x120) and added to the position (+0x50) with w = 1.
   - **Planar clamp:** a delta added through the clamped planar helper has its x and z lanes each clamped to [−1.5, 1.5] and added to the position; its y lane (unclamped) is added only while the no-gravity flag (+0x144) is set. (Its caller is the ground-follow probe; this agrees with the 1.5 clamp of the platform carry in item 6b, Medium that it is the same clamp.)
   - **Step along a direction, clamped:** step = speed × dt ÷ weight; candidate = velocity + (direction + force) × dt ÷ weight, len = |candidate|, unit = candidate ÷ len; if |step| > len then step = −len (negative on purpose); the velocity gets unit × step added; when the alternate-friction flag (+0x2EC) is set the velocity's y lane is restored to its value before the call.
   - **Box probe and slide** (used by ring sprite probes): the reference point is the position plus the pending offset (the offset is then cleared); a surface probe with reach 0.75 is made; a hit gives the slide response (depth, normal, flags 1, 1, 0) and the result is whether it responded. With no hit, the predicted point = reference + (velocity + (force ÷ weight) × dt) × dt, plus 0.5 × the last displacement when its length exceeds 0.1, w = 1 (dt = 1/30 from the physics block word 1); a second probe there on a hit gives the slide response with move-out 0 when the normal's y > 0.1, else 1, and returns 1.
   - **Orientation view rows** (used by effect and enemy surface blocks, not by characters): from an object block with frame rows at +0x10, +0x20, +0x30 and a pivot at +0x40: eye Q = pivot − 4 × row(+0x10) − 4 × row(+0x30), y lowered by 2.0, w = 1; view rows +0x90 = row(+0x30), +0xA0 = row(+0x10), +0xB0 = row(+0x20), each with w = −dot3(row, Q); the constants 2.0, 8.0 and 8.0 are stored at +0x74, +0x78 and +0x7C and the quad at +0x80 is zeroed.

### 14B. Chain-controller sample ring (not the history of Behavior 14)
