Confidence: Medium-High for the layout and chain-follow controller route; Low for the ultimate owner. The chain-follow controller keeps a pointer to the sample-ring object at +0x74. A sample is 48 bytes (0x30), and entries sit 0x80 bytes apart. A push writes at the current index, advances it by one, then wraps to zero at the count stored at ring-object +0xBC. The chain-follow update invokes the push twice per update call. This ring is distinct from the five-slot motion-record history of Behavior 14: that history stores 16-byte positions, samples every 60 ticks, and its slot index does not advance in the examined path. The ultimate character or world-object owner and update cadence remain open (Open question 147).

### 14C. Hero root-step travel (High)

The heroes move over the ground by the root motion of the clip they play. The travel comes from the clip's per-key root records (table A of SPEC-0005: x, y and z as s16 × 0.000977, one displacement per key in the hero's local frame, x to the side, y up, z along the facing), not from the bone-0 channels. The scale 0.000977 is a fixed program constant (the single-precision value with bit pattern 0x3A800EAE), about 0.04 percent larger than 1/1024; the clean side uses that exact float. Confidence: High.

1. **Root step.** When the hero's animation object evaluates the playing clip (step 3 of the hero order, Behavior 3), layer 0's root step is set to the root record of the current key k (the whole part of the cursor) times the clip speed c = R × 0.5 ÷ S (SPEC-0005, Playback 1a and 5). It is stored at sub-object +0xA0 (Layout, animation player layers) and is used on the next tick.
   - **Clip change (High).** When a new clip starts on layer 0, the root step comes from the new clip from its first sampled tick, at full size, while its pose is still fading in (SPEC-0005, crossfade). The travel therefore switches at once and does not ramp with the fade. Example (Tor, walk to run, R = 1): the step goes from 0.2252 to 0.3214 per tick on the run clip's first tick, although the run pose takes 16 ticks to fade in.
   - **Held one-shot (High for the rule; Medium for its effect in play).** While a one-shot clip is finished and held at its last key with a non-zero clip speed, its root step is doubled every tick: 2 × root(F − 1) × c, where F is the clip's key count. That form is for mode 2 clips; modes 0 and 1 differ (SPEC-0005, Root step in modes 0 and 1). This is an original quirk; which held one-shots have a non-zero last root record is not counted.
   - **Speed 0 (High).** When layer 0's clip speed is 0, the root step is set to (0, 0, 0, 1) and nothing else of the layer changes, so the travel step adds nothing.
2. **Gate.** The travel step runs only when the animation player's gate word (sub-object +0x40) is nonzero.
3. **Read.** A hero class accessor returns the whole stored root step, all four lanes (x, y, z and w).
4. **Zeroing.** The step is set to zero for the tick when the stick direction points more than 90 degrees away from the facing (dot product below 0), except in hero states 21, 23 and 41, so the hero turns in place until the facing is within 90 degrees (SPEC-0033). It is also zero while the hero state is 53, and, for the partner hero, while it is within the sum of the two heroes' radii (motion record +0x08 of each) of the active hero (Medium for the partner rule).
5. **Basis.** From the facing f (flattened, unit length): B3 = f; B1 = normalise((0, 1, 0) × f), or (1, 0, 0) when that cross product is shorter than 0.01; B2 = B3 × B1. The basis is stored in the motion record at +0x290 (B1), +0x2A0 (B2) and +0x2B0 (B3) on every tick, except while the hero state is 53. This builder belongs to the motion record and is not the one of Behavior 8c. Example: f = (0, 0, 1) gives B1 = (1, 0, 0) and B2 = (0, 1, 0).
6. **Displacement.** D = x B1 + y B2 + z B3 for the root step (x, y, z).
7. **Ground projection.** While the record's height above the ground (+0x24, used when the record's word at +0x150 is zero) is below 1.0, D is projected onto the ground plane, D minus dot(n, D) times n with the ground normal n. Hero states 21, 30 and 41, and records with the flag at +0x144 set, skip the projection. The normal and the gap are those of the ground attach probe at the start of the same tick (Behavior 14A, item 5b), and the normal is the true tilted sample normal, not a straight-up vector (SPEC-0018, Surface normal of a sample). On a slope of angle a along the direction of travel the step length becomes |D| cos a.
8. **Apply.** D is stored as the record's last displacement (+0x120) and added to the record position (+0x50), with w set to 1.

**Timing (Medium-High).** The step used in tick n was written at the end of tick n − 1 (Behavior 3, hero order). From rest, the first tick of a stick push moves 0, and every later tick moves one full step. After the stick is released the hero moves one more step, then stops. There is no other acceleration or deceleration. A change of tilt or status changes the clip speed in the hero update (Behavior 3A); the root step written at the end of that tick carries the new speed, so the change shows from the next tick.

**Stick re-aim in the walk-and-turn state (High).** In hero state 32 the stick handler (SPEC-0033) re-aims the stored root step before the travel step reads it: the new step is unit(side, 0, forward) times the length of the old step, with the unit vector and the length both taken over the x, y and z lanes only. It sets no speed: the step keeps the length that the playing clip gave it. No rule makes the step settle at a fraction of the stick.

**Ground speeds (High).** Both heroes, R = 1, flat ground, 60 ticks per second:
- Walk (animation ids 3 and 8): root z 0.4504 per key (461 × 0.000977), S = 1, so 0.2252 units per tick, 13.51 units/s.
- Run (ids 4 and 9): root z 0.6429 per key, S = 1, so 0.3214 per tick, 19.29 units/s.
- Slow walk (ids 2 and 7): root z 0.1671 per key, S = 1, so 0.0835 per tick, about 5.0 units/s (Tor's clip adds a small side sway).
- Reverse (ids 6 and 11 for both heroes; ids 5 and 10 for Roberta): root z −0.9008 per key, S = 2, so 0.2252 per tick backward, 13.51 units/s. Tor's ids 5 and 10 (his clip 96) have root z −0.2501 per key, S = 2, so 0.0625 per tick backward, 3.75 units/s.
- Sidestep and turn-step clips: root x about ±0.88 to ±0.90 per key, S = 2, about 13.3 to 13.5 units/s.
- Carry walk (id 106): about 0.0575 per tick, about 3.45 units/s, the same for both heroes.
- Idle: 0.

At other rates the speed is (units per tick at R = 1) × P × 60, with the per-tick scale P of Behavior 3A. The tilt tiers and the status factors that set P are in SPEC-0033 and SPEC-0036. One walk cycle (20 keys, 40 ticks) and one run cycle (14 keys, 28 ticks) each cover 9.0 units.

### 14D. Non-hero travel (townsfolk, enemies and bosses)

Confidence: High unless marked.

Non-hero characters move by root motion exactly as the heroes do. Every character that a region creates (townsfolk, common enemies and bosses) is one object of the shared NPC kind with a motion record of the same record kind as the heroes'.

1. **Per-tick order.** For each active character of a region, in this order: (a) the NPC update (Behavior 3, step 3; Behavior 3E), including its class arbiter when allowed by the base state; (b) the motion-record tick: facing, basis, displacement, pre-step, gravity, driver and integrator (Behaviors 8, 14A, 14C and 14E); (c) the animation pass: start any pending clip request, evaluate the playing clip, and store layer 0's root step for the next tick. After the active region characters, selected hero and petpet complete their passes, the global behavior schedulers advance other eligible coroutines; they skip class coroutines because their auto-run word is 0 and the calls use a zero mask. Class-coroutine changes to goals, steering and clip requests made in the NPC update can affect that character's motion-record tick and animation pass in the same tick.
2. **Step.** The step V of a non-hero character is layer 0's root step (the root record of key k times the clip speed c = R × 0.5 ÷ S; Behavior 14C item 1, with the same clip-change, held one-shot and speed-0 rules) plus F, where F = (0, 0, 0) for ground rows. Flying rows add the row speed of item 7. In AI mode 20 the step is (0, 0, 0) (confirmed 2026-11-09 for the Can Fly 1 path only; Can Fly 2 has no such reset).
3. **Displacement.** D = Vx B1 + Vy B2 + Vz B3 with the record basis of Behavior 14C item 5, then the ground projection and the apply of Behavior 14C items 7 and 8.
4. **Zeroing.** The step is discarded for the tick when the move direction points more than 90 degrees from the facing (dot product below 0; as Behavior 14C item 4). In normal states this never happens, because the facing equals the move direction (Behavior 8); it can happen in base states 16, 33, 51 and 55, where the facing is kept. Corrected 2026-11-09 (High; the step routine was read in full): the facing-mismatch zeroing has exemptions the earlier text omitted: owner base states 0x15 (21), 0x17 (23) and 0x29 (41) keep the step even when the dot is negative, and base state 0x35 (53) always discards it. The near-hero zeroing applies to a non-hero owner (character id not 0) whose class method answers non-zero, while a selected hero exists, with distance ≤ (own radius + the selected hero's record radius) (confirmed High); the step is the owner method's body-axis step D = x × side + y × up + z × forward followed by the ground-normal-component removal (confirmed). The step is also discarded when the character is within (its record radius + the active hero's record radius) of the active hero, as a 3D distance, while it is in AI mode 19 with agro sub-state 2 and sequencer state 0 to 3 (the attack sequencer of SPEC-0038), or while a per-tick stop flag is set (High for the flag: it is cleared at the start of each NPC update and by the character reset, and only the Black Knight's slow-swing and angry-swing hit routines set it, on each tick they run; SPEC-0012).
5. **Timing (High for the order; Medium-High for the one-tick root-motion lag).** A class-coroutine animation request made in the NPC update on tick n is started by that character's animation pass on tick n. The motion-record tick on n still uses the root step saved by the previous animation pass; the newly evaluated clip's first root step moves the character on tick n + 1. Thereafter the record uses the previous animation pass's root step each tick. When the clip changes, the old clip's last step is used once more (Behavior 3; SPEC-0005).
6. **Ground following.** Ground characters follow the ground by the heroes' path: D is projected onto the ground plane while the record is within 1.0 of the ground (Behavior 14C item 7), then the record's gravity and integrator run (Behavior 14A, items 5 to 7).
7. **Flying rows (High for the rule, the row words and the target height; Medium for the Can Fly 2 height in play).** A row flies when its Can Fly word (monster row +0x94; SPEC-0029) is 1 or 2. For Can Fly 1 the flight rules apply only while the character's grounded counter is 0 or less (a hit can ground it); otherwise it moves as a ground character.
   - Movement classes 0, 1 and 2 become 7 (forward), 3 and 4 become 8 (backward), 5 becomes 9 and 6 becomes 10, and the flying animation slots are used.
   - Row speed v = Flying Agro Velocity (row +0x90) while the AI mode is 19 (agro), else Flying Idle Velocity (row +0x8C). Each tick F = v × (1/30) (the physics block's time scale, Where it lives) along the class axis: class 7 +B3, class 8 −B3, class 9 +B1, class 10 −B1. In units per second the row speed is 2 × v. F is added only while the animation being played is the slot animation of the current class. During the attack sequencer (agro sub-state 2) in sequencer states 7, 8, 9 or 12, F = +B3 × v ÷ 30 whatever the animation. While animations 138 or 139 (Can Fly 1) or 53 or 54 (Can Fly 2) play, F = 0 (the root step only). Corrected 2026-11-09 (High): the agro sub-state 2 requirement applies to Can Fly 1 only; for Can Fly 2 the test is the sequencer state (7, 8, 9 or 12) alone, with no sub-state test, no hero-height lift and no AI-mode-20 reset. The step keeper needs the first class predicate (+0x154) false and, for Can Fly 1, the second (+0x19C) true. The rate is the row float at +0x8C, or +0x90 in AI mode 0x13, times 1/30; Can Fly 0 returns the root step only and any other Can Fly value returns (0, 0, 0, 1) (confirmed High).
   - Height: each tick dy = target height − y. If |dy| > 0.1, y moves 0.1 toward the target (6 units/s); otherwise y is set to the target. The keeper writes the motion record's position directly, before the record tick. For Can Fly 1 only, during sequencer states 7 to 9 and 12 the target is the hero's height + 2.0; Can Fly 2 always uses the goal height.
   - Target height (High): the goal height of Behavior 21. For Can Fly 1, the hero's y + min(Max Fly Height (row +0xB4), 0.2 × the horizontal distance to the hero), and at least the ground under the goal + 2.0 (the words 0.2 at 0x2DD87C and 2.0 at 0x2DD880, Where it lives); then, while the character's ambush word is 0, the home point's height replaces it. The ambush word is -1 at creation, 0 only while the enemy waits in state 8, 1 and 2 during the emerge and stays 2 afterwards, so the home-height rule applies only to an enemy waiting in state 8. The goal height is computed by the walk-target setter, at the moment a walk target is set (every steering call of a moving sub-state), not every tick; sub-states that set no walk target keep the previous goal height. The ground floor term probes from 8.0 above the goal point. In the hit-reaction sub-state of kind 6 the walk target is set to the character's own position and the goal height is only capped at Max Fly Height. In sequencer states 7, 8, 9 and 12 the goal height is the hero's y + 2.0. The keeper moves 0.1 per tick and runs only for characters for which the dead predicate (base state 16) and the parked predicate are false. For Can Fly 2, the hero's height (or the home height while the ambush word is 0), at most ground + Max Fly Height, with no lower bound. In AI mode 10, sub-state 6, the goal height is only capped at Max Fly Height.
   - The shared NPC step getter also handles this height and contributes to movement. The motion-record calls it only when the animation sub-object is present and its movement gate is nonzero. For Can Fly 1, its height-adjustment branch requires the first class predicate to be false and the second to be true; for Can Fly 2 it requires the first to be false. It moves position y by 0.1 toward the target, or sets y to the target within 0.1. Where the row-speed gates allow, the getter combines that directional row-speed vector with the layer-0 root step; the motion-record basis then turns the returned vector into world displacement and adds it to position. The row-speed mode and sequencer gates are listed above; the meaning of the two class predicates remains unknown. The same getter belongs to the shared region-character kind used by townsfolk, enemies and bosses. **[High for the known paths; Medium for the predicate meanings]**
   - Can Fly 1 characters that are flying set the record's no-gravity flag (+0x144), which zeroes the vertical part of the gravity of Behavior 14A item 5 and skips the ground projection of Behavior 14C item 7; their height comes only from the height rule above.
   - Can Fly 2 rows never set the no-gravity flag (the flag is set only by the Can Fly 1 flying test), and their gravity gain stays 1.0. Gravity and the keeper therefore both act (Medium for the result in play): in the air the downward speed grows by 32/30 per tick, so the fall per tick (vy ÷ 30) passes the keeper's 0.1 lift after 3 airborne ticks, and the character sinks to the ground. On the ground (gap within 0.1, Behavior 14A item 5) gravity is projected away and the keeper can lift it again; the net effect is a body that stays within a few tenths of a unit of the ground, bobbing, rather than at its goal height. A black-box check of a Can Fly 2 character (the Restless Spirit, type 222) would confirm it.
8. **Townsfolk speeds (main-family value -7, body 526).** At R = 1 every townsfolk locomotion clip has S = 3, so c = 1/6 key per tick. Per tick = root z × 0.000977 ÷ 6:

| Movement type (SetAI_MovementType) | Animation id | Body 526 clip | Per tick | Units/s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 walk (walk classes, path walk, wander) | 1 | 104 or 107 (table variants with those clips) | 0.08337 | 5.00 |
| 0 walk, table variant B | 1 | 108 | 0.02850 | 1.71 |
| 0 walk with speed code 6 (patrol, movement class 0 only; classes 1 and 2 use the rows below) | 8 | 104 or 107 (all variants) | 0.08337 | 5.00 |
| 1 run | 6 | 105 or 111 | 0.22504 forward, with a small mean side drift of about −0.0065 | 13.50 (side about −0.39) |
| 2 fast run | 5 | 106 | 0.32160 | 19.30 |
| 3 and 4 | 3 | 48 (idle) | 0 | 0 |
| carry walk | 59 | 110 | 0.02084 | 1.25 |

   The move type defaults to 0 and is set per character by SetAI_MovementType (0 walk, 1 run, 2 fast run). These speeds equal the heroes' slow walk, walk and run speeds (Behavior 14C). Other characters travel at the root speed of their own clip bank (SPEC-0038, SPEC-0019, SPEC-0020).
9. **Size selector and travel (High).** The per-bone translation scale chosen by a character's size selector (SPEC-0005, Per-bone translation scale) multiplies the bone translation samples only. The root record is sampled, multiplied by the clip speed and stored as the root step before, and apart from, that scale, so it is never scaled. A giant variant that plays the same clip as its small kind (Giant Drackonack and Drackonack, for example) travels at exactly the same speed per tick, drawn with a larger body. The rewrite must not multiply the root step by the size factor.
10. **Held one-shot clips keep moving the body (High for the rule; Medium for how it looks in play).** While a non-looping main clip is finished and still selected, its root step in a mode 2 clip is 2 x root(F - 1) x c every tick (SPEC-0005, Layer step, step 8; modes 0 and 1 differ, SPEC-0005, Root step in modes 0 and 1), where F is its key count and c its clip speed. The region character loop runs the motion-record tick for every active character, dead ones included, and the displacement step has no dead-state gate. So a corpse (base state 16) whose death clip ends on a key with root keeps sliding at that doubled step, along its kept facing, until a navmesh border stops it, the edge guard of Behavior 14E cancels the step, or the character is released; a reanimating enemy lying in its class state 16 does the same for its whole countdown (the reanimating skeletons end their death clip on a zero key and lie still; SPEC-0064). One-shot gait clips do the same once they outlast their keys (for example a back-off clip that is a one-shot). Per-kind values are in the enemy build guides (SPEC-0063 to SPEC-0065). Example: a Minion Wizard corpse (death clip 70 keys at speed byte 1, last key -0.214) slides 14.98 units back during the clip's 140 ticks and then 0.214 units per tick (12.8 units per second) for as long as it is held.
11. **No impulse from hits (High).** No hero attack, hero shot or Mote object writes a region character's velocity or force. A hit moves a common enemy only through the root travel of its hit-reaction clip, after the take-hit hook has snapped its facing to the attacker (SPEC-0038 Behavior 37 step 11), so the slide points straight away from the attacker. Gravity, friction and the ground constraint act on the velocity of a region character only when something else (gravity on a drop, a moving platform) gives it one.
12. **Side gaits (High).** The record basis has B1 = up x B3. A side clip whose root x is positive moves the body along +B1 and a negative one along -B1, whatever direction the steering asked for: the side steering forms of Behavior 8a set only the facing. SPEC-0038 Behavior 51 item 7 gives the result for the strafe circles of common enemies (each circle runs away from its own steering point).
13. **Ledges (High for the rules; Medium-High for their combined effect).** The edge guard of Behavior 14E compares the ground gap measured at the start of the record tick, before this tick's step, so it cancels the step of a body that is already more than 2.0 above the ground; it does not look ahead. What stops a walking region character at a ledge is the navmesh confinement step of its driver: border edges act as walls and an off-mesh position is put back to the last on-mesh position in the same tick (SPEC-0056, Wall confinement). A body that is off an edge with no mesh under it (no mesh assigned, or carried past a border by a push) falls under gravity with its horizontal steps cancelled while its gap stays above 2.0.

### 14E. Edge guard (motion-record pre-step) (High)

Each motion-record tick saves the start position P0 (+0x110), runs the root-motion step (the root displacement D is added to the position P; Behaviors 14C and 14D) and then the record's pre-step (Behavior 14, item 5). The pre-step begins with the edge guard. It fires when all of these hold: the owner's class kind query returns 1; the record's lock word (+0x280) is 0; the ground gap (+0x24) is above 2.0 in magnitude; and the owner is neither in AI mode 2 nor in AI mode 1 with its timer expired. When it fires, the position becomes P' = P + 2 (P0 - P) = 2 P0 - P = P0 - D, with w = 1: this tick's root step is reversed. The rest of the pre-step (the deadly-ground test and the ground-record tests) is then skipped for that tick. The 2.0 is a fixed program constant, and the value 2.0 that the method returns is not used. (Reconfirmed 2026-11-09 against the readable form of the pre-step, High: class query +0xFC equal to 1, lock word 0, |gap| > 2.0, the class methods at +0x19C and +0x1A4 false, P' = P + 2.0 × (P0 − P), w = 1.) (Confirmed from code: the gap test is on the magnitude, the lock word is 0 and the two class questions are both 0.) The hero classes return 0 for the kind query, so the guard never applies to Tor or Roberta; it keeps NPCs and enemies from walking off edges higher than 2.0. The two "neither" tests are the owner class's vtable questions: for class-17 common enemies they are the Can Fly 1 airborne test (Can Fly 1 and the retarget countdown at most 0) and the Can Fly 2 test, so an airborne flyer is never guarded while a knocked-down Crokabek (countdown above 0) is, and its falling or sliding root step is reversed whenever the gap exceeds 2.0. SPEC-0033 Behavior 38 points here. The live petpet answers 2 to the kind query, so it is never edge-guarded and walks off any ledge (SPEC-0047).

Rest of the pre-step (ground-record tests; Low-Medium for the roles), run when the guard does not fire, on the record's current ground record, in this order:
- If the ground record's word at +0x38 is 2: when the SIGNED ground gap (no absolute value; corrected from code, High) is at most 0.085 (a fixed constant): if the ground entry's byte at +0x0D is 0xFF, both heroes are killed (the same double kill as below); otherwise the owner's death method runs with TWO arguments, 1 and 1 (High from code; the earlier text said a class call with the value 1; this is the deadly-ground test of SPEC-0055).
- Otherwise, if the ground record's short word at +0x40 is 2: a flag is set when the SIGNED gap is below 0.2 (corrected from code, High: no absolute value, so a hero below the surface also counts).
- Otherwise, if the character's navmesh height cache (+0x17C) is set: compare the record's reference height (+0x140) with the current motion-position height (+0x50 Y) plus 2.3 (word 0x2DD43C). When reference height is at most that sum, the flag is set if the ground record's +0x40 short word is 2. When the height comparison fails, the helper that runs is the double kill of the hero and its partner (Resolved: the linked overlay is more than 2.3 units above the position; SPEC-0057, Ground hazards rule 3). The comparison is an inclusive upper bound of 2.3 units above the character's current position height (High, confirmed from code). Open check: one note reads the +0x17C of the motion record as the nearest-item distance (Behavior 14A item 1); this pre-step tests a +0x17C of the owner's own record, whose identity as a navmesh cache was not re-verified in the same pass (see Open questions). These ground-record tests act only on a character with the hero flag set and a ground entry; they are the hero hazard rules of SPEC-0057.
- When the flag is set, a count of 0.4 times the run-time word 0x368F94 is formed as a whole number. When 0x368F94 minus the count times 2.5 is below the tick length (1/60 s), a timed class call runs with the arguments 10 and 2. Resolved: the timed class call is the hurting-surface hit of 10 HP with attacker -1 and reaction flag 0, once every 2.5 s of game clock (SPEC-0057, Ground hazards rule 2; High). Test vector: clock 5.0 with dt 1/60: 5.0 − 2 × 2.5 = 0 < 0.0167, the hit fires; clock 5.02: 0.02 is not below 0.0167, no hit. The runtime time word starts at zero and advances by the frame delta at the end of each pass (SPEC-0007); no other static writer was found.
Then the ground constraint of Behavior 14A item 5b runs.

### 15. State numbers (what is and is not known)

Confidence: High for the enemy state label table; Medium for the enemy class; High for the hero route list (taken from SPEC-0033 Behavior 3).

- The state numbers 0 to 23 are the enemy states that have labelled debug text. The labels belong to an enemy debug overlay. They are not game behaviour and are not specified here, and they are never drawn in the shipped game: the labels are written into a per-character text buffer, and the only routine that queues those buffers for drawing is never called (High). The state numbers used by enemies are the ones in SPEC-0038 (state 19 is the agro state there).
- The enemy debug overlay table is not the hero's action-state table. It gives no hero behaviour and must not be used as a hero state list.
- Hero action states are one number per hero (SPEC-0033, Hero action state field). The routes, the routines that have a description and the default are in SPEC-0033 Behavior 3. In summary, by number:
  - 14: no routine; the hero's update ends for the tick.
  - 28: hang from a grab point and leap between grab points; 32: strafe (walk and turn); 43 to 46: strafe jumps; 56: bounce pad (SPEC-0033, Behaviors 9, 10, 11 and 50).
  - 16: death (SPEC-0055); 18: push and pull a block; 50 and 52: scripted walks (RunHero, WalkHero, WalkHeroAndOrient); 17: hurt (SPEC-0036); 49: feed a petpet (SPEC-0047) (SPEC-0033 Behavior 3). States 51, 55, 59 and 60 are in Behavior 15.3.
  - 1 to 6, 22, 39 and 40: one shared stance and sneak routine; 20: shield guard; 7 to 11: the jump and air states (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50).
  - 13 (attack, SPEC-0036); 19, 21, 23, 26, 27, 29, 30, 35, 37, 38, 47 and 58 (traversal states, SPEC-0033 Behavior 51); 41 (ladder, SPEC-0033 Behavior 13); 48 (long-fall landing, SPEC-0033 Behavior 9B). Routes 12, 15, 24, 25, 33, 34, 36, 51, 53, 54, 55, 57, 59 and 60 are described in Behavior 15.3.
  - Any other value, including 31 and 42 (which have no route), runs the default: requests animation 0 and calls a routine that only prints a debug message (no reset); no state is written (Behavior 15.3).
- State 30 (0x1E) is a route in the hero dispatcher and is one of the states counted as busy (Behavior 15.2). It is the hang from a ledge (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51), written by the in-air ledge chooser of the jump states; this spec assigns it no behaviour of its own.
- No state number above 19 that is not a hero route has a known meaning.

### 15.1 Timed attack release

Confidence: Medium. Three enemy attack routines share one shape (SPEC-0029 owns their kinds). Each keeps a fired flag and a recovery phase for its attack. The routine checks two tests each tick: whether it is in its attack state at all, and whether its attack has ended (the attack-ended test).

1. Not in the attack state: the routine starts its attack animation (animation 0x16 in one routine, animation 7 in the other two).
2. In the attack state and the attack has not ended: at the animation's release event, if the fired flag is clear, the routine sets the fired flag and launches one projectile at the aim point of Behavior 17. The flag is set once per attack and is not cleared inside these routines, so a release that comes after the flag is set does nothing. Resolved (Medium-High): the three routines belong to the Faerie Sisters (Behavior 15.1a), and the flag is cleared by the sister's state chooser, which writes 0 to it each time it picks one of the three attack states (4, 5 or 6), and by the sister's recovery step; so every new attack choice can fire again.
3. The attack has ended: the routine clears its phase word and draws a recovery timer, a whole number of ticks uniform from 30 to 45 (0.5 to 0.75 s at 60 ticks per second) in two routines, or from 120 to 240 (2 to 4 s) in the third. A release that comes after the attack has ended never fires.

In the 2 to 4 s routine, while the attack is running, the owner also turns toward the target every tick with a rate argument of 0.25. The turn method that takes that rate is the shared aim rule of Behavior 8, called with weight 0.25 (Resolved, Open question 52; High). The 0.5 and 0.75 values and the 2 and 4 values are code multipliers of the tick rate; the tick rate itself is the boot word of Where it lives (60.0) and the tick length of SPEC-0007.

### 15.1a Timed charge = the Faerie Sisters' hit routine (High)

Confidence: High for the owner and the mapping; the behaviour is specified in SPEC-0011 (shield and damage). Live: it runs in every Faerie Sisters fight.

The "timed record" is one sister's state block (SPEC-0011), and the routine is the sisters' hit routine, called by the sisters' dodge-and-hit check when a hero's melee swing (flag argument 0) or a hero shot (flag argument 1) hits a sister. The fields map as follows:
- the kind word is the sister's state (7 group attack, 8 flinch, 9 dead; 2 and 3 are the two dodges); kinds 2, 3, 8 and 9 take no damage;
- the sub-state is the group attack sub-state (2 attack, 3 hit);
- the integer step is the hit's damage, the float "timer" is the sister's total damage taken, and R is the sister's current HP (the character's hit points); "reset the timing helper" is the HP deduction, which runs the death hook when HP reaches 0;
- the integer counter is the hits taken during one group attack;
- "request animation 1 through the body" is the character's state setter with state 1;
- the run-time gate word G (0x36DE34, part of the sisters' shared block at +0x1D4) is the shield flag: 0 while shielded. Each sister's main loop writes 0 when it starts, and the first shot hit while shielded writes 1. While G is 0, a melee hit deals no damage; it passes the selected hero's world position and body-forward vector to the one-shot shield-effect 4008 frame helper, then sets Tor's sub-state to 13 when Tor is selected (Roberta's sub-state is unchanged). A shot hit raises level event 1 and sets shared G = 1, with no damage; that path requests hero action state 1 and does not set a per-sister flag;
- the 1.5 offset is the height of the hit effect above the sister; the helper whose return becomes the sub-state draws the flinch animation choice.

Do not implement this routine from this section; SPEC-0011 gives the fight-level rules.

### 15.2 Hero busy predicate by state

Confidence: High for the list, for the other three parts and for the busy test being the same routine as IsHeroOccupied; Medium for the meaning of the list. The predicate is the one given by SPEC-0033 Behavior 3 and is repeated here so that the hero's busy test is in one place.

The hero counts as busy when any of four tests holds. (1) The action state is one of 7 to 19, 21, 23 to 30, 35, 37, 38, 41, 43 to 49, 53, 54, and 56 to 60 (the "list"; 41 equality tests, state 15 twice). (2) The action state is one of the script-held states 33, 48, 50, 51, 52, 53 or 55. (3) The stun status (status 0) has a positive timer. (4) The screen effect is moving. The input step of the hero also treats the hero as busy while the game-state box word is above 0 (a test at that call site, not part of the routine). Never busy by the routine: states 1 to 6, 20, 22, 31, 32, 34, 36, 39, 40 and 42 (when no stun and no screen effect applies).

The busy list reads no sub-state and no air phase: state 13 and states 7 to 11 are busy whatever the sub-state or air phase. The sub-state and air-phase readings (state 13 with sub-state 2 to 12 for Tor, 5 or 12 for Roberta; states 7 to 11 with air phase 8 or 9) belong only to the attack-state test of Behavior 18 (High).

The busy test of Behavior 18 is not a status-flag test: it is this routine, and it is IsHeroOccupied itself (below; High).

**IsHeroOccupied (High).** The level-script query IsHeroOccupied (SPEC-0030, row 170) calls the very routine described above, with the selected hero. It is true when the selected hero is in action state 7 to 19, 21, 23 to 30, 33, 35, 37, 38, 41 or 43 to 60; or has an active stun (status 0); or while the screen effect is moving (the fade alpha differs from its target, or the letterbox bar alpha or bar progress differs from its target; SPEC-0052). Otherwise it is false. The only difference between the script query and the hero's input step is the call-site test of the game-state box word, which the script query does not make. The routine has no second, narrower version: the button chain, the speed tiers, the save-point prompt, the item showcase decision and the script query all call it. A hero in state 50 or 52 (a scripted walk) or 55 (a looped scripted animation) is busy and occupied alike, and any hero state becomes busy during a screen fade or while stunned.

### 15.3 Hero routes

These are hero action states (Behavior 15), described by number only. The routes are the ones SPEC-0033 Behavior 3 names. Busy status by the routine in 15.2: states 12, 15, 24, 25, 33, 51, 53, 54, 55, 57, 59 and 60 are busy; 34 and 36 are not. A meaning is given per state where it is known (High unless marked). The animation codes are decimal (137 = 0x89, 143 = 0x8F, 175 = 0xAF, 178 = 0xB2, 179 = 0xB3, 180 = 0xB4, 181 = 0xB5).

- **12** (clear the target list): clears ten hero target-list slots, which hold the values 0 to 9, to -1. No request, no state change, no exit. High. No writer of state 12 by a constant is known (Medium-Low for the meaning).
- **15 and 24** (empty): empty routines. No request, no write, no exit. High.
- **25** (talk): entered by SetConversationTarget and SetConversationTargetWithOrient (SPEC-0022, Conversations), and by LevelMsg when the hero is not busy (without saving the previous state). It clears two hero input words to 0. One of them is an animation code that state 33 later requests. A helper is called with argument 1; if it returns a record, a rate of 0.1 is sent along that record's direction. The state stays 25. Medium. The cleared code word is the hero's default idle animation (+0x938), which SetDefaultHeroIdleAnim sets (SPEC-0030) (High).
- **33** (script hold): entered by the host calls MoveHero, GrabHeroControl and BlockPlayerControl, by the arrival of a scripted walk with the hold option (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 4A.4 and 4A.5), and from 51 and 55. It requests the animation code held in the hero input word that state 25 uses. No timer and no exit in the routine. High for the request and for the word: it is the default idle animation (+0x938), 0 after a talk until a script sets another one.
- **34** (stagger back): requests 58 and moves the hero by -0.075 times the facing each tick; at progress 0.99 the state becomes 1 and the hero is pushed by -1 times the facing. A second case uses animation 72 (not described). No writer by a constant is known. Medium; Medium-Low for the meaning.
- **36** (debug animation preview): reads the debug animation record kept in the pad object (Behavior 1C), which only a debug set-up fills (a character id, an animation id and a loop flag). While the record's character id is negative nothing happens. When it equals the hero's id, the hero requests the record's animation; with the loop flag set the clip progress is also tested against 0.99, with no effect on the state. For any other id the state setter is called with 1, so the hero returns to state 1. No game path writes state 36. Medium-High.
- **51** (scripted animation, entered by the host call PlayHeroAnim): waits for the action progress to pass 0.99 (Where it lives). Then, if the hero flag word at +0x3D0 is nonzero the state becomes 1; if it is zero the state becomes 33. High for the writes. These are state writes through the state setter, not animation requests (High).
- **53** (held by an enemy; written by the grab-and-throw enemy, SPEC-0038 Behavior 15): requests animation 143. Scans the global character list and calls a per-entry test; if any entry reports, the state stays 53; otherwise it becomes 1. Medium. The drawn height is not written in this state (Behavior 14). While it holds the hero, the enemy sets its carry slot to the hero every tick, and the hero is placed at the enemy's attach point k each tick, with the position and basis of Behavior 8c (SPEC-0005, Attach points and carrying; High). The motion record's own basis rebuild is skipped in this state, so the carried basis stays. The hero is not held where he stands.
- **54** (get up after the Plague Serpent's lunge; High for the body, Medium-High for the meaning): requests animation 137 (the long-fall landing clip, 21 keys, 80 ticks, no root travel) while that action is not current. When it is current and the progress passes 0.99, the state becomes 1, 81 ticks after the request. That is the whole body: no position, velocity, gravity, facing, speed, sound or flag write, no button-mash speed-up (that belongs to state 48). The state is busy (no control), is not a hittable target (common enemies pause new attacks), and gives no invulnerability: a strike that lands during it writes state 17 over it. Written by the Plague Serpent's landed lunge (SPEC-0016, Hero get-up state 54) after the take-hit, whatever state the hit left. Test vector: SPEC-0016, Hero get-up state 54.
- **55** (looped scripted animation, entered by the host call LoopHeroAnim; the counter is its loop time): counts the hero counter at +0x8CC down by one per tick, stopping at zero. At zero, if the flag at +0x3D0 is zero the state becomes 33; otherwise it becomes 1. High for the writes; they are state writes, as for 51.
- **57** (carry a Miamouse): entered by Square when the prompt shows the catch-Miamouse label (label 27 of the UI label table, design table 13; SPEC-0022); the drop-Miamouse label (28) ends the carry. It runs on a hero sub-mode word with values 0 to 3. Sub-mode 0 requests 178 until that action is current and progress passes 0.99, then goes to sub-mode 1. Sub-mode 1 runs a stick test; on success it goes to sub-mode 2, and it then requests 179. Sub-mode 2 requests 180 and returns to sub-mode 1 when the stick test fails. Sub-mode 3 requests 181 until its gate, then sets the state to 1 and the sub-mode to 1. Medium. During the carry the hero sets its carry slot to the Miamouse every tick, so the Miamouse is placed at the hero's attach point k (SPEC-0005, Attach points and carrying). The Miamouse is put in its state 33 with its own id 12 requested (the id 12 of this state is the Miamouse's request, not the hero's; High), and it gets an extra offset of scale 1.0 with angles from a run-time vector that is zero unless set (Medium-Low).
- **59** (the arrival hover after a Continue or a load, and the placement written by death end mode 1; not a game-over hold; SPEC-0055): when its action test fails, requests 175 and runs two helper calls (one plays an effect: from code, High, the death effect spawned at the hero's position). When the test passes and the progress reaches 0.99, the state becomes 1 and music stinger 0 plays (added from code, High). High for the exits; Medium for the other helper. The action test is "the current animation is 175" (High). The writers of state 59 and the pose of clip 175 are in SPEC-0055 (sections 2A and 8A).
- **60** (death end, respawn; death end mode 2, SPEC-0055): when its action test fails, requests 137 and, at that moment, the hero takes damage of min(5, hp - 1), so this state never kills (added from code, High; the second helper is not identified). When the test passes and the progress passes 0.99, the state becomes 1. High for the exits. Its action test is "the current animation is 137" (High).
- **Default (0, 31, 42 and any value above 60)**: requests animation 0 and calls a routine that only prints a debug message (one text for state 0, another for every other value); it is not a reset (corrected from code, High; resolves Open question 68). No state is written. High (dispatch).

### 15.4 Action-end resolver (states 33 and 1)

Confidence: High for the writes and for its callers. A resolver ends an action by a hero flag. When the hero flag word at +0x3D0 is zero, it sets the hero's action state to 33. When the flag is nonzero, it sets the state to 1. This is the same rule as routes 51 and 55 of Behavior 15.3. The resolver runs at the arrival of the hero's scripted walks: in the walk-and-orient state once the facing test passes, and in the plain scripted walk when the stick distance is short. The hero's navmesh walk-to-point helpers hold a copy of the same rule. Routes 51 and 55 do not call it; they make the same test themselves.

### 15A. State requests and priority (Medium-High for the acceptance rule; Medium for the setter)

A character's state can be requested with a priority. A request is a state code and a priority p.

1. **Acceptance.** A request with p of 0 or more is accepted only when p is at least the applied priority and strictly greater than the pending priority. An accepted request becomes the pending request, with its priority and its state code.
2. **Hold word.** When the hold word is zero, an accepted request is applied at once: the state is set and the applied priority becomes p. When the hold word is set, the request stays pending. After an immediate apply the pending priority is reset to 0 and the pending state to -1 (High). An accepted request also clears the arbiter's wait counter.
3. **Class default.** A negative state code requests the class default state with priority 1. The default request also sets the townsfolk flag to 1 when the default is class 6 and to 0 otherwise; the flag selects the townsfolk walk slots (Behavior 3C) (High).
4. **Setter.** A state equal to the current state is ignored. A request whose priority is below the applied priority is ignored. Otherwise the state, the applied priority and the state's routine entry are set. States 1 to 39 use the class's routine table. A state of 40 or more is reported as an error by the original.
5. **Current state.** The state read by callers is the pending state when a pending request exists and its priority is at least the applied priority; otherwise it is the applied state.
6. **Walk calls.** The walk calls of SPEC-0030 (rows 106, 108 and 111) request their states with priority 2. So the class priorities are 2 for a script walk and 1 for the default class (High).
7. **Parking (Medium-High).** These requests are applied by the class arbiter inside the NPC update (Behavior 3, step 3). When a higher-priority request is applied while a behaviour coroutine is running, that coroutine is parked, not ended, and the new class starts. When the new class's coroutine ends, the class word and the applied priority reset to 0 and the parked coroutine resumes where it last yielded. So a townsfolk character keeps its mode and its wander point across a script walk and carries on afterwards, while its class word then reads 0.

### 16. Breakable walk over the hero's cell list and contact

Confidence: High for the list identity, the walk, the script hook, the element gate, the break with its piece push and the sound cues by material; Medium for which walk calls each handler. Not described: the setup step of each walk.

The list at hero +0x174 holds world breakables, not characters. The pointer at +0x174 is the grid cell record under the hero's motion record, refreshed on every record tick (Behavior 14); the walks use its cell list B, whose entries are the type-29 second-list entries of SPEC-0028 section 13, and the breakables of SPEC-0048 are the entries these walks act on. The attack's volume is tested against each entry. For kinds 4 and 7, both the static shape-value branch (value at or below 0) and the moving branch (value above 0) use the no-margin segment-versus-rectangle rule of SPEC-0018; the moving branch reuses one segment endpoint pair across five candidate rectangle frames, and neither branch uses the margin rule. The common test requires a plane crossing parameter from 0 through 1 and projected coordinates from 0 through each extent. The candidate frames are the five side/bottom faces described in Open question 41. Nine contact handlers exist: five with a fixed kind, three keyed by the owner, and one unfiltered (the table below). They are called from the attack-family sweeps and from the hero-list walks; which walk calls which handler is Medium (Open question 44). Each walk runs a setup step first (not described). The global hittable list of Behavior 5 is a different list.

Walk, per entry, in order:

1. The entry is considered only when its state word (+0x40) is 1 or 3 (intact) and it has a volume record (+0x60). The volume's kind selects the overlap test: kind 1 sphere, kind 3 finite cylinder (swept against the swing triangle once the weapon tip has moved; SPEC-0036, Hit detection step 6), kinds 4 and 7 a rectangle (a static test when the shape value +0x2C is 0 or below, a moving test when it is above 0); kind 2 is never hit (SPEC-0036, Attack sweeps).
2. An entry that passes is handed to the contact step.

Contact, per entry, in order:

a. Script hook: if a level script registered a breakable callback for the entry's script index (+0x58; RegisterScriptBreakable, SPEC-0030), that callback is started as a script thread (unless it is already running), and the default break and cue are skipped.
b. Element gate: let E_t be the breakable's element byte (+0x5E). E_t = 0 always passes. Otherwise E_t must equal the handler's kind (table below). For the two owner-field handlers, the contact also passes when the owner's field +0x148 or +0x100 is 7. The unfiltered handler applies no gate. On a mismatch the handler does nothing.
c. Direction: unit(entry position - attacker reference point for this handler).
d. Break and cue: the break effect of SPEC-0048 runs with a piece push of 0.25 along (c); it breaks only a state-1 entry, so a state-3 entry stays intact. Then one positioned sound cue plays on the main sound slot at the entry position (+0x30), chosen by the breakable's material word (+0x5C): 2 gives cue 143, 3 gives cue 145, 4 gives cue 146, and any other value (0, 1, 5 and above) gives cue 144. The cue plays even when the entry did not break. These are sound cue ids (SPEC-0031), not effect ids.

Handler kinds (High for the tests; Medium for the walks that call each one):

| Handler | Kind source | Pass rule (breakable element E_t) | Mote ids (SPEC-0021) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Element 2 | fixed kind 2 (Leaf, Rock) | E_t is 0 or 2 | 164, 165 |
| Element 3 | fixed kind 3 (Light, Sun) | E_t is 0 or 3 | 170, 171 |
| Element 4 | fixed kind 4 (Bubble, Water) | E_t is 0 or 4 | 162, 163 |
| Element 5 | fixed kind 5 (Fog, Wind) | E_t is 0 or 5 | 166, 167 |
| Element 6 | fixed kind 6 (Smoke, Shadow) | E_t is 0 or 6 | 168, 169 |
| Owner first word | the owner's first word | E_t is 0 or equal; no kind-7 exemption | none fixed |
| Owner field +0x148 | the owner's field +0x148 | E_t is 0 or equal, or the field is 7 | none fixed |
| Owner field +0x100 | the owner's field +0x100 | E_t is 0 or equal, or the field is 7 | none fixed |
| Unfiltered | none | no test | any |

Kind 1 (Fire, Mote 160 and 161) has no fixed handler. The kind-7 exemption applies only to the two owner-field handlers (+0x148 and +0x100): they pass whenever the owner's field is 7. Kind 7 (Nova, Mote 172 and 173) has no fixed handler. Three further routes use the same core (SPEC-0036, Contact routines and cues): the hero melee route, whose element is that of the hero's slot-2 item, which also passes when E_t = 7, and which sends the hero to action state 4 on a mismatch; Roberta's shot, which also passes when E_t = 7 and always plays cue 144; and a boss route with no element gate.

Contact applies no damage, no knockback impulse, no area effect and no status, and it starts no effect by id (High). Damage for the charm attacks is in SPEC-0036 (Charm attack hits). On disc (2,073 breakables in 79 packs) the material word is 0 in 1,557, 1 in 294, 3 in 19, 4 in 203 and 2 in none, so cue 143 is never chosen by disc data.

Notes:

- The 10-slot lists read beside this code are the beam and bubble target lists of SPEC-0036 ("Beam and bubble lists"). The melee hit list is the 10-slot list at +0x400 of Behavior 12A.
- Section 17 (Shots) covers Roberta's Shadow and Sun beams and her Rock skipping shots. They are separate from the hero projectile of SPEC-0036. Each runs its own character walk and then this breakable walk (Behavior 17).
- The volumes tested here are the breakables' own volume records. They are not character hurt shapes (Behavior 12A).
- The contact step has no hit list: its first gate is the script hook (step a).

Percent reactions after a hit (High). This scan is the missile defence of the common enemies' agro machine (SPEC-0038, Behavior 44); the full state rules are there. The owner is an enemy. The scan runs when its defence hold (the reaction cooldown) is below 1, a hero exists, its outer agro sub-state is not 10, and it is not in outer sub-state 2 with the attack sequencer in state 5. The six slots are the hero's six shot slots. For each live shot, a path runs from the shot's position to its position plus its velocity times T, where T is the whole number at +0xE0 of the enemy's class parameter block (the missile look-ahead, in ticks). When that path meets one of the enemy's own body shapes with a positive radius, two reaction tests run for that slot:

1. Draw r, a whole number uniform from 1 to 100 (100 values, both ends inclusive). If r is below the float at +0xE4 of the class parameter block (the missile dodge chance), the enemy enters outer sub-state 6, the missile dodge.
2. Draw r again from 1 to 100. If r is below the float at +0xE8 (the missile block chance), the enemy enters outer sub-state 8, the missile block. When both pass, the block is entered last and wins.

Each reaction that starts sets the defence hold to T, the same +0xE0 value, so T is both the look-ahead and the cooldown (High). The hold is checked only once, at the start of the scan, so one scan can start reactions for several slots in the same tick. A percent of 0 never starts a reaction. What the dodge and block states then do (animations 53 or 54, and 56) is in SPEC-0038, Behavior 44.

### 17. Shots: owners, hit tests and friendly fire

Confidence: High for the owners, the start gate, the life count, the per-tick order, the lists walked and the friendly-fire rule; Medium-High for the shape tests (their geometry is SPEC-0036's); Medium for whether every townsfolk class passes the hittable query.

Owners (High). The launch with a count and the step with the 250.0 travel limit, which earlier text joined into one "shot", are two separate hero objects. Both belong to Roberta, the active hero, and both are specified in full in SPEC-0036:

- **Launch with a count = the beam of Shadow (169) and Sun (171).** These are the full-release ids that fire no hero projectile (SPEC-0036, Power attack and full release; Family C beam). The "shot count" is the beam's remaining life in ticks. It is not a number of shots, and the beam fires no volley. The two beam objects live in the hero's effect object group.
- **Step with the 250.0 limit = the Rock (165) skipping shots.** There are three rock objects, 0x180 bytes apart, in one group at 0x3777B0 (run-time memory). See SPEC-0036, Rock skipping shot.

Neither object uses a hero shot slot (SPEC-0036, Hero shot slots), and no enemy owns either one. Each object reads its damage scale from the active hero when it starts. The Roberta shot projectile of SPEC-0036 is a third, separate object, and it tests characters directly too (SPEC-0036, Hit on characters).

Aim point, target pick, reach test and spread (Medium). These are the helpers that the shot rules and the enemy routines use.

- Aim point. With no target id set, the aim point is the owner's position plus 20.0 units along its aim (facing) direction. With a target id set, the aim point is the target's position. Confidence Medium.
- Aim modes. Codes 0, 1 and 6 and above use the owner's target when its target flag is set (code 1 always runs the target check, and a stored point is used when there is no target). Codes 2 to 5 pick a random target with the target pick below and blend the aim direction toward that target's direction with random weights drawn uniformly from -1 to 1. The blend is the two-draw fan of SPEC-0036, Roberta shot projectile, Launch: with the unit aim direction d, the side axis s = unit(d × up) and a first draw r1, d1 = r1 s + (1 − |r1|) d; then with a second draw r2, d2 = r2 up + (1 − |r2|) d1; d2 is not renormalised. Test vector: d = (0, 0, 1), r1 = 0.5, r2 = −0.25 gives s = (−1, 0, 0), d1 = (−0.5, 0, 0.5) and d2 = (−0.375, −0.25, 0.375). Confidence Medium-High (the only modes 2 to 5 users are Roberta's launch modes).
- Target pick. A random target within 60 units. The picker walks the active character list and first calls each candidate's class-specific query at method slot +0xCC; a nonzero result is required. It then applies the strict 60-unit distance gate and two line tests against the world. Only the first 10 candidates in list order that pass these gates are considered, and one is chosen uniformly. The result is that candidate's id, or none (-1) when no candidate passes. This class-dispatched gate has three layers. The shared character predicate rejects action states 0x10, 0x21, 0x24, 0x35 and 0x36, then requires the invulnerability flag at +0x7F0 to be clear (SPEC-0030, SetEntityInvulnerable). The region-character (NPC) override adds vetoes for action state 0x10, nonzero +0xC54, +0xAF8 in 5..6, and four further internal predicates; it delegates to the shared predicate only if none veto. The hero override rejects action states 0x11, 0x19, 0x35, 0x36, 0x39, 0x3B and 0x3C, a second set {0x21, 0x30, 0x33, 0x34, 0x35, 0x37}, and the combination +0xF80 == 1 with +0xF90 in {8, 13}; otherwise it delegates to the shared predicate. These queries are eligibility gates, not a single health or alive-field test. The +0x7F0 gate is the entity's invulnerability flag (SPEC-0030, SetEntityInvulnerable); the four NPC predicates retain their separate operational descriptions in the following section. This pick is separate from the hero's candidate lock of Behavior 20. Confidence High for these direct checks and their ordering; Medium for the underlying meaning of the NPC helper predicates.
- Reach test. The owner must pass its validity test. The shared geometry query then returns a Boolean hit result: the reach helper accepts a hit from its primary shape query, or, if that misses and the prepared endpoints differ, accepts a hit from its secondary sweep. Otherwise it returns false. The primary query also writes a scalar and direction through output parameters, but this reach caller does not read either output. No comparison of the two radii minus 1.5 occurs in this call path. Confidence High for the Boolean result and caller use; the unused scalar output is read by no caller here and has no effect on play. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): return only the Boolean.
- Spread. Applied to the aim point as a horizontal offset. Its magnitude is 5.0 when the owner is off the active player's forward axis (the dot product of the unit vector from the player to the owner with the player's facing is below 0.85), plus a uniform random value from 0 up to S, plus 3.0 when the owner's attack-manager hold flag is nonzero. S is the current attack k's aim-inaccuracy value at the selected monster row's +0x30C + 4k (SPEC-0029, Layout section 4); k is the owner's selected attack index. The flag is looked up by the owner's actor id; an id absent from the list returns zero. Mere presence in the list is insufficient because listed, unheld owners also return zero. The attack-manager hold rule is specified in SPEC-0038 Behavior 45. The offset direction is a random unit vector in the horizontal plane. Confidence High for the S source and units, the flag condition and owner key; Medium for the full spread behavior.

**Beam start gate (High).** A beam is active when all of these hold: its life is above 0, both of its effect handles exist, and its segment is running. A full release with Shadow or Sun in word 2 starts the beam only when it is not active. A release while the beam is active does nothing and does not refresh the life. On start:

1. The ten-slot hit list is cleared.
2. The segment starts, and the two effects are made from the beam's effect definition (definition 0x222 for Shadow, 0x224 for Sun).
3. Life = trunc(L × R), with L = 5.0 s (word 0x2DCB98 for Shadow, 0x2DCB9C for Sun) and R the tick-rate word at 0x368FA0 (60). So life = 300.
4. The damage scale is read from the active hero.

**Beam tick (High).** Each tick, while the beam is active, the hero's effect object group runs these steps:

1. Life = life − 1. If the new life is below 1, the beam ends: the segment stops, both effects are released, the life becomes 0 and the hit list is cleared. Nothing else runs that tick.
2. Otherwise the beam spans from the hero's query point Q to E = Q + 20.0 × unit(aim), with the aim's height part removed (a horizontal beam; 20.0 is a code constant). The segment and the two effects are placed on Q and E.
3. Hit-list timers: when the list holds any entry, every used slot's timer drops by 1. A slot whose timer falls below 1 is freed (id −1, timer 0), and the used count drops by 1, never below 0.
4. The character walk runs (below). Then the breakable walk runs over cell list B of the beam's grid cell (the routing of Behavior 16; SPEC-0036, Attack sweeps).
5. Cue 5 of the global bank is kept playing at the beam's point. On the tick the beam ends, cue 4 plays once instead. These are sound cues, not visual effects.

A beam therefore lives 300 ticks, and its walks run on 299 of them.

**Beam character walk (High).** The beam visits every entry of the active character list in list order. This is the per-pass list of at most 45 region characters (count word 0x35C8F8, pointer word 0x35C8FC; SPEC-0029, Behavior 17). An entry is hit when all three tests pass, in this order:

1. **Hittable.** The entry's hittable query passes (SPEC-0036, Hittable predicate).
2. **Shape.** The beam segment meets the entry's body cylinder. The cylinder's radius is the motion-record word +0x08, its height is the word +0x0C, its axis is up, and its centre is the position + (height / 2) × up.
3. **Hit list.** The hit list accepts the entry. The list refuses an entry whose character id (+0x14C) is in a used slot whose timer is above 0. Otherwise the first free slot (id −1, or timer below 1) takes the id, with timer trunc(1.0 × 60) = 60 (word 0x2DCB2C times the tick-rate word), and the used count rises by 1. When all ten slots are busy, the entry is refused and a debug message prints, so the entry takes no hit.

On a hit:

- Damage = trunc(F × P). F comes from the shared damage helper, given the beam's damage scale, the hero, the target, element 6 (Shadow) or 3 (Sun), and attack id 169 or 171. P is Roberta's power multiplier for that id (SPEC-0036, Damage).
- The target's take-hit receives the damage with attacker id −1 (no attacker) and blocked flag 0. The two further take-hit arguments are 3 and 1.
- Cue 3 plays at the target's position.

The walk does not stop at a hit, so every entry that passes is hit in the same tick. A target that stays in the beam is hit again each time its slot frees, which is every 60 ticks.

**Rock character walk (High).** A rock runs this walk on each tick that it is still alive after its move, bounce and travel steps (SPEC-0036, Rock skipping shot, tick step 5). It visits the active character list in order. An entry is hit when both of these pass:

- **Hittable.** The entry's hittable query passes.
- **Shape.** The rock sphere meets the entry's body cylinder (the same cylinder as above). The sphere's centre is the rock position, and its radius is the rock's effect scale (1.0 when the rock has no effect).

The first entry that passes is hit, and the walk stops there. Damage = trunc(F × P), with element 2 and attack id 165. The take-hit gets attacker id −1, blocked flag 0, and further arguments 1 and 0. The rock then ends, as in SPEC-0036 End: effect 0x3AA when it is available, then cue 4, then release. A rock has no hit list, so it hits at most one character. Its breakable walk still runs after the character walk in the same tick, also on a tick when a hit has ended it. On a tick when a bounce or the travel limit ended it, neither walk runs.

**Rock velocity and travel (High; SPEC-0036).** Each tick the rock moves by its flat unit direction times its speed s. The rock's vertical speed is added to its height; the vertical speed starts at 1.0 and changes by −0.1 per tick. The speed s is 0.6 ± 0.3 units per tick (words 0x2DCB88 and 0x2DCB8C). The travel total counts only the flat (horizontal) distance moved. A total above 250.0 ends the rock; a total of exactly 250.0 keeps it live.

**Friendly fire (High).** The active character list holds only the characters of the loaded regions: enemies, bosses, townsfolk and other region characters. The two heroes live in the hero table and are never on that list. So the hero swing, beams, rocks, the charm walks and Roberta's shots, which all walk that list, never hit a hero. There is one exception: a Roberta shot made hostile by a deflect tests the hero's body cylinder and can hit the hero (SPEC-0036, Hit on characters). An enemy projectile of the enemy pool tests one character per tick: the selected hero, or after a reflection the character that fired it (SPEC-0029). Enemy projectiles therefore never hit other enemies; the only exception is a reflected shot hitting its own shooter. A region character can be hit by hero attacks only when its hittable query passes: it is not in base state 16, 33, 36, 53 or 54, and its invulnerable word +0x7F0 is 0 (SPEC-0036). Whether every townsfolk class passes that query in play is not confirmed (Medium).

### 18. Busy and attack-state tests

Confidence: High for both tests and their inputs; Medium-High for the callers' roles. Both tests are live.

Busy test = IsHeroOccupied (High). The "41 flag tests" are 41 equality tests of the hero's action-state word (character +0x144) against the values 7 to 19, 21, 23 to 30, 35, 37, 38, 41, 43 to 49, 53, 54 and 56 to 60 (state 15 is tested twice). The three other queries are: the state is one of the script-held states 33, 48, 50, 51, 52, 53 or 55; status 0 (stun) has a positive timer; and the screen effect is moving (the fade alpha differs from its target, or the letterbox bar alpha or bar progress differs from its target). The union of these is exactly the IsHeroOccupied rule of Behavior 15.2, so Behavior 18's busy test and IsHeroOccupied are one predicate. It is the same routine as the hero's busy test of Behavior 15.2 (one routine; the 41 equality tests are only its first part) and not the action-lock predicate of Behavior 4. Besides the level-script query, the region character loop, the hero update and several hero and item routines call it.

Attack-state test (High). This is a hero class method (one record of the hero kind table; the base kind's record answers 0, so NPCs and enemies are never in an attack state by it). It reads the hero index (+0xF80; 0 Tor, 1 Roberta), the action state (+0x144), the sub-state (+0xF90) and the air phase (+0x1508):

1. Tor: in an attack state when the action state is 13 and the sub-state is 2 to 12, or when the action state is 7, 8, 9, 10 or 11 and the air phase is 8 or 9 (the plunge attack, SPEC-0033 Behavior 50).
2. Roberta: in an attack state only when the action state is 13 and the sub-state is 5 or 12.
3. Any other hero index: not in an attack state.

State 13 with a sub-state outside the ranges above gives "not attacking"; there is no fall-through, and none is possible, because state 13 is not one of 7 to 11. The test is read by about 35 enemy and boss routines (one shape: on the tick the hero starts attacking, a draw from 0 to 100 against a per-owner percent starts a reaction such as a block or dodge; SPEC-0029, SPEC-0011) and by the hero's candidate engagement of Behavior 20. The Undead Draik's own hit check (SPEC-0029, Section 6) uses this test as its melee condition.

A third hero method, the hero-is-down test, returns true when the hero's action state is 16. The Gnarfas controller uses it for its hold, its knockback gate and its death gate (SPEC-0020). It is not the busy test.

### 18A. Mote reflect test of an enemy projectile (High)

Confidence: High. This is not a character busy test: its input is a projectile record of the enemy projectile pool (SPEC-0029), and the three values it tests are the projectile's shot code, its element and the element of the hero's equipped Mote. It has no relation to the busy list of Behavior 15.2.

When an enemy projectile reaches the selected hero, the hit routine first tests the shield block: the hero's shield test passes and dot(projectile direction, hero body forward) < 0. Only when that block passes is this test run. It passes when all of these hold:
1. The projectile's kind word is 0 or 10.
2. Its shot code is one of 0 to 5, 7 to 14, 16 or 23.
3. The hero has a shield Mote equipped (equipment word 1 of SPEC-0021, Mote charge; the word is not -1).
4. The projectile's element is nonzero, and it equals the Mote's element or the Mote's element is 7.

Mote element by item id: 160 and 161 give 1; 162 and 163 give 4; 164 and 165 give 2; 166 and 167 give 5; 168 and 169 give 6; 170 and 171 give 3; 172 and 173 give 7. Any other id gives 0. Element 7 matches every projectile element.

Result. When the block and this test both pass, the projectile is sent back (toward the character that fired it, or with its velocity reversed when that character is gone), no damage is applied to the hero, and the shield Mote's count is charged (SPEC-0021, Mote charge). When the block passes and this test fails, the hit is a blocked hit; when the block fails, it is a normal hit (SPEC-0029).

### 19. Hero per-record wall contact and ledge guard (pointer)

Confidence: High.

There is no per-element movement step and no ground snap. The routine that runs over the grid cell's frame records inside driver branch 0 (Behavior 14A, item 1) is the hero's per-record wall contact with its ledge guard, and it is specified in SPEC-0018. It works on the hero's motion record itself (position, displacement, velocity, weight, radius and height; Layout, body size); there is no list of sub-parts and no per-element divisor. Its gate states 21, 23, 30, 35, 37, 38, 41 and 47 are hero action states in which the hero skips the wall contact. The ledge guard (during Tor's lunge sub-states and while guarding) blocks the hero when the ground 3.0 ahead along the facing (behind when guarding), or 3.0 times the displacement ahead, is more than 2.0 below the feet; it moves the hero by the contact response and never sets the hero's height to the ground. Code reading (High unless marked): the guard is active only in attack-type states (the first attack query true and the second false) or while the guard query is true; the 2.0 test is the reference y minus the ground height; the velocity probe (taken only when the drop test is clear and the speed is above 0.01) uses the same signed step, so while guarding it probes opposite to the motion; the ground height comes from the ceiling-capped probe called with argument reference y + 10.0 (High, 2026-11-09: see Behavior 14 item 6 and Open questions). The exact eight skipped states are 21, 30, 35, 37, 23, 41, 38, 47 (confirmed). Region characters do not run it (driver branch 1), and the petpet runs its own frame-record step (driver branch 2).

Ordinary walls (High for the rules, Medium for the walk-level filter). A frame record acts on the hero when the feet are above (top − h − H) and below (top − 0.3), where h is the record's depth (+0x44), H = 3.5 the hero's height, and top is the record's top-edge height at the tested point minus 0.25. Walls whose top edge is less than 0.55 above the feet are stepped over (the window is tested at both the current and the previous position, and either one is enough). The reach is the hero's own radius (1.175) only; the frame record supplies the plane, the run length and the depth, and no radius. The test runs at the hero's position and, if that misses, at a predicted point; a predicted hit clips the motion and undoes the into-wall part of it but does not push. The response is a direct position push: position += (reach − d) N, where d is the distance from the point to the wall plane and N the wall normal. The into-wall part of this tick's root step is removed, and the displacement and velocity lose their into-wall components. The plane part of this frame-record test is the wall rule of Open question 133 (signed plane distances dP at the position and dB at the previous position, with the record's offset in the fourth lane): a position behind the plane (dP and dB both negative) is no hit for ordinary records and, for type-6 records, flips the normal and both distances; a plane hit needs dP ≤ r with dB > r, or dP ≥ r with dB < r, or |dP| ≤ r. The sideways range is the same as there, with the side axis at +0x20 and the run length at +0x40: the position or the previous position must have u in [−r, run length + r]. There is no v range: the top-edge window above replaces it, and it is tried at the position first and, when that fails, at the previous position with the top edge at that point. The output distance is r − dP. Test vector: record with normal (0, 0, 1), offset 0, run length 10, top edge 50.0 at every point, depth 120 (top used 49.75), r = 1.175, H = 3.5; position (5, 49.0, 1.0) and previous (5, 49.0, 1.3): dP = 1.0, dB = 1.3 (above r), u = 5 in range, feet 49.0 is inside (−73.75, 49.45), a hit with distance 0.175; the same with u = 11.5 (above 11.175) at both points: no hit; feet 49.5: no hit at either point. Ordinary walls are not raised: only the ledge guard raises a record's top by 10 units. Every frame-record normal on disc is near-vertical (|y| ≤ 0.2), so no separate near-horizontal branch is needed. The walk that supplies these records skips a record whose 16-bit filter (+0x42) is nonzero and shares no bit with the world's mask word (+0x56B4); the hero routine itself does not read the filter.

Two plane tests, one decision per wall (High from 2026-11-09, when both tests were confirmed in code, including the radius used by the frame-record test, the ceiling band, the near-horizontal branch and the object-panel 1.1 and 0.01 terms; refinements in SPEC-0018, Character frame test: the frame-record test's back-side mirror applies at any normal when the call is two-sided, and its near-horizontal branch accepts when the signed plane value is below 0.05 (positive normal) or below the reach (negative normal) with no lower bound, then requires the feet strictly between top − depth − reach and top). The hero has two wall contacts that run on different lists and use different tests, and each one decides by itself whether its wall blocks. (1) Frame records of the grid cell (the contact above): the plane test with the top-edge rule decides. A record blocks only while the feet are inside a window that runs from H + h below the record's top (top minus 0.25) up to 0.3 below that top; above the window the hero walks over the wall, below it the hero passes under. This test never changes the hero's height and never steps the hero up: climbing onto a low top edge is the ordinary height follow (SPEC-0033, Behavior 7). The test also has a near-horizontal branch (the window is the feet between the top minus the depth minus H and the top) that no frame record on disc uses. (2) Object panels (the type-10 object walls and the movable-block walls, listed after the cell records in the contact order of Behavior 14A): a different plane test decides, with no top-edge height. It blocks while the height of the position (or of the previous position) above the panel's bottom lies between minus the hero radius and the panel height minus 1.1 minus 0.01 (the 1.1 is dropped when the panel's type byte is negative, so such a panel blocks right up to its top), and the sideways span is the panel's run length plus the hero radius at each end. So an object panel is stepped over when its top is within about 1.11 above the feet, a frame record when it is within about 0.55. Both tests report the same pair (wall normal, distance) to the push response; for a wall the distance is the hero radius minus the signed plane distance. Neither test walks the hero off a ledge: that is the ledge-drop test and edge acceptance test that follow each record (SPEC-0033, Behaviors 37 and 49; they request hero states 10, 11 and 37, the object-panel contact requests state 38 in the same place), and, for Tor's lunge and guard only, the raised-by-10 copy with the ground probe 3.0 ahead (above). The object-panel contact has the same raised-by-10 retest for lunge and guard, and it runs it with the first kind of test. In the type-10 loop every climb panel and every ladder face is a solid wall; ladder records whose normal's vertical part is at least 0.9 (the top records) are skipped; the loop is skipped in hero states 21, 38 and 41, and ladder records are skipped in state 30. A ladder-face touch sets the object-wall flag (+0x240) for that tick, which blocks the type-10 drop into state 38 on that tick (SPEC-0018, Climb and hang data; SPEC-0033, Behavior 13A).
Test vector (real wall, frame record 0 of the first model-set entry of pack 678; radius 1.175, H = 3.5): top edge starts at (-1564.87, 49.40, 1120.80), normal (0.4448, 0, -0.8956) with plane offset 1699.87, run direction (-0.8956, 0, -0.4448), run length 15.409, depth 120, type byte 0. Hero 5.0 along the run and 1.0 in front of the wall, at x -1568.90 and z 1117.68, previous position 0.3 further out along the normal: the plane distance is 1.0 (not above the radius) and the previous one is 1.3 (above it), so the plane is hit with distance 0.175. The top edge above that point is at y 49.37, so the top used is 49.12 and the blocking window is -74.38 < feet < 48.82. Feet y 48.80: blocked. Feet y 48.85 and 49.37: stepped over. Feet y -74.0: blocked (under the window's lower end by 0.38); feet y -75.0 passes under. The same numbers read as an object panel with type byte 0: feet y 48.17 gives a height of 118.8 above the panel bottom, which is within -1.175 to 118.89, so it blocks; feet y 48.37 gives 119.0 and is stepped over.

### 20. Hero candidate list, lock and facing toward a lock (High from 2026-11-09 for items 1 to 6; Medium-High before)

Confidence: High for the list, the engagement, the order and the lock choice (items 1 to 5; confirmed by a full reading of the update routine 2026-11-09, with two corrections in items 2 and 3); Medium for item 6; High for the attack turn of item 7. Live: the hero update runs this every tick, after the hero's state dispatch. The call is not gated by the hero's busy state or by the game-state box flag that locks input: that flag gates only the quick-item selector at the end of the same update, and the update has no early return before the call (High). So the list refresh, ordering and lock choice keep running while input is locked or a dialogue box is up; engagement still needs an attack state (item 3), which a locked pad cannot start, so a fresh lock cannot form but an existing one persists and its distance and range tests continue.

The hero keeps one candidate list: up to 15 records of 0x1C bytes from hero +0xDC0, with the count at +0xF64. A record holds the candidate's character id (+0x00; −1 = free), its distance to the hero (+0x08), an in-range flag (+0x10), an alignment value (+0x14) and an engaged flag (+0x18). The 1.0e9-sentinel nearest search with the same-group test (data word 0x31E890) belongs to the enemy target latch (SPEC-0029), not to this list. The Faerie Sisters' loops mark their own candidate record engaged and in range on every tick they are not in state 9 (SPEC-0011). This list (+0xDC0) is not the target scan list at +0xA70 (SPEC-0053, section 3.9): the scan routine that fills the +0xA70 list does not touch this one, and its rules differ from items 2 to 4 below in two ways (confirmed by a full reading of that scan; High): its distance and direction are horizontal (the height difference is dropped before the length and the dot), and its ordering is by distance with a +1.0 swap margin, with the first record pinned for up to 90 ticks before a re-sort from index 0. The list at +0xA70 is refreshed every tick; the 90 is a pin limit, not a rescan period. Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) by the two list-reset routines: the candidate list reset sets the count (+0xF64) to 0, every one of the 15 records of 0x1C bytes from +0xDC0 gets id −1, four words 0 and the alignment value (+0x14 of the record) = −1.0 (a float; the reset value of the alignment was not stated before), and then the suppressed lock id (+0xF68) = −1. The target scan list reset sets the count (+0xC14) to 0 and, for each of its 15 records of 0x1C bytes from +0xA70, id −1, three words 0 and the candidate flag (+0x10 of the record) = 0. The removal helpers (candidate removal, talker removal and the record-queue compaction) shift the later 0x1C-byte records down over the removed one.

1. **Adding (High).** Every character of the world actor list that is not in the list yet is added to the first free record when it passes the candidate test: not in hero action state 16 or 42, alive (+0x800 nonzero), not in class-17 behavior state 16 while its countdown is positive, passes the class-state eligibility gate, and is not a petpet by the spawn record's u16 key at +0xAC. The petpet lookup maps the six recognized keys to kinds 0, 1, 3, 6, 7 and 8; all other keys return a negative result, which is required. The class-state eligibility predicate is separate from the alive word (+0x800 must be nonzero) and the class-17 state-16 reanimation gate (state +0xA34 = 16 with countdown +0xD14 > 0). Its exact veto rules are: if +0xE60 = 0 and the row 'Display in Default' word (+0x82) = 0, veto when either the current behavior state equals the configured word at behavior block +0x84 or state = 21 with current animation id not 82, unless the active hero's actor table has a record for this candidate id whose first word is nonzero and whose pointed record word at +0x0C is not 126. If 'Display in Default' is nonzero, veto when +0xE3C is nonzero; otherwise, when +0xBC0 = 0, veto only if behavior-block +0xC4 = 0 and current animation matches the animation selected by +0xC8 (0–4 select 9, 128–131; other values select 9), or, when +0xBC0 is nonzero, if state = 14 and +0xC04 = 1.
