A nonzero +0xE60 disables this predicate's veto. The active-hero exception's design meaning remains unknown. A new record starts with its distance and with the in-range and engaged flags clear. With 15 records in use, further characters are not added. There is no distance limit for adding. **[High for the gate order, petpet lookup and class-state predicate branches; Medium for the active-hero exception's higher-level meaning]**
2. **Refresh (High).** For each record in order: a candidate that fails the candidate test is removed; the records after it move up by one, and the walk then moves to the next index, so the record that moved into the freed place is not refreshed until the next tick (reproduce). Corrected 2026-11-09 (High, from the code): (a) a record whose id is not found (looked up in the world character list, then the petpet children, then the second world) is left exactly as it is: it is neither removed nor refreshed, and its flags stay stale; removal happens only when the character is found and fails the candidate test (state 16, not alive, state 42, either class-17 predicate fails, or it is a petpet kind). (b) Otherwise its distance d is the horizontal distance (the y component is dropped before the length) and the alignment a is dot(the hero's forward axis, the horizontal unit direction from the hero to the candidate); this is the same rule as the target scan list. Only the value stored when the record is added (before the same tick's refresh overwrites it) is a 3D distance. (The earlier statement that d and a are 3D is withdrawn.)
3. **Engagement (High).** A record that is not engaged becomes engaged when a > 0.25 and the hero is in an attack state (Behavior 18) or Roberta is in action state 13. An engaged record is in range while d < 20.0 (data word 0x2DD6C0; strictly below); at d ≥ 20.0 it loses both flags (the engaged flag is cleared too, and the in-range counter grows by one for each record that is in range). Corrected 2026-11-09 (High): d is the horizontal distance, so the lock range of 20.0 is horizontal and the earlier example of a target 15 units above the hero (a 13.229 horizontal limit) does not apply. A candidate is therefore never in range until the hero has attacked while facing within about 75.5 degrees of it (acos 0.25, strict), and it stays in range after that while it is closer than 20.0 horizontally. The engagement test needs the hero's virtual in-attack-state query to be nonzero, or the hero variant 1 (Roberta) in state 13. The in-range counter used by items 4 and 5 counts only the records refreshed this tick (stale records do not count); after the front-swap pass the head record can be a stale record.
4. **Order (High).** The in-range records are moved to the front of the list by pairwise swaps. Then, unless a forced or held target applies (item 5), the n in-range records are ordered by a pairwise pass: for each i, for each j > i, records i and j swap when d_j < d_i + 1.0. Quirk (Medium; reproduce): two in-range candidates within 1.0 of each other swap on every tick, so the head of the list, and with it the lock, alternates between them each tick.
5. **Lock choice (High).** In priority order:
   - a forced target id at +0x15E0 (set by other hero routines, and written every tick during the Werelupe King's fight, SPEC-0019) is used once and reset to −1. The Black Knight also writes the forced lock id every tick, as the Werelupe King does (SPEC-0012); the Cloud Beast writes its own id every tick, but only while its companion exists (SPEC-0010); Gnarfas, the Gelert Assassin, Juppie (outside its hub, finale and idle hold), the Ixi Chieftain (outside idle, hold, stage transition and death) and the Plague Serpent also write their own id at the end of every pass (SPEC-0020, SPEC-0013, SPEC-0015, SPEC-0014, SPEC-0016); the Faerie Sisters do not write the forced lock id: their controllers set their own candidate record engaged and in range each tick (SPEC-0011); the forced id reaches only this lock and the hero's head look-at (which accepts the head of the hero target list when that record's character is the forced id, even with its candidate flag clear); Roberta's shot launch never reads the forced id or this lock, because it aims at the first record of the hero target list of SPEC-0053 (SPEC-0036, Roberta shot projectile, Launch) (High);
   - while the hero's separate target scan list (SPEC-0053, section 3.9; 15 records of 0x1C bytes from +0xA70, count word at +0xC14, which is a count and not a timer) is not empty and its first record's candidate flag (+0xA80) is set, that first record's character (+0xA70, the "held target" below) is kept when it is still an engaged, in-range record (the ready test: the candidate list holds that id with engaged and in-range both set; no in-range count is needed for this choice; confirmed 2026-11-09, High);
   - otherwise, when at least one record is in range and the head record's d < 20.0: when a world lock-out counter is positive the head record's engaged flag is cleared and there is no lock; else the lock is the head record's character.
   With no record in range there is no lock. A locked character that leaves the list is removed in item 2 of the same tick, so the next in-range record can take the lock in that same tick.
6. **Target flag and point (High for selection; shared method and default attachment point High; Darkest Faerie default Medium-High).** The hero's target flag (+0x15CC; SPEC-0027 camera) is 0 when there is no lock. In action state 4 (run) the current lock is stored as suppressed (+0xF68) and the flag is 0. Otherwise, when the lock differs from the suppressed id, the suppressed id is reset to −1, the flag becomes 1 and the locked character's target point is requested through that actor's virtual target-point method and stored at +0x15D0. When the lock equals the suppressed id the flag is 0. The shared non-hero method returns its cached target point. Each ordinary NPC update clears the override latch, then refreshes the cache from render attachment point 7 unless a controller supplies an override during that tick. Known direct override callers include four Black Knight attack handlers, Cloud Beast, Juppie and Plague Serpent; these are branch-specific and do not replace the shared default globally. No direct setter call is known in the Darkest Faerie controller family, so point 7 is the best-supported default for her (Medium-High), while indirect overrides are not excluded. The semantic name and exact authored placement of attachment point 7 may differ by character model. So after a run the same target stays unflagged until the lock moves to another character; a tick with no lock does not reset the suppressed id.
7. **Facing toward a target (attack turn; High).** The hero's attack routines (the Tor and Roberta attack tables of SPEC-0036 and one other attack routine) call this turn while they run, and only while the auto-facing flag is set; no other hero step calls it. The auto-facing flag is the hero word at +0xFA0 of the hero object (the one that holds the attack sub-state at +0xF90): the attack reset sets it to 1, Roberta's attack routine sets it every tick, her leap clears it, and the carry routine clears it on stick input. It is a different word from the ready word at +0xFA0 of the locomotion sub-object (Behavior 14). Roberta's sub-state 13 has no turn here because the landing step does its own turn at rate 0.25 (High, confirmed by a full reading). All distances are horizontal: the direction from the hero's position to the target point has its vertical part set to 0 before it is measured and normalised. The turn is the shared aim rule of Behavior 8 (the same method for heroes and NPCs): with current facing f, unit target direction d and weight w, v = w d + (1 − w) f; when dot(f, d) < −0.99, 0.1 times the body side axis is added to v; the new facing is v normalised. The weight is 1.0 (an instant turn) when the target's distance D is below 4.0 (strictly; a fixed program constant), and 0.25 (data word 0x2DD58C) otherwise. In order:
   - When the hero's animation player reports its flag test 64 (meaning open), the hero aims along its own body forward with weight 1.0 (no change of heading) and nothing else runs.
   - Roberta (variant word +0xF80 = 1): in attack sub-state 13 (+0xF90) there is no turn. Otherwise, when the forced-point flag (+0x1490, the aim mode word of SPEC-0036) is nonzero, the target is the forced point (+0x14A0). Otherwise, while the target scan list count (+0xC14) is positive and the held target flag (the first record's candidate flag, +0xA80) is set, the target is the held target's target point (a class method of that character), or the forced point when that character no longer exists. Otherwise there is no turn.
   - Tor: while the target scan list count is positive and either the held target flag is set, or the attack sub-state is 7 or 8 and the held target's stored distance (the first record's distance, +0xA78) is below 7.0, the target is the held target as for Roberta. Here the instant turn also needs dot(d, body forward) > 0; when D < 4.0 and that dot is 0 or less, there is no turn.
   - Otherwise, for Tor only: the target is the nearest intact breakable (state word 1 or 3; Behavior 16) of the grid cell list under the hero whose direction has dot(d, body forward) > 0.5 and whose distance is below the hero's reach word (+0xF84, set at hero creation: 12.0 for Tor; Roberta's 25.0 is not used by this turn). With none, there is no turn. Roberta never turns toward a breakable here.
   After a turn toward a target, and also in the Tor case refused at D < 4.0, the moving flag at +0x10 of the hero's locomotion block (the block at character +0x7CC, the same flag SPEC-0033 uses; corrected from code, High: it is not a word of the motion record at character +0x16C) is set to 1. The first case (flag test 64) and the cases with no target leave it unchanged.

The words 0x2DD69C (4.0), 0x2DD6A0 (−4.0) and 0x2DD698 (0.25) are not facing words. They are read only by the hero's head look-at step, which places the look point 5.0 units ahead of the hero's head joint and 4.0 above or below it, and lowers a look blend by 0.25 when the forward and the look direction are less than 0.99 aligned. Refined 2026-11-09 (High for the structure; see Behavior 23A item g for the full state-by-state rule): the 5.0 is measured from the head joint (joint 6), not the hero's origin, and the 4.0 vertical offset applies only in action state 0x29 (clips 0x5B and 0x5A up, 0x5F and 0x5E down, and the stick up or down) and state 0x15 (clips 0x38 up and 0x2D down); other states look sideways (±5.0 along the right vector in states 0x15, 0x1E and 0x17), use the heading blend, or use a held target. The look point drives the head turn of the animation player, not the body facing (High for the readers; Medium for the look roles).

These turns are hero routines. They are not part of the shared runtime's facing rule of Behavior 8.

### 21. Flyer goal height (move-goal clamp)

Confidence: High. Live for every flying row. The two "goal modes" are the row's Can Fly values 1 and 2, and the 0.2 is a distance factor, not a step limit. The enemy framework (SPEC-0029) calls this routine after it sets the move goal (+0x9D0); only the goal's height is changed, and the height keeper of Behavior 14D item 7 then moves the character toward it.

Let H be the row's Max Fly Height (+0xB4), y_h the focus hero's height, d_h the horizontal distance (x and z only) from the character to the hero, and g the ground height under the goal (the ground probe of Behavior 9 from 8.0 above the goal; SPEC-0018).

1. **AI mode 10, sub-state 6 (first).** Whatever the Can Fly value: when the goal's height is above H, it becomes H (an absolute height, not ground-relative); nothing else is done.
2. **Can Fly 1.** y = y_h + min(H, 0.2 × d_h) (0.2 at 0x2DD87C); y = max(y, g + 2.0) (2.0 at 0x2DD880). Then, while the ambush word (+0xBC0) is 0, the home point's height replaces y (after the clamp, so the home height is not clamped). The ambush word is -1 at creation, 0 only while the enemy waits in state 8, 1 and 2 during the emerge and 2 afterwards, so this rule applies only to an enemy waiting in state 8. The routine runs in the walk-target setter, when a walk target is set, not every tick.
3. **Can Fly 2.** y = y_h, or the home point's height while the ambush word is 0. Then, when y − g > H, y = g + H. There is no lower bound: a Can Fly 2 goal can lie below the ground plus 2.0, or below the ground.
4. **Can Fly 0.** The goal is left unchanged.

**The goal before the first walk target (R-0139; High for the creation value and the think-start rule, Medium-High for the cases below).** The move goal is a stored point (+0x9D0, x, y, z) that the height keeper of Behavior 14D reads every tick; the clamp above rewrites its height only when a walk target is set. Its life for a region enemy:
- Creation: the reset writes the fixed point (1529.0, 0.0, 1700.0), so the stored goal height is 0.0 (an absolute height, meaningless in play).
- First think tick, before any state runs: when the enemy has no navigation mesh yet (the mesh word is -1; it is -1 at creation), the think start picks one (a flying row picks from the pack's flying meshes, kind 2) and, when it finds one, stores the enemy's own position as the move goal, so the goal height is the spawn height. The state setter and the first state routine run after that. A flyer therefore holds its spawn height from the first tick, not height 0.
- When the pick fails (no kind-2 mesh in the pack, or the placement outside every flying mesh: the Steppe Plateau and three Meridell Castle Courtyard Crokabeks) the goal keeps the creation point, so the keeper moves the flyer 0.1 per tick toward the absolute height 0.0 until a walk target sets a real goal. Those enemies have no navigation route either, so in practice the first walk target comes from a state that sets one (wake, agro, wander).
- State 8 (hidden at home): the home-walk step sets a walk target every tick it is not already within 0.35 of home, and the clamp above gives the home point's height while the ambush word is 0; an enemy that starts within 0.35 of home sets no target, and its goal stays at the spawn height (the same value).
- A patrol with a route, or without one (SPEC-0038 Behavior 49): the first tick is a walking tick that sets the walk target to the current point, so the goal is set on tick 1 by the clamp (Can Fly 1: hero height + min(H, 0.2 d), at least ground + 2.0; the home height does not replace it because the ambush word is -1). No patrol tick uses the spawn-height goal.
Rule (these are the original's steps, not a choice): initialise the goal to the creation point (1529.0, 0.0, 1700.0), replace it with the enemy's own spawn position when its navigation mesh is found at the first think tick, keep the creation point when no mesh is found, and compute the clamp on every walk-target set exactly as above; do not compute it on the first airborne step.

A far Crokabek on patrol (R-0139, point 3; High for the rule, Medium for how it looks): the clamp is not gated by the state, so a Can Fly 1 flyer far from the hero is held at the hero's height plus min(H, 0.2 d). With H = 30 (Crokabek) the cap is reached at 150 units horizontal distance, so in a pack where the hero stands 45 above the Crokabek's ground a patrolling Crokabek 150 or more units away hovers 30 above the hero, 75 above its own ground; at 50 units it hovers 10 above the hero's height. The original behaves the same: the patrol route fixes only the horizontal path. It comes down only when the hero comes within range (the term 0.2 d shrinks) and the floor term keeps it at least 2.0 above the ground under the goal.

### 21A. Navigation target and cached index (Medium)

A character that walks to a navigation target keeps a cached navigation index for its route. When a new navigation target is set:

- If the new target is more than 1.0 unit away from the previously stored target, the cached index is reset to -1, which forces the route to be planned again. The new target is then stored as below.
- If the new target is 1.0 unit or less away, or lies inside the cached navigation triangle (tested first, while a cached triangle is set), the cached index is kept.
- In every case the new target is stored and the arrival flag is cleared. The two class hooks are tests: when either passes, the flyer goal-height clamp of Behavior 21 runs on the new target (High for the order; Medium for the tests being the two flight tests).
- The re-plan writes no position.

### 22. Emote playback

Confidence: High for the queue layout, the drop rule, the pop rule, the consumer's branches, the clip start rule and the clearing points (queue entries of 12 bytes with the emote id as the third word; the clear sets every id to −1 and the count at queue +0x30 to 0; the pop shifts entries 1 to count − 1 down by one place, sets the id of the entry at count − 1 to −1 and lowers the count; confirmed 2026-11-09); Medium for the stale-emote quirk.

Each character has two emote queues in its runtime record: a body queue at offset +0x8D0 and a face queue at offset +0x904. Each queue holds four entries of 12 bytes and a count at queue +0x30. An entry holds a reference to a clip directory, a directory position and an emote id. The current-emote words are at +0x938 (body) and +0x93C (face). Clearing a queue sets every entry's id to -1 and the count to 0.

An emote id is an animation id: a body emote is a main-table id and a face emote a face-table id of the character (SPEC-0005). Entries are added by the dialogue control records (SPEC-0022). Body requests (kinds 0x10 and 0x17) go to the body queue. Face requests (kinds 0x18 and 0x16) go to the face queue; kind 0x16 is the partner's face emote with no clip (no directory, position -1), not a face clear.

Adding an entry: the new entry is appended. When the queue already holds four entries, the oldest entry is dropped first.

Clearing: the queues are cleared only when the character spawns and when SetConversationTarget starts a conversation (it clears the queues of both characters). Nothing else empties them.

**Consumer.** The consumer runs only in the talking state 25 (0x19), once per tick. For NPCs, the call is inside the base-state-25 branch of the character update, before that character's motion-record tick and animation pass. For the hero, state 25 calls the consumer from the hero update before its motion-record tick and the later animation hookup. Thus the consumer's progress/pop check gets first opportunity to act when a one-shot face emote reaches 0.99; the hookup's automatic override-revert check runs afterward and sees the override value left by the consumer. **[High; see Open question 74]**
- For the hero: on every tick in hero state 25, with body emotes enabled.
- For an NPC: on every tick in base state 25, with body emotes enabled unless the NPC is busy: AI mode 10 to 12, or main id 65 to 74, 78, 97, 98, 146 or 147 (seated and lying poses). A busy NPC plays no body emote and no body clip; its face path still runs.
- Before each run the caller sets both current-emote words to 0 and turns the character toward its partner with the aim rule of Behavior 8 (weight 0.1 for the hero, 0.3 for an NPC).

**Pop rule.** A pop removes the head entry: the other entries move forward by one, the last entry's id becomes -1 and the count drops by 1. Body queue, head id e (only while body emotes are enabled):
- e = -2: the head is popped (a return to idle).
- e = -1: nothing.
- Any other e, when the main id already equals e: the head is popped when its clip loops and a second entry exists, or when its clip does not loop and its progress is 0.99 or more (Behavior 22B). While the main id differs from e, nothing is popped.
- The wanted body id B is the head id after this step, or 0 when the queue is empty.

Face queue, head id f: the same rule, using the face override id, the face clip's loop flag and the face clip's progress. An empty queue, or a popped -2, gives the wanted face id F = 0. A pop that leaves an empty head gives F = -1.

**Then:**
- If body emotes are disabled, or the main id already equals B: when the face head has a clip and the face override differs from F, the face head's clip starts; then the face override is set to F through the face-id setter (which writes only +0x3B8).
- Otherwise main id B is requested through the main request store of Behavior 3D (main id B, second main id -1, face override F), and the head's clip starts: the face head's when it has one, else the body head's.

So while a conversation runs, the face override is held at the queued face emote, or at face id 0 when the face queue is empty; any mouth motion is part of those face clips. A face override that the consumer stops holding reverts at progress 0.99 to the face clip of the main id (Behavior 3D, step 6).

**Clip start.** A clip starts only on a body restart (the request of the second branch) or when a new face emote is set, and only when the entry has a directory, its position is at least 0 and inside the directory, and its length is nonzero (SPEC-0009, Emote clip lookup, which gives the position). It streams from the directory entry's start sector, offset by the bank's base sector, for the entry's length in sectors, at the gain of the Voice option. A clip that is refused at that moment (the shared emote handle busy, the music bank not active, or a volume of 0 or less) is not retried. When a new body emote and a new face emote both have clips on the same tick, only the face clip plays. A row with no clip still plays its gesture, silently.

**Stale emotes (quirk; Medium).** Because the queues are cleared only at spawn and at SetConversationTarget, hero emotes queued while the hero was not in state 25 stay queued, up to the four newest, and play at the next level message that puts the hero into state 25.

### 22A. Attachment effect by selector (Medium)

Confidence: Medium. This is an effect attachment, not an emote clip; the effect ids are not emote clip positions (Behavior 22).

The Darkest Faerie's orb-volley cast glow uses one global attachment slot that holds at most one effect attachment. On transition into local controller state 3, the controller draws an integer from 0 through 5 inclusive, adds 1 and stores the result in its state record at +0x10. The state-3 animation-7 path passes that stored value as the chosen orb kind. On the first tick of animation 7, the chosen orb kind selects a glow: 1→1617, 2→1619, 3→1622, 4→1618, 5→1620, 6→1621; the glow is stopped when the orb fires. When the chosen id differs from the one in the slot, the old attachment is replaced. Selector 0 clears the slot. This global slot is distinct from the two per-character attachment handles in Layout. The glow's position is the whole 16-byte vector at +0x1420 of the boss's locomotion sub-object, with the fourth lane forced to 1: it is the translation row of weapon frame 0, the posed attach point 0 of the character (SPEC-0005, Weapon frame blocks; the five 64-byte frames start at sub-object +0x13F0 and each frame's translation row is its fourth row, at +0x30 inside the frame). The selector routine copies it into the slot, and the state-3 per-tick step copies it again on every tick of state 3; in any other state that step stops the glow, so the glow follows attach point 0 while the controller is in state 3 (High for the source, the copy and the lane rule; Medium-High for the visible placement). Other readers of the same vector use it as the world point of attach point 0 (the Roberta shot start of SPEC-0036 and the weapon-tip lookups). The effects themselves are described in SPEC-0034 (Open question 150).

### 22B. Clip progress and its threshold (High)

Confidence: High.

The progress of an id is (c + t) / F, taken from the playback record that holds the id's clip on the layer the clip names (clip header byte 0x28), where c is the record's cursor, t its stored fraction and F the clip's frame count (SPEC-0005, Playback). It is 1.0 when that record is finished or empty, and 0 when no record holds the clip. Quirk: the fraction is added on top of the cursor, so the value runs ahead by up to one frame over F.

The threshold is not stored state: each progress test compares with a constant that its call passes (0.99 in most callers, 0.95 in some). The progress tests named in Behavior 15.3 (states 51, 54, 57, 59 and 60) and the hero progress test of Behavior 4 pass 0.99. The face-override revert (Behavior 3D, step 6) uses its own fixed 0.99 on the face record. The clip cue step that closes each update (Behavior 3A, step 6) is a different test: it compares the layer-0 cursor, in frames, with the per-id cue start frame of the main table entry, not a progress fraction.

### 23. Hero right-stick aim counter (pointer)

Confidence: High. The words +0x1490 and +0x1494 are the aim mode word and the aim counter of the right-stick aim mode. Its start, end, counter reset and stick bytes are specified in SPEC-0036 (Right-stick aim mode). It is separate from the pad idle counter of SPEC-0033.

### 23A. Look-at controller (head and neck look)

Confidence: High for the controller (units, formulas, reset order, rate cap, limits and users; steps 1 to 7 confirmed 2026-11-09 against the readable controller, the constants 0.10, −1.57, 1.57, −0.35 and 0.35 verified in the boot image); High for the serpent neck look (raised 2026-11-09: joint list, unclamped A, side test, τ = ∓A/6, step 0.01 and the one-direction quirk confirmed); Medium-High for the hero's target choice (structure raised 2026-11-09, see item g below); High for the head step's level rotation (Medium for its sign in strongly turned poses) and High for the head step's turn toward the look point (raised 2026-11-09: the yaw about up by acos(head forward · horizontal direction to the look point), negative on the −X side, then the pitch about −X by the elevation, negated when D · R > 0, with R = (0, 1, 0, 0), are all in the head-look routine). The rate cap and the limits are boot data (Where it lives). The reference vector R is the up vector (0, 1, 0, 0) at 0x3932E0 (SPEC-0007).

**Request and users (High).** A look is requested per tick and stored on the animation player: a target point, a reset flag and a mode (the player's look mode word +0x3AC, default −1, and its pending word +0x3A8, default 0). The skeleton pose pass applies it after the animation and before the chain step (Behavior 25), then clears the request. Two characters use it:
- Mode 0, the hero: head joint 6, measured from joint 4. The hero requests it on each tick that word A bit 13 of its current main id is set (SPEC-0005, head-look bit).
- Mode 1, the Plague Serpent: the six neck joints of the joint list at 0x320F48 (joints 6, 7, 8, 11, 14 and 17). The serpent requests it on every tick of its behaviour state 0, aimed at the active hero (SPEC-0016).

On a tick with no request nothing turns, and the six neck angles (player +0xF48 to +0xF5C) are set to 0. The hero's stored yaw and pitch (player +0xF30 and +0xF34) are kept. Both start at 0 the first time a player ever looks (a first-use mark at player +0xF38). Corrected 2026-11-09 (High): the "reset flag" the controller and the head step read is the word at player +0xF3C (the turn-hold flag of Behavior 8b), NOT the first-use mark at +0xF38. With the reset flag set, the controller also takes the forward and side axes from joint 0's frame (rows 2 and 0) instead of the root rows (+0x280 and +0x260). The frame argument of the controller is copied and never read. The head-rotation quaternion of the head step is pose slot 6 (+0xC0) and is first set to (0, 0, 0, 1); chains 4 and 6 are evaluated, the neck position is chain 6's row 3, and the look point comes from the controller.

**Controller (mode 0), each call (High).** O is the head position and t the target. F is the forward axis (Z) and X the side axis of the root frame, or of joint 0 when the reset flag is set. The angles are radians: they feed the half-angle sines and cosines of quaternions.
1. Fh = unit(F with y = 0), Xh = unit(X with y = 0), Dh = unit((t − O) with y = 0) and D = unit(t − O).
2. Yaw target = acos(min(1, Fh · Dh)), made negative when Xh · Dh < 0.
3. Pitch target = acos(min(1, Dh · D)), the elevation of the target, made negative when D · R > 0. So a target above the head gives a negative pitch.
4. Reset flag set: the stored yaw and pitch both become 0, and step 5 is skipped. Step 6 still runs.
5. Otherwise each stored angle moves toward its target by the rate cap 0.10 per call. When the difference is at most 0.10, the angle takes the target.
6. Limits: the yaw is clamped to [−1.57, 1.57], then the pitch to [−0.35, 0.35]. The limits clamp; they do not wrap.
7. H = unit(Fh cos(yaw) + Xh sin(yaw)) and U = unit(H cos(pitch) + R sin(pitch)). The look point is O + 2.0 U.

Quirk (reproduce): because of the sign in step 3, the look point lies below the head's horizontal plane when the target is above it. The head step mirrors it back (Medium-Low for the net direction).

**Head step (mode 0; High for the level rotation's formulas and product, Medium for its sign in strongly turned poses, Medium-Low for the rest).** Head joint 6 first gets a level rotation, stored as its local rotation, that cancels the tilt of joint 4's world frame. Joint 4's frame has rows X4 (side), Y4 (up) and Z4 (forward). The character's placement matrix gives the side row S and the forward row F (its first and third rows); world up is (0, 1, 0). Two angles are formed (both 0 with the reset flag):
- Pitch alpha. X4h is X4 with y set to 0, made unit. The reference forward F' is F, reversed when X4h . S is negative (joint 4 is turned more than a quarter turn away from the placement frame). Fr is the unit part of F' perpendicular to X4h. alpha = acos(min(1, Fr . Z4)): the angle between joint 4's forward axis and the horizontal forward direction that is perpendicular to its flattened side axis. It is positive when Z4 points down and negative when Z4 points up (High for an upright joint 4 within a quarter turn of the placement frame; in strongly tilted or turned poses the code orders three more dot-product sign tests that can flip it, so the sign is Medium there).
- Roll beta = acos(min(1, X4 . X4h)): the angle between joint 4's side axis and its horizontal projection, negative when X4 has a positive y component (High).
The level quaternion (x, y, z, w) is the product q_alpha times q_beta, where q_alpha is a rotation by alpha about the local negative X axis and q_beta a rotation by beta about the local positive Z axis (half-angle sine and cosine; standard quaternion product, so q_beta acts first). Joint 4's yaw about the up axis is not corrected. Joint 6's world frame is then recomputed. It then turns toward the look point: a yaw about its local up axis by acos(head forward · horizontal direction to the look point), negative when the point is on the −X side; then a pitch about its local −X axis by the elevation, negated when the look point is above. The net effect is that the head turns toward the target in yaw and pitch.

**Hero target choice (Medium-High; item g structure raised 2026-11-09 from a full read of the choice routine).** The hero picks its look target in this order (head = the translation row of joint 6's matrix; the default target is head + 5.0 × body forward; the "blocked" state is the current clip 0x13 or 0x12, which skips the scans):
1. The first record of the hero's target scan list, when the list is not empty and the hero is not blocked: used when its candidate flag (+0x10 of the record) is set OR its id equals the forced id (+0x15E0) (the spec earlier named only the flag; corrected 2026-11-09, High). The object found by that id supplies its own look point (class method +0x294); if its model has a head joint (value at +0x154 at or above 0) the cached point at model +0x15E0 is used instead (the hero's own target point is at hero +0x15D0).
2. Otherwise, when not blocked and the hero is in a grid cell: the nearest object of list B of the cell (count at cell +0x10, pointer +0x14) whose kind (+0x40) is 1 or 3, with the direction from the hero to the object's +0x30 point normalised, dot with body forward above 0.5, distance below the range word +0xF84 (12.0 for Tor, 25.0 for Roberta), nearest so far starting at 1.0e9; the target is that point with y + 1.5.
3. Otherwise, when neither class test (class methods +0x184 and +0x27C) is true, by action state: state 0x15: clip 0x35 gives head − 5 × right, clip 0x34 head + 5 × right, clip 0x2D forward × 5 with y − 4.0, clip 0x38 forward × 5 with y + 4.0, clip 0x39 head + joint 0's row 2 × 5 and the play-request flag 1; state 0x29: clips 0x5B and 0x5A forward × 5 with y + 4.0, clips 0x5F and 0x5E forward × 5 with y − 4.0, clip 0x87 hero position + forward × 5, otherwise by the left stick Y (centre 127.5, dead range 40: none keeps the default, up gives y + 4.0, down y − 4.0); state 0xD: only for hero kind 0 (+0xF80) and clips 0x13 and 0x12: head + joint 0's row 2 × 5 with flag 1; state 0x1E: clip 0x3D gives −5 × right, 0x3C +5 × right; state 0x17: clip 0x4F gives −5 × right, 0x51 +5 × right. Other states keep the default.
4. When one of the two class tests is true, the target steers by the locomotion heading: along = dot(heading, forward); at 0.995 or more keep the default; side = dot(heading, right); the camera forward flattened and normalised, and when dot(forward, camera) < 0.99, along −= 0.25; with side > 0: along ≤ 0 gives head − 5 × right, else head + 5 × along × forward + 5 × (1 − along) × right; with side ≤ 0: along ≤ 0 gives head + 5 × right, else head + 5 × along × forward − 5 × (1 − along) × right.
Finally, if the animation request table says the current main request has the head-look bit (bit 13), the target and flag are submitted as the animation player's look request. The default of step 4 above remains the fallback.

**Neck look (mode 1; Medium-High).** h_t is the horizontal unit vector from joint 6 to the target, and h_b the horizontal unit vector from joint 6 to joint 17. A = acos(h_t · h_b); the dot product is not clamped before the acos. The side test is s = unit(h_b × R) · h_t. The per-joint target is τ = −A/6 when s > 0, else τ = +A/6. Each of the six angles then steps by 0.01 per tick:
- when it is within 0.01 of τ, it takes τ;
- otherwise, when s > 0 it may only increase, and when s ≤ 0 it may only decrease.

Each of the six joints is then turned about R by its angle, on top of the animated pose, by the joint-turn helper (Behavior 25). The neck turns about the up axis only.

Quirks (reproduce):
- The allowed direction points away from τ (τ ≤ 0 when only increases are allowed, τ ≥ 0 when only decreases are allowed). So from 0 an angle changes only by the snap to a τ within 0.01. When the target then moves so that τ is more than 0.01 away, the neck freezes until τ comes back within 0.01, or until a tick without a request sets the angles to 0.
- The serpent's neck angles go back to 0 on any tick without a request; the hero's yaw and pitch persist.
- Whether joints 6 and 17 place A near 0 or near π when the serpent faces the hero, and so whether the neck visibly turns in play, is open (SPEC-0016).

Edge cases:
- Turning from one yaw limit to the other (3.14 radians) takes at least 32 calls at the rate cap.
- A target beyond a limit is held at that limit on every call. The yaw and pitch do not wrap past a limit.

### 24. Light slots of a character (High)

Confidence: High. The four slots are the character's light context, and the evaluator is the light slot choice of SPEC-0046 (Character lights and Character light slots), which owns the full rules. They hold lights, not contacts: nothing in them affects movement, hits or blocking.

The block lives in the character's body. Per slot (0 to 3) it holds the chosen light entry, its weight, a light vector and a lock flag, plus a per-slot mark for dynamic lights. A light entry is a 0x50-byte record: position, a vector, type (1, 2 or 3), strength, flag word, radius, a squared-radius gate value and a zone key.

Walk order each pass (High): all four slots are reset (no entry, weight -1.0, vector zero, lock and mark clear); then each slot still empty takes the character's own entry of the same index (the four scene default lights); then the area's light-set records (type 2 entries and the first type 1 entry, then the type 3 entries); then the dynamic point lights, which may displace the weaker of slots 2 and 3 and are marked. The walk runs once at character set-up and then once per pass while the character's zone passes the zone query. A scene light context (for world objects and props) uses the same slot choice.

Rules in brief (all as SPEC-0046):
1. An entry whose zone key fails the zone query is rejected. A type 1 or type 3 entry with a radius above 0 is rejected unless the squared distance from the entry position to the character is below its gate value.
2. Weight: strength s for types 1 and 2. Type 3 with radius r and distance d: s for d at most 0.5 r; s × (1 − (d − 0.5 r) / (0.4 r)) for d up to 0.9 r; -1 above 0.9 r.
3. Type 1 takes slot 0. A type 2 or type 3 entry with its flag word set takes slot 1.
4. Otherwise slot 2, then slot 3, when empty. When both are full, a displacement candidate c is chosen from slots 2 and 3 among those holding a type 2 light: with both type 2, the smaller weight (slot 2 on a tie), but the unlocked one when exactly one is locked; with one type 2 light, that slot if unlocked; else none.
5. A type 2 entry replaces c when its weight is at least weight(c). A type 3 entry with d above 0.9 r does not take a slot: instead weight(c) becomes (d − 0.9 r) / (0.1 r) × the strength of c's entry and c is locked for this walk (the cross-fade). Inside 0.9 r a type 3 entry replaces c when its weight is 0 or more.
6. A type 3 entry with no candidate is rejected, unless it is a dynamic light with weight above 0: it then replaces slot 2 when weight(2) < weight(3), else slot 3.
7. An accepted entry stores its weight and a light vector: for type 1 the entry's own vector; for type 2 the entry vector expressed in the character's body frame; for type 3 the unit direction from the light to the character, in the body frame.

### 25. Chain bodies (secondary-motion parts)

Confidence: High for the owner, the modes, the field layout, the chains on disc, the constants and the defaults; High for the formulas and Medium-High overall for the mode 1 and mode 2 step; High for Roberta's corrected rest pitches, the axes and signs, the write-back and the worked example; Medium for whether Roberta's chains are ears or hair.

**Owner and selection (High).** Chain bodies are part of the character's animation player (animation sub-object + 0x30; Layout), not a separate object. After the pose is built each tick, the player's mode word (player +0x30) selects one secondary step:
- −1: none. This is the default written when every player is built.
- 0: the bone-chain Verlet step of SPEC-0005 (Secondary-motion chains), used only by Gnarfas. The Gnarfas behaviour sets it at its start, with four chains of 4, 4, 5 and 5 links on main joints 46 to 49, 38 to 41, 29 to 33 and 15 to 19.
- 1: the pendulum chain below with one chain. Tor's spawn sets it: his tail.
- 2: the pendulum chain with two chains. Roberta's spawn sets it: two chains on her head.

No other character sets a mode, so every other character has none (High). Up to five chain records sit in the player at a stride of 0x240 from +0x3F0. Each holds a link count (+0x3F0), an active flag (+0x3F4, read only by mode 0) and an initialised flag (+0x3F8, mode 0 only). The links follow at 0x70-byte steps from +0x400.

**Link fields (High).** Player offsets for chain 0, link 0; add 0x240 per chain and 0x70 per link.

| Offset | Field |
| --- | --- |
| +0x400 | saved local rotation (mode 0 only) |
| +0x410 | B, the previous point |
| +0x420 | C, the current point |
| +0x430 | a, the distance from the link joint to this joint |
| +0x434 | L, the distance from this joint to its point |
| +0x438 | v, this joint |
| +0x43C | link joint (the joint this link hangs from) |
| +0x440 | next joint (−1 for the last link) |
| +0x448, +0x44C | rest yaw α, rest pitch β |
| +0x450 | spring k |
| +0x454, +0x458 | yaw minimum and maximum |
| +0x45C, +0x460 | pitch minimum and maximum |
| +0x464 | velocity keep b |
| +0x468 | drag c |

Modes 1 and 2 index the sub-part joint list (joint = main-joint count + index). Mode 0 indexes main joints.

**Chains on disc (High).**
- Tor (outfits 504 to 511): one chain on sub-part joints 8 to 12 (mesh bones 40 to 44). Link 0 hangs from sub-part joint 0 (bone 32, at hip height) and each later link from the joint before it; the next joints are 9, 10, 11, 12 and none. In the bind pose the chain runs straight back along −Z for 1.88 units: the tail.
- Roberta (outfits 512 to 519): two chains. Chain 0 is joints 7 to 10, with link joints 3, 7, 8 and 9 and next joints 8, 9, 10 and none. Chain 1 is joints 11 to 14, with link joints 5, 11, 12 and 13 and next joints 12, 13, 14 and none. Sub-part joints 3 and 5 (bones 35 and 37) sit on the head joint at x = ±0.267 and height 3.03, and each chain runs back 1.35 units. At rest they hang down behind the head (Roberta's rest pose below); whether the art is hair or ears is not labelled (Medium).
- Roberta's segment lengths a equal the bone lengths of all eight of her outfits. Tor's last three equal his mesh; his first two differ (0.188 against 0.037 on the mesh, and 0.448 against 0.478). Use the constants below, not the mesh lengths.

**Constants (fixed program constants, High; confirmed 2026-11-09 for both characters against the chain set-up routines: the chain and link joints, the per-link a and L values, the rest pitch β, k = 0.15, the ±10 yaw limits, the pitch limits [β, β + 10], b = 0.2 and c = −0.01 then −0.015 for Roberta, every Tor constant for mode 1, the limit block written at the start of every tick by the set-up call with its init argument 0, and the Roberta joint lists 7 to 10 and 11 to 14 with the chain-1 links (joint, link joint, next joint) = (11, 5, 12), (12, 11, 13), (13, 12, 14), (14, 13, none)).** Per link, in link order.
- Tor, links 0 to 4:
  - a = 0.188, 0.448, 0.466, 0.410, 0.340; L = 0.448, 0.466, 0.410, 0.340, 0.340 (L of link i is a of link i + 1, and the last L repeats).
  - α = 0 for every link; β = −0.6 for link 0, then 0.4 for links 1 to 4.
  - k = 0.05.
  - Yaw limits: ±1.25 for link 0, then ±1.25, ±1.57, ±1.7 and ±2.0.
  - Pitch limits: [−10.6, 0.45] for link 0, then [−1.4, 2.0], [−1.4, 1.9], [−1.5, 2.3] and [−1.6, 2.4].
  - b = 0.85 for link 0, then 0.7.
  - c = −0.01 for link 0, then −0.015.
- Roberta, both chains, links 0 to 3:
  - a = 0.038, 0.383, 0.457, 0.470; L = 0.383, 0.457, 0.470, 0.470.
  - α = 0; β = 0.25, −1.25, −0.75, 0.2 (radians; positive pitch raises the link, negative lowers it, see the axes below).
  - k = 0.15.
  - Yaw limits ±10 (no effective limit).
  - Pitch limits [β, β + 10] for every link: [0.25, 10.25], [−1.25, 8.75], [−0.75, 9.25] and [0.2, 10.2]. The minimum is the rest pitch, so the rest pose lies on the lower clamp of each link.
  - b = 0.2.
  - c = −0.01 for link 0, then −0.015.
  - Roberta's per-link constants are written again at the start of every tick, with the same values.

**Per-tick step (modes 1 and 2; High for the formulas, Medium-High overall).** The links of each chain run in order, i = 0 to n − 1:
1. Compute the link joint's world frame M, with axes X, Y and Z (rows 0 to 2) and position P_L. Link 0 computes it from the root; each later link from its parent, which is the joint the previous link has just rotated.
2. Set joint v's local rotation to identity and its local translation to (0, 0, −a). Its position is T = P_L − a Z.
3. Define dir(θ, φ) as −Z turned by pitch φ about X, then by yaw θ about Y (both M's world axes, so q = q_yaw(Y) ⊗ q_pitch(X)); θ and φ are in radians. In M's axes this is (−sin θ cos φ, sin φ, −cos θ cos φ). The rest target is X1 = T + L dir(α, β).
4. Spring: F = −k (C − X1) (2026-11-11, High: confirmed for the x, y and z lanes). The spring routine also stores the w lane of F as −k |C − X1|^2, the negated k times the squared length of the separation; nothing in this step reads that w lane. A zero separation gives a zero force, not an invalid value.
5. Predict: X3 = C + b (C − B) + c û + F. Here û is the unit vector of the owner's displacement this tick (motion record +0x160, copied to player +0x3D0 at the start of the animation sub-object's tick), and the zero vector when the owner does not move (High for the source and copy order).
6. Measure, with d = unit(X3 − T):
   - Yaw θ: p = unit(d − (d · Y) Y). θ = acos(min(1, p · (−Z))), made negative when p · X > 0. If the projection is zero, θ = 0.
   - Pitch φ: φ = acos(min(1, p · d)), made negative when d · Y < 0. If the projection is zero, φ = 0.
7. Clamp θ to [yaw min, yaw max], then φ to [pitch min, pitch max].
8. N = T + L dir(θ, φ). Then B = C and C = N.
9. Joint v's local rotation becomes the same yaw and pitch about its local Y and X axes (q = q_yaw ⊗ q_pitch); its local translation stays (0, 0, −a).

Measure and rebuild are exact inverses, so an unclamped point keeps its direction. There is no gravity term; the chain hangs from its springs. In mode 2 only, when the forward row of the root matrix has a negative Y component, the two axes handed to the rotation of step 9 (yaw axis (0, 1, 0), pitch axis (1, 0, 0)) are negated. The root matrix is at player +0x260 (rows X, Y, Z, position), so the tested word is player +0x284. CORRECTED 2026-11-09 (High; the aim routine was read in full, and the earlier sentence "The same quaternion also places the new point N, so on such a tick the whole pose takes the mirrored yaw and pitch" is withdrawn): two different quaternions are built from the same yaw and pitch angles. The first, q1, uses the frame's OWN Y and X rows (never flipped) and moves the new point N (N = the aim point from the unflipped frame). The second, q2, uses the fixed axes (0, 1, 0) and (1, 0, 0), negated on a flipped tick, and is only stored as the joint's local rotation (step 9). So on a flipped tick the stored point N and the spring target are unmirrored and only the joint's rotation (hence the drawn pose and the next link's frame, which is built from that rotation) is mirrored; a body tilted forward and down (for example on a downward slope) therefore snaps the drawn pose between the two. A value of exactly 0 or −0 does not trigger it, so keep the forward row's Y exactly 0 on flat ground (High for the test and the two-quaternion rule; Medium for the purpose). Also confirmed 2026-11-09 (High): the strict test is on the third row's Y lane (player +0x284); modes 1 and 2 loop over every link (the matrices are not rebuilt after the last link, see Tip orientation); mode 1 runs chain 0 only and mode 2 runs chains 0 and 1 after re-applying Roberta's tuning; the helper that moves each joint's local rotation takes the yaw measure from the first angle routine (limits at +0x454 and +0x458) and the pitch measure from the second (limits +0x45C and +0x460), both signed angles despite their names; the spring is F = −k (C − X1), the prediction X3 = C + (C − B) × b + unit(owner displacement) × c + F with the zero vector staying zero, and the aim routine returns N = origin + (offset direction × −L) × (basis × rotation(yaw, pitch)).

**Setup and seed quirk (High).** At spawn each link stores v, its link joint and its next joint. The link add sets the main-block joint v's local rotation to identity and seeds B = C = the world position of main-block joint "next". That is the wrong block for modes 1 and 2, and the rotation write is overwritten as soon as the animation plays. For the last link, next is −1: read as unsigned it is above the 83-joint limit, so the joint-transform step returns at once and the seed is the 16 bytes just before the main joint matrix buffer. The seeds only shape the first tick, because step 8 puts the point back on its sphere. A clean build may seed B = C = X1 (Low for reproducing the original seed).

**Axes and signs (High for the structure; High for the sign of pitch by the agreement of measure, rebuild and rest target).** M is the world matrix of the link joint, with rows X, Y and Z as its axes. Yaw turns about M's Y row, pitch about M's X row, and the chain's forward axis is M's −Z. The bind chains run along −Z, so a character facing +Z has both chains trailing behind it. Positive pitch raises the link toward M's +Y; negative pitch lowers it. Positive yaw turns the forward axis toward −X. Joint v's frame is M turned by its rebuilt rotation, and the next link's M is that frame, so the pitches of successive links add (the cumulative pitch down a chain is the running sum). Pitch is measured as an elevation, so a measured or written pitch stays within ±π/2 and a stored rest pitch outside that range could only act as a spring target.

**Write-back (High).** The result of step 9 is stored as joint v's local rotation (a quaternion in x, y, z, w order, relative to the link joint) and the local translation (0, 0, −a) in the animation player's sub-part joint-record list (records of 0x20 bytes: rotation at +0, translation at +0x10). Nothing is written in world space. The points B and C stay in world space between ticks and are not carried along with the body; only the spring (through T and X1) pulls them. Before the rebuild the same record is set to the identity rotation and the translation (0, 0, −a) so that T can be computed. Joint v's world matrix is rebuilt (parent times local) when the next link starts, so the joints of links 0 to 2 are drawn with their rebuilt rotation. The last link's joint is not rebuilt after its rotation is stored, so it is drawn with an identity local rotation and takes its parent's orientation (see Tip orientation).

**Roberta's rest pose (High for the numbers; Medium for calling the strands hair).** With the constants above the cumulative pitch of her four links at rest is +0.25, −1.00, −1.75 and −1.55 rad. Each chain leaves the head slightly upward and backward, then falls, and its end hangs almost straight down behind the head, about 1.2 units below the head joint's height. The rest pose is the lower stop of each pitch (minimum = rest pitch), so the drag term and body motion can only lift a link above it. From the straight bind placement with no motion the chain reaches this pose in about 59 ticks (change below 0.0001 per tick). Damping is the velocity keep 0.2 and the spring 0.15.

**Per-tick order, and what makes a port stand up or jitter.** Order inside the character tick: clips are evaluated and the pose pass builds every joint record and world matrix (this writes the chain joints' records too); the pending look is applied; then the chain step runs for chain 0 (links 0 to 3) and then chain 1, each link using the frame the previous link has just rebuilt; the unit displacement û was copied at the start of the animation sub-object's tick. Typical causes of upright or jittering chains:
1. Using the rest pitches −3.5 and −6.0 (an earlier wrong reading of the stored words): link 1's rest direction then points back toward the head (yaw +π) and the strands fold forward and stand above the head, with yaw flipping between +π and −π.
2. Using one fixed frame (bind or animated) for all four links instead of the frame the previous link has just rebuilt, so the pitches do not add.
3. Clamping the pitch in another frame, or not applying the sign of the elevation before the clamp. The rest pose sits on the lower clamp, so any error there shows as a shake.
4. Normalizing a tiny rounding-noise displacement into a full unit drag vector. Retail uses the exact zero vector when the owner does not move, so the drag (−0.01 or −0.015 per tick) is absent at rest.
5. A root matrix whose forward row has a slightly negative Y on flat ground (the axis flip above).
6. Letting the arccosine argument pass 1 or −1 by rounding (clamp it to −1..1).

**Test vector (Roberta chain 0, flat ground; chain 1 is the same with x negated).** The root matrix is the identity at the origin (the character faces +Z, Y is up) and every animated local rotation is the identity. Link 0's link joint is sub-part joint 3 at (0.267375, 3.02515, −0.000143) with M = identity; L and a are the constants above, no owner motion.
- Rest (C = B = the rest points; another tick leaves everything unchanged, yaw 0 for all links): link 0: T (0.2674, 3.0252, −0.0381), pitch 0.25, N (0.2674, 3.1199, −0.4092), rotation (0.1247, 0, 0, 0.9922). Link 1: T = N of link 0, pitch −1.25, N (0.2674, 2.7354, −0.6562), rotation (−0.5851, 0, 0, 0.8110); its link joint's frame has Y row (0, 0.9689, 0.2474) and Z row (0, −0.2474, 0.9689). Link 2: pitch −0.75, N (0.2674, 2.2729, −0.5724), rotation (−0.3663, 0, 0, 0.9305); link joint frame Y row (0, 0.5403, −0.8415), Z row (0, 0.8415, 0.5403). Link 3: pitch 0.2, N (0.2674, 1.8030, −0.5822), rotation (0.0998, 0, 0, 0.9950); link joint frame Y row (0, −0.1782, −0.9840), Z row (0, 0.9840, −0.1782). The world directions of the links are (0, 0.2474, −0.9689), (0, −0.8415, −0.5403) and (0, −0.9840, 0.1782).
- One tick from the straight bind placement (B = C = (0.2674, 3.0252, −0.4211), (0.2674, 3.0252, −0.8781), (0.2674, 3.0252, −1.3481), (0.2674, 3.0252, −1.8181)): link 0 measures below its minimum and is clamped to pitch 0.25 (N = (0.2674, 3.1199, −0.4092)); link 1 pitch −0.5573, N (0.2674, 2.9817, −0.8448); link 2 pitch 0.2544, N (0.2674, 2.9569, −1.3142); link 3 pitch 0.2 (clamped), N (0.2674, 3.0258, −1.7791).
- One tick at rest with the owner displacement û = (0, 0, 1): link 0 unchanged (already at its minimum); link 1 pitch −1.2229, N (0.2674, 2.7422, −0.6665); link 2 pitch −0.75, N (0.2674, 2.2776, −0.5953); link 3 pitch 0.2, N (0.2674, 1.8082, −0.6178).

**Tip orientation (High).** The chain step updates the world matrix of each joint it changes by recomputing that joint from its current parent chain. The terminal joint is not included in the pendulum update loop, and no later full pose pass runs in the same character tick. Its world matrix therefore remains the one built before the chain step, so the tip orientation quirk described above persists through this tick (Behavior 3, steps 5–7; this specifies runtime ordering only).

**Defaults (High).** The animation player constructor stores its config pointer in the player's first word and sizes three per-entry buffers (4, 32 and 64 bytes per entry) from the count in the config's first word. The four 0x60-byte records it fills are the four playback records (Layout, Animation player layers). Their defaults:
- clip 0, mirror 0, weight 0;
- fade step 1.0, fade count 0, fade mode 0;
- cursor 0, fraction 0, speed 0.5;
- root step and root accumulator zero (copied from the boot-written zero vector at 0x3932C0).

The constructor also sets the look mode word +0x3AC to −1, the look pending word +0x3A8 to 0, the word +0x2C to −1, and the words +0xF40 and +0xF44 to 1. The base init sets the chain mode word +0x30 to −1, every chain inactive and the root to identity.

**Joint-turn helper (High, raised 2026-11-09).** A helper turns joint j of the main pose by an angle about an axis: half = angle ÷ 2; the axis is transformed by the joint's frame matrix rows (the chain frame of the joint, then its frame matrix); r = axis_local × sin(half); the joint's local rotation q becomes q × (r, cos(half)), that is w' = qw cos(half) − q · r and v' = qw × r + cos(half) × qv + qv × r, stored lane by lane. It is the per-joint step of the serpent neck look (Behavior 23A), with the up axis R. It is not camera code. Seeding confirmed 2026-11-09 (High): a link add sets the main-pose rotation of joint v to the identity, builds the frame of the "next" joint with the root matrix and seeds B = C = the translation row of that main joint matrix, saves the joint's rotation, and for the last link (next = −1) the build returns at once because −1 is above the 83-joint index limit (index limit 0x52).

### 25A. Pool drift ("chain-body follow") and four-count trigger (dormant)

Confidence: High that the drift belongs to a world object pool that is never filled, and for its rule; Low for the trigger, which is not located.

1. **Drift.** This is not a chain-body step: it is a step of the world's +0x29F0 object pool (SPEC-0028, section 15.1), which never runs in play. Each record update moves its position p by p ← p + 0.99 (p − p_prev) + (0.0003 i, 0.005, 0.0003 j), where i is a random whole number from 4 to 8 and j one from 5 to 9 (closed draws from the shared random source, SPEC-0008). Then p_prev ← p and w = 1. The previous sample p_prev is the last tick's position at record +0x150. The 0.99 is a fixed program constant, not the data word at 0x2DD854.
2. **Four-count trigger.** A walk over entries counts those that meet a condition, and when exactly four meet it, one further routine is called. The condition, the routine and the owner are not located; it is probably another step of the dormant pool or of the mode 0 chain step (Low).

### 26. Character colour block and palette presets

Confidence: High for the block, the presets, the push and its callers, and the variant select; Medium for the three bytes being red, green and blue (the preset loader packs word = blue × 65536 + green × 256 + red, byte 3 = 0, so byte 0 is the red byte of the preset triple; confirmed 2026-11-09, High for the packing); High for the override set (raised 2026-11-09: the push replaces a channel by (table value & 0xFF) when the table entry of the 15 triples is not −1, only slot 1 = (155, 123, 193) and slot 2 = (101, 81, 126) have entries, and the push writes both buffers for an argument below 0 and only buffer 0 or 1 for an argument of 0 or 1); High that the run-time colour table and its apply never run.

1. **Colour block = the spawn record (High).** The block pointer at character +0xA44 is the character's kind-3 spawn record data (SPEC-0006; SPEC-0029, Layout). The record holds 15 colour words w0 to w14 at data +0x48 + 4 i, i = 0 to 14. A colour word packs byte 0 in bits 0 to 7, byte 1 in bits 8 to 15 and byte 2 in bits 16 to 23; byte 3 (0x80 or 0x00 on disc) is not used. On disc w0 is 0x80FFFFFF in all 3,036 records, 1,653 records have all 15 words at 0x80FFFFFF, and there are 275 distinct sets. A debug label list names w1 to w14 as two skin colours, two shirt colours, two pants colours, hair, hat, two eye colours, belts, shoes and two spare colours (Medium). The variant is the record's u16 at +0xB2 and the monster type its u16 at +0xB4 (SPEC-0006). The character's +0xA48 is its row-530 pointer (SPEC-0029).
2. **Presets (High).** Preset p (1 to 7) is block p − 1 of the preset table (Where it lives). The loader copies the 15 RGB triples: triple k (block offset 3k) becomes colour word k, with byte 3 = 0. Triple 0 is zero in every preset, so applying a preset turns w0 black (0x00000000; the disc value is 0x80FFFFFF). Triple 14 is white in every preset. Presets 1 to 6 equal the disc colour sets of variants 0 to 5 of the minion types 77 to 83 (apart from w0); type 80 also uses them for its variants 7 to 12. Preset 7 is never selected.
3. **Variant select (High).** Only the kind-6 spell contact (SPEC-0029, Behavior 9) selects a preset. It draws p from 1 to 6 (both ends included), sets the variant (+0xB2) to p − 1, re-reads the row-530 pointer (+0xA48) from (type, variant), loads preset p (item 2) and pushes with −1 (item 4). A preset number outside 1 to 7 would pack uninitialised bytes, but the draw cannot produce one.
4. **Push (High).** For colour word j = 0 to 14 the push forms the vector (byte 0 / 255, byte 1 / 255, byte 2 / 255, 1.0) and stores it at +0x10 of slot record j. The slot record pointer is at +0x1340 + 8 j (buffer 0) or +0x1344 + 8 j (buffer 1) of the per-slot sub-object that the character's pointer at +0x170 names. Argument −1 writes both buffers; k = 0 or 1 writes buffer k only. The slot records are the 15 colour slots that SPEC-0034, section 21.4, blends.
5. **Override (Medium-High).** While the character's flag +0xC58 is set, the push takes its bytes from the override set at 0x316CA0 instead of the record: three words per colour word, where −1 keeps the record's byte. Only w1 and w2 (the two skin colours) are overridden, to (155, 123, 193) and (101, 81, 126). A short setter sets +0xC58 and pushes. A region-wide switch sets the flag (to 0 or 1) on every character of a character block. Its trigger (High): the switch runs when a character block is loaded (both block load paths), with the result of the world's variant test: 1 when progress flag 216 is on and the world's variant key check gives 1 (SPEC-0022, Variant key check), else 0. The variant key is the pack's sound selector word, a disc value from 0 to 11 that no run-time code writes (High). A townsfolk character bound later also gets the flag set when the same test gives 1. A third routine that refreshes every block of the world has no caller (dormant). Medium for what the override means in the story (it recolours the two skin colours to violet tones). The same switch also sets the block's draw mask word: 0x80000000 when the test result is 1, 0x40000000 otherwise (the value a block has from creation). The mask is matched against the layer flags of the character models' type-4 nodes (SPEC-0004, Drawing a node tree, step 3a), so in the override state the model parts flagged 0x80000000 draw instead of the parts flagged 0x40000000 (High for the mask values and the match; Medium for the story meaning). Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High): the switch sets the slot's draw mask word (+0x40) to 0x80000000 when its flag argument is non-zero, else 0x40000000, and then runs the colour refresh (flag setter) on every non-null character of the slot (the count is the byte at +0xAE9); the preset loader reads block p − 1 at stride 0x30 (45 bytes used), writes the 15 colour words at (colour block pointer + 0x48 + 4 i), and for a preset outside 1 to 7 copies nothing (the words would be uninitialised stack bytes).
6. **Callers of the push (High).**
   - The region-character update, every tick, with the current display buffer index (a word toggled every frame).
   - The character bind, with −1, when its model set's flag word is 1.
   - The variant select (item 3) and the override setter (item 5).
   - The hero has no per-tick push, so after a status tint ends (the override slot is cleared) its colour records keep the last blended values until one of the callers above runs (Resolved, High for the code path; the per-frame colour upload reads the records without a slot test, and only the blend is gated by the slot being on). Whether the original shows the persisting tint is a play check (USER-CHECKS).
   - The hero has no per-tick push. Its only push is the character bind (above), which uses the same 15 colour words of its colour block (or the overridden w1 and w2 while the override flag is set). Nothing restores the hero's colour records when a status ends (SPEC-0034, section 21.5 and 21.6), so a tint that the blend left stays until one of these pushes runs (Medium-High).
7. **Run-time colour table and apply (High; dormant).** The 14-entry table at 0x32CE44, the routine that applies it and the player-index word at 0x2D759C belong to a PC-link debug tuner: they are callbacks and the selected-character variable of a debug variable registrar that has no caller, so none of them runs in play. The clean side omits all three. Confirmed and extended 2026-11-09 (High): the rebuild routine that reads the table from the selected character runs only from that debug registrar and the apply routine is a registered callback. Selector 0 or 1 means a hero (hero table stride 0x1680): the rebuild reads, for entries 1 to 14, the vec4 at +0x10 of colour packet [2 i] of the hero's anim object and stores the four bytes (float × 255 truncated) as R, G, B, A; any other selector value is a character id looked up by id, whose spawn-record colour words 1 to 14 (+0x48 + 4 i) are copied byte-wise. The apply routine (it is not a fade) writes for a hero byte ÷ 255 into lanes x, y, z AND w of the vec4 at +0x10 of BOTH colour packets [2 i] and [2 i + 1] (lane w from byte 3, NOT forced to 1.0, unlike the real push of item 4), and for another selector packs the four bytes into the character's spawn-record colour word i (byte 3 in bits 24 to 31) and then runs the colour push with −1. This differs from item 4 only in the dormant tuner.
8. **Other users of the block (High).** The class 17 behaviour block (not in this spec) reads +0xB2, +0xB4, +0xB7, +0xBC, +0xC0 and a u16 at +0x84 of the same block. These are fields of the same spawn record (SPEC-0006), so the two uses agree and there is no conflict.
9. **Where the colours show: why an NPC with no colour handling is white and untextured (High).** The 15 colour slots do nothing by themselves; a model part reads one of them when it is drawn.
   - **Group to slot.** Every material group has a colour slot byte, 0 to 14, at group offset 0x27 (SPEC-0003). Before each mesh leaf is drawn, the draw sets the draw colour scale s (SPEC-0004, Colour; SPEC-0046) to the vector of slot record *j*, where *j* is the slot byte of the leaf's group. That vector is (byte 0 / 255, byte 1 / 255, byte 2 / 255, 1.0) of colour word *j* of the spawn record (item 4). The scale then multiplies the lit colour of every vertex of the leaf, so the part's RGB is L × (vertex byte / 16) × s per channel, truncated, and alpha is unchanged. It applies to textured leaves and to untextured leaves, with no test of texture state.
   - **Why it matters for NPCs.** The vertex colours of an NPC's untextured parts are only grey shading (bytes 20 to 31); skin, hair, shirt, pants, shoes and the like are untextured groups whose entire hue is s. In the 1,157 character model sets 7,802 groups are untextured, and 4,733 groups use a slot above 0. A hero outfit uses slot 0 almost everywhere (9 of 331 groups use a higher slot), and colour word 0 is white, so a hero is correct even when the slot colour is never applied. **This is the statement that explains white, untextured NPCs:** a client that draws region characters with s = (1, 1, 1, 1), or that applies the slot colour only to the hero, draws every untextured NPC part white or grey and leaves only the few textured parts with detail.
   - **Timing.** The slot vectors must be filled at bind time (item 6, when the model set's first mesh child has chunk type 13, true in all 1,157 model sets and 16 outfits) and again every tick for region characters, so a character that is bound with all-white words, or whose words change, shows the change on the next frame.
   - **Check.** On disc the first kind-3 record of region 1002 (name "Lady Marissa", model set 0) has word 1 bytes 255, 152, 83 (orange skin), word 2 bytes 255, 64, 0, word 3 bytes 150, 105, 190 and word 13 bytes 187, 137, 255; her leaf groups 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12 are untextured with slots 1, 2, 2, 8, 3, 4, 7, 8 and 13. Draw her with s = (1, 1, 1, 1) and she is grey-white; draw her with the slot colours and she is orange with a lavender and plum outfit.
10. **Monster row defaults (new 2026-11-09; High, from the row reset routine).** A fresh monster row (0x504 bytes) is written with these values before the design table overrides any of them, so a clean build needs them for fields the design table leaves out: hit points 50; radius 1.175; height 3.5; weight 5.5; draw distance 300; low-resolution distance 200; difficulty 2; death-disappear timer 510; default behaviour −1; approach routine 0xFFFF; display-in-default 1; max fly height 100; gimped standoff 10; awareness timer 120; map-search timers 120 and 300; ghost reveal max 120; ghost opacity max 1.0; footstep sound −1 and footstep visual −1; per attack slot (four slots): wind-up, strike and recovery speed 1.0, melee range 5.0, counter trigger −1, spawn count 1, status id −1, spawn key 0xFFFF, spawn entity count 4, steal selector 0xFFFFFFFF and all six missile effect ids 0xFFFF; agro strafe backbiter 1.0; everything else (about 450 words) 0. The hero's motion parameters 5.5, 1.175 and 3.5 (Behavior 1B) equal the weight, radius and height defaults.
11. **Placement from a spawn record, release and bind details (new 2026-11-09; High unless marked).**
   - **Ground placement.** The probe origin is 8.0 above the record position (x, z, y + 8). When the probed height is above −0.9 × 1.0e9 (so the 1.0e9 "no ground" value is excluded) AND the monster row's Can Fly word (+0x94) is 0, the record's y is replaced by the probed height, so flyers keep their authored height. The ground entry is stored (also at +0x184) and the cell-enabled word is set from the zone-grid query of the entry's column with the character's world id when an entry was found. The record's signed byte at +0xA6 is a navigation-mesh start slot (−1 none): with no mesh list a message is printed and nothing else happens, with a slot at or above the mesh count another message is printed, otherwise the walk target is set to that slot. The world id is the slot word at +0x28 (negative: the world's own id). Then the full reset of Behavior 13 runs with the record facing, the row's weight (+0x118), radius (+0x28) and height (+0x2C); the root matrix is the identity, a layered clip is started at blend 0.5, one pose snapshot is taken, the record's first word is set to 1 and the class hook at +0x10C receives the target point and the facing. Default AI mode 9 records get the slot's id base (+0x24) added to their config word (+0xBC, the follow-target id). A summon byte (+0xB6) of 0 sets the enemy class to −1. A townsfolk in a world where the progress switch is on gets the colour refresh with 1.
   - **Release.** A release by tag looks the slot up by the bound-record tag byte; when found and the kill flag is 1 the kill bit is set with the tag and the slot number; not found returns 0. A slot whose active flag is not 1 is left unchanged. Otherwise the attached count (+0xAF2) drops by 1, the flag is cleared and the tag becomes 0xFF; unless the actor's state is 7 the position and body forward (passed through the class hook at +0x10C, which may adjust them) are stored into the spawn record (+0x00 and +0x10, full 16 bytes each) so BOTH position and facing are written back (the line in SPEC-0056 saying only the heading is stored understates this), the cell-cache word at slot +0xE4 + 4 × (actor +0xA40) is written with the ground entry (when the index is below 40) and then zeroed (net effect: cleared); a character the class says is not dead goes to base state 42; the start position (+0x3E0) and start facing (+0x3F0) go to the placement reset and the class teardown hook (+0x6C) runs.
   - **Timed target list entries.** The slot clear sets id −1 and timer 0 on entries of the 8-byte list at +0xB0 (a slot below 11 passes the guard, including index 10 one past the ten entries); the list reset first resets the particle link and then clears entries 0 to 9; the "already listed" test returns 1 when any of entries 0 to 9 holds the owner's character id (+0x14C) (Behavior 27, confirmed High).
   - **Character-attached held items and scan helpers** are in Behavior 29. The random scatter point for a petpet is the character position + (dx, 0, dz), each of dx and dz drawn from 3.0 to 10.0 with a coin-flip sign (order: dx, sign, dz, sign).
   - **Contact list** (confirmed High): a new normal whose dot with a stored entry's normal is above 0.999 refreshes that entry's lifetime to 40; otherwise it is appended (capacity 19, 0x20-byte entries at +0x280 with the lifetime word at +0x10 of the entry, count at +0x500) with lifetime 40; a full list prints a warning.

### 27. Timed target lists and timer re-arm

Confidence: High for the list structure, the free test, the 60-tick timer, the count-down and the re-arm rule; Medium-High for the identity of the two beam and bubble lists; High that the re-arm owner is the Starlight Potion's object (item 57) and for its position 2.0 above the hero, its effect start at the end of the timer and its 100-damage call to Minion-corps characters; Medium-High for its hit geometry and radius curve; Medium for plus or minus 1 tick in the burst timing.

1. **Lists.** Two lists of ten slots hold the beam and bubble target lists of SPEC-0036 ("Beam and bubble lists"); a third list of the same layout belongs to the item-effect object of item 4. Each slot is 8 bytes: a target id word, then an s16 timer at slot offset +4. (High for the structure, confirmed 2026-11-09 by the slot-clear, list-reset and membership routines: id −1 and timer 0 mark a free slot, ten slots, the membership test compares the character id +0x14C; Medium-High for the identity of the two lists.)
2. **Insert.** An insert takes the target id from the source record at +0x14C, places it in the first free slot (slots 0 to 9, in order), and sets that slot's timer to 60 ticks, one second at 60 ticks per second (SPEC-0007). A list with no free slot refuses the insert, and no slot changes. A slot is free when its id word is -1; the timer is not tested.
3. **Count-down.** Once per tick, before its character and breakable walks, each list owner lowers the timer of every occupied slot by 1 and frees the slot (id -1, timer 0) when the timer reaches 0 or less. So a target added on tick n is freed by the count-down of tick n + 60 and can be added again by that tick's walk (the 60-tick re-hit window of Behavior 17). A reset of the owner frees all ten slots.
4. **Timer re-arm and the item-effect object (High for the position, the timer and the call order; Medium-High for the geometry).** The word at +0x114 is the timer of one world effect object (a singleton at data address 0x37B280 + 0x280) that item 57, the Starlight Potion, starts (SPEC-0021). Starting it binds its two effects once (records 4020 and 568), stores the point 2.0 ABOVE the hero's position, offset (0, 2.0, 0) along world up (not 2.0 ahead), and sets the timer to the tick-rate word (60) only when the timer is negative (idle); a running timer is not extended. The use does not start either effect. Each tick a positive timer drops by 1; on the tick it reaches 0 BOTH effects start (the main effect with the particle link of item 5, the second effect without it) and all ten slots of the object's own list are freed; on the next tick a timer of 0 becomes -1 (idle). The object's list count-down of item 3 runs in the same tick, before the hit walks. The object is active while the timer is 0 or above, and afterwards while its main effect's linked particle exists.
5. **Hit rule of the item-effect object (High for the structure, Medium-High for the geometry).** Each active tick, after the count-down, the object walks the global character list and then the breakables of the grid cell that holds its stored point. Its shape is a vertical cylinder of height 4.0 centred on the stored point (the shared family A test of SPEC-0036: horizontal centre distance at most the sum of the radii, vertical intervals touching or overlapping, the target taking its feet to feet + height). Its radius is the size of the linked particle, 0 while no particle is linked: so 0 during the 60 timer ticks, then 0.5 + 14.5 x age / 0.7 (age in seconds; the main effect is a single burst of 3 particles with life 0.7 s, the same emitter as the Tor Water particle), ending when age + 1/60 reaches 0.7 (ages 0 to 40, 41 ticks). Eligible characters that overlap and are not listed are entered in the object's own ten-slot list (item 2: first free slot, timer 60) and then, when the character's spawn-record key is one of the Minion-corps keys of SPEC-0021 (2801 to 2807, 2811 to 2817, ... 2871 to 2877), its take-hit method receives damage 100, attacker -1, blocked flag 0, and the arguments 2 and 0; no other character is damaged. The 60-tick slot timer outlasts the active burst, so each target is hit at most once per use (the list is cleared when the effects start). Overlapping intact breakables of any element are broken as in Behavior 16 (the object's element field is 7). Test vector 179 gives a worked example.

### 28. Character-attached effect definitions (Medium-High)

Confidence: High that the known binders use the runtime effect-definition registry; Medium-High for the caller split. SPEC-0040 owns the per-character state controller and attachment positions. SPEC-0034 owns the effect definitions and instances.

These paths look up integer keys in registry group 3, copy a found definition into an effect instance, then position and update that instance. They do not establish archive model/resource bindings. The ordinary character state controller is called by both the hero updater and the shared region-character updater. Its cooldown and state checks select which keyed handler runs; several states have no effect handler.

1. The state controller uses keys 190, 191, 192, 200, 201, 202, 203, 204 and 205. Most instances use the character position at record offset 0x15E0. Key 202 uses three positions: 0x15E0, 0x1420 and 0x1460. Key 190 adds a fixed position offset; key 204 also makes an additional sound or effect call. A refresh path creates, positions, updates and releases key 205. The exact fixed offsets and visible appearance are unresolved.
2. A hero-specific updater can request key 129 or 188 according to a selector, using the hero position. Another hero state requests key 2010 at a supplied position; when its flag is set, its height adjustment uses an 8.0 distance test.
3. A region-character setup path looks up keys 400 and 404 when its setup lookup is empty, initializes two effect slots from them and clears their active flags.

Keys 129, 188 and 2010 are effect-definition keys on these paths, not display-model resource IDs. Whether each key resolves in every active region, and what each resolved definition renders, remain unknown. No test vectors: the exact attachment offsets were not worked out.

### 29. Weapon-drawn and shield-raised flags; held items of other characters

Confidence: Medium-High for the writers; Medium for the meanings; High for the other characters' draw.

The two flags live on the shared party inventory, so both heroes see the same pair. The weapon-drawn flag selects whether the hero's weapon is drawn in the hand or sheathed, and the shield-raised flag whether the shield is on the arm or stowed (SPEC-0046 and SPEC-0021 hold the display rules). Setting the weapon-drawn flag to a different value plays a sound on the focus hero's sound handle, one variant for drawing and another for sheathing. The shield flag has no sound.

Writers:
1. **Set both to 1:** an attack start (Roberta's wand attack, the attack-state handlers and the action-machine sub-kind 8); the L1 guard when the hero has a shield and no cloak (it also requests state 20); the interaction-chooser branches that start an attack-like action; a character-runtime action that also clears the held item; and the showcase of a weapon or a shield (SPEC-0021).
2. **Take hit:** when weapon-drawn is 0 and the hit's attacker id is not -2, the take-hit sets weapon-drawn to 1 when Tor's weapon entry (hero index 0) is not empty, whichever hero is hit, and sets shield-raised to 1 when the hero has a shield (Medium; Edge cases).
3. **Square with nothing to interact with** toggles: with weapon-drawn 0 it requests state 22 (or state 1 when the hero is already in state 22, 39 or 40); with weapon-drawn 1 it clears both flags, unless a hero method blocks it.
