4. **Clear both:** setting state 25 or 28; state 27 sub-mode 0 (which also sets held item 36 on entry); the state 37 and 38 hand-offs; the in-air ledge-grab chooser's no-target branch; a record action; the talk, buy and other interaction branches; the hero swap task (SPEC-0053); and equipping the cloak (item 126), which also hides all held items. Taking the cloak off also goes through the cloak swap: starting the attack step (state 13), or taking a hit, while the cloak is worn runs a removal task (white fade, one-shot cue 0xB1, swap, fade back, then state 1); see SPEC-0021 "Tattered Cloak".
5. **Clear the weapon flag only:** the script hero switch.
6. **SetHeroEquippedState(b)** (SPEC-0030): sets both flags to b.
7. **Threat-in-front scan:** it runs only while weapon-drawn is set and the hero has a shield, and sets the hero's threat flag and its 40-tick timer. Its own write of 1 to both flags (the item manager's music cue and its field 0x18) is unreachable, because it needs the variant flag to be 0 while the scan only runs when that flag is non-zero, so the scan never changes the flags; only the 40-tick timer is raised (High, confirmed by a full reading). The scan walks the ten projectile slots (live and not kind 9; unit horizontal direction dotted with the body forward above 0.5) and then every active character within 10.0 whose attack-active query is true and whose horizontal direction has a dot above 0.5; when the threat flag was already raised by something else, the timer is raised without the scan.

Body items have no held model; they select the outfit (SPEC-0021).

**Other characters (High).** Petpets and world characters have no weapon, shield or sheath logic. Each active one with a render object draws only the current held entry of its own item holder, at its attach point 0 with the same axis swap as the heroes, when that entry's id has a registry model, with no other gate and no scale. Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) against the held-attachment routine: for each active slot (counting down from the last) with its flag 1 and a drawable object whose anim object exists, the routine asks the object's item holder (class slot +0x64) for the held entry, converts the entry's id to a registry range (a held id that maps to nothing draws nothing), builds the held frame from attach matrix 0 with the hero's axis swap (rows: attach Z axis row, attach Y row, minus attach X row, attach position row), sets the draw anchor's offset to (the view point argument minus the object's drawn position at +0x18B0 of the render object, the same offset the hero uses), binds the shared material list (the game material list group 3) and submits the frame; the burst-emitter update of the same loop adds its result to the returned total. A thief that has stolen an item also draws that item's model at the same point scaled by 0.45, while its stolen-item flag is set and its countdown is above 0. The countdown starts at 3 × the frame-rate word (180) when the item is stolen and drops by 1 per draw, so the item shows for 180 draws (3 s at one draw per tick); at 0 the flag clears.

### 30. Character helper routines read in full (new 2026-11-09; High unless marked)

- **Start placement of a pet / hero (full reset form).** Motion body init (weight, radius, height); the model light hook; the chase camera pose: eye = start position − start facing × 10 + world up × 2.5, side = normalise(world up × start facing), the camera set-pose call (eye, side, up, facing) and set-position call (eye); when the character has a pad and a pet exists, the pet's world id = the animation set index and the pet's placement reset runs. The death-end mode word (+0x1564): 1 gives state 0x3B, 2 gives state 0x3C, otherwise, unless already in 0x3B, the default state and its reset call; then the death-end mode = 0, speed scale = 1.0 and the hit list cleared; a full reset also restores maximum HP, clears the three item slots, sets line mode 0, pending and current target −1 and the grab cooldown (+0xA04) to a boot constant.
- **Default state of a character record.** Class table = default, all ids −1, motion, model, locomotion, grid, ground-entry and platform pointers 0, action state 1, speed scale 1.0, +0x7C0 = 20.0, ground height = the negated boot "no ground" constant, flags cleared, vectors +0x1C0 and +0x1E0 = (0, 0, 0, 1), hero flag +0x3D0 = 1, hero sound slots cleared, alive flag 1, three words +0x380 to +0x388 = 0.
- **Move state reset.** Plays the footstep cue, clears the air-attack flags, sets the motion's gravity gain to 1.0 and no-gravity to 0, zeroes the velocity's x and z; when the character stands on a platform (+0x190) the velocity's y = (platform translation change y ÷ the physics block word 1) × 1.05.
- **Root-motion sync.** Clears +0x174 and +0x178; with a model: orientation block at model +0x1880 = side (facing × up), up (0, 1, 0, 0) at +0x1890, facing at +0x18A0 (w = 0), position at +0x18B0 (w = 1); root matrix at +0x290 = identity; layered clip restarted; owner snapshot; flag +0x4000 = 1 (the model's pose-ready word). Then the motion body init, the motion's first word = 1 and the class hook +0x10C (position, facing).
- **Inventory item use.** Needs the item manager (+0x168) to give an equipped entry that is not empty; a consume test (item id, 1, 1, 0) through class slot +0x274; if accepted the manager gives the item back (item id, 1); when the item id equals the highlighted item (+0x155C) and the entry's stock (+0x60) is 0, the marker is cleared; the consume result is returned.
- **Anchor point and climb plane.** Anchor = the first vector of the model's bounds block + the motion position, with x += 0.5 × bounds +0x10 and y += 0.25 × bounds +0x14. The climb-plane snap (called "heading from the camera" in the old routine name; it uses no camera): plane at the climb target +0x10 (normal xyz, offset w); d = dot4(position, plane); when d ≠ the radius the body moves by normal × (radius − d); heading = −plane; placement reset.
- **Stomp test.** True when the hero is in a movement-busy state and falling (motion velocity y < 0), a flat-ended cylinder test (the shot-against-cylinder routine of SPEC-0018; corrected from "capsule" 2026-11-09) against the object at the parameter +0x30 (radius, height, object's w, up (0, 1, 0, 0)) succeeds, and (object y + object w) − 1.5 < the hero's y (Medium for the name and use).
- **Stagger.** While no-gravity is set: clip 0x3A moves the body 0.075 backwards per tick along the forward axis while the track is below 99%, and at 99% or more sets the idle state (1), runs class slot +0x254 (0, −1, −1) and rebuilds the facing basis with the reversed forward; clip 0x48 moves the body 1.0 backwards once, then the same idle state and class call.
- **Ledge sub-mode and strafe-family tests.** The strafe-family states are 0x20, 0x2D, 0x2C, 0x2B and 0x2E, and 0x11 and 0x14 only when the previous state (+0x7FC) is 0x20. The ledge sub-mode is active when the action state is 0x1E and the ledge sub-mode (+0x9AC) is 0, 1 or 2.
- **Moved-this-tick test and sub-entry test.** The character has moved this tick when the length of (motion position − the tick-start position at +0x110) is above 0. A model has a positive hurt-capsule sub-entry when any of its bone records (0x50 bytes, count at +0x38 of the set) has a positive float at +0x40.
- **Petpet idle-follow scatter point and ground-height test.** The scatter point uses the character position + (dx, 0, dz) (Behavior 26 item 11); the near-ground test is true when |y(position of the other) − the ground height probed from 8.0 above the position| < 2.0.
- **Distance and facing helpers (dormant in the shipped call graph).** The leash-style test takes an outer range A and an inner range B: D1 = unit(target − self) with the 3D distance in lane w; D2 = the same from the start position (+0x3E0); if D2.w < B the moving-toward test = (target − the target's tick-start position) · D1 ≤ 0, a latch toggles with a counter (counter > 0 ? counter − 1 : 20) and −1 is returned while the latch is 0; otherwise −1 unless D2.w < A; then a ground triangle must exist for the target (else −1) and 1 is returned when D1.w < 5.0, else 0. The face-the-target helper aims at the flat unit vector from "self position + 1.5 × the flat direction" to the target and returns its length.

## Edge cases and error handling

- **Repeated death call** (reproduce it, Medium): the damage function does not check for an existing zero HP. A damage of any size when HP is already 0 takes the "damage at or above HP" branch, so the death hook runs again. A caller guard may prevent this in practice; not confirmed. For heroes, the death hook on a repeated death skips the gate (so the callback is not started again) and runs the default steps again (SPEC-0036, Death).
- **Zero damage**: damage 0 is applied (HP unchanged) and the damage-applied notification still runs.
- **Overheal**: heal above max HP clamps to max HP.
- **Negative or out-of-range skeleton indices**: the main family treats -1 as none. Index values above 7 are errors in the original and must be treated as invalid data. The main-family values -5, -6 and -7 select the Roberta main set, the Tor main set and the shared townsfolk set (Pools and caps). A clip request whose main clip index is at or above the bound main set's clip count (an unsigned compare) starts no main clip, and the clip-length query returns 0 for it. Resources 1278 and 1279 hold 16 records with value -6 whose main indices are all 924 to 1,082 against the Tor main set's 161 clips, so every main request of those records starts nothing; their sub-part and face families play normally (SPEC-0005, Body 526).
- **Pool reuse**: a torn-down record is reused without clearing fields that spawn does not write. Clean-side code should write every field in creation.
- **Status clear and regeneration**: Resolved. The trickle regeneration gate tests the poison status only (section 6, Health and the second pool), so clearing or expiring any other status never changes it. A status that is active has a timer of 1 or more; expired (0) and cleared (-1) both read as inactive.
- **Missing main skeleton**: a character with no main skeleton (index -1) is still built: the main block stays empty, the sub and face blocks attach by their own indices, and the initial-pose pass is skipped. Whether such a record draws anything beyond its other blocks needs a running game. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): build it with an empty main block, draw only its other blocks, and do not assume the main block draws.
- **Block with footprint patch zero**: does not occur on disc, since every placement writes the patch (SPEC-0048, Push start, step 2; SPEC-0033, Behavior 15A, step 5). The skip path (no list test, no height step) is needed only for other data.
- **Character variant with an other character's record inactive**: the record is skipped; its block is not copied.
- **Other-character copy**: the first position copy is dead (overwritten by the second). Reproduce the final value only.
- **Request record reuse**: a record whose active flag is still 1 from an earlier tick is not free; the writer must not overwrite it.
- **Placement velocity**: a placed ball record starts with a vertical velocity of 1.0 (Behavior 13, item 5). Do not treat a negative velocity as a "not placed" marker; the ground placement routine does not read the vertical velocity at all (Resolved, Open question 29; High).
- **Tracked points**: a mode word other than 0 or 1 runs the initialiser of Behavior 8b, not an error. The dispatcher has no caller in the shipped build, so the mode word has no effect there (8b, dormant). A zero-length direction or blend has no known guard.
- **Turn-hold flag set**: the heading solve of Behavior 8b leaves the heading unchanged.
- **Ring slot without advance**: every 60-tick sample writes the same slot, so the ring holds only its latest sample. Possible original quirk (Open questions).
- **Non-hero motion records**: the height follow is skipped; the step and the position sample still run.
- **AI speed writes can affect the current update's playback rate (Medium-High):** class-coroutine routines write P inside the NPC update after its reset and status factors but before the current clip is re-timed and before a requested clip starts in the animation pass. The next NPC update resets P and reapplies statuses before the next re-time. Confirm the ordering for each specific AI caller before treating every attack or boss speed write as effective (Behavior 3; Behavior 3E).
- **Base state 42 keeps the start speed**: a character that stays in base state 42 (in play, only the Gelert Assassin's waiting mirror images) has its clip speed set only when the clip starts, so a status that changes P later does not re-time the clip already playing; the next clip started picks it up (Behavior 3E).
- **Facing kept in base states 16, 33, 51 and 55**: a non-hero character in one of these states keeps its facing, so a move direction more than 90 degrees from it zeroes the step (Behavior 14D item 4).
- **Blocked move versus contact push**: a move that a move check refuses leaves the character where it is. A character already overlapping a primitive of its grid cell is pushed out by the character contacts of the motion-record driver (Behavior 11), not by the refused move.
- **Breakable with a registered script hook**: the script callback starts (unless already running); no break and no cue (Behavior 16, step a).
- **Breakable volume of an unlisted kind**: kind 2 is never hit; an entry whose volume kind is not 1, 2, 3, 4 or 7 has no known test; treat it as not hit (Low).
- **Material values for the contact cue**: 0, 1 and every value above 4 all give cue 144. There is no special case for them. Material 2 (cue 143) does not occur on disc.
- **Intact state-3 breakable hit by a matching attack**: the cue plays but the entry does not break (Behavior 16, step d).
- **Hit list full**: an eleventh add overwrites slot 0 in the base rule (Behavior 12A).
- **Monster weapon of zero size**: a point at the anchor; it hits only when the point lies inside the bone's solid round cylinder (the containment rule of SPEC-0018; Medium-High; Behavior 12A).
- **Bone table with no capsule**: a character whose bone records all have a length of 0 or below cannot be struck by melee or shots (Behavior 12A).
- **Beam life of N ticks**: a beam started with life N runs its walks on N − 1 ticks, because each tick lowers the life before it tests it (Behavior 17). With N = 300 that is 299 ticks. Reproduce it.
- **Second release during a beam**: the start does nothing while the beam is active, so the life is not refreshed (Behavior 17).
- **Beam hit list full**: an eleventh distinct target inside the 60-tick window is not hit, and a debug message prints (Behavior 17).
- **Rock meets two characters in one tick**: only the first in list order is hit, and the rock ends (Behavior 17).
- **Move goal with no ground value**: the goal routine has no special case for a probe that finds no ground; it uses whatever value the ground probe returns (SPEC-0018; Behavior 21).
- **Emote queue full**: a new entry drops the oldest of the four entries (Behavior 22). A zero-length entry plays nothing.
- **Stale emotes** (reproduce; Medium): hero emotes queued while the hero is not in state 25 are not cleared; up to the four newest play at the next level message that puts the hero into state 25 (Behavior 22).
- **Body emote on a seated or lying NPC**: with AI mode 10 to 12, or main id 65 to 74, 78, 97, 98, 146 or 147, the body queue is not consumed and no body clip plays; the face path still runs (Behavior 22).
- **Refused emote clip**: a clip refused when its emote starts is never retried; the gesture plays without it (Behavior 22).
- **Progress runs ahead** (reproduce): progress adds the stored fraction on top of the cursor, so a 0.99 gate can pass up to one frame early (Behavior 22B).
- **Aim rule with opposite directions**: when dot(c, t) is below −0.99 the 0.1 side nudge keeps the blend from vanishing, so the turn has a defined side (Behavior 8).
- **Villager pair radius 0.0**: the pair search needs a horizontal distance strictly below the row's pair radius, which is 0.0 on every townsfolk row, so no pair forms on disc (Behavior 3B).
- **Dead test, second arm**: an NPC whose AI mode word is 16 is dead only while its counter at +0xD14 is above 0 (Behavior 2, item 6).
- **Released world**: its bound characters are deactivated without writing their positions back, so they start from their disc points on the next load (Behavior 2, item 6).
- **Hero in a script-held state**: states 33, 48, 50, 51, 52, 53 and 55, a stun and a moving screen effect make the hero busy for the game code and occupied for scripts alike, because both use one routine (Behavior 15.2).
- **Rock exactly at the travel limit**: a flat travel total of exactly 250.0 keeps the rock live; the next tick with any movement ends it (High; the test is strict).
- **Flag 13 set with a state outside 2 to 12**: the attack-state answer is not confirmed for this case (Behavior 18).
- **Reaction cooldown inside one scan**: the cooldown is checked only at the start of the reaction scan. Within one scan, several valid slots can each start reactions in the same tick, and each reaction resets the cooldown. Reproduce it; confirm with a black-box test.
- **Reaction draw boundary**: a draw equal to the percent does not start the reaction (the test is strict). A percent of 0 never starts; a percent of 100 starts on every draw of 1 to 99 (probability 99 in 100).
- **Target pick with more than 10 candidates**: only the first 10 in list order are considered, so a later eligible character cannot be picked in that pick. Reproduce it.
- **Recovery window after the attack ends**: the recovery draw is a whole number of ticks (30 to 45, or 120 to 240). A release that arrives after the attack has ended does not fire (Behavior 15.1).
- **Friction on landing** (quirk to keep): friction acts along the predicted velocity including its vertical part, so a record loses 0.64 of its fall speed on its first grounded tick (Behavior 14A, item 5c).
- **Edge guard skips the deadly-ground test**: on a tick when the edge guard of Behavior 14E fires, the deadly-ground and ground-record tests of the pre-step do not run for that character.
- **Hero wall-contact gate**: in hero states 21, 23, 30, 35, 37, 38, 41 and 47 the hero skips its per-record wall contact (Behavior 19; SPEC-0018). There is no per-element step, no divisor and no ground snap to reproduce.
- **Take-hit draws Tor's weapon** (reproduce; Medium): the take-hit's weapon-drawn write tests hero index 0's weapon entry, so a hit on Roberta draws the shared weapon flag when Tor has a weapon, and leaves it at 0 when Tor has none even if Roberta holds her wand (Behavior 29).
- **Candidate at exactly 20.0**: a candidate whose distance is exactly 20.0 is not in range (Behavior 20, item 3). Reproduce it.
- **Lock with a candidate leaving the list**: the locked character is removed in the refresh, before the lock choice, so the next in-range candidate takes the lock in the same tick (Behavior 20, items 2 and 5).
- **Candidates within 1.0 of each other** (reproduce; Medium): the ordering pass swaps them every tick, so the lock alternates between them (Behavior 20, item 4).
- **Never-engaged candidates**: a foe the hero has not attacked while facing it is in the list but never in range and never locked, however close (Behavior 20, item 3).
- **State 30 for a hero**: a hero in state 30 is busy (Behavior 15.2) and has a route whose routine is not described here. The clean side must not invent its behaviour.
- **Characters in state 16 or 42**: never added to the hero's candidate list, and removed from it when they enter either state (Behavior 20, items 1 and 2).
- **Hero busy with the global input lock on**: busy regardless of the state (Behavior 15.2). The candidate list refresh, order and lock choice keep running in that case; only a fresh engagement cannot form (Behavior 20).
- **Strafe weight at exactly 0.9**: the test is "at most 0.9", so a dot of exactly 0.9 keeps w = 0.1 (Behavior 8a).
- **Degenerate steering blend**: if the blend or the target has zero length, the normalisation divides by zero. No guard exists, and the result is settled (Resolved, Open question 79): the console's vector divide returns the largest finite number for a zero divisor, and zero times that is zero, so a zero-length vector stays the zero vector and never becomes a not-a-number (High).
- **Hero route 51 or 55 with the flag at +0x3D0**: the exit depends on that flag, not on the animation (Behavior 15.3).
- **State 33 with no code set**: the request uses whatever code the input word holds. The word is the default idle animation (Behavior 15.3, state 25): 0 at hero set-up and after a talk, or the id a script last stored with SetDefaultHeroIdleAnim.
- **Timed charge with an armed counter of zero and a step not below R**: only the counter changes; the sub-state and the kind are unchanged (Behavior 15.1a).
- **Timed charge with the run-time gate at zero**: no timing runs; the set-up step runs on each call that reaches it (Behavior 15.1a item 4).
- **Idle look with the flag clear**: the counter does not run. With the flag set and the counter at exactly 60, no look runs; above 60 it does (Behavior 23).
- **Action-end resolver with the flag word nonzero**: the hero goes to state 1, not 33 (Behavior 15.4).
- **Character table reset with a slot not in use**: its class clear method is not called (Behavior 2A).
- **Contact walk with a zero shape value** (kinds 4 and 7): a zero value takes the zero-radius contact, the same as a negative value (Behavior 14A, item 2).
- **Motion step with a zero-length step vector**: the length comparison is applied as written; no guard for zero length is known (Low).
- **Driver branches by class**: region characters (branch 1) never test frame records, cell list A or the object walls; only cell list B, the navmesh confinement and the character push keep them out of the world, so a region character off any navmesh has no world wall except cell list B. The petpet (branch 2) runs no cell list B walk (Behavior 14A, item 1).
- **Character with chain mode −1** (every character but Gnarfas, Tor and Roberta): no secondary step runs (Behavior 25).
- **Player buffers**: the three per-entry buffers of the animation player are sized from the config count at construction only (Behavior 25, Defaults).
- **Last link seed**: the last link's next joint is −1, so its seed points B and C are the 16 bytes just before the main joint matrix buffer. They only shape the first tick (Behavior 25, Setup and seed quirk).
- **Point light between 0.9 r and r**: the point light takes no slot, and the directional light it would displace is faded to (d − 0.9 r) / (0.1 r) of its strength and locked for that walk (Behavior 24).
- **Palette push with buffer index k (0 or 1)**: only that buffer's 15 slot records are written; −1 writes both (Behavior 26, item 4).
- **Debug colour tuner**: the run-time table at 0x32CE44, its apply and the index word at 0x2D759C never run in play; do not implement them (Behavior 26, item 7).
- **Preset turns w0 black**: every preset load sets colour word 0 to 0x00000000, where the disc value is white. Reproduce it (Behavior 26, item 2).
- **Neck freeze**: the serpent neck angles change only by the 0.01 snap, so a target that moves fast freezes the neck until a tick without a request (Behavior 23A).
- **Colour word 14**: it is white in every preset, so selecting a different preset does not change it (Behavior 26, item 2; High).
- **Insert into a full timed list**: refused; no slot is overwritten and no timer is set (Behavior 27, item 2).
- **Re-arm with a zero timer**: left at zero, so the 60-tick timer is not started (Behavior 27, item 4).
- **Pool drift with a zero last move** (dormant pool): the drift reduces to the draw offsets and the 0.005 lift (Behavior 25A).
- **Sample ring with a count of zero**: not described; treat as open and do not index with it (Behavior 14B).
- **Mote reflect needs the shield block**: a matching shield Mote does nothing unless the shield block of that hit passes first; a projectile from behind the hero is a normal hit whatever Mote is worn (Behavior 18A).
- **Surface normal at or above 0.999**: the blend of Behavior 9, item 7 does not run; the replacement is not described.
- **Attachment selector outside 0 to 6**: not described; do not assume an effect id.
- **Held request with equal priority**: while the hold word is set, a second request with the same priority as the pending one is rejected, because it is not strictly greater (Behavior 15A).
- **Request for the class default while a higher priority holds**: a negative state code has priority 1, so it is ignored whenever the applied priority is 2 or more (Behavior 15A).
- **Target moved exactly 1.0**: the cached index is kept, because the test is "more than 1.0" (Behavior 21A).
- **First tick of a push**: the hero does not move on the first tick of a stick push, and moves one more step on the tick the stick is released, because the travel uses the root step written on the previous tick (Behavior 14C). Reproduce it.
- **Facing parallel to up**: when the cross product of (0, 1, 0) and the facing is shorter than 0.01, B1 is (1, 0, 0) (Behavior 14C, item 5).
- **Hero state 53**: the basis is not rebuilt and the travel step is zero (Behavior 14C).
- **Slope**: on ground tilted by angle a along the travel direction, the projected step is |D| cos a, so each step on a slope is shorter than on flat ground (Behavior 14C, item 7).
- **Requested id with no clip**: main clip 0 of the bound set starts (not the old clip, and not id 0), unless clip 0 already plays; the sub-part and face families behave the same (Behaviors 3C and 3D). Reproduce it.
- **Held one-shot with non-zero speed**: its root step doubles to 2 × root(F − 1) × c on every held tick (Behavior 14C, item 1). Reproduce it.
- **Face override at its end**: once the face record holds the override's clip at progress 0.99 or more, the override is cleared to -1 and the main id's face clip starts in the same tick (Behavior 3D, step 6).
- **Set HP below 0**: the set accessor stores the value with no lower clamp and runs the damage-applied notification, not the death hook (Behavior 6). Reproduce it.

## Test vectors

Synthetic, hand-checkable:

1. Damage: HP 50, damage 20 -> HP 30; damage-applied notification runs once; no death.
2. Damage: HP 30, damage 30 -> HP 0; death hook runs with its two flags set.
3. Damage: HP 30, damage 0 -> HP 30; damage-applied notification still runs.
4. Heal: max HP 150, HP 140, heal 25 -> HP 150 (clamped).
5. Second pool: MP 10, spend 3 -> MP 7. MP 10, spend 12 -> MP 0 and the empty hook runs. MP set 200 with max 150 -> MP 150.
6. Statuses: timers [0, 1, -1, 5] -> active flags [off, on, off, on]. Clear all -> [-1, -1, -1, -1].
7. Ground: a probe result of -8.9e8 counts as a hit; -9.0e8 is a miss; -1.0e9 is a miss.
8. Facing: a tick that sets facing to (0, 0, 1) followed by a tick that sets (1, 0, 0) shows (1, 0, 0) at the end of the second tick (no smoothing).
9. Motion-record leash (Medium-Low for the sign): radius 5, target 8 units from the anchor after adding the scaled root -> target moved to 5 units from the anchor; the step returns 8.
10. Root sign: a root vector (2, 0, 0) appears as (-2, 0, 0) in the motion copy and the canonical group. The group is a rendering input only (Behavior 10 item 2), so this sign changes no position; character travel comes only from the layer 0 root steps (High).
11. Held-item draw record (Behavior 12): hero slot index 1 uses the record at list base + 112; its active flag is at list base + 112 + 80 and its frame at list base + 112 + 16. A record with item count 0, or with a model word of 0, is not drawn and adds 0 to the draw count.
12. Footprint move (synthetic): block with a non-zero footprint patch, ahead point passes the list test, second point fails it, ground heights 2.0 under the block and 2.8 at the second point -> blocked (difference 0.8). With 2.7 -> allowed (difference 0.7).
13. Character variant with move flag M nonzero: another active character's box is tested only when M is zero; with M nonzero the move is not blocked by the box test.
14. Placement reset: a motion record placed at world position (3, 9.3, -2) with passed vector (0, 0, 1). Afterwards the transform position is (3, 9.3, -2), fields +0x120 and +0x2C0 contain the passed vector, the displacement words at +0x44 and +0x4C are zero, and the vertical velocity is 1.0 (Behavior 13).
15. Motion sample ring: a record with its ring slot at 3 and samples due at ticks 60, 120 and 180. Without an advance, all three write slot 3. If the slot index were advanced by one each sample (not confirmed), they would write slots 3, 4 and 0.
16. State numbers: a hero in state 30 is busy (Behavior 15.2) and has a route whose routine this spec does not describe. A character in state 30 must not be mapped to any hero or enemy action by the clean side. A hero in state 31 is not busy by the list, and a hero in state 31 with the global input lock on is busy.
17. Blocked move and camera push (synthetic): a move check that refuses a move leaves the character at its start point, with no displacement. In the follow camera's eye pass (not a character step), an eye point whose nearest face depth inside a box's grown region is 0.5 is pushed out by depth + push radius = 0.5 + 1.25 = 1.75 along the face's outward normal, by the push-distance rule of SPEC-0018.
18. Camera push, sphere-style check (synthetic, from SPEC-0018): a ball of radius 2.0 at the origin and a point at (1.0, 0.5, 0) with push radius 1.25 is moved to (2.906889, 1.453444, 0), a distance of 3.25 from the centre. This checks the arithmetic only; the camera eye pass is not known to test spheres as primitives.
19. Shot start (synthetic): the owner's reference point at (0, 0, 0), aim direction (0, 0, 1), start offset 20.0 -> the shot starts at (0, 0, 20).
20. Rock travel, flat step of 50.0 per tick (synthetic): the travel total after ticks 1 to 5 is 50, 100, 150, 200 and 250; the rock is live at each of those. At tick 6 the total is 300, which exceeds 250.0, so the rock ends. A rock at the middle speed 0.6 units per tick moving straight reaches the limit after 417 ticks (416 × 0.6 = 249.6, 417 × 0.6 = 250.2), unless a bounce ends it first.
21. Shot travel, step of 40.0 per tick (synthetic): the total is 240 after tick 6 (live); at tick 7 it is 280, so the shot ends on tick 7.
22. Shot count (synthetic): a starting count of 3. The first launch leaves 2 and creates a shot; the second leaves 1 and creates a shot; the third leaves 0, which is below 1, so no shot is created and the launch ends. Two shots in total.
23. Breakable contact (synthetic): attacker reference (0, 0, 0), a state-1 breakable at (1, 0, 0) with element 0, no script hook and material 3 -> the break runs with a piece push of 0.25 along (1, 0, 0) and cue 145 plays at (1, 0, 0); no damage is applied. The same contact with material 9 plays cue 144. A state-3 breakable with material 0 plays cue 144 and does not break.
24. Busy test (synthetic): no status flag set and all three other queries return 0 -> not busy. Flag 40 set -> busy.
25. Attack-state test (synthetic): flag 13 clear, flag 9 set and the extra value word 8 -> attack state. The same with the extra value word 7 -> not an attack state.
26. Percent reaction (synthetic): enemy defence hold 0, outer sub-state 1, T = 30, dodge chance 40, block chance 40. A hero shot at (0, 1, 10) with velocity (0, 0, -0.5) per tick gives the path from (0, 1, 10) to (0, 1, -5); it passes through the enemy's body shape of radius 1 centred at (0, 1, 0). Draw 37: outer sub-state 6 (37 is below 40) and the hold becomes 30. Draw 40: no block (40 is not below 40). With the same shot and velocity (0, 0, -0.2), the path ends at (0, 1, 4) 4 units from the shape's centre, so it misses and no draw is made.
27. Timed release (synthetic): the fired flag is clear, the release event fires during the attack state: one projectile launches and the flag is set. Later the attack-ended test turns true and the recovery draw is 33: the recovery lasts 33 ticks (0.55 s). In the 2 to 4 s routine a draw of 150 gives 2.5 s.
28. Aim point (synthetic): owner at (0, 0, 0), aim direction (0, 0, 1), no target set. Aim point = (0, 0, 20.0).
29. Target pick (synthetic): candidates A, B and C pass the tests, D is 70 units away and fails the radius. A draw of 2 picks C. With no candidate passing the result is none (-1).
30. Spread (synthetic): owner off the player's axis (dot 0.5, below 0.85) adds 5.0; a spread draw of 0.5 with S = 1.0 adds 0.5; owner id 17 has nonzero attack-manager hold flag and adds 3.0. Total magnitude 8.5. With the horizontal direction (1, 0, 0) the offset is (8.5, 0, 0). If id 17 is absent or its hold flag is zero, the total is 5.5.
31. Reach (synthetic): owner validity passes; primary query returns hit -> true. If primary misses, distinct prepared endpoints and secondary-sweep hit -> true. If primary misses and endpoints are equal, or the secondary sweep misses, -> false. The primary query's scalar and direction outputs do not affect these outcomes.
32. Body size (synthetic, Layout): a hero placed by the player selector gets weight 5.5, radius 1.175 and height 3.5 in its motion record; a later re-placement (respawn or teleport) leaves the three words unchanged. A region character bound to a monster row with radius 4.0, height 7.0 and weight 5.5 gets those three values.
33. Driver branch (synthetic, Behavior 14A item 1): a hero (owner query 0) runs the frame records, cell list A, cell list B, the object walls, the pending shapes and the character push; a townsfolk or enemy (query 1) runs the navmesh confinement, the character push and cell list B, and no frame record or cell list A entry; the petpet (query 2) runs its own frame-record step and cell list A.
34. State-18 move-check size (synthetic, Behavior 11): a movable-block record whose +0x68 points to a type-11 source entry with +0x10 = 2.0 uses size 2.0; in the footprint variant with distance 1.0 the ahead point is position + direction × (1.0 + 0.5 × 2.0 + 0.1) = position + 2.1 × direction. The fixed actor-box margin remains 0.25.
35. Candidate range (synthetic): candidates at distances 19.9, 20.0 and 35.0. Only the 19.9 candidate is in range (the test is strict), so the lock is that candidate. With candidates at 12.0 and 7.0 in range, the lock is the one at 7.0.
36. Lock hand-over (synthetic, Behavior 20): the locked character leaves the list on a tick, and another engaged candidate at distance 5.0 is still in the list. The removal runs in the refresh, so the 5.0 candidate is the lock on that same tick.
37. Flyer goal, Can Fly 1 (synthetic, Behavior 21): Max Fly Height 10, hero at height 0 and 30 units away horizontally (with a height difference of 40, which is ignored), ground 3.0 under the goal, ambush word 1: y = 0 + min(10, 6) = 6.0, above 3.0 + 2.0, so the goal height is 6.0. With the hero 10 units away: y = 2.0, raised to 5.0. With the ambush word 0 and home height 1.0: the goal height is 1.0.

38. Steering, backward mode (synthetic): mode 3, caller weight 0.3, move direction (1, 0, 0), stored facing (0, 0, 1). The target is (-1, 0, 0). The blend is (-0.3, 0, 0.7), length 0.761577, so the new facing is (-0.393919, 0, 0.919145).
39. Steering, strafe mode 5 (synthetic; the perpendicular target is taken as given): body forward (1, 0, 0), target (0, 0, 1), stored facing (1, 0, 0). The dot is 0, so w = 0.1. The blend is (0.9, 0, 0.1), length 0.905539, so the new facing is (0.993884, 0, 0.110432).
40. Strafe snap (synthetic): the same target (0, 0, 1) with body forward (0, 0, 1). The dot is 1.0, above 0.9, so w = 1.0 and the new facing is (0, 0, 1), whatever the stored facing (1, 0, 0).
41. Goal-move with the boot weight (synthetic): an unlisted mode, move direction (0, 0, 1), stored facing (1, 0, 0), w = 0.1. The new facing is (0.993884, 0, 0.110432), the same as vector 39 by construction.
42. Timed charge, armed, first call (synthetic): kind 7, sub-state 2, counter 0, step 1, timing helper reads R = 5, reset then reads 4. The sub-state becomes 3 and the counter becomes 1; the kind stays 7.
43. Timed charge, armed, later call (synthetic): kind 7, sub-state 2, counter 3, step 2. The sub-state becomes 3; the counter stays 3 and the timer is unchanged.
44. Hero state 51 (synthetic): progress 0.995 and flag +0x3D0 equal to 0 gives state 33. The same progress with the flag at 2 gives state 1. Progress 0.98 gives no change.
45. Hero state 12 (synthetic): ten target slots holding 3, 0, 9 and seven other values. After the routine all ten are -1 and the state stays 12.

47. Player-slot strides (synthetic): for slot 2, the locomotion sub-object is at the table base plus 2 × 0x1BA0 (14144 bytes), the motion record at the base plus 2 × 0x760 (3776 bytes), and the locomotion block at the base plus 2 × 0x50 (160 bytes).
48. Action-end resolver (synthetic): flag word +0x3D0 = 0 gives state 33; flag = 1 gives state 1.
49. Idle look (synthetic): flag set, counter 61: the look helper is called with 180 (after the counter is above 60). Counter 60: no look.

46. World pool record set-up (synthetic; the pool is never filled in play): frame R0 = (1, 0, 0), R1 = (0, 1, 0), R2 = (0, 0, 1), P = (10, -20, 5). After the set-up +0x154 reads 1.75, +0x15C reads -1, +0x100 and +0x104 read 8.0, and the corners are C0 = (6, -20, 1), C1 = (6, -20, 9), C2 = (14, -20, 1) and C3 = (6, -28, 1); the face plane through C0, C2 and C1 is the plane y = -20. With script flag 0 set, after 100 world ticks P is (10, -19.0, 5) (within float rounding); after about 500 ticks the second component reaches -15.0 (float rounding decides the exact tick) and then stays there. With the flag clear, P does not move.

50. Contact dispatch (synthetic): an owner's contact list holds three entries: kind 1 (passes the owner test), kind 5 (ignored) and kind 4 with a float field of 0.3 (passes). The kind-1 helper runs once; the kind-4 entry takes its first helper because the field is above zero; the kind-5 entry calls nothing.
51. Contact dispatch (synthetic): a kind-7 contact with a float field of zero takes the second helper (zero is not above zero; the boundary is Medium-Low).
52. Driver branch 2 (synthetic): owner query 2 (the petpet) runs its own frame-record step over the cell frame records and walks cell list A through the dispatcher; the cell list B contact walk is not run in that branch.
53. Light slots (synthetic, Behavior 24): a type 1 light that passes its gates takes slot 0. A type 2 light with its flag word set takes slot 1. A point light (type 3, strength 1.0, radius 10, gate 100) at distance 9.5, with slots 2 and 3 both holding unlocked type 2 lights of weights 0.3 and 0.8 (strengths 0.3 and 0.8): it takes no slot; slot 2 (the smaller weight) gets weight (9.5 − 9.0) / 1.0 × 0.3 = 0.15 and is locked.
54. Light slots (synthetic, Behavior 24): point light, strength 1.0, radius 10: distance 4 gives weight 1.0; distance 7 gives 1 − 2 / 4 = 0.5; distance 8.8 gives 0.05. At distance 7 with slot 2 empty it takes slot 2; with slot 2 full and slot 3 empty it takes slot 3. At distance 10.5 the gate (110.25 not below 100) rejects it.
55. Chain body (synthetic): record 0, part 1 is vertex 8 with link 7 and next 9. Record 0, part 3 is vertex 10 with link 9 and no next index.
56. Chain mode (synthetic, Behavior 25): a townsfolk character keeps mode −1, and no link moves. Tor has mode 1 with one 5-link chain; Roberta mode 2 with two 4-link chains.
57. Colour word packing (synthetic): bytes 0x10, 0x20 and 0x30 at byte positions 0, 1 and 2 pack to one word with bits 0 to 7 equal to 0x10, bits 8 to 15 equal to 0x20 and bits 16 to 23 equal to 0x30. Pushing that word as colour word 0 writes the vector (16/255, 32/255, 48/255, 1.0), about (0.0627, 0.1255, 0.1882, 1.0) (Behavior 26, items 1 and 4).
58. Variant select (synthetic, Behavior 26, item 3): a kind-6 hit on a minion with draw 5 sets the variant to 4, re-reads the row-530 pointer, loads preset 5 and pushes both buffers. Afterwards w0 = 0x00000000.
59. Push and override (synthetic, Behavior 26, items 4 and 5): the word 0x80B6997E pushes (0.49412, 0.60000, 0.71373, 1.0). With the override flag set, w1 pushes (0.60784, 0.48235, 0.75686, 1.0) whatever the record holds.
60. Timed list insert (synthetic): slot 0 occupied and slot 1 free: an insert writes the target id into slot 1 with timer 60. With all ten slots occupied the insert is refused and all ten slots are unchanged (Behavior 27).
61. Timer re-arm (synthetic): timer values -1, 0 and 5 give 60, 0 and 5 (Behavior 27, item 4).
62. Tracked-point settle, moving point (dormant rule; synthetic, Behavior 8b rule 2): saved (0, 0, 0), current (0.03, 0, 0). The difference 0.03 is above 0.02, so the point is moving and its saved copy becomes (0.0045, 0, 0).
63. Tracked-point settle, still point (dormant rule; synthetic): saved (1, 2, 3), current (1.01, 2, 3). The difference 0.01 is not above 0.02, so the point is still and its saved copy becomes (1.01, 2, 3). If both points are still on a tick, the settled flag is set; on a later still tick with the settled flag set, the second latch is set.
64. Heading solve (synthetic, Behavior 8b rule 5): direction (1, 0, 0), forward (0, 0, 1). The dot product is 0, so the turn angle is 1.5708 rad (pi over 2). The half-angle cosine and sine are both 0.7071 in magnitude. With the turn-hold flag set, the heading is unchanged.
65. Mode-0 angle limit (dormant rule; synthetic, Behavior 8b rule 3): a computed angle of 2.0 rad is limited to 1.7 rad.
66. Mode dispatch (dormant dispatcher; synthetic, Behavior 8b): mode word 0 runs rule 3, mode word 1 runs rule 2, and mode word 7 runs rule 4.

67. Look-at yaw rate and limit (synthetic, Behavior 23A): yaw target 2.0, stored yaw 0, reset flag clear. After one call the yaw is 0.10. After 15 calls it is 1.50. The 16th call would reach 1.60, and the yaw is limited to 1.57.
68. Look-at pitch limit (synthetic, Behavior 23A): pitch target -0.5, stored pitch 0, reset flag clear. After 3 calls the pitch is -0.30; the 4th call gives -0.35.
69. Look-at reset (synthetic, Behavior 23A): stored yaw 1.2 and pitch -0.2 with the reset flag set: both become 0 on that call.
70. Pool drift (synthetic, Behavior 25A; dormant pool): p = (0, 5, 0), p_prev = (0, 5, −0.1), draws i = 6 and j = 7: p becomes (0.0018, 5.005, 0.1011).
71. Sample ring (synthetic, Behavior 14B): count 3, write index starting at 0. Four pushes write entries 0, 1, 2 and then 0 again.
72. Mote reflect (synthetic, Behavior 18A): shield block passed, projectile kind 0, shot code 9, element 2. Shield Mote 164 (element 2): reflected, no damage. Mote 160 (element 1): blocked hit. Mote 172 (element 7): reflected. Shot code 6 with Mote 164: blocked hit. Element 0 with Mote 172: blocked hit. Shield block failed: normal hit whatever the Mote.
73. Surface basis gate (synthetic, Behavior 9 item 7): a normal with first component 0.5 runs the blend. A normal with first component 0.9995 does not.
74. Attachment selector (synthetic, Behavior 22A): slot empty, stored orb-kind value 3 attaches effect 1622. Selector 3 again changes nothing. Selector 0 clears the slot. Random draw 0 stores selector 1; draw 5 stores selector 6.
75. Id split (synthetic, Layout): id 1234 gives block 24 and offset 34; id 49 gives block 0 and offset 49; id 50 gives block 1 and offset 0.
76. Player selector (synthetic, Behavior 1B): table base B, slot 1 selects B + 5760 (0x1680); slot 2 leaves the selected pointer unchanged.
77. Region lookup (synthetic, Layout): key -4 gives -1; a key with no active matching record gives -2.
78. Default spawn (synthetic, Behavior 1B): the default spawn is (2429.0, 0.0, 2295.0, 1.0) and the default direction (1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0). A world with entries never gives the default spawn, and the original never takes the default-spawn branch, because its presence test always passes. A world with no entries gives spawn (0, 0, 0, 1) and, its direction pointer being null, direction (1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0).

79. Actor pair test (synthetic, Behavior 11B): radius 2.0 for a. b at x = 1 and z = 2 passes with flag 0 (window [1, 3]) and with flag 1 (window [−1, 1]). b at x = 5 with flag 0 fails (window [5, 7] does not reach the band). b at z = −0.1 fails (depth below 0). b at z = 2.6 fails (depth above r + 0.5 = 2.5).

80. Request applied at once (synthetic, Behavior 15A): applied priority 0, hold word zero. A walk request with priority 2 is applied at once: the applied priority becomes 2 and the state is the walk state.
81. Held request (synthetic, Behavior 15A): applied priority 2, hold word set, no pending request. A request with priority 2 becomes pending. A second request with priority 2 is rejected (not greater than the pending 2). A request with priority 1 is rejected (below the applied 2), and so is a negative class-default request, which has priority 1.
82. Navigation re-plan (synthetic, Behavior 21A): stored target (0, 0, 0), cached index 5. A new target (1.2, 0, 0) resets the cached index to -1. A new target (0.9, 0, 0) against the same stored target leaves the cached index at 5.
83. Villager pair (synthetic, Behavior 3B): two eligible villagers 5.9 apart on one nav mesh, with a pair radius above 5.9, pair up; the approach ends at once, and one villager talks while the other waits. Two villagers 6.1 apart do not settle; the approach continues. With the disc pair radius 0.0, two eligible villagers 0.5 apart do not pair (0.5 is not below 0.0).
84. Contact handler (synthetic, Behavior 16): the kind-3 handler (Light) with an entry element of 0 passes (break, cue by material); with element 5 it is skipped and no cue plays.
85. Owner-field handler (synthetic, Behavior 16): with the owner's field at 7, an entry element of 5 passes. With the owner's field at 3 and the same element, it is skipped. A passing entry with material 4 plays cue 146.
86. Fall law (synthetic, Behavior 14A item 7): g = 32, R = (0, 1, 0), from rest, no surface, gain 1.0. Drop after K ticks = 0.0177778 K (K + 1): K = 1 gives 0.0356; K = 2 gives 0.1067; K = 3 gives 0.2133; K = 10 gives 1.9556; K = 60 gives 65.067. Gain 0.3: K = 10 gives 0.5867; K = 20 gives 2.24. The result does not depend on the weight W.
86A. Friction (synthetic, Behavior 14A item 5c): a character record on flat ground with v = (0, 0, 5) and G = 1 has speeds 4.36, 3.72, 3.08, 2.44, 1.80, 1.16, 0.52, 0 on the following ticks and travels 0.5693 in all. On a 30-degree slope from rest it stays; on a 35-degree slope its speed after 10 ticks is 0.876.
86B. Ground constraint (synthetic, Behavior 14A item 5b): gap -0.0177, vy -7.5167, flat ground: c = -0.268, which is at most -0.1, so vy becomes 0 and y becomes the ground height.
86C. Edge guard (synthetic, Behavior 14E): a generic character (not locked, kind 1, AI mode 0) with P0 = (0, 0, 0), a root step to P = (0, 0, 0.3) and ground gap 2.5 ends at (0, 0, -0.3). With gap 1.5 it stays at P. Any hero stays at P.
86D. Ground-record height gate (synthetic, Behavior 14E): with motion-position Y = 4.0 and reference height = 6.3, the inclusive 2.3-unit bound passes; reference height = 6.31 fails. The ground-record short-word test is evaluated only after the height bound passes.
87. Cadence (synthetic, Behavior 14): over 120 ticks the hero's record tick, velocity update and driver each run 120 times, and the history ring is written twice (once every 60 ticks, on the 60th and 120th tick after the record is built; see vector 172).
88. Villager gesture (Behavior 3B): a role-2 villager draws 3 and requests id 47; a draw of 15 requests id 96. Ids 28, 40, 62 and 95 (positions 16 to 19) are never requested by this draw. A role word of 0 replays the stored gesture id.
89. NPC walk slot (Behavior 3C): a townsfolk character of table variant C in movement class 0 requests id 1 and plays body 526 clip 107; with speed code 6 it requests id 8, also clip 107. Movement class 3 requests id 3, which plays the idle clip 48.
90. Main clip range (Edge cases): a character bound to the Tor main set (161 clips) that requests main clip index 924 starts no main clip; its sub-part and face clips are unaffected.
91. Hero walk from rest (Behavior 14C): full forward tilt in walk mode, flat ground, facing unchanged. Distance after N ticks = 0.225198 × (N − 1): N = 1 gives 0; N = 2 gives 0.2252; N = 10 gives 2.027; N = 30 gives 6.531; N = 60 gives 13.287.
92. Hero run from rest (Behavior 14C): full tilt in run mode, flat ground. Distance after N ticks = 0.321433 × (N − 1): N = 60 gives 18.965.
93. Root step (Behavior 14C, item 1): a walk clip with root record z = 461 (0.4504 after × 0.000977) and S = 1 at R = 1 has clip speed 0.5 keys per tick, so the root step is (0, 0, 0.2252) (0.225198; 13.512 units/s). At R = 0.8 the clip speed is 0.4 and the step is (0, 0, 0.1802), 10.81 units/s.
94. Basis and displacement (synthetic, Behavior 14C, items 5 and 6): facing f = (1, 0, 0) gives B3 = (1, 0, 0), B1 = (0, 0, −1) and B2 = (0, 1, 0); the root step (0, 0, 0.2252) gives D = (0.2252, 0, 0), and the side step (0.2252, 0, 0) gives D = (0, 0, −0.2252).
95. Stick release (Behavior 14C, timing): after walking at full tilt, the stick is centred on tick n. The hero moves 0.2252 on tick n and 0 from tick n + 1 on.
96. Stick behind the facing (Behavior 14C, item 4): a walking hero whose stick points 135 degrees from the facing moves 0 on that tick and turns; the step is used again once the facing is within 90 degrees of the stick (SPEC-0033).
97. Stick re-aim (synthetic, Behavior 14C): stored step (0, 0, 0.2252), stick (side 1, forward 0) in state 32. The new step is (0.2252, 0, 0): the length is kept.
98. Cycle length (Behavior 14C): one walk cycle (20 keys at 0.5 keys per tick, 40 ticks) moves 40 × 0.2252 = 9.01 units; one run cycle (14 keys, 28 ticks) moves 28 × 0.3214 = 9.00 units.
99. New game (disc, Behavior 1C): entry 0 of pack 909 is at (-1852.8021, 50.0, 1137.4628) with facing (0.59588, 0, -0.80307). Both heroes spawn at x = -1852.8021, z = 1137.4628 with y = H(-1852.8021, 1137.4628, 58.0), or y = 50.0 if that probe gives less than -1e-7. After the respawn with heal the camera eye is (-1858.761, y + 2.5, 1145.493).
100. Teleport, upright (disc, pack 909, teleport plane 0): P = (-1892.4012, 39.9597, 1173.0106), w = 2.81902, F = (0.95330, 0, 0.30201), D = (0.30201, 0, -0.95330), Q = (0, 1, 0). Before the probe L = (-1887.209, 39.960, 1173.177); the probe ceiling is 45.960; the facing is F, or -F with reverse.
101. Teleport, upright (disc, pack 909, teleport plane 1): L = (-2021.700, 44.600, 964.659) before the probe, ceiling 50.600, facing F = (-0.55806, 0, 0.82980).
102. Teleport, flat (synthetic): P = (0, 10, 0), F = (0, 1, 0), D = (1, 0, 0), Q = (0, 0, 1), h = 2, w = 4. L = (4, 5, 2), no probe, facing (0, 1, 0).
103. Teleport refused (synthetic): plane index 3 in a world with 3 teleport planes: nothing moves and the landing reports failure.
104. Teleport into the interior slot (synthetic): the focus hero lands on any valid plane of an interior whose entry 0 is (1, 2, 3) with facing (0, 0, 1). It ends at (1, 2, 3) facing (0, 0, 1), unprobed, and its start pose is the same entry.
105. Respawn camera (synthetic): start position (0, 0, 0), start facing (0, 0, 1). The eye is (0, 2.5, -10) and the side vector is normalize((0, 1, 0) × (0, 0, 1)) = (1, 0, 0).
106. Teleport camera (synthetic): hero at (10, 0, 0) facing (1, 0, 0) after the landing: camera eye (5, 0, 0).
107. Bone capsule (synthetic, Behavior 12A): bone frame identity at the origin, base (0, 0, 0), length 2.0, radius 0.5: the capsule is centred at (0, 1, 0) with axis (0, 1, 0) and spans y = 0 to 2. A vertical weapon cylinder of radius 1.0 and length 3.5 centred at (1.2, 1, 0) overlaps it (radial distance 1.2 is within 1.5); centred at (1.6, 1, 0) it does not.
108. Attack windows (synthetic, Behavior 12A): an enemy whose current frame has table B value 0x6 tests weapons 2 and 3 only; a value of 0x40 tests none. A hero frame value of 0x401 is attack-active; 0x400 is not.
109. Weapon row fields (synthetic, Behavior 12A): a monster row whose word at +0x04 is 3.0 and whose word at +0x18 is 2.0 gives weapon 2 a radius of 3.0 and a full height of 2.0.
110. Hit list (synthetic, Behavior 12A): ids 3 and 7 in the list: target 7 is skipped. After ten adds to an empty list, an eleventh add overwrites slot 0.
111. Hittable predicate (synthetic, Behavior 12A): a target in state 33 is not hittable; a target in state 1 with the invulnerability flag +0x7F0 clear is hittable by the base rule, and with it set is not. A hero in state 57 is not hittable.
112. Beam volume (synthetic, Behavior 12A): beam centre (0, 1, 0), beam direction (0, 0, 1): the volume is a cylinder of radius 1.0 and length 5.0 spanning z = -2.5 to 2.5 around that centre.
112a. Weapon against bone (synthetic, Behavior 12A; exact test of SPEC-0018): a weapon of radius 3.0, height 1.0, centre (1.2, 0, 0), axis (0, 0, 1) against a bone capsule of radius 0.5, length 0.9, centre (0, 0.5, 0), axis (0, 1, 0) hits (no edge of the card crosses the weapon, the card is wholly inside and both sizes are smaller); with length 1.0 it misses (not strictly smaller than the weapon's height).
112b. Weapon against bone, solid overlap that misses (synthetic): a weapon of radius 2.0, height 2.0, centre (0.9, 2.0, 0.5), axis (0, 0, 1) against the same capsule with length 1.0: the round body of the capsule is partly inside the weapon, yet only the long edge of the card crosses the weapon and its second crossing lies beyond the edge, so there is no hit. A weapon of radius 2.0, height 1.0, axis (1, 0, 0), centre (0.9, 0.5, 0) against that capsule hits (both end edges cross the weapon's flat end); at centre (1.1, 0.5, 0) it misses.
113. Grid cell refresh (synthetic, Behavior 14): a record that moves from one grid cell to the next during tick n has the new cell at +0x174 from the record tick of tick n + 1 on, because the refresh runs first in each record tick. A record over no cell stores 0.
114. Play rate (synthetic, Behavior 3D): speed scale 1.0 and per-tick speed scale 0.6 give R = 0.6; a clip with S = 2 started that tick gets speed 0.6 × 0.5 ÷ 2 = 0.15 keys per tick.
115. Face override revert (synthetic, Behavior 3D): main id 0 and face override id 12. While the face record holds id 12's face clip below progress 0.99, the override stays. On the tick its progress reaches 0.99, the override becomes -1 and the face clip of id 0 starts.
116. Id miss (synthetic, Behaviors 3C and 3D): a main table of 3 entries and a request for id 5 start main clip 0 (if clip 0 is not already playing on that layer).
117. Held one-shot (synthetic, Behavior 14C): F = 10, S = 2, R = 1 (clip speed 0.25), last root record z = 0.4. Once held, the root step is z = 0.4 × 0.25 × 2 = 0.2 per tick.
118. HUD re-arm (synthetic, Behavior 6): a hero with HP 100 of 150 and the HUD change timer at 0. Deduct 10: HP 90, the damage-applied hook runs and the timer becomes 240. Set HP to 90 again: no notification, the timer is unchanged. Set pool max to its current max: MP becomes that max, no notification.
119. Townsfolk walk from idle (Behavior 14D): a townsfolk character (clip 104, root z = 512 per key, S = 3, R = 1) whose class coroutine requests the walk during the NPC update on tick 0 starts the clip in the animation pass of tick 0. Motion on tick 0 still uses the old idle root step (0); the new clip's first root step moves the character on tick 1 by 512 × 0.000977 ÷ 6 = 0.08337, then by that amount each following tick. Distance over T ticks beginning at tick 0 = 0.08337 × (T − 1): T = 2 gives 0.0834; T = 60 gives 4.919.
120. Townsfolk run, world frame (Behavior 14D): clip 105 at R = 1 gives the root step (0.01840, 0, 0.22504); with facing (1, 0, 0) (B1 = (0, 0, −1)) the world move is (0.22504, 0, −0.01840). At R = 2 the forward step is 0.45007.
121. Flyer step (Behavior 14D item 7): Can Fly 1, not grounded, Flying Idle Velocity 3.0, movement class 7, the slot animation playing with root z step 0.08337 per tick: F = 3.0 ÷ 30 = 0.1, so the step is (0, 0, 0.18337) per tick (11.00 units/s). A row with velocity 3.0 and a clip with no root moves 6.0 units/s. Minion Grunt (idle 3.0, agro 7.0): wander 6.0 + 8.68 = 14.68 units/s, agro rush 14.0 units/s (its rush clip has no root). Crokabek (3.0, 8.0): 6.0 and 16.0 units/s (its flying clips carry no root).
122. Flyer height (Behavior 14D item 7): y = 0, target 0.25: y becomes 0.1, 0.2, then 0.25 on the third tick (|dy| = 0.05 is within 0.1, so it snaps).
123. AI speed write timing: the NPC update on tick n resets P to 1.0 and applies status factors, then the class arbiter can write P and request a clip before the playing clip is re-timed and before the animation pass. A write P = 1.5 can affect the current clip's rate and a clip started in that pass; the motion-record tick still uses the prior animation pass's root step. On tick n + 1 the NPC update resets P before re-timing and applies current status factors. A slowed enemy still uses P = 0.5 when the status is applied unless its class coroutine writes P afterward.
124. Non-hero facing (Behavior 8): an NPC facing (0, 0, 1) whose steering wrote the move direction (1, 0, 0) faces (1, 0, 0) after its next record tick, a 90-degree turn in one tick; a moving hero turns at most 18.19 degrees per tick (SPEC-0033).
125. Held flags (synthetic, Behavior 29): both flags 0, Tor holds a sword, Roberta is the active hero and has no shield. An enemy hit with attacker id 5 sets weapon-drawn to 1 and leaves shield-raised 0. A poison tick (attacker id -2) instead leaves both at 0. Square with nothing to interact with then clears both flags.
126. Stolen item (synthetic, Behavior 29): a thief steals an item on draw 0; the item is drawn at 0.45 scale on draws 1 to 180 and not on draw 181, when the flag is clear.
127. Villager eligibility (synthetic, Behavior 3B): social gate 0, mode 5, playing id 1: eligible. Playing id 3: not eligible. Playing id 113 in mode 3: not eligible.
128. Villager timers (Behavior 3B): a talk timer draw lies in 300 to 720 ticks (5 to 12 s); a cooldown draw in 600 to 1800 ticks (10 to 30 s).
129. Flying idle (synthetic, Behavior 3C): Can Fly 1 with grounded countdown 0: base idle 97 and fidget 142. The same row with countdown 30: base idle 0 and fidgets 115 to 119. In mode 19: Can Fly 2 gives 141; Can Fly 0 gives 1.
130. Aim rule (synthetic, Behavior 8): facing (0, 0, 1), direction (1, 0, 0), weight 0.3: v = (0.3, 0, 0.7), new facing (0.39392, 0, 0.91915). Facing (0, 0, 1), direction (0, 0, −1), weight 0.5: the dot is −1, below −0.99, so v = 0.1 times the side axis; with side (1, 0, 0) the facing becomes (1, 0, 0).
131. Clip progress (synthetic, Behavior 22B): F = 20, cursor 19.25, stored fraction 0.5: progress 0.9875, below 0.99. A one-shot that has finished reports 1.0; an id whose clip no record holds reports 0.
132. Dead test (synthetic, Behavior 2): base state 1, AI mode 16, counter +0xD14 = 3: dead. The same with the counter at 0: not dead. Base state 16: dead.
133. IsHeroOccupied (synthetic, Behavior 15.2): hero in state 50, no stun, no fade: occupied and busy (the state-50 test is the second part of the one routine). Hero in state 1 while the screen fade alpha differs from its target: occupied. Hero in state 1, no stun, no moving effect: not occupied.
134. Arbiter states (synthetic, Behavior 3): a class request stored while an NPC is in base state 33 is not applied, and its coroutine is not stepped. After ReleaseEntityControl sets base state 1, the next update applies the request and steps the coroutine. An NPC in base state 17 steps its coroutine after the state's own step.
135. Parking (synthetic, Behavior 15A): a townsfolk character wandering (class priority 1) gets a script walk (priority 2). The wander coroutine is parked and the walk runs; when the walk ends, the class word and priority become 0 and the wander resumes toward the same wander point.
136. Emote pop, body (synthetic, Behavior 22): body queue [51, 30], main id 51, clip one-shot at progress 0.995: 51 is popped, B = 30, and main id 30 is requested with its clip. Body queue [-2]: popped, the queue is empty, B = 0 (idle).
137. Emote pop, loop (synthetic, Behavior 22): body queue [51] with main id 51 on a looping clip: no pop, because no second entry exists, so the loop keeps playing. Body queue [51, 30] with the same loop: 51 is popped and B = 30.
138. Emote face (synthetic, Behavior 22): body queue empty and main id 0. Empty face queue: the face override is held at 0. Face queue [126], override 0: the face clip of 126 starts (when the entry has one) and the override becomes 126.
139. Seated NPC (synthetic, Behavior 22): NPC in base state 25 playing main id 65 with body queue [51]: the body queue is not consumed and no body clip plays; a face entry in the face queue is still handled.
140. Stale emote (synthetic, Behavior 22): five hero body emotes are queued while the hero is in state 1; the four newest stay. The next level message puts the hero into state 25, and the oldest of the four (the second one queued) is requested first.
141. Idle pick, townsfolk (synthetic, Behavior 3C, item 1): townsfolk flag 1, mode 5 pausing, a main-family -7 table of variant A, hold timer 1, base idle playing. Each call returns 0, the hold timer stays 1, and the shared random generator advances by no draws. The same with Can Fly forced to 1 and grounded countdown 0: still 0, not 97.
142. Idle pick, enemy (synthetic, Behavior 3C, item 1): townsfolk flag 0, otherwise the same state. The call drops the hold timer to 0 and draws k in 0 to 4 (fidget id 115 + k). With k = 2 and a table that has id 117: the result is 117. With a table that lacks id 117: the hold timer is redrawn in 120 to 600 ticks and the result is 0 (two draws used).
143. Chain link, still owner (synthetic, Behavior 25): Tor link 1 constants with L = 1 and α = β = 0, T = (0, 0, 0), M = identity, C = B = (0.5, 0, −0.86603). Tick 1: X3 = (0.475, 0, −0.87272), θ = −0.49844, N = (0.47805, 0, −0.87833), local rotation (x, y, z, w) = (0, −0.2466, 0, 0.9691). Tick 2: X3 = (0.43879, 0, −0.89303), N = (0.44099, 0, −0.89751). Tick 3: N = (0.39425, 0, −0.91900).
144. Chain clamp (synthetic, Behavior 25): the same link with the point at yaw 1.4 to the +X side is clamped to θ = −1.25, N = (0.94898, 0, −0.31532).
145. Chain drag (synthetic, Behavior 25): the owner moves along +X and the link rests at (0, 0, −1). X3 = (−0.015, 0, −1), so θ = +0.0150: the point trails toward −X.
146. Look yaw (synthetic, Behavior 23A): O = (0, 2, 0), t = (1, 2, 1), F = (0, 0, 1), X = (1, 0, 0). The yaw target is 0.7854. After call 1 the yaw is 0.1 and the look point is (0.1997, 2, 1.9900). Call 8 reaches 0.7854, with look point (1.4142, 2, 1.4142).
147. Look pitch (synthetic, Behavior 23A): the same head with t = (0, 3, 1). The pitch target is −0.7854, and the pitch reaches the limit −0.35 at call 4. The look point is (0, 1.3142, 1.8787), below the head.
148. Neck look (synthetic, Behavior 23A): A = 0.6 with s ≤ 0 gives τ = 0.1; from 0 nothing moves. A = 0.05 gives τ = 0.00833, taken at once. If A then rises to 0.2 in one tick, τ = 0.0333 and the angle stays at 0.00833. A tick with no request sets it to 0.
149. Pool separation (synthetic, Behavior 11A; dormant pool): r_s = 1 at (0, 0, 0) and r_o = 3 at (2, 0, 0): self moves to (−1.5, 0, 0) and the other to (2.5, 0, 0).
150. Busy test (synthetic, Behavior 18): hero in action state 50, no stun, no screen effect: busy (script-held state). Hero in state 1 with a status 0 timer of 12: busy. Hero in state 1, no stun, fade alpha 0.5 with target 0.5 and the bars at their targets: not busy. Hero in state 55: busy, although state 55 is not among the 41 state tests.
151. Attack-state test (synthetic, Behavior 18): Tor in state 13, sub-state 12: attacking; sub-state 1: not. Tor in state 9, air phase 8: attacking; air phase 7: not. Roberta in state 13, sub-state 5: attacking; sub-state 6: not. Roberta in state 9, air phase 8: not. A townsfolk character: never.
152. Engagement (synthetic, Behavior 20): a foe at distance 6.0 with alignment 0.3 while the hero is not attacking: in the list, not in range, no lock. Tor enters state 13 with sub-state 2: the record engages, is in range and becomes the lock. The foe moves to 20.0: both flags clear and the lock is lost; back at 6.0 it stays out of range until the hero attacks again while facing it.
153. Ordering quirk (synthetic, Behavior 20): two engaged in-range records in the order A (d = 5.0), B (d = 5.5). The pass swaps them (5.5 < 6.0): lock B. Next tick, in the order B, A: 5.0 < 6.5 swaps them back: lock A. With A at 3.0 and B at 5.5 (order A, B): no swap, lock A on every tick.
154. Run suppression (synthetic, Behavior 20): lock id 120 with the hero in state 4: target flag 0, suppressed id 120. Next tick in state 1, lock still 120: flag 0. When the lock becomes 135: suppressed id −1, flag 1 and the target point of 135 stored. Back to 120: flag 1.
155. Removal skip (synthetic, Behavior 20): list [A, B, C] where A fails the candidate test this tick: the list becomes [B, C]; B is not refreshed this tick (it keeps last tick's distance and flags); C is.
156. Pending contact-shape capacity (synthetic, Behavior 14A, item 2a): count 9 accepts one append and becomes 10; count 10 refuses the next append and stays 10. The next consume pass processes the stored entries and resets the count to 0.
156. Flyer goal, Can Fly 2 (synthetic, Behavior 21): Max Fly Height 4, hero height 20, ground 10 under the goal, ambush word 1: 20 − 10 = 10 > 4, so the goal height is 14. With the hero at height 8 (below the ground plus 2.0): the goal height is 8.
157. Flyer goal, AI mode 10 sub-state 6 (synthetic, Behavior 21): Max Fly Height 4, goal height 9: it becomes 4; goal height 3: unchanged; no other rule runs.
158. Can Fly 2 sink (synthetic, Behavior 14D item 7): a Can Fly 2 character 5.0 above the ground, at rest, with its goal at that height. Tick 1: vy = −1.0667 and y falls 0.0356; the keeper puts it back (within 0.1, so it is set to the target). By tick 3 the fall per tick is 0.1067, beyond the 0.1 lift, and from then on the character sinks until it is grounded.
159. Colour override at load (synthetic, Behavior 26; key 13 does not occur on the disc, whose selectors are 0 to 11): progress flag 216 on and world variant key 13 (always 1): every character of a newly loaded block gets the override, and w1 pushes (0.60784, 0.48235, 0.75686, 1.0). With flag 216 off the override flag is 0 for every character of that block.
160. Sisters' hit (synthetic, Behavior 15.1a): shield word 0, a shot hits a sister: no damage, level event 1, shield word 1. Then a melee hit of 30 on a sister with 100 HP in idle: total taken 30, HP 70, state 8 (flinch). A melee hit of 80 on a sister with 70 HP: state 9 (dead).
161. Attack turn (synthetic, Behavior 20 item 7): Roberta facing (0, 0, 1), held target point at (3, 5, 0) from the hero: horizontal direction (1, 0, 0), D = 3 < 4.0, weight 1.0, new facing (1, 0, 0). Target at (8, 0, 0): weight 0.25, v = (0.25, 0, 0.75), new facing (0.31623, 0, 0.94868).
162. Attack turn, Tor (synthetic, Behavior 20 item 7): facing (0, 0, 1), held target at (3, 0, 0): D = 3 but dot(d, forward) = 0, so no turn. Held target at (3, 0, 1): d = (0.94868, 0, 0.31623), dot 0.316 > 0, instant turn to d.
163. Attack turn, breakable (synthetic, Behavior 20 item 7): no held target, facing (0, 0, 1), one intact breakable at (5, 0, 10): dot 0.894 > 0.5 and D = 11.18. Tor (reach 12.0): weight 0.25, v = (0.11180, 0, 0.97361), new facing (0.11408, 0, 0.99348). A breakable at (0, 0, 13): no turn (13 is not below 12.0). Roberta with no held target and no forced point: no turn, whatever breakables are near.
164. Held-item scale (synthetic, Behavior 10 item 5): item model x extent 0.8: first list s = 1.6 / 0.8 = 2.0; second list 2.6 / 0.8 = 3.25, capped to s = 0.6. Extent 5.2: first list s = 0.30769; second list s = 0.5. The position row of the frame is not scaled.
165. Held-item draw order (synthetic, Behavior 12): hero slot index 0 with both records owned: two draws, first list then second list. Slot index 1 with the inventory flag word 0: no draw. Slot index 1 with that word nonzero: the second list only.
166. Held-item scale for a model 1.6 wide (synthetic, Behavior 10 item 5): first list s = 1.6 / 1.6 = 1.0; second list 2.6 / 1.6 = 1.625, capped to s = 0.6. With the hero's slot index 1 only the second list's record is drawn.
167. Beam life (Behavior 17): life starts at trunc(5.0 × 60) = 300. Tick 1 lowers it to 299 and the walks run. Tick 299 lowers it to 1 and the walks run. Tick 300 lowers it to 0, so the beam ends, cue 4 plays, and no walk runs. The walks run on ticks 1 to 299 (299 ticks).
168. Beam re-hit window (Behavior 17): one hittable target stays inside the beam from tick 1. Its slot timer is set to 60 at the tick 1 hit. Ticks 2 to 60 lower the timer to 1, so there is no hit. On tick 61 the timer drops to 0, the slot is freed, and the walk on that same tick hits again. Over the whole beam the hits fall on ticks 1, 61, 121, 181 and 241: five hits.
169. Beam hit list full (Behavior 17): twelve distinct hittable targets are all inside the beam on tick 1. In list order the first ten take the ten slots and are hit; the eleventh and twelfth are refused. On tick 61 all ten slots free together. The walk then takes the first ten that pass again, in list order, so the same two are refused again.
170. Rock hit (Behavior 17): two hittable characters A and B, in that list order, both overlap a rock's sphere on one tick. A is hit with element 2 and attack id 165, and the rock ends; B takes no hit. The rock's breakable walk still runs that tick.
171. Friendly fire (Behavior 17): Tor stands inside Roberta's Sun beam. Tor is not on the active character list, so he takes no hit on any tick. An enemy beside him is hit on ticks 1, 61, 121, 181 and 241.
172. History ring (Behavior 14): a record built on tick 0 samples on ticks 60, 120 and 180. After tick 60 slot 0 holds the tick 60 position; after tick 120 slot 0 holds the tick 120 position; slots 1 to 4 never change.
173. State request (Behavior 15A): applied priority 0, pending priority 0, hold word 0. Request (state 23, priority 2): accepted and applied at once; applied priority 2, pending priority 0, pending state -1. A second request (state 24, priority 2) on the same tick: accepted (2 is at least 2 and above 0) and applied. With the hold word set, (23, 2) becomes pending (pending priority 2) and a following (24, 2) is refused (2 is not above 2).
174. Respawn cooldown and second-object relocation: before respawn, hero +0xA04 is 0; respawn writes 60. After 59 selected-hero updates it is 1 and the grab-point and state-28 exit gates still fail; after update 60 it is 0 and those gates may pass if their other conditions do. If hero +0x148 is nonzero and the global second-object pointer exists, respawn copies the world id and start pose to that object; if either condition fails, it does not relocate it. The object is the live petpet: with a petpet out in another world, a respawn of a hero (+0x148 = 1) sets the petpet's world id to the hero's and moves it to the hero's start pose; with no petpet out, nothing is relocated.
175. Teleport facing fourth lane: two plane records with identical low 64 bits and different fourth lanes pass the same explicit facing input to the basis builder. Reversing the plane negates the same low-64-bit input; neither fourth lane changes the rebuilt basis.
174. Navigation target (Behavior 21A): stored target (0, 0, 0), cached index 7, no cached triangle. New target (0.9, 0, 0): index stays 7, arrival flag 0. New target (1.2, 0, 0): index -1. Both store the new target.
175. Timed list (Behavior 27): slot 0 holds id 5 with timer 1, slot 1 is free. On the next tick the count-down frees slot 0 before the walks; an insert of id 9 in that walk takes slot 0 (the first free slot) with timer 60.
176. Action end (Behaviors 15.3 and 15.4): PlayHeroAnim with CTL 1: when the progress passes 0.99 the state becomes 1. With CTL 0 the state becomes 33, and state 33 requests the default idle animation: 0 after a talk, or 48 after SetDefaultHeroIdleAnim(48).
177. Death end (Behavior 15.3): state 59 with current animation 0: animation 175 is requested and the effect starts. Later, with 175 current at progress 0.995: state 1. State 60 behaves the same with animation 137.
178. Stop flag (Behavior 14D): on a tick on which the Black Knight's slow-swing hit routine runs, its root step is discarded and its position does not change by root motion; on the next tick without a swing routine it travels again.
180. Pair-test lane check (synthetic, Behavior 11B): let a = (0, 10, 0, 1), b = (1, -10, 2, 1), r = 2.0. The helper uses dx = 1 and dz = 2, so flag 0 passes. Changing only b.y to 100 leaves the result unchanged; using y as depth would incorrectly reject the pair.
179. Re-arm owner (Behavior 27): timer -1, start: timer 60. Started again 10 ticks later (timer 50): stays 50. After 50 more ticks the timer reaches 0, both effects start and the object's list is cleared; on the next tick the timer is -1. Worked example of the hit rule (Medium-High): the hero at (100, 5, 200) uses the potion, so the stored point is (100, 7, 200) (2.0 above, not ahead) and the cylinder spans y 5 to 9. A Minion Warrior (monster row radius 1.25, height 3.0, health 100, key 2864) with feet at y 5 stands 8.0 away horizontally in class state 19. The radius is 0 for the 60 timer ticks, so nothing is hit; the effects start when the timer reaches 0. On particle age 18 ticks R = 0.5 + 14.5 x 0.30 / 0.7 = 6.714 and 6.714 + 1.25 = 7.964 is below 8.0: no hit. On age 19 R = 0.5 + 14.5 x (19/60) / 0.7 = 7.059 and 7.059 + 1.25 = 8.309 reaches 8.0: the Warrior is entered in the object's list (slot 0, timer 60) and its take-hit gets damage 100 with attacker -1: health 100 - 100 = 0, the death hook runs. A Warrior in another state takes 200 (doubled, state 19) and also dies. A character with a key outside the Minion groups, or the hero standing on the point, is entered in the list but takes no damage. A Warrior 16.0 away is never hit (the last active radius is about 14.3).
182. Footprint height probes (Behavior 11): moved-object position = (5, 2, 10), selected hero position R = (10, 4, 20), forward direction = (1, 0, 0), distance = 2.0. The first ground query is at (5, 2, 10) with ceiling 10.0; the second is at (13.025, 4, 20) with ceiling 12.0 because (2.0 + 0.75) × 1.1 = 3.025.
180. Target-pick gate (Behavior 17, synthetic): candidate query returns 0; candidate is one unit away and both line tests would pass. The candidate is still excluded before distance and line tests. Changing the query result to nonzero advances it to the strict range and line gates.
183. Status 0 on class 6 (synthetic): with no active timer, P remains 1.0 and no mode-4 request occurs; ordinary status application is ignored. If the status-0 timer is active through another route, the shared tick sets P to 0 and requests mode 4. That other route is unproven.
181. Breakable rectangle gate (synthetic): on the no-margin helper, a segment that crosses a rectangle plane at t = 1.01 misses even when the crossing projects inside both extents; a t = 1.0 crossing at the same projected point is accepted. Both static and moving breakable branches use this gate.

184. Flyer mode remap (synthetic, Behavior 8a): with Can Fly = 1 and grounded countdown 0, request 4 stores mode 8; request 6 stores mode 10; request 7 remains 7. With Can Fly = 0 or grounded countdown 1, request 4 remains 4.

185. Flyer height-keeper gate (synthetic): base state 16 makes the first class predicate true and skips height correction; if base state is not 16, a positive reanimation countdown with animation-kind field 16 also makes it true. With that first predicate false, row Can Fly = 1 and grounded countdown 0 pass the second predicate; countdown 1 fails it. Any other Can Fly value fails it.

NPC colour test vector (disc data, Behavior 26, item 9). Region resource 1002 (pack 991 plus 1 plus slot argument 10), kind-3 record at ordinal 0, name "Lady Marissa", main skeleton -7, model set 0, monster type 19, variant 3. Colour words w0 to w14 (bytes red, green, blue): w0 255, 255, 255; w1 255, 152, 83; w2 255, 64, 0; w3 150, 105, 190; w4 110, 50, 110; w5 and w6 0, 0, 0; w7 108, 54, 0; w8 110, 50, 110; w9 to w12 0, 0, 0; w13 187, 137, 255; w14 0, 0, 0. Model set 0 is the first top-level type-18 chunk of the region; its shader list has 14 groups and one texture chunk with three 32 by 32 textures and one 16 by 16 palette (palette base block 16, shared by every textured group). Mesh leaves use groups 0 to 13 once each: untextured groups 0 (slot 1), 1 and 2 (slot 2), 3, 4 and 6 (slot 0), 5 and 11 (slot 8), 7 (slot 3), 8 (slot 4), 10 (slot 7) and 12 (slot 13); textured group 9 (slot 3, texture base block 0) and textured group 13 (slot 0, texture base block 4). Colour scales: slot 1 gives (1.0, 0.59608, 0.32549, 1.0), slot 2 (1.0, 0.25098, 0.0, 1.0), slot 3 (0.58824, 0.41176, 0.74510, 1.0), slot 0 (1, 1, 1, 1). Group 0 vertices have colour bytes between 11 and 31 and no texture, so a vertex of byte 20 lit at L = 128 comes out at (160, 95, 52): orange skin. Group 9's texture is multiplied by (0.588, 0.412, 0.745). With every scale left at (1, 1, 1, 1) groups 0 to 8 and 10 to 12 are grey or white, which is the broken look. A second example (region 914, pack 909 plus 1 plus slot argument 4, the first record, name "Lucy"): w1 bytes 233, 205, 97 and w2 bytes 255, 255, 195, so her slot 1 groups 0 and 4 (one untextured, one a 32 by 32 texture) come out gold and her slot 2 group 5 pale yellow.

## Open questions

- Resolved: the object mode word is the active flag of colour-override slot 1, written by the hit-flash set and clear routines (Question 208). It is 0 except while a flash runs; a character with the word at 0 uses each part's own slot byte.

1. **Root block writer and use**: Resolved in part: in the examined active-hero and region-character routes, the stored pose-block root group is copied into a per-character request record and reaches a lit model-draw submission. This is a rendering consumer, not a character contact sweep or travel-position store (Behavior 10 item 2; High for the known route). The group is a rendering input only, so its writer and the identity of the rendered object change no rule; the draw-submission route above is all the rewrite needs. Character travel comes from layer 0 root steps (Behaviors 14C and 14D; High). The five weapon frame blocks of SPEC-0005 are per-weapon frames chosen by damage bits, not root samples (High). The 64-byte group written by the held-item draw is the item's hand frame (Behavior 12), not this root group.
2. **Final store**: Resolved: for the heroes the travel is the root-step displacement of Behavior 14C, added to the motion record's position, with a one-tick delay (Medium-High). Every non-hero character uses the same store: its motion record adds the root step of layer 0 (plus the row speed for flyers), turned by the record basis, to the record position; a class-coroutine animation request made in the NPC update first moves the character on the next tick (Behavior 14D; High for the store, Medium-High for the one-tick delay).
3. **Root-group sign and use**: Resolved (High for position): the canonical group submitted to the per-character draw path carries the inverted root, but the group is a rendering input, not the character's travel displacement (Behavior 10, items 2 and 9).
