Layer-0 travel follows the animation root translation with its sign unchanged: the displacement is x along the side axis, y up and z along the facing, added to the position (Behavior 14C items 6 and 8), so a positive forward root step moves the character forward (Tor's walk moves 0.2252 units forward per tick). The inversion has no effect on position. The rendered object's identity and the visual meaning of the inverted root are not encoded anywhere and change no rule. The held-item frame is unrelated (Behavior 12).
4. **Motion records**: Resolved in part: the known hero path binds records from a fixed three-entry backing array, with two hero records selected; region-character construction allocates one record per character from its owning character block, with at most 15 records per block. These paths do not establish a single shared fixed-capacity table. The owner of the world-context count/head structure, a system-wide cap and a generic allocation path are memory-bookkeeping details with no rule depending on them; the rewrite needs at least the two hero records plus 15 records per block (Pools and caps; High for the known paths and per-block limit).
5. **Status timers**: Answered: the enemy start rules, element gate and per-tick rules are in SPEC-0029 (Behavior 27); the hero rules are in SPEC-0036.
6. **Clock**: Resolved: a character has no seconds clock. The body object's time field that the animation sound step reads is the cursor of main layer 0 of the body's animation player (keys, advanced by the clip speed each tick; SPEC-0005 Playback 1a). The instance time with its 15 frames-per-second rule belongs to the animated world objects (SPEC-0028), not to characters (Medium-High for the field identity, from the record offsets; High that no character path converts seconds).
7. **Pose sampling order**: Answered: the animation slot samples at its current cursor, advances the cursor afterward, and the shared skeleton pose pass then builds matrices from that sample (Behavior 3; SPEC-0005 Playback 2–4; High).
8. **Skeleton combine rule**: Answered: SPEC-0005 (Playback records and layers; Joint composition) specifies the family order and ownership: the two main layers write the main joint list in order, while sub-part and face records write their own lists.
9. **Enemy thinking hook**: Answered: class behavior coroutines are stepped by the class arbiter inside the NPC update, in the default arm and after state-specific work in states 17 and 31. The two global behavior schedulers run after the region-character, hero and petpet passes but skip class coroutines because their auto-run word is 0 and the scheduler calls use a zero mask (Behavior 3; High). Resolved: the enemy notice rule is in SPEC-0038 (Summary part 1 and Behavior 18) and death, despawn and drops for every class are in SPEC-0029, Behavior 34.
10. **NPC pool**: Resolved: up to 15 character objects per character block (min(records, 15)), bound by proximity up to the block's active cap of 6 (or 15; SetMaxActiveAI up to 15); see Pools and caps and SPEC-0006, Character activation. **[High]**
11. Resolved: **Non-hero death.** The non-hero death hook and the corpse rules are SPEC-0038 Behaviors 34 to 36.
12. **Hero defence and blocking**: Answered: shield blocking, damage reduction, parry and dodge rules, and the absence of a post-hit invulnerability window are specified in SPEC-0036 (Enemy attack damage to a hero; Taking a hit). Resolved for respawn: the respawn at the start pose exists (Behavior 1C), and timer F uses it after a death on deadly ground (SPEC-0055, section 8A).
13. **Bone limit**: Resolved: both hold. 74 is the largest bone count on the disc, and 83 is the pose composition's cap (SPEC-0005). Use 83 as the hard cap; no disc data comes near it (High).
14. Resolved: **Constant direction of the timed walk**. The state scales the startup world-up vector (0, 1, 0, 0) by −1/180 and adds it to the motion-record position on eligible ticks, moving downward along world Y. The vector is initialized by the startup table walk (SPEC-0007); the state’s timer and class-predicate gates still control when the step runs. **[High]**
15. **Root-motion gate scope**: Resolved for type-29 animated world objects (SPEC-0028, Behavior 7.1): per-tick delta accumulation requires a positive f32 at +0x08 of the locator record linked through the instance's +0xB0 pointer, and a zero global override word (0 on disc; no writer found in the boot program). The link pointer is set by the link forms of MoveObjToLocator and MoveObjToAnimatedLocator. Only the sign of the linked-record float matters here (a positive value enables the accumulation); its further design meaning is not encoded anywhere. This gate and object accumulator are separate from the character playback-layer accumulator; its stored pose-group consumer and writer remain open (Behavior 10, items 2 and 9).
16. **Negative main-family indices**: Resolved: -1 is none, -5 the Roberta main set, -6 the Tor main set and -7 the shared townsfolk set of body 526 (Pools and caps; SPEC-0005, Body 526). What the -6 tables of resources 1278 and 1279 (main indices 924 to 1,082) were made for is developer intent and not encoded anywhere; they never start a main clip (SPEC-0005).
17. Resolved for core combat paths (Behavior 12A; High): the ten-entry character hit list is at +0x400; an add uses the first empty slot, and a full list overwrites slot 0. Beam and bubble target lists (SPEC-0036; Behavior 27) are separate. The character list clears at initialization and release; both heroes' charge routines clear it on every sub-state transition; hero attack-start reset and entry to state 13 clear it; hero state 12 clears it each update; and class-17 agro attack start clears it. A target can be hit again after a later phase transition. Some hero air-attack and boss callers also clear it, but their full phase mapping remains open. Confidence High for listed core paths, Medium for additional special-attack callers.
18. **Animation sound-cue hooks**: Resolved for the known shared/base, hero and region-character tables. The base/shared table leaves both hooks empty. At the cue start frame, the hero table starts its animation cue (the shipped hero animation entries contain no cue requests); the region-character table starts the cue from its sound block. After the cue-start check, the region-character table refreshes a matching keep-alive cue each tick; the hero refresh hook is empty. These hooks are not clip-completion callbacks. No dynamically installed or resource-loaded character table is known beyond the constructor paths, and the rewrite uses those only.
19. **Overheal and set rules**: Resolved: the add path caps at max HP; the set path fills to max HP for a value above it and otherwise stores the value with no lower clamp (Behavior 6). **[High]**
20. **Player slot 2**: Resolved: a spare. Three per-slot records are built but only two hero records; hero record k has hero index k (0 Tor, 1 Roberta) and attaches slot k; region characters attach with ids of 100 or more, so slot 2 is never attached (Pools and caps). **[High; Medium-High that nothing else uses it]**
21. Resolved: **Held-item scale branches** (once called root scale branches). The branch is the draw list: the first list's record uses s = 1.6 / |dx| and is skipped when the hero's slot index is 1; the second list's record uses s = min(0.6, 2.6 / |dx|). The 1.6 is a fixed program constant; 2.6 and 0.6 are boot data words (Behavior 10, item 5; Behavior 12). **[High]**
22. **Collision job**. Resolved: there is no collision job. The record and the program are the hero's held-item draw, a lit model draw of the hand-held items with the character's lights (Behavior 12). It has no radius and no wall response and writes nothing that matters to the character; its return value is a draw count that the caller ignores.
23. **Mode and slot words**. Resolved: the held-item draw writes both each pass before it draws: the mode word is a per-list lookup result passed to the draw, and the slot word is the shared draw list the item model is bound to (Behavior 12).
24. **Character collision block**. Resolved: it is the character's light context, the four light slots in the character's body (Layout; Behavior 24; SPEC-0046).
25. Resolved: R is the current position of the selected hero, read from its motion record. The first height probe is at the moved object current position; the second is at the ahead point built from R. Both use an 8.0-unit ceiling lift (Behavior 11).
26. Resolved: **Movable-block box geometry in the move check and follow-camera eye pass**: the record loop is the world movable-block list, not a region-character list. Each block links to its type-11 source entry and authored kind-4 box primitive. Both paths set the primitive center from block position data; in the move check, +0x40 is overwritten by +0x50 before the point-vs-box test. The primitive supplies its own half-extents and axes; these are not default scratch values. The follow-camera eye pass uses the same box source with a 1.25 push radius (SPEC-0027). [High; exact per-block geometry is read from its authored type-11 record]
27. **Attack volumes**. Resolved: monster weapon N (N = 1 to 5) is a finite cylinder whose radius is the row word at +4(N - 1) (schema name "Weapon N Collision Length") and whose full height is the row word at +0x14 + 4(N - 1) (schema name "Weapon N Collision Radius"), so the schema names are swapped; 40.0 is the fields' upper clamp, not a default. Each weapon is active while its window bit of table B is set and is tested against the target's bone capsules (Behavior 12A; High). The class-five contact test of SPEC-0018 is not the attack volume.
28. **Movable-block size source (Behaviors 11 and 11B)**: Resolved: the state-18 fit check and the movable-block pair scan use the same world list of 0xB0-byte movable-block records. A record's +0x68 points to its type-11 source entry, whose +0x10 authored size/clearance supplies the size to both tests. This is distinct from hero/NPC motion-record radius and from the fixed 0.25 point-box margin. **[High for shared source/read path; Medium-High for authored-size naming]**
29. Resolved in part: the known character ground-placement routine (SPEC-0018) does not directly read the placed record's vertical-velocity field. It probes the ground from 8.0 units above the placement position. The fallback choice is: the probe result counts as a hit when it is above minus 0.9e9 (a miss returns minus 1.0e9); only when it hits and the character's row Can Fly word is 0 does the placement height become the probed ground height, otherwise the placement keeps the height it was given. The signed byte in the caller record is a world selector (minus 1 means the current world), not a fallback input (High). The vertical velocity belongs to a separate world-pool ball record, whose own step uses it (SPEC-0010). No indirect virtual effect is established; that possibility remains open (High for the direct distinction, Medium for excluding indirect effects).
30. **Placement fields**: Resolved for the known ball-slot path: its +0x30 direction vector is initialized from the owning character facing and participates in the ball step; the fixed 1.0 write targets the +0x48 vertical-velocity field described in item 5, not a distinct second field. No other placement caller is known, and the rewrite uses the ball-slot path only (Behavior 13; High for the ball path).
31. **Ring slot advance**: Resolved: the slot index starts at 0 and is never advanced, so every sample overwrites slot 0; no reader of the ring by slot is known (Behavior 14; High). Test vector 172.
32. **Order of the motion-record sample**. Resolved: the sample runs inside the motion-record tick, which runs after the character's class update hook and before its skeleton pose pass (Behavior 3, loop-level order; Behavior 14, cadence; High).
33. **Motion-record travel inputs**: answered for ordinary hero and region-character travel (Behaviors 14C and 14D): animation root steps are transformed through the record basis and conditionally projected using the record's ground normal. The travel path does not read the placement vector at +0x2C0 or the camera contact-normal list; +0x120 is overwritten with the computed displacement. The travel path does not use +0x2C0, so its role is not needed, and the alternate friction branch selected by +0x2EC is specified in item 103 (Behavior 14A, items 3 and 6b; SPEC-0033, Behavior 8).
34. **Hero displayed height**: Answered: owned by SPEC-0033 (Behavior 7 and its Open questions).
35. Resolved: **Hero motion constants.** Hero ground speeds come from the clip root motion (Ground speeds; SPEC-0033 5.3): walk 13.51, run 19.29, slow walk about 5.0 and reverse 13.51 (Tor's ids 5 and 10: 3.75) units per second at playback rate 1. There is no acceleration beyond the one-tick start delay (Behavior 14C, Timing). Jumps are SPEC-0033 Behavior 50; other jump-like movement is in SPEC-0033 (leap, hop, landing branch). Falls of motion records follow Behavior 14A, item 7. No swim or climb rule is known for the shared runtime or the hero.
36. Resolved: **State 30 routine.** State 30 (hang from a ledge) is a hero route and a busy state (Behavior 15, Behavior 15.2). It is written by the in-air ledge-grab chooser of the jump states (result 0), and from state 37. The body is SPEC-0033 Behavior 51 (High).
37. **Place of the contact pass in the tick**. Resolved: a character's contacts run inside its motion-record tick (cell refresh, ground step with the moving-object contacts, stick target, pre-step (edge guard, deadly ground, ground constraint), gravity, the driver with the contact walks, integrator), after the class update hook and before the skeleton pose pass (Behavior 3, loop-level order; High). The pass with the 1.25 radius is the follow camera's and runs in the camera update. The held-item draw described in Behavior 10, items 5, 6 and 10, is separate from these character contacts; there is no character root sweep in that step.
38. **Contact pass owner**. Resolved: the pass that SPEC-0018 calls the contact pass is the follow camera's eye collision pass (SPEC-0027). Character contacts are run by the motion-record driver of each character (Behaviors 11 and 14A; High).
39. **Hittable list owner**. Resolved: the word at hero +0x174 is the grid cell record under the hero's motion record, refreshed every record tick; the hero's walks use its cell list B, which holds world trigger objects (the breakables of SPEC-0048), not characters. It is not the global hittable list of Behavior 5 (Behavior 16; High).
40. **Hit list update on contact**. Resolved: the breakable contact has no hit list. Its first gate is the script-breakable hook keyed by +0x58, and it starts no effect by id (Behavior 16; High).
41. **Breakable volume tests**. Resolved: volume kind 1 is a sphere, kind 2 is never hit, kind 3 is a finite cylinder (swept once the weapon tip has moved, SPEC-0036 Hit detection step 6), and kinds 4 and 7 are rectangles with a static test for a shape value of 0 or below and a moving test above 0 (Behavior 16; SPEC-0036, Attack sweeps). Both branches directly use the no-margin segment-versus-rectangle helper in SPEC-0018: segment parameter 0 to 1 and in-plane coordinates 0 to each extent; neither uses its margin helper. The static branch tests one rectangle candidate. The moving branch tests five candidates in this order, reusing the same segment endpoints: high B, low B, high A, low A, and bottom. Using the box convention of SPEC-0018 (low-corner origin o; axes A, B, V; extents eA, eB, eV), the candidate frame origins and outward normals are (o + eB·B, +B), (o + eA·A, −B), (o + eA·A + eB·B, +A), (o, −A), and (o, −V). Each candidate is a zero-thickness face rectangle spanned by the other two box axes and extents. The top face is not tested. For example, with o=(10,20,30), A=(1,0,0), B=(0,0,1), V=(0,1,0), eA=2, eB=3 and eV=4, the five frame origins are (10,20,33), (12,20,30), (12,20,33), (10,20,30), and (10,20,30); normals are +B, −B, +A, −A and −V. Consequence: a swing that moves into a box from above only (a segment that crosses just the top face) never hits it, while a swing crossing any of the other five faces does; the reason the top face is omitted is not a program fact, so reproduce the five-face rule as stated.
42. **Class codes**. Resolved: the code tested by the contact handlers is the breakable's element byte (+0x5E), compared with the attacker's Mote kind 2 to 7 (Behavior 16, handler table; High).
43. **Knockback**: Answered: SPEC-0036 (Knockback on the hero) gives the common-enemy knockback; bosses follow their own specs. No shared contact or charm routine applies one.
44. **Charm and contact callers**. Resolved: the contact handlers are called from the attack-family sweeps and from the hero-list walks (Behavior 16). Each handler belongs to one attack family of SPEC-0036 (Contact routines: mapping; family A to Roberta Lava, Tor Water, Tor Nova and the Tor Rock sub-shots, B to Tor Lava and Roberta Supernova, C to Roberta Shadow and Sun, D to Tor Shadow, E to Tor Sun, F to Roberta Rock). None is tied to a hero state: the objects are spawned by the full-press release (Tor sub-state 10, Roberta sub-state 9) and are then ticked by the attack-family driver on every pass of play whatever the hero is doing, so a handler runs for as long as its object lives (High for the callers; Medium-High for the mapping).
45. **Attack volume source**. Resolved: the four-corner beam volume is a cylinder of radius 1.0 (0x2DCB3C) and length 5.0 (0x2DCB40) along the beam, tested against the target's body cylinder. The attacker-side volumes are the weapon cylinders on the attachment frames and the family volumes; the hurt shapes are the bone capsules and the body cylinder (Behavior 12A; High for the values, Medium-High for the roles).
46. **Shot start gates and starting count**. Resolved: both gates are the beam's active test: the life is above 0, both effect handles exist, and the segment is running. The full release starts a beam only when it is not active. The per-tick update runs only when it is active. The starting count is the beam life, trunc(5.0 × 60) = 300 ticks, so the walks run on 299 ticks (Behavior 17). **[High]**
47. **Shot owner and hit test**. Resolved: the active hero (Roberta) owns both objects. The beam's reference point is the hero's query point. Both objects test characters directly, each with its own character walk over the active character list, and then run the breakable walk of Behavior 16:
    - Resolved (High): the beam hits every hittable entry whose body cylinder meets the segment, through a ten-slot hit list with a 60-tick re-hit window. An eleventh distinct target inside the window is not hit; a target that stays inside the beam is hit again 60 ticks after its previous hit. Test vectors: Edge cases (Beam hit list full) and Test vectors 168 and 169.
    - Resolved (High): a rock hits the first hittable entry, in active-list order, whose body cylinder meets its sphere, and then ends; a second overlapping entry on the same tick is not hit. Test vector: Test vectors 170.
    - Heroes are never on that list, so there is no friendly fire (Behavior 17). **[High]**
48. **Shot velocity**. Resolved: the step is the Rock skipping shot's tick. Each tick the rock moves by its flat unit direction times the speed 0.6 ± 0.3 units per tick (words 0x2DCB88 and 0x2DCB8C), plus its vertical speed, which starts at 1.0 and changes by −0.1 per tick. The 250.0 rock limit and the 20.0 beam length are fixed program constants (Behavior 17; SPEC-0036). **[High]**
49. **Busy test meaning**: Resolved: the 41 tests are tests of the hero's action state, and with its three queries (script-held states, stun timer, screen effect) the busy test of Behavior 18 is IsHeroOccupied (Behavior 15.2). The note that it differs from a separate hero busy list is withdrawn: only one routine exists. It is separate from the action-lock predicate of Behavior 4. **[High]**
50. **Attack-state test meaning**: Resolved: it is a hero class method. Tor attacks in action state 13 with sub-state 2 to 12, or in states 7 to 11 with air phase 8 or 9; Roberta only in state 13 with sub-state 5 or 12; non-heroes never. There is no fall-through (Behavior 18). **[High]**
51. **Beam and bubble counts**: Answered: the count is a 60-tick re-hit timer (Behavior 27; Open question 118).
52. Resolved: **Timed-release turn method**. The class-37 recovery routine turns toward its target each tick through the shared aim rule of Behavior 8, with weight 0.25. The general rule needs no correction. See SPEC-0029, Behavior 7. **[High]**
53. **Resolved: apparent boss heading cap** (Behavior 8). The owner is the Cloud Beast lightning effect controller. The capped scalar is the distance lane of the normalized direction result, not a heading component; its per-object step is initialized from the Cloud Beast lightning tuning word 500.0. The common facing update has no corresponding boss-only cap. **[High]**
54. Resolved: the class-query implementations are specified under Behavior 17. The shared predicate has a five-state denylist and requires the invulnerability flag +0x7F0 to be clear (SPEC-0030, SetEntityInvulnerable); hero and region-character overrides add their own deny gates before delegating. An eligible candidate must also pass a center segment check and two parallel checks offset by 1.0 in either direction perpendicular to the query. Each check rejects when adjusted endpoint heights differ by more than 50 or when an eligible object intersects either queried region; result 1 means blocked and 0 means clear. The helper raises both endpoints by 1.5, and the picker separately raises its hero-side endpoint by 1.5. Its mode 0 leaves class-6 objects eligible (Behavior 17). The NPC override's four remaining guards are now operationally specified: a configuration-dependent action/animation gate keyed by the monster-parameter row's schema-labeled “Display in Default” field (+0x82), with a selected-hero actor-record exception; two checks for the spawn row's +0xAC value through the two subsets of the shared PetPet type-to-kind lookup (covering kinds 0, 1, 3, 6, 7, 8 and 2, 4, 5, 9; the first also matches the active hero); and a veto for current animation ids 0x4A, 0x4B and 0x4C. The known actor-table constructor graph assigns base, common-character, hero, and region-character/NPC tables. Hero and region-character constructors replace the common-character table before returning; the base table is only seen in initialization and destructor resets, and its +0xCC word is not an established standalone eligibility method. The known behavior-class setter does not change the actor table. No additional actor-table assignment appears in the known constructor graph. Resolved: a search of the whole program finds each class table built at one constructor only (common-character, hero and region-character once each; the base table at five sites that give it to other object kinds), so no other eligibility implementation exists. The five shared denied action states are 16, 33, 36, 53 and 54. Test vector: a candidate in action state 33 is rejected by the shared predicate; a candidate in state 1 with +0x7F0 clear passes it and goes on to the segment checks; with +0x7F0 set it is rejected. 
55. Resolved: reactions 6 and 8 are the common-enemy missile dodge and missile block (outer agro sub-states 6 and 8, SPEC-0038 Behavior 44). The two percents are the floats at +0xE4 and +0xE8 of the enemy class parameter block, and the cooldown is the whole number at +0xE0, which is also the look-ahead in ticks (Behavior 16; test vector 26). **[High]**
56. **Spread and blend**: Answered in part: SPEC-0036, Roberta shot projectile, Launch, specifies the two-draw side/up random fan used by aim modes 2 to 5, including its unnormalized result and test vectors. The +3.0 spread bonus tests the aim owner's nonzero attack-manager hold flag by actor id (SPEC-0038 Behavior 45), and S is the current attack k's aim-inaccuracy value at the selected monster row's +0x30C + 4k (SPEC-0029, Layout section 4). The reach caller uses the shared geometry routine's Boolean hit result and ignores that routine's scalar output; the earlier proposed comparison against the two radii minus 1.5 is not present in this call path. The unused scalar's general geometric meaning remains open.
57. Resolved: **Tick word and time scale.** The motion update, including its driver, runs once every tick at 60 ticks per second (Behavior 14, cadence; High). The time scale 1/30 at 0x3A1A74 is a per-step factor, not a cadence: each tick the velocity gains the force divided by the weight times 1/30, and the position gains the velocity times 1/30 (Behavior 14A, item 6). The physics block is initialized once at start-up from fixed program values and has no later writer, so the factor remains 1/30; see SPEC-0007 for the tick rate.
58. **Divisor**: Resolved: there is no per-element step. The divisor of the motion update is the motion record's weight at +0x04, which divides the force (Layout, body size; Behavior 14A items 5 and 6). It cancels from gravity. **[High]**
59. **Slope response**: Resolved: there is no ground snap and no slope response. The routine described under that name is the hero's per-record wall contact and ledge guard (Behavior 19; SPEC-0018). **[High]**
60. **Candidate height**: Resolved: there is no snap and no candidate height. The ledge guard compares the ground 3.0 ahead (or 3.0 times the displacement ahead) with the hero's feet, and a drop of more than 2.0 blocks the hero (Behavior 19; SPEC-0018). **[High]**
61. **Timer 0.75**: Resolved: it is not a timer. In the ledge hand-off after the wall contact (Behavior 19; SPEC-0018 and SPEC-0033), the edge acceptance path sets a latch flag and keeps the larger of its stored edge value and the edge distance plus 0.75, then requests hero state 10. The 0.75 is that margin. **[Medium]**
62. **Animation code 10 and the snap trigger**: Resolved: there is no snap trigger. The hero states that the wall contact hands off to (10, 11 and 37) are requested by the ledge-drop and edge acceptance tests of SPEC-0033 that run after it (Behavior 19). **[High]**
63. **Per-element gate states**: Resolved: the states 21, 23, 30, 35, 37, 38, 41 and 47 are hero action states in which the hero skips its per-record wall contact; there are no elements and no pending element displacement (Behavior 19; SPEC-0018). **[High]**
64. **Candidate lists**: Resolved: there is one hero candidate list, described in Behavior 20; the 1.0e9 nearest search and same-group test belong to the enemy target latch (SPEC-0029), not to a second hero list. The collection scanned by the same-group test is the per-pass active character list of Behavior 17 (up to 45 region characters; the count word at 0x35C8F8 and the pointer word at 0x35C8FC). The test skips characters in state 16 or 42, characters for which the dying test of the candidate test is true, and the searcher itself. A listed character c at list position i makes the test true when its group value (+0x9BC, the character's navigation-mesh index) equals the searcher's and its stored index (+0x9B4) equals i. The cell index that the search passes to the test is not used by it, so the result is the same for every cell of one search (High). The search walks the cells of the searcher's group in the world's group table (up to the cell count of that group), measures the 3D distance from each cell's centre (the mean of three corner points) to the target point, and for each cell strictly nearer than the best so far (starting from 1.0e9, so the lower cell wins a tie) accepts it only when the test is false; an accepted cell index is stored in two target fields (+0x9B0 and +0x9B4). When the test is true no cell is accepted and the fields are unchanged. The spawn-record type test (the u16 at +0xAC of the character's spawn record) is the primary petpet type table: the ids 450, 703, 1314, 1460, 2090 and 2094 map to petpet kinds 0, 1, 3, 6, 7 and 8, and every other id gives a negative result. A hero candidate must give a negative result, so wild petpets are never candidates and are never locked on (High). Test vectors: a spawn type of 450 gives kind 0, so the character is not a candidate; type 109 (Whinny) gives a negative result, so it can be a candidate if it passes the other tests. Search (synthetic): a group with cells 0, 1 and 2 at distances 6.0, 4.0 and 4.0 from the target point, and no conflicting character: cell 1 is accepted (cell 2 ties and loses). With one same-group character at list position 3 whose stored index is 3: the test is true, no cell is accepted, and the fields keep their old values. **[High for the collection, the exclusions and the type test; Medium-High for the search]**
65. **Lock and nearest search**: Resolved: there is no separate nearest search in the hero's lock; the list is ordered (in-range first, then by distance with a 1.0 margin) and the head record is the lock, with a forced target and a held target taking priority. The 1.0e9 search belongs to the enemy target latch (Behavior 20). **[Medium-High]**
66. **Facing words**. Resolved: the turn uses the shared aim rule of Behavior 8 (v = w d + (1 − w) f, plus 0.1 times the side axis when dot(f, d) < −0.99, then normalised), with weight 1.0 when the horizontal distance is strictly below 4.0 (a fixed program constant) and 0.25 (0x2DD58C) otherwise. The fallback target is the nearest intact breakable in reach (Tor only), not a candidate. The words 0x2DD69C, 0x2DD6A0 and 0x2DD698 belong to the hero's head look-at point (Behavior 20, item 7).
67. **State 30 route**: Answered: state 30 is the ledge hang of SPEC-0033 (Behavior 51).
68. **Hero routes not described here**: Answered: owned by SPEC-0033 (Behaviors 3 and 51). The default route of the action-state switch (states 0, 31, 42 and above 60) requests animation 0 and calls a routine that only prints a debug message; it resets nothing (High, from code; Behavior 15.3).
69. **Predicates P1 and P2 of the hero locomotion**: Answered: owned by SPEC-0033 (P1 is the weapon-out flag; P2 stays open there).
70. Answered: the boot word is the rate table entry read once at start-up (SPEC-0007; SPEC-0008, Rate words). Character code uses the derived run-time words, not the table.
71. **Move goal, mode 2 speed limit**: Resolved: there is no speed limit; mode 2 is Can Fly 2, whose goal height is the hero's (or the home) height capped at ground + Max Fly Height (Behavior 21). **[High]**
72. **Move goal, step term**: Resolved: 0.2 is not a step limit; it multiplies the horizontal distance to the hero in the Can Fly 1 goal height (Behavior 21). **[High]**
73. **Move goal, other modes and callers**: Resolved. The goal clamp reads the character row's Can Fly value: 1 and 2 have the height rules in Behavior 21; all other values leave the goal unchanged. The 259 monster rows in resource 530 contain only values 0, 1 and 2. The separate AI mode 10/sub-state 6 cap is also described in Behavior 21. The clamp's only direct caller is the navigation-target setter, which reaches it when either of two virtual class tests succeeds. Resolved: the first test is the flying test (row Can Fly = 1 and the grounded countdown +0xBF4 not positive) and the second is the row Can Fly = 2 test, both implemented only in the common-character method table (every character built by the common-character constructor: common enemies and bosses). The hero table and the region-character (NPC) table answer 0 to both, so the clamp never runs for heroes or townsfolk; characters with Can Fly 0 pass neither. The setter has about 65 direct callers, all in enemy, boss and NPC AI routines, and no data word anywhere in the program points to it, so there is no indirect path. Test vector: a Can Fly 1 row with +0xBF4 = 0 passes the first test and runs the clamp; the same row with +0xBF4 = 30 passes neither test (Can Fly is 1, not 2) and the goal is unchanged. **[High]**
74. **Emote consumer**: Resolved: the consumer runs once per tick only in state 25; for NPCs, it runs inside the character update before the motion-record step and animation pass. The hero's state-25 routine also calls the consumer inside the hero update, before the later animation hookup. Therefore the consumer evaluates and may pop a one-shot face emote at 0.99 before the hookup checks for automatic override reversion; the hookup sees any override value the consumer left. The head entry is removed by the pop rule (-2, or the matching clip looping with a second entry, or at progress 0.99); an empty queue means id 0; a clip starts only on a body restart or a new face emote and is not retried when refused; the volume is the Voice option's gain; the queues are cleared only at spawn and at SetConversationTarget (Behavior 22; High). This settles execution order, not the design intent of the face pose after the queue changes. **[High]**
75. **Emote stream format**: Answered: owned by SPEC-0009 (Open questions).
76. **Stored facing to the body basis**: Resolved (High for the hero, Medium for other characters). The hero's motion record rebuilds its basis from the stored facing on every record tick, right after the facing update (step 4 of Cadence and order): forward B3 is the facing flattened to the horizontal and normalised, side B1 = normalise((0, 1, 0) x B3) (or (1, 0, 0) for a vertical facing) and up B2 = B3 x B1 (SPEC-0033, Ground speed: root motion of the playing clip). So the body forward equals the stored facing whenever the facing is horizontal, and differs from it only by the removed vertical part and the normalisation. The fourth word of the stored block is not read by that rebuild. Test vector: stored facing (3, 1, 4) gives forward (0.6, 0, 0.8).
77. Resolved: strafe modes 5 and 9 use normalise(up × d), with the constant world-up vector (0, 1, 0, 0); modes 6 and 10 use its negative. Thus modes 5/9 are the code's “one side” and 6/10 the opposite (Behavior 8a; High).
78. Resolved: the 0.9 snap test reads the motion record's forward basis B3 through the accessor at +0x2B0 (Behavior 14C); refer to it as the motion-record forward. Equality with the separate rendered body basis is not established here (Behavior 8; High for the source).
79. Resolved: **Degenerate blend** (Behavior 8a). There is no guard. The normalise divides by the length with the console's vector divide, which returns the largest finite number for a zero divisor, and zero times that is zero; a zero-length vector therefore stays the zero vector and never becomes a not-a-number. In play the blend can be zero only if w is 1.0 and the target is zero; the callers pass 0.1 or 0.3, and the strafe snap to 1.0 happens only when the target has a dot product above 0.9 with the body forward (so the target is nonzero). A zero-length move direction (or a strafe target from a vertical direction) with w below 1 gives v = (1 - w) c, which normalises back to the stored facing: the facing is unchanged. Test vector: mode 0, w 0.3, move direction (0, 0, 0), stored facing (0, 0, 1) gives v = (0, 0, 0.7) and new facing (0, 0, 1). **[Medium-High]**
80. Resolved in part: modes 0 to 2 are forward gaits and mode 7 is the forward flyer form; modes 8 to 10 are the flyer backward/strafe forms. Character setup initializes the mode to 0; runtime mode changes pass through the shared mode setter, which stores the requested mode or, for Can Fly 1/2 while not grounded, remaps input modes 0 to 6 through the flyer-mode table. Known boss controllers write modes 3, 5 and 6; class-17 enemies select gait modes. Resolved (High for the direct callers): the flyer remap is exactly requests 0, 1 and 2 to mode 7, 3 and 4 to mode 8, 5 to mode 9 and 6 to mode 10, applied only when the row's Can Fly value is 1 or 2, the grounded countdown is below 1 and the request is below 7 (Behavior 8a). The boot program calls the shared mode setter from 95 places. Constant requests: 0 from 53, 2 from 12, 5 from 6, 6 from 6, 3 from 3 (one Ixi Chief routine and two places in the common-enemy behaviour of class 17) and 1 from 2. Ten places pass the value returned by a getter on the character's own movement block (a restore of an earlier mode; Medium), one stores the character's current mode word again, and two pass computed values. No direct call requests mode 4 or any value above 6, so mode 4 is never requested by a direct call and a Can Fly 1 or 2 character never reaches mode 8 through a request of 4 unless a computed value is 4; for a walker, mode 4 steers like mode 3 (backward, Behavior 8a) and has no other use. Modes 3 and 4 need no finer labels than "backward". Calls made through a class table rather than directly are not part of this count. [High for the survey of direct calls; Medium for the computed values]
81. Resolved: goal-move uses weight 0.1 when the current mesh lookup has no triangle and on route-edge steering; it uses 0.3 only in the settled current-triangle = target-triangle branch when arrival/block gates do not fire. A missing target triangle does not steer. The known path-segment route-step passes 0.1 at its steering call (Behavior 8a).
82. Resolved (High for the call survey; Medium for per-state reachability): **Boss heading step and steering**. The steering rule of Behavior 8a has exactly three call sites: two inside the generic goal-move routine and one inside the generic path-segment routine. The path-segment routine has exactly one caller, which is the goal-move routine. The goal-move routine has 65 call sites, in the Black Knight, the Draik Skeleton, the Gelert Assassin, the Ixi Chief and Juppie, the Werelupe King, the other boss controllers, the common-enemy routines and the NPC routines. None of the three routines is referenced by a data word or built as an address for an indirect call, so no other path reaches the rule. Every boss turn that goes through the generic goal-move routine therefore uses the steering rule of Behavior 8a, and no boss calls the rule directly. Which states of each boss call the goal-move routine on a given tick is stated in that boss's own spec. The boss-only heading step of Behavior 8 is a separate rule (the Cloud Beast lightning distance update) and does not turn any character. Test vector: a boss in a movement mode 5 (strafe) asked to steer with move direction (1, 0, 0) and stored facing (0, 0, 1) takes the strafe branch of Behavior 8a: target direction normalise((0, 1, 0) x (1, 0, 0)) = (0, 0, -1).
83. **Routes 51, 55, 59 and 60**: Resolved: all four write the action state through the state setter, not an animation request: 51 and 55 go to 33 or 1 by the control-flag word, 59 and 60 go to 1 (High). SPEC-0033 Behavior 3 should state them as state writes.
84. **Hero flag word at +0x3D0**: Resolved: it is the control-flag word (CTL) of SPEC-0030. PlayHeroAnim, LoopHeroAnim, the hero walk calls and the entity walk and loop calls store 1 when CTL is 1, else 0; character set-up stores 1. With 1 the hero returns to state 1 at the end of the action; with 0 it stays in the script hold, state 33 (High; Medium-High for the hero walk calls).
85. **Hero input code word**: Resolved: it is the default idle animation (+0x938). SetDefaultHeroIdleAnim stores it (SetDefaultEntityIdleAnim on a named entity); state 25 and the character reset clear it to 0; state 33 requests it every tick (High). Test vector 176.
86. **State 36**: Resolved: a debug animation preview driven by the debug animation record of the pad object; for a non-matching id it calls the state setter with 1 (Behavior 15.3; Medium-High).
87. **State 59 action test**: Resolved: the test is "the current animation is 175"; state 60 tests 137 (High). Test vector 177.
88. **State 26**: Answered: SPEC-0033 gives it as the statue turn, present but unreachable in play.
89. **Timed charge**: Resolved: it is the Faerie Sisters' hit routine; kind = sister state, step = damage, R = current HP, gate word = the shared shield flag (Behavior 15.1a; SPEC-0011). Animation 0x36 is not used by it. **[High]**
90. Resolved: on a shielded melee hit, the first helper receives the selected hero's world position and body-forward vector to prepare the one-shot shield-effect 4008 frame; the second helper sets Tor's sub-state to 13 and does nothing for Roberta. On a shielded shot, the routine requests hero action state 1 and sets the shared sisters' shield word at 0x36DE34 (+0x1D4); this is not a per-sister flag (Behavior 15.1a). [High]

91. Resolved: the 1.75 scalar belongs to a record of the world pool at +0x4260, not to a character motion record, and nothing in the boot program reads it (Behavior 13, World pool record set-up). **[High]**
92. Resolved: the -1 word is at +0x15C of the same world pool record. It is not the character lock index of Behavior 20, and nothing reads it (Behavior 13). **[High]**
93. Resolved: the 8.0 and 8.0 helper builds a square patch of 8 by 8 units on the record frame and stores three planes and the two sizes. It does not touch the character move checks of Behavior 11 (Behavior 13; test vector 46). **[Medium-High]**
94. Resolved: the three axis rows go to +0x70, +0x80 and +0x90 and the position to +0xA0, with the same four rows copied to +0x20 to +0x50 (Behavior 13). **[Medium-High]**
95. Resolved: no order question arises for characters, because the set-up is not a character step. It runs only when a world pool record is taken, and nothing takes one in play (Behavior 13). **[High]**
96. Resolved for known boot-image paths: the three hero per-slot backing arrays start at 0x32E880 (sub-object), 0x333C50 (motion record) and 0x333B60 (locomotion block), with strides 0x1BA0, 0x760 and 0x50. The start-up initializer assigns their separate base-pointer globals; hero records are in the separate two-entry table at 0x335270, stride 0x1680. Hero and region-character constructors both reach the shared slot-binding step; region characters use an owning-block sub-object pool. Runtime-loaded or indirect callers beyond the known boot-image paths remain outside this survey. **[High for arrays and known paths]**
97. **Character table reset**: Resolved for the fixed level-lifecycle path: record setup calls the reset during initialization; the matching release branch destroys the two records and does not call the reset. If release and initialization run in sequence, reset follows teardown. Other callers include world-character pool creation. Whether every reset is preceded by teardown needs a runtime check; default if unresolved (rewrite choice): always tear down the old records before a reset, as the release-then-initialize order does (Behavior 2A; Medium-High for the lifecycle branches).
98. **Action-end resolver callers**: Resolved: the arrivals of the hero's scripted walks and the hero's navmesh walk-to-point helpers (a copy of the rule); routes 51 and 55 make the same test themselves (Behavior 15.4; High).
99. **Idle look timer**: Answered: it is the right-stick aim mode of SPEC-0036 (Right-stick aim mode), which gives the start, the end, the counter reset and the stick bytes (Behavior 23).
100. **Run-time words**: Resolved: the frame-rate word (0x368FA0, 60), tick-length word (0x368FA8, 1/60 s) and half-rate word (0x368FA4, 0.5) are written at start-up from the boot rate table (SPEC-0007, Per-tick words; Where it lives). **[High]**
101. **Motion-record driver, branch 0**: Resolved in part: the branch is chosen by the owner's class (0 heroes, 1 region characters, 2 petpet), and branch 0 clears the touched, object-wall and floor flags and runs the steps listed in Behavior 14A, item 1. Branch 1's final step is the movable-block contact pass after its owner pre-step, navmesh confinement, character push and cell-list-B walk; it initializes +0x184 to 1.0e9, returns early for owner states 18/19, and otherwise writes the nearest qualifying block index to +0x180 and the square-root distance result to +0x184. Each block response skips owner states 58, 41 and 30, and requires an active block with a box. See Behavior 14A, item 1 and the movable-block contact pass. Resolved: the hero pre-step clears +0x9B4 on every call; only when +0x9D8 is zero and the state is not 30, 35, 37, 38 or 47 does it also reset +0x7C8, clear +0x9D4 through the target setter, and clear +0xA14/+0xA18 (Behavior 14A, item 1). This means an eligible later block contact can refresh +0x9B4 for the next hero update; it is not persistent until area entry. The reset fields have state-scoped uses: +0xA18 is the phase selector for states 37 and 38; +0xA14 supplies the action/sub-kind selector read by their shared approach helper. State 37 writes +0xA14 from its action choice (values 1, 2, 3, 4 or 6); state 38 starts it at 3. The direct known readers/writers found here are those state paths and this pre-step (SPEC-0033, Behaviors 15 and 51; High for the field use). **[High for order and gates; Medium-High for contact response]**
102. **Contact dispatch helpers**: Resolved: kind-1 sphere, kind-2 cone, kind-3 cylinder and kind-4/7 branch inputs, geometry, class-bit ordering, retry-point math, event argument rule and hit routes, plus the list-B entry gate, are described in Behavior 14A, item 2. The kind-2 list-B helper is concrete but has no static kind-2 type-29 placement in the 192 area-pack survey; the cell-list-A dispatcher independently routes cones. The hero's kind-2 owner predicate returns 0; for the other kinds its entry-kind landing-break test can break the entry and skip dispatch, while other character owners return 0.
103. Resolved in part: the segment-step helper previously grouped here belongs to a separate 12-entry update path; it does not read +0x2EC or the history ring, and it does not write the character position. Its probe/half-offset semantics are covered under item 104. The character +0x2EC flag is set by moving-platform penetration, cleared before the physics core, and selects an alternate friction velocity update: zero selects the ordinary update and any nonzero value selects the alternate update. Both paths first form predicted velocity u = v + (q + F) k / W, with L = |u.xyz|, then compute s = input × k / W and replace s with −L when |s| > L. The scaled direction supplied to the shared velocity-add helper is s × (u.x/L, u.y/L, u.z/L, L); the fourth lane is the length L, not zero. Resolved: the alternate branch keeps the vertical velocity. After the helper has added its increment, the velocity is rebuilt as (x of the helper result, y of the velocity before the helper, z of the helper result, w of the helper result), so friction never changes the vertical component on this branch while the ordinary branch lets the increment's y part change it. Synthetic helper vector: u = (3, 4, 0, any), s = −6 gives L = 5, bounded s = −5 and helper increment (−3, −4, 0, −25). From prior velocity (8, 7, 6, 2), the helper result is (5, 3, 6, −23); the ordinary branch keeps that tuple, and the alternate branch stores (5, 7, 6, −23). The flag does not select a history block (Behavior 14A, items 3, 4, 5c and 6b; High for branch gate, helper vector and the alternate lane selection; Medium for helper call semantics).
104. **Segment probe**: Resolved in part: a known point-construction helper reads the owner's motion-record position and locomotion frame vectors; it combines the first vector with half of the second and adds one quarter of a frame scalar to the vertical lane. The direct-call graph has no caller for this helper, so it does not establish that Behavior 14A item 4 uses it. A separate extent/clamp helper reads the same owner data but takes two caller-supplied vectors; its nine known callers do not prove a link to the point-construction helper. The shared three-probe reach routine has callers across combat and non-combat paths, but no link to this segment-step path is established. The point-construction helper has no caller, so the segment-step probe is not built from it; the segment-step owner and its owner-vector block are not needed by any rule in the specs. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): leave the segment-step probe out and use the wall and ground rules of Behavior 14A (needs a runtime check for any case where it matters) (Behavior 14A item 4).
105. Resolved: **Skeleton pose pass joints**. The three pointer slots for input lists and the three matching output-matrix slots, their record sizes and family order are given in Behavior 3, step 5. SPEC-0005, Families and Posing a skeleton, gives the family index mapping and composition. **[High for the pointer and record roles; Medium for one face-index boundary]**
106. **Chain bodies**: Resolved: mode 0 is Gnarfas only (four chains of 4, 4, 5 and 5 links), mode 1 Tor only (a 5-link tail), mode 2 Roberta only (two 4-link head chains); the tuning values are the pendulum spring, rest angles, limits, velocity keep and drag of Behavior 25. **[High]**
107. **Chain body last part**: Resolved: the last link's seed is read from the 16 bytes before the main joint matrix buffer and only shapes the first tick (Behavior 25, Setup and seed quirk). **[High]**
108. **Chain body defaults**: Resolved: the four 0x60-byte records are the playback records, with clip 0, weight 0, fade step 1.0, speed 0.5 and a zero root step and accumulator (Behavior 25, Defaults). **[High]**
109. **Contact slots**. Resolved: they are the character's four light slots; the weight, vector, free-slot, displacement and cross-fade rules are those of SPEC-0046 (Character lights, Character light slots), summarised in Behavior 24. No cooldown timer exists: the value set beyond 0.9 r is the displaced light's faded weight.
110. **Contact slot callers**. Resolved: the evaluator is called by the character light walk (set-up and once per pass, Behavior 24), its own-entry step and its dynamic-light step, and by the scene light context set-up.
111. **Run-time colour table writer**: Resolved: the table at 0x32CE44 is written only by a PC-link debug tuner that has no caller, so it stays zero in play and its apply never runs (Behavior 26, item 7). **[High]**
112. **Preset trailing bytes and mapping**: Resolved: the loader copies 15 triples, triple k to colour word k; the triple at offset 42 is the fifteenth colour (white in every preset) and triple 0 is black (Behavior 26, item 2). **[High]**
113. **Preset selection caller and range**: Resolved: the caller is the kind-6 spell contact, which draws the preset from 1 to 6; there is no range check, and none is needed (Behavior 26, item 3). **[High]**
114. **Second preset value at +0xA48**: Resolved: +0xA48 is the character's row-530 pointer, re-read from (type, variant) after the variant changes (Behavior 26, item 3; SPEC-0029). **[High]**
115. **Render side of the palette push**: Resolved: the push targets are the 15 colour slot records that SPEC-0034, section 21.4, blends (Behavior 26, item 4). **[High]**
116. **Lane order and fourth byte of the apply**: Resolved: the apply belongs to the dormant debug tuner and never runs. The push lane order is (byte 0, byte 1, byte 2) / 255 with w = 1 (Behavior 26, items 4 and 7). **[High]**
117. **Preset index and class 17 offset**: Resolved: the block is the spawn record, +0xB2 is its variant field, and the class 17 reads are fields of the same record, so there is no conflict (Behavior 26, items 1 and 8). **[High]**
118. **Timed target lists**: Resolved: free means id -1; each owner counts every occupied slot down once per tick before its walks and frees it at 0; the +0x114 word is the timer of the singleton item effect object of the Starlight Potion, which sits 2.0 above the hero, starts its effects when the timer ends and deals 100 damage to Minion-corps characters (Behavior 27, items 4 and 5; High for the owner and the call, Medium-High for the geometry). Test vectors 175 and 179.
119. **Tracked-point dispatch caller**: Answered: none. No routine calls the dispatcher of Behavior 8b and no code pointer refers to it, so the settle family is dormant in the shipped build (High).
120. Answered: the dispatcher that consumes this mode has no caller in the shipped build, so the word has no gameplay effect (Behavior 8b; see Open question 119).
121. Answered: these tracked-point reports and ease slots belong to the dispatcher with no caller in the shipped build; they are not runtime inputs during play (Behavior 8b; Open question 119).
122. Answered: the settle details are unreachable because the Behavior 8b dispatcher has no caller in the shipped build (Open question 119).
123. Answered: the mode-0 output is unreachable because the Behavior 8b dispatcher has no caller in the shipped build (Open question 119).
124. Answered: the four-point ease is unreachable because the Behavior 8b dispatcher has no caller in the shipped build (Open question 119).
125. Answered: the heading solve is unreachable because the Behavior 8b dispatcher has no caller in the shipped build (Open question 119).
126. **Gelert mapping**: Answered: SPEC-0013 section 4.14 is a reference only, since the dispatcher is dormant (Open question 119).
127. Resolved: the root accumulator is cleared when a clip starts or changes and is not reset on a loop wrap; the wrap tick also adds the last key's root record (SPEC-0005, Playback 3 and 5; High).
128. **Look-at units**: Resolved: radians; the angles feed half-angle quaternion sines and cosines (Behavior 23A). **[High]**
129. **Look-at pitch formula**: Resolved: the pitch target is acos(horizontal direction · full direction), negated when the target is above (Behavior 23A, step 3). **[High]**
130. Resolved: **Look-at reference vector.** The vector at 0x3932E0 that sets the sign of the pitch is the unit up vector (0, 1, 0, 0) (SPEC-0007; Where it lives).
131. **Look-at reset order**: Resolved: the reset flag sets both angles to 0 in place of the rate step, the limits then apply, and the axes come from joint 0 (Behavior 23A, steps 4 to 6). **[High]**
132. **Look-at caller**: Resolved: the hero's head (joint 6 measured from joint 4) and the Plague Serpent's six neck joints (list at 0x320F48), which turn about the up axis only (Behavior 23A). **[High]**
133. **Step and ledge test**: Resolved: the test below is the second of the two plane tests of Behavior 19: the hero's object-panel contact (type-10 object walls and movable-block walls) runs it, and its normal and distance go to the push response. The hero's frame-record contact uses the first test (with the top-edge window), not this one. Inputs: the hero's radius r (motion record +0x08) and height H (+0x0C); the current position P (+0x50, fourth lane 1); the position one tick earlier B (P minus the displacement vector at +0x60 times the tick scale); and a frame record with a plane normal N (record +0x10, whose fourth lane is the plane offset), a side axis U (+0x20), an up axis V (+0x30), a side length LU (+0x40), an up length LV (+0x44) and a sign byte (+0x4C). The wall contact calls it in one-sided mode. Let dP = N · P and dB = N · B be the signed plane distances (offset lane included), u and v the same kind of products with U and V, and LV' = LV − 1.1 when the sign byte is 0 or more (otherwise LV).
- Behind the plane: when dP and dB are both negative and N's vertical part is below 0.9, there is no hit (one-sided mode).
- Near-horizontal record (the absolute vertical part of N strictly above 0.9): the window is w = 0.05 when the vertical part is 0 or more and w = H when it is negative (a ceiling). A hit needs −2w < dP < w, and P or B must have u in [−r, LU + r] and v in [−r, LV' + r]. The output distance is 0.
- Any other record (a wall): a hit needs dP ≤ r with dB > r, or dP ≥ r with dB < r, or |dP| ≤ r; and P or B must have u in [−r, LU + r] and v in [−r, LV' − 0.01]. The output distance is r − dP, the penetration into the shell of thickness r (negative when the segment has crossed from beyond it).
- In both cases the output normal is N, and the contact response gets the pair (normal, distance). The primitive reads no step height and writes no height: stepping up and ledge behaviour are the guard of Behavior 19 (the ground 3.0 ahead more than 2.0 below the feet) and the record-top rules there.
Test vectors (r = 1.175, H = 3.5, record large enough in u and v): a wall with N = (0, 0, 1) and offset 0 and P at z = 1.0, B at z = 1.3: dP = 1.0 ≤ r, hit, distance 0.175. P at z = 2.0, B at z = 0.9: dP ≥ r and dB < r, hit, distance −0.825. P and B both at z = 2.0: no hit. A floor N = (0, 1, 0) with P at 0.04 above the plane: hit, distance 0; at 0.06 above: no hit; the window is −0.1 < dP < 0.05 (both ends excluded), so 0.09 below is a hit and exactly 0.1 below is not. A ceiling N = (0, −1, 0): the window is −7.0 < dP < 3.5. Sign byte 0 or more and LV = 5: LV' = 3.9. **[High for the inputs, windows and outputs read directly; Medium-High for the meaning of the previous position B and for the one-sided flag]** Boundary and relevance: the cutoff is strict. With a unit normal whose vertical component is exactly +0.9 or -0.9, the record uses the wall branch; +0.90001 uses the upward-facing near-horizontal band and -0.90001 uses the ceiling band. For a point P = B on the plane and inside its extents, the wall branch returns distance r, whereas either near-horizontal branch returns 0. Every top-level frame record has |N.y| at most 0.2 (Open question 207), so frame-record contact uses the wall branch; the near-horizontal bands remain relevant to the object-panel contacts described above.
134. **Low-health warning (hero update step 2)**: Answered: SPEC-0036 (Low-health warning): current health below maximum health x 0.15, tested every hero update tick.
135. **Hero update, unitemised steps**: Resolved: Behavior 3A now lists every call of the hero update in order: the pre-steps (1a to 1k), the warnings, the input, look, cue and scan steps (3a to 3i), the late counters and flags (4), the action-state switch and the tail (6). Two routines in that list are empty. The three words once listed as unknown are settled: the flag at record +0x284 is the body-push participation flag (step 1a), the word at +0x1578 is the attack-active word (step 1d), and the sub-object routine of step 3a raises the swing-trail request (step 3a). **[High for the order; Medium for the roles marked there]**
136. **Kind tables of NPCs and enemies**: Resolved for region characters: townsfolk, common enemies and bosses are all created with one shared NPC kind table (Behavior 14D; High); their differences come from the AI routines and the monster row, not from separate kind tables. Resolved for heroes: Tor and Roberta use the same hero kind table; per-hero behavior can still vary by hero index and per-hero data. **[High for the shared region-character table and common hero table]**
137. **Actor class owner**: Resolved: the 15-record class is the motion-record class at 0x3167A0, used by every character (Behavior 4A). **[High]**
138. **Actor class creator**: Resolved: the hero pool setup and the character loader build it in place for every motion record (Behavior 4A). **[High]**
139. **Unowned routine: motion integrator.** Resolved: it is the integrator of the motion-record class, the routine of Behavior 14A, item 6; no further spec is needed. **[High]**
140. **Unowned routine: launch routine.** Resolved: it has no call, no code pointer and no address-forming pair, so it is never reached. Nothing to implement. **[High]**
141. **Unowned routine: four-state oscillator.** Resolved: it is a particle step of the world effect group, owned by SPEC-0034; it does not step under pause. **[Medium-High]**
142. **Unowned routine: command-stream interpreter.** Resolved: it is the compiler runtime's call-frame interpreter used for C++ exception unwinding, not game code. No spec owns it. **[High]**
143. **Chain follow lanes**: Resolved: the drift adds (0.0003 i, 0.005, 0.0003 j): the first draw to x, the 0.005 to y and the second draw to z (Behavior 25A). It belongs to a dormant pool. **[High]**
144. **Chain follow sources**: Resolved: the draws are closed, and the previous sample is the last tick's position at pool record +0x150 (Behavior 25A). **[High]**
145. **Chain follow constant**: Resolved: the 0.99 is a fixed program constant, not the word at 0x2DD854, and the owner is the dormant +0x29F0 pool of SPEC-0028, section 15.1 (Behavior 25A). **[High]**
146. Answered: the four-count trigger belongs to the +0x29F0 world pool step, and that pool is never filled in the shipped build (Behavior 25A; SPEC-0028, section 15.1). Its trigger mechanics have no effect in play.
147. **Sample ring owner and layout**: Resolved in part: the 48-byte samples are pushed by the chain-follow controller through its ring-object pointer at +0x74; the count is at ring-object +0xBC, and this is not the five-slot motion-record history of Behavior 14. The pool it belongs to is dormant (item 145), so the ring's owner and cadence do not matter to play (Behavior 14B; Medium-High for the separation and known writer).
148. **Lock-gated busy set** (Behavior 18A). Resolved: it is the Mote reflect test of an enemy projectile hitting a shield-blocking hero, owned by the enemy projectile pool (SPEC-0029). The "lookup result 7" is Mote element 7, which matches every projectile element. It is not related to the hero busy list of Behavior 15.2.
149. **Surface basis blend** (Behavior 9, item 7): Resolved for working roles and lane behavior; the world-axis labels are developer naming and not encoded anywhere. The gate checks slot 1's second float lane `< 0.999` and slot 9's integer flag `== 0`. The blend uses slot 1's first lane embedded in the X component and the global up vector `(0, 1, 0, 0)`, weighted 0.9 and 0.1, then normalizes. Slot 0 is a consumed reference vector and is cleared; slot 1 is the primary evolving frame vector; slot 2 is a derived perpendicular vector. Failing the gate performs no writes. Call sites do not establish normal/tangent/binormal names or handedness.
150. **Attachment selector** (Behavior 22A): Resolved. The selector is the Darkest Faerie orb-volley's chosen orb kind (1 to 6, from the state-3 random draw stored at +0x10 in the controller's state record and passed by the animation-7 path), and the glow effects are 1→1617, 2→1619, 3→1622, 4→1618, 5→1620, 6→1621. Its global slot is distinct from the two per-character attachment handles. The vector at +0x1420 is not a scalar setter argument: all four lanes are used, with the fourth forced to 1, and it is the posed position of weapon frame 0 (attach point 0), written by the per-frame pose pass that fills the five weapon frames (SPEC-0005, Weapon frame blocks). Test vector: orb kind 3 with the boss's attach point 0 at (10, 2, -4): the slot attaches effect 1622 at (10, 2, -4, 1), and while the controller stays in state 3 the glow moves with the point each tick. **[High for the selector, the effect map and the vector's lanes; Medium-High for the visible placement]**
151. **Clip progress threshold** (Behavior 22B): Resolved: the threshold is not stored; each progress test passes its own constant (0.99, or 0.95 in some callers), and progress is (cursor + stored fraction) / F, or 1.0 when the record is finished or empty (Behavior 22B). **[High]**
152. **Visual binders** (Behavior 28). Resolved in part: SPEC-0040 owns the per-character controller and attachment lifecycle; SPEC-0034 owns the group-3 effect definitions and instances. The known state controller runs from hero and shared region-character updates; the 129/188 and 2010 paths are hero-specific. These keys are effect-definition keys, not display-model resource IDs. The exact attachment offsets and rendered appearance of the keyed definitions are presentation details that are read from the effect definitions (SPEC-0034) and are not repeated here; their appearance in play needs a runtime check. **[Medium-High for caller and ownership split; Low for visible result]**
153. **Object separation owner**: Resolved: the routine acts on the 0x160-byte records of the world's +0x29F0 pool, not on characters, and runs only inside that pool's update (Behavior 11A; SPEC-0028, section 15.1). **[High]**
154. **Object separation share**: Resolved: each object moves by overlap × (the other object's radius) / (sum of radii), so the smaller object moves more (Behavior 11A). **[High]**
155. **Object separation timing**: Resolved: it runs only in the update of the +0x29F0 pool, the second world sub-table of the world tick, and that pool is never filled, so it never runs in play (Behavior 11A). **[High]**
156. **Generic init** (Behavior 1A). Resolved in part: a dormant world object-separation pool owns two runtime slots of 0x160 bytes each, with the radius field at +0x140; a separate world pool has 0xE0-byte slots. This establishes that the +0x140 radius and the two-slot pool are runtime ownership facts, not the layout of the copied init record. The 96-byte record's fields, the object classes using this initializer and the role of the two pool steps and their 2.0 spacing belong to a dormant object-separation pool and are developer detail not encoded anywhere; the rewrite leaves this generic initializer out. **[High for the pool layouts and separation; Medium for the dormancy]**
157. **Selector slot range** (Behavior 1B, first step): Resolved: there are only two hero records, so slot 2 has no selection path (Open question 20). **[High]**
158. **Character init parameters** (Behavior 1B, step 4): Resolved: the three values 5.5, 1.175 and 3.5 are the motion record's weight (+0x04), radius (+0x08) and height (+0x0C), the same for both heroes. The placement writes them once and every re-placement keeps them (Behavior 1C). Region characters take the three from their monster row (Layout, body size). **[High]**
159. **Slot table layout** (Layout, Character slot table): Resolved in part. The table is the one SPEC-0030 section 4 describes: SlotAllocate supplies its capacity, and the code read has no fixed upper bound on that capacity. The 40-entry limit belongs to each slot record's object list, not to the world slot table. The main record at +0x58E8 is searched separately before the table records, but its semantic name is developer intent and not encoded anywhere. **[High for capacity and the distinct 40-entry list; Low-Medium for the main-record role]**
160. **Default spawn position** (Behavior 1B, step 2): Resolved. The code constant is (2429.0, 0.0, 2295.0, 1.0); 3045.0 is wrong. The default is not a data word and is effectively unreachable, because the presence test checks the field's address, not the world pointer. **[High]**
161. Resolved: **Basis constants** (Behavior 8c): the builder scales one axis by a fixed 1.25 constant; it is not the separate 1.25 data word used by the follow camera. The parallel test is a strict "above 0.9999" with the single-precision constant 0.9999, and the fallback is the constant world-up vector (0, 1, 0, 0); see the test vector in Behavior 8c. **[High for the scale source and the threshold]**
162. **Packed table word width** (Layout, animation tables): Resolved: the packed first word in each indexed type-2 animation-table entry is 32 bits wide (Layout, Character slot table; High).
163. **Node init record length** (Behavior 1A, steps 1 and 4). Resolved in part: the 96-byte and 48-byte reads are distinct record sizes, and neither can be assigned to the two-slot 0x160-byte radius pool or the separate 0xE0-byte pool from the known constructors alone. The two node readers belong to the dormant generic initializer of item 156, so their relationship, fields and 2.0 spacing do not affect play and the rewrite leaves them out. **[High for the distinction from the known pool records; Medium for the dormancy]**
164. **Second actor and strides** (Layout, player-slot table). Resolved in part: hero slot setup assigns sub-object, motion-record and control-block pointers from three separate fixed arrays, using their respective bases and strides 0x1BA0, 0x760 and 0x50 with the shared slot index 0–2. Only hero slots 0 and 1 have hero records; slot 2 is initialized in the static arrays but has no hero selection path. Region-character setup instead allocates a 0x1BA0 sub-object on demand and stores it through the owning character block's per-slot pointer array. Whether the separate global second-actor pointer is always set to the second hero record across all lifecycle paths needs a runtime check; default if unresolved (rewrite choice): treat it as the second hero record at all times. **[High for array binding; Medium-Low for second-global lifetime]**
165. **Pair test lanes (Behavior 11B).** Resolved: the depth difference is z(b) − z(a), the third position component, and the side difference is x(b) − x(a), the first component. The helper reads the first component directly and uses the lane-pack operation whose low lane is the third component; the position record is x, y, z, w. No captured pair is needed to identify these source lanes. **[High]**
166. **Pair test scan modes (Behavior 11B).** Resolved: the only known call chain starts at the hero's state-18 block-move check, passes through the movement-start helper and reaches the pair-clearance scan. The scan walks the region-owned 0xB0 movable-block list, reads its count at +0x580C and base at +0x5810, skips the moving record, and selects among the four geometric tests described in Behavior 11B from a movement-direction selector and the held block-face selector m. For the four state-18 actions, the selector pair (movement direction, m) is: action 41 (pull), ((m + 2) mod 4, m); action 42 (push), (m, m); action 144 (side step), ((m + 1) mod 4, m); action 145 (opposite side step), ((m + 3) mod 4, m). The action is chosen by which way the projected stick points relative to the held face and side axes (SPEC-0033, Behavior 4A.2). Example for m=0: actions 41, 42, 144, 145 pass (2,0), (0,0), (1,0), (3,0), respectively. This resolves the known selector map; runtime effects of collisions with a particular other block still depend on the pair-test rules in Behavior 11B. **[High]**
167. **Resolved: Pair test margin inputs (Behavior 11B).** Both margins apply to the pair test's r, which is the type-11 source entry's authored size/clearance at +0x10. It is not the +0x140 radius field of Behavior 11A's separate 0x160-byte pool records. The 0.05 side margin and 0.5 depth slack remain independent constants. **[High for the distinct sources and applies-to rule]**
168. **NPC mode setter (Behavior 3B).** Resolved: its second argument is the requested mode value; it performs mode-specific entry setup, saves the prior value when the mode changes, stores the new value, then updates the timer. Mode values are interpreted by the active behavior class. In class 6, modes 5 and 6 dispatch to wander and patrol and are present on disc; modes 1 and 8 have empty bodies and no placement codes. In class 17, modes 5, 6 and 8 have active handlers and appear in placement data; mode 8 also has a known retreat/hide transition. Class-17 mode 1 has a handler, but its only known setter path requires a non-hostile row, while every shipped class-17 row is hostile and none uses placement code 1. Villager pairing requests mode 3 for both partners; the class-6 pair radius is zero on disc, so this request is not reached in ordinary shipped play (Behavior 3B; SPEC-0038). The entry setup of every mode is listed in Behavior 3B (Mode entry setup); the mode-1 initializer only clears one word and installs one gait id (20, flyer id 154), and no design label for it exists in the program. **[High for setter input, dispatches, entry setup and disc survey; Medium for excluding other mode-1 entry paths]**
169. **Villager timer scale (Behavior 3B, step 6).** Resolved: k is the frame-rate word, 60 ticks per second, so the talk timer is 300 to 720 ticks and the cooldown 600 to 1800 ticks (Behavior 3B, steps 6 and 7). **[High]**
170. **Villager pair radius and eligibility gate (Behavior 3B, steps 1 and 2).** Resolved: the pair radius is the monster-row word +0x44, which is 0.0 on every class-6 row, so pairs never form on disc; eligibility needs the social gate at 0, a current animation id in the list of step 1 and a mode other than 3. The gate is set by EnableVillagerSocialize (0) and DisableVillagerSocialize (1) and cleared by the NPC reset (Behavior 3B). **[High]**
171. **Pending record after an immediate apply (Behavior 15A, step 2)**: Resolved: the pending priority is reset to 0 and the pending state to -1 (High). Test vector 173.
172. **Stop distance use (SPEC-0030 rows 106 and 108)**: Answered: walk classes 24 and 26 stop when the distance to the target is below the stored stop radius (SPEC-0030 rows 106 and 108; SPEC-0056).
173. **NPC walk speed.** Resolved: non-hero characters travel by the table-A root step of the clip they play, as the heroes do (Behavior 14D). Body 526 carries forward travel in the third lane of table A (walk 104 and 107: 512 per key; 108: 175; run 105 and 111: 1382 with a small x sway; fast run 106: 1975; carry walk 110: 128). Townsfolk speeds at R = 1 (S = 3): walk 5.00 units/s (table variant B 1.71 on id 1; patrol id 8 5.00), run 13.50, fast run 19.30, carry walk 1.25; movement types 3 and 4 play the idle clip (Behavior 14D, item 8). Same answer as SPEC-0056, Walk speed. **[High]**
174. **Re-plan at 1.0 or less (Behavior 21A)**: Resolved: the cache is kept; the target is stored, the arrival flag cleared, and the flyer goal clamp runs when either class test passes, as for a far target (High; Medium for the test meanings). Test vector 174.
175. **Modes 1, 2, 4 and 7 to 9 (Behavior 3B).** Resolved: in the townsfolk class (class 6) modes 1, 2, 4, 7, 8 and 9 have no body, so the character keeps its playing animation; the class-17 bodies of these modes are in SPEC-0038 (Behavior 3B). **[High]**
176. **Fall law of the motion-record velocity update (Behavior 14A, items 5 to 7).** Resolved: +0x60 is the velocity and +0x70 the force accumulator, which the integrator clears every tick. Each tick v += (F / W) k, F = 0, p += v k with k = 1/30, and gravity adds minus W times 32 times G times R to F (G the gain at +0x188). In the air vy falls by 32 G / 30 per tick and the drop from rest after K ticks is 0.0177778 G K (K + 1), a continuous gravity of 128 G units per second squared (Behavior 14A, item 7; Test vector 86). **[High]**
177. Resolved: **Reference vector at 0x3932E0.** It is the up vector (0, 1, 0, 0), magnitude 1, written once at boot by the start-up table walk (SPEC-0007; SPEC-0027 Open question 47). It is read by the velocity update (Behavior 14A, item 5), the driver's probe (item 1) and many other routines. **[High]**
178. **Stick target of the hero.** Resolved: there is no separate stick target. The field at sub-object +0xA0 is layer 0's root step of the hero's animation player, written each tick by the clip evaluation (key k root record times the clip speed). The accessor returns all four lanes, and the travel step maps them as D = x B1 + y B2 + z B3 (Behavior 14C). In the walk-and-turn state the stick handler only re-aims the step, keeping its length (Behavior 14C). The hero walks at 13.51 and runs at 19.29 units per second at full tilt (Behavior 14C). Resolved: the shared region-character kind (townsfolk, enemies and bosses) installs the non-hero step getter used by its motion-record travel path. Under the Can Fly and movement-mode gates in Behavior 14D item 7, it reads the layer-0 root step, combines it with the directional row-speed vector, and returns the composed step for the shared basis transform and position update. Its flyer height keeper separately adjusts position y toward the target by 0.1 per tick, snapping within 0.1. The animation sub-object movement gate must be nonzero for the motion-record step call. Resolved: the first class predicate used by the Can Fly 1 height keeper is a dead/reanimation exclusion: it returns true when the character is in base state 16, or when its reanimation countdown is positive while its animation-kind field is 16. The height adjustment requires this predicate to be false and the second class predicate to be true. The second predicate is true exactly when the row's Can Fly value is 1 and the grounded countdown is not positive. Thus the correction is skipped for the dead/reanimation condition and while the character is grounded; the adjustment applies only when both class tests pass. **[High for known predicates and gates; Medium-High for the dead/reanimation label]**
179. **Physics block roles.** Resolved: +0x00 (30.0) is the inverse of the time scale and +0x10 (80.0) is unused; neither is read through the block. +0x14 (0.03) is the friction coefficient of the base motion-record class only; character records use 0.6 (Where it lives; Behavior 14A, item 5c). **[Medium-High]**
180. **Shots (section 17) and the hero projectile.** Resolved: section 17 covers Roberta's Shadow (169) and Sun (171) beams, whose count is the beam life in ticks (no volley), and her Rock (165) skipping shots. The Roberta shot projectile of SPEC-0036 uses the hero shot slots and is a separate object (Behavior 17, Owners). **[High]**
181. **Class code, element byte and contact type.** Resolved: two fields of the breakable. The class code of the handlers is the element byte (+0x5E); the contact type is the material word (+0x5C), which selects sound cue 143, 145, 146 or 144, not an effect id (Behavior 16). **[High]**
182. **Integrator position correction (Behavior 14A, item 5b).** Resolved: there is no fixed scale. The ground constraint moves the position by minus n times the gap times the Y part of n, against the penetration tolerance -0.1 at +0x28, and sets the gap to 0. **[High]**
183. **Lying-idle switch (Behavior 3C, item 1).** Resolved: the switch is the flying test (row Can Fly = 1 and the grounded countdown +0xBF4 not positive). Base idle 0, or 97 while flying; fidgets 115 to 119, or 142 while flying; in mode 19 the idle is 141 when flying or when Can Fly is 2, else 1 (Behavior 3C). **[High]**
184. Resolved in part: the role-2 fidget draw cannot reach ids 28, 40, 62 or 95 (Behavior 3B). Whether another routine or level script plays these clips cannot be settled from the import-site records (needs a runtime check); default if unresolved (rewrite choice): never play ids 28, 40, 62 or 95. SPEC-0005, Open questions, covers the wider question of the remaining villager-bank ids. **[Medium]**
185. Resolved (High for the setter; Medium-High for lifecycle): binding the active hero's main animation set writes the player gate to 1, and the hero-slot setup/rebind path uses this binding step for Tor and Roberta. No explicit clear appears in the hero setup/rebind path. The gate is therefore nonzero after active-hero binding; a transient pre-bind value and unrelated indirect writes are not established (Layout and Behavior 14C).
186. **Character speed scale of the heroes**: Resolved: no script or routine changes it; it is 1.0 for every character (Behavior 1C; High).
187. **One-tick lag (resolved statically, High).** The hero motion-record step runs before the animation pass stores the current clip's root step. Starting from a zero stored step, a constant 0.2252-unit walk step therefore gives displacements 0, 0.2252, 0.2252, ... on successive ticks (cumulative positions 0, 0.2252, 0.4504, ...). The same order gives one final stored step of movement after input release. This follows directly from Behavior 3's call order and Behavior 14C's root-step writer/reader; no emulator result is claimed.
188. Answered: the mask checks bits 0, 2, 5 and 6 of the singleton runtime-object word at +8, not pad input. It initializes to zero and no later writer was found in the checked boot-program paths, so normal controls and the debug-input path cannot reach the skip in this build.
