If entered by external mutation, the condition skips loading 909 into the exterior slot and making it current, leaving 691 current (SPEC-0006, “New-game load mask”; High for the checked paths).
189. **Init order against the post-load placement** (Behavior 1C): Answered: Init runs inside pack load completion before arrival placement; activate-world and on-enter callbacks start after placement (SPEC-0030, section 6; High).
190. Resolved: the teleport landing path passes only the plane facing's first two 32-bit lanes into the facing-basis rebuild (negated when reverse is set); the plane's fourth lane is excluded. The exact xyz mapping remains subject to imperfect vector argument typing, but the fourth lane cannot affect the rebuilt basis. **[High for fourth-lane exclusion]**
191. Answered: this call is the camera pull-in pose setter; see SPEC-0027, “Pose setter with pull-in,” for its pose and height adjustment.
192. Resolved: the respawn cooldown is hero +0xA04, a 60-update counter decremented on selected-hero updates (step 1g of Behavior 3A) and used by grab-point acquisition and the state-28 exit to state 48. Respawn argument 2 is the heal option, not a relocation flag. Relocation requires hero +0x148 nonzero plus a non-null second-object pointer, then copies world id and start pose. Hero +0x148 is the player-controlled flag (1 for the two heroes, 0 for every other character), and the second object is the live petpet (second focus character); the respawn, the teleport landing and the world switch all keep it in the hero's world (High). The cooldown is counted in selected-hero updates, so it is 60 ticks (1 s) only while the hero is the selected one; a hero that is not selected is not updated and its cooldown stands still. Resolved: hero +0x3D4 is the character speed scale, a factor of clip play rate R (Behavior 3D; High). **[High]**
193. Resolved: charm attack walks centre the body cylinder on the character position point (position ± half-height); the class-0 world contact uses a feet-to-head cylinder instead (Behavior 12A; High).
194. Answered for the indexed direct callers: the Black Knight enraged floor strike appends tag 0; Juppie's slam and dazed blocker append tags 3 and 0; the Plague Serpent bite/mesmerize path appends tag 1; and PetPet's geometry/effect helper appends tags 2 and 1. Appends stop at a ten-entry capacity, and the per-tick mover consumes and resets the list (Behavior 14A, item 2a). Indirect or unindexed writers are not excluded. Caller-specific shape parameters remain partly open.
195. **Speed-write timing, whole tick**: Resolved: class-coroutine writes to P occur in the NPC update after its reset and status factors but before clip re-time and the animation pass, so they can affect playback in that same tick. In the common-enemy attack sequencer (SPEC-0038), the wind-up path writes its speed before requesting the clip. On the recovery-to-strike continuation, it requests the continuation clip, then writes the strike speed and enters the continuation state; the later animation pass sees that final strike speed. Gnarfas's class writes its animation-speed setting at the start of every class-coroutine tick, before state dispatch and its state-specific clip requests; the shipped setting is 1.0 (SPEC-0020). Resolved for all other AI callers: the order of a speed write against a clip request inside one class-coroutine tick does not matter, because a requested clip starts only in the animation pass, which reads the speed scale as it stands after the whole class-coroutine pass; the last write of the tick is the one that counts, and the next update resets it to 1.0 (Behavior 3E). **[High for these known caller paths; Medium-High for every other writer]**
196. **Hero-proximity stop flag**: Resolved: only the Black Knight's slow-swing and angry-swing hit routines set it, on each tick they run (Behavior 14D, item 4; SPEC-0012; High). Test vector 178.
197. Resolved: Can Fly 2 characters keep gravity gain 1.0 while the height keeper moves y by at most 0.1 per tick. In free fall, vertical speed increases by 32/30 units per tick, so its per-tick displacement exceeds the keeper step after three ticks; the character therefore remains near the ground and bobs when ground constraint and height correction alternate. This is derived from the tick rules in Behavior 14A and 14D; the exact in-game bob amplitude has not been measured (Medium-High).
198. **Flyer target height** (Behavior 14D, item 7): Resolved: the distance is horizontal, the ground is probed under the goal, the home height replaces the Can Fly 1 result after its clamp, Can Fly 2 has only the upper cap, and AI mode 10 sub-state 6 caps the goal at Max Fly Height (Behavior 21). **[High]**
199. **Movement type 7 caller (partial).** In the post-Gnarfas aftermath, entity 28 is a Minion Archer using the Can Fly 1 row and behavior class 17, so this is not a ground-character example. Movement type 7 is stored as requested and is the forward flyer form when its flight gate is active. The row's forward-speed contribution is its Flying Idle Velocity or Flying Agro Velocity divided by 30 per tick; this row supplies 4.0 or 8.0 respectively. The script sets the type before walking to locator 46 and only later explicitly sets the archer to agro. Whether flight is active during each part depends on its grounded counter and the character's movement and animation gates. The exact clip and observed trajectory during the scripted walk need a runtime check; default if unresolved (rewrite choice): fly the walk with the Can Fly 1 forward form and the row speed above. **[High for identity, row values and conditional movement rule; Medium for which gates hold during the scripted walk]**
200. **Roberta's head chains** (Behavior 25): Answered in part. Visibility is settled (Medium-High): the chain-skinned upper leaf belongs to the one visible type-13 child of each outfit and the layer node has flags zero, so the two head strips are drawn whenever the outfit is drawn; no outfit, held-item or status path hides them, and the only thing that moves them is the chain tuning of Behavior 25. Only their anatomy (hair, ears or a cap) remains open; the variant 519 difference is explained below. Her outfit's optional type-33 block is bone-attached stencil-shadow hull geometry, not a visible hair or cap mesh. Each outfit resource has one visible type-13 child and one type-3 selector plus one type-4 node; the selector/layer words are zero across the eight outfit variants, so their asset hierarchies provide no per-leaf selector partition for hiding a head part. Each outfit retains upper-region geometry near head height. In variants 512–518, the same 548-vertex upper mesh leaf (material group 13) receives 481.59 of normalized skin weight from all eight chain joints, about 87.9% of its total; this ties that geometry to the moving chains but does not identify it as hair or explain a visible gap. The leaf is untextured, uses character colour slot 0, and has full stored alpha at every vertex, so indexed-texture transparency cannot explain a gap in this candidate. Weight-weighted vertex centroids for the eight chain joints form two mirrored tracks: their X positions are about +0.26 and −0.27, their Y positions about 3.02–3.04, and their Z positions progress backward from about −0.22/−0.25 to about −1.50 along the four-link runs. This confirms elongated bilateral geometry follows both chains, but does not identify its anatomy or reveal its runtime pose. Variant 519 has no separate counterpart to that chain-dominated leaf: its material-group-13 leaf has 732 vertices, spans nearly the full body height, and receives about 3.17% of its weight from the chain joints; its selected texture is fully opaque, but the leaf's different geometry and weak chain weighting do not identify what should appear in the same pose. Its leaf-to-packet match and decoded weights are internally consistent, so this is a real geometry/skin organization difference rather than a packet-association mismatch. Resolved (Medium-High): resource 519 is the model Roberta wears with the Tattered Cloak (item 126 in the body entry selects outfit 7, so resource 519 for her; SPEC-0021, Outfit items), a full-height cloak body rather than a dress with two head strips. Its leaf is not the chain-skinned strips of 512–518, only about 3 % of its weight follows the chains, and the cloak draws over the head area, so the two strips are not seen in the cloak (Medium). Item 134, the other item that would map to this outfit, can never be put on. Each of outfits 512–519 has one role-1 core-model child and no auxiliary role-10-to-20 attachment child. The standard held-model attachment points are the hand, shield, sheath and back-stowed shield; point 7 above the head is a status-effect anchor, not a held-item slot. No separate hair or cap model is identified by these outfit and held-item paths. The direct binding is also explicit in outfits 512 and 519: bone roots 35 and 37 both parent to joint 6, which parents to joint 4; the runs continue 35→39→40→41→42 and 37→43→44→45→46. Their bind reach and head-side attachment are established, but the asset does not label them as ears or hair; at rest they hang behind the head like hair strands (Medium). No other draw covers the top of her head: the outfit tree has one core model child and one layer node with flags zero (always drawn), no second draw with another node mask exists, the model-set slots serve hands, projectiles and the shield arm only, and attach point 7 is a status anchor, not a model slot. The behavior's angles are radians; in the bind frame the chain bones point backward along −Z, while the chains at rest fall behind and below the head. The stored rest pitches are 0.25, −1.25, −0.75 and 0.2 rad with the pitch minimum equal to each rest pitch (an earlier reading of −3.5 and −6.0 for links 1 and 2 was wrong and made the chains fold forward and stand above the head). The cumulative rest pitches are +0.25, −1.00, −1.75 and −1.55 rad, so each chain leaves the head slightly upward and backward and then hangs almost straight down; the rest positions, rotations and a tick-by-tick test vector are in Behavior 25. The upper mesh leaf that these chains move is two strips lying on the top-sides of the head and trailing back; see the head cover note in SPEC-0004. Attachment point 7 is a status-display anchor above the head, not an identified hair source. The purpose of the mode-2 axis flip when the root forward axis has a negative Y component is developer intent and not encoded anywhere; apply the flip as specified. **[High for type 33 as shadow geometry, no outfit selector partition, radians and the 512–518 chain-skin weights; Medium-High that no separate outfit or held-item head model is identified; Low for anatomical intent and flip purpose]**
  Bind-pose clarification for item 200: the chain bones and their corresponding weighted upper geometry trail backward along −Z at nearly constant height; they do not hang vertically down in the authored bind pose. This does not determine the dynamic silhouette after the secondary-motion step. Across outfits 512–518, all 548 vertices of the repeated upper leaf receive positive weight from joints 39–46, forming two long backward tracks. Outfit 519 differs: only 88 of 732 vertices in its group-13 leaf receive chain weight (3.17% total weight, from joints 39, 40 and 43), while a small 43-vertex group-16 leaf has seven touched vertices and negligible chain weight. This is a stored mesh/skin difference only; it does not identify anatomy or establish visibility in the rendered pose. **[High for the bind pose and weight crosswalk; open for anatomy and the animated render]**
  Resolved by R-0126 for the chain pose: the rest pose, axes, write-back, order and a test vector are in Behavior 25 (Roberta's rest pose). Head cover: with the corrected pose the leaf's head-side part stays on the head and its strips fall behind it; with the earlier wrong pitches the strips fold up and forward and uncover the sides of the head top. Resolved by R-0128: the head dome leaf is drawn solid with the black hair texture (its vertex alpha of 0 is not an opacity, SPEC-0046 and SPEC-0004), so it closes the top and back of the head under the two strips and no slit or opening shows [High]. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): draw all 22 leaves of the outfit with the corrected chain pose, draw the head dome opaque as stored, and add no extra head geometry.
201. Resolved: the known Tor and Roberta chain path refreshes only the joints it changes, and no later full pose rebuild runs in that character tick. The terminal joint therefore keeps its pre-chain world frame for that tick; see Behavior 25, “Tip orientation.” This establishes runtime timing, not authoring intent or any separate renderer rebuild. **[High for known character tick]**
202. **Head level step** (Behavior 23A): Resolved. The level rotation is the product of a rotation by the pitch alpha about the local negative X axis and a rotation by the roll beta about the local positive Z axis, where alpha is the angle between joint 4's forward axis and the horizontal forward perpendicular to its flattened side axis (positive when pointing down) and beta is the angle between joint 4's side axis and its horizontal projection (negative when the side axis points up); yaw is not corrected (Behavior 23A, Head step). Test vectors (placement side row (1, 0, 0), forward row (0, 0, 1), reset flag clear): forward axis 20 degrees below horizontal with the frame otherwise level gives (-0.1736, 0, 0, 0.9848); a side axis tilted up by 30 degrees gives (0, 0, -0.2588, 0.9659); a frame turned 90 degrees about the up axis gives the identity (0, 0, 0, 1); rows X4 = (0.766, 0.5567, 0.3214), Y4 = (-0.6428, 0.6634, 0.383), Z4 = (0, -0.5, 0.866) give alpha 37.0 degrees, beta -33.8 degrees and (-0.3036, -0.0923, -0.2759, 0.9073). **[High for the formulas and the product order; Medium for the sign of alpha in strongly tilted or turned poses]**
203. **Drift vector of the chain step** (Behavior 25, step 5): Resolved. The motion record writes the position change from this tick at +0x160 after its movement step; the animation sub-object copies that vector to player +0x3D0 before the secondary-motion pass. A stationary tick therefore supplies a zero vector. The pendulum step uses the unit direction of that displacement. **[High for the source and copy order]**
204. **Region-wide colour override** (Behavior 26, item 5): Resolved: the switch runs at character block load with the world variant test (progress flag 216 on and the variant key check giving 1; SPEC-0022). **[High; Medium for its story meaning]**













205. **Navigation steering caller weights (Behavior 8a).** Resolved: the goal-move path uses 0.1 for its no-mesh fallback and route-edge steering, and 0.3 only for direct goal steering after the current and target mesh triangles match and arrival/block gates do not fire. A missing target triangle does not steer. The known route-step/path-segment routine passes 0.1 (Behavior 8a; High for the tested branches).

206. **Locomotion mode field and flyer forms (Behavior 8a).** Resolved in part: modes 0 to 2 are forward gaits; 7 is the forward flyer form; 8 to 10 are backward and side flyer forms. Initialization sets 0, and runtime requests use the common mode setter. With Can Fly 1/2 and the grounded countdown clear, requests 0–2 map to 7, 3–4 to 8, 5 to 9 and 6 to 10; otherwise the request is stored unchanged. Known boss-controller writes are established for modes 3, 5 and 6, while class-17 enemies choose gait modes. No other writer is known to select mode 4, and finer semantic labels for modes 3 and 4 are developer naming that is not encoded anywhere; the rewrite implements the dispatch and remap as stated. [High for dispatch and remap; Medium for writer survey]
207. Resolved (R-0011; Medium for the walk-level filter): the 16-bit word at record +0x42 is applied by the walk that supplies the hero's frame records, not by the hero routine. A zero word passes; otherwise the record passes only when a bit of the word is also set in the world's mask word (+0x56B4). Near-horizontal records: every top-level frame record has a normal with |y| at most 0.2, so the hero routine needs no separate branch for near-horizontal faces (Behavior 19).
208. Resolved in part: (1) Each render object owns 15 slot pairs, one pair per colour word, with a buffer for each of the two draw buffers. Before each mesh leaf is drawn, the walker reads the slot number from the material group record (byte +0x27 of the group's 0x270-byte record; the group index comes from the leaf's group field). It sends slot number's packet for the buffer of the current pass, and then sets the group and draws the leaf. So the colour is chosen by the material group, not by the batch or the leaf. The walker does this only when the object's first word is non-zero. If the object's mode word (render object byte offset 0x12B0) is 1, the walker sends the object's own colour instead of the slot packet, for every part. That word is the active flag of colour-override slot 1 (slots are 0x30 bytes apart from byte offset 0x1280), the object's own colour is slot 1's vector at 0x12C0, and the lane flags are its two mask words at 0x12D0 (red, green and blue) and 0x12D4 (alpha). Its writer is therefore known: the set routine that the hit flash uses (SPEC-0046, draw step 6; SPEC-0034, Flash). While the flash runs, every part of the hit character is drawn with the flash vector in place of its slot colour, and the slot colours return when the slot is cleared (High for the offsets and the walker rule; Medium-High for the on-screen flash). (2) The packet is sent for every mesh leaf, textured or not: the walker tests no texture state. Whether a texture is tinted depends on the vertex program (SPEC-0046). The packet is sent only when its colour differs from the last colour queued, lane by lane over the lanes it sets. (3) Partly settled: the walker has a second node kind, a switch (kind 4). Such a node has an attached child (at byte offset 0x78) and a child list (at 0x40). Its node mask (the word at byte offset 0x5C) decides the order: a zero mask draws the attached child and then the list; a nonzero mask that shares no bit with the object's word 0x49C draws the list only; a nonzero mask that shares a bit with that word draws the attached child and then the list. The walker has no separate path for eyes, face parts or attached sub-models. Each of them is a mesh leaf in a switch node's attached child or child list, and takes the colour slot of its own material group record like any other leaf; the switch node itself sends no colour. Which of a character's parts sit under switch nodes is in that character's node data and is not listed here (Medium). Test vectors: a group whose slot byte is 0 uses slot 0; 14 uses slot 14. Two parts in a row with slot 3 and the same colour send one packet; slot 3 then slot 5 with different colours send two. An object mode word of 1 (override slot 1 active, the hit flash) sends the object colour for every part and ignores the slot bytes. **[High for (1), including the mode word (override slot 1); High for (2) as to the walker; High for (3) as to the walker's switch rule, Medium for the placement of the parts]**












209. Resolved (R-0100): class-17 setup initializes the flyer retarget countdown to -1, meaning disabled until a caller enables it. The flyer height keeper adjusts the y component of the character's stored movement goal; its input is the one stored move goal (the point at +0x9D0), not a separate height-only point. When the enemy is not walking to a set goal, that point is the last one stored: the creation point (1529.0, 0.0, 1700.0) until the first think tick, then the enemy's own spawn position (when its navigation mesh is found), then whatever the walk-target setter wrote last with its height clamp (Behavior 21, goal before the first walk target; Behavior 21A, target height). Strafing, hit reactions and idle ticks that set no walk target keep that point and its height. [High]
210. **R-0102 target-point implementation and candidate filters (Partially resolved).** Ordinary non-hero characters return their cached target point, refreshed by default from render attachment point 7; a controller can override it with an explicit point. The Darkest Faerie uses the shared non-hero actor table, and no override is known in its controller family, so attachment point 7 is the best-supported default for her (Medium-High). The semantic name/placement of that attachment may differ by model, and another override path is not excluded. Hero candidate distance is full 3D length and must be strictly less than 20.0; a vertical separation of 15 therefore leaves a horizontal distance strictly below 13.2288. This differs from Roberta's shot finder, whose 60-unit range is horizontal (SPEC-0036). The spawn-record type predicate uses the petpet lookup that recognizes six petpet kinds; a negative lookup result passes this exclusion. The second predicate is a class-17 state/animation/configuration gate, chiefly controlled by the monster row's “Display in Default” field and behavior fields; its operational veto is known, but its design meaning is not. Further gates require the candidate to be present and alive, reject action states 16 and 42, and reject behavior state 16 while its countdown is positive. Controller overrides are real: the Cloud Beast sets its target from its companion's attachment point 1. **Default if unresolved (rewrite choice):** use attachment point 7 for the shared non-hero path unless an explicit controller override is established; implement the documented candidate gates without assigning an undocumented semantic name to the class-17 predicate; do not infer an aim point from body height or collision geometry. **[High for the range and listed Boolean gates; Medium-High for attachment dispatch; design meaning is developer intent and not encoded anywhere.]**
211. **Hero ledge-guard probe ceiling (Behavior 19; Behavior 14 item 6)**: the guard calls the ceiling-capped height probe with the argument reference y + 10.0, and the probe adds its argument to the probe point's own y. Read directly, the ceiling is about twice the height plus 10, not feet + 10. Likely an original slip. Settled 2026-11-09 by two independent code readings: the ceiling is probe y + reference y + 10 (2 × reference y + 10 + forward.y × s), and the rewrite must reproduce it. Open check: an in-game test, especially at negative y, to see what the probe returns. **[High for the reading and for the behavior to reproduce; open for the effect at negative y]**
212. **Identity of +0x17C in the edge-guard pre-step (Behavior 14E)**: the code reading of the driver says the motion record's +0x178 and +0x17C are the nearest-item token and distance; the pre-step rule above tests a +0x17C and treats it as a navmesh height cache. They may be different records (owner versus motion record). Open check: confirm which record the pre-step reads. **[Open]**
213. **Four-point ease targets and first-tick test (Behavior 8b, rule 6; resolved 2026-11-09).** The targets are computed (a rotation turning the pose direction between each joint pair onto a fixed direction, and a yaw of 0, −1.57 or +1.57 for the second joint of the pair; identity when the speed word is negative), and the reset path runs when the first-tick flag (+0x394) is NONZERO (it is cleared there and the ease count set to −1). The routine is dormant. **[High]**
214. **Mode-0 tracked-point routine (Behavior 8b, rule 3; resolved 2026-11-09).** It is a two-bone reach solve toward a fixed point and writes pose slots 0x13 and 0x14 every call; the 1.7 limit belongs to one Euler angle. Dormant. **[High]**
215. **Wrap flag at exactly F − 1 (SPEC-0005; recorded 2026-11-09).** The animation notes disagree by record kind: main layers do not set it, the sub-part and face records do. See SPEC-0005 open questions; no effect on play.
216. **Character-class integrator and friction (Behavior 14A items 5c to 6; recorded 2026-11-09).** The base-class routines were read in full (gravity without gain, friction using only the gravity term, integrator with force (0, 0, 0, 1) and the direction word carrying the speed); the character-class integrator and friction variants were not re-read in the same round, so whether the character-class integrator also stores force (0, 0, 0, 1) and the speed in the direction word's W lane is open. **[Open; Medium for the character class]**
217. **Acos argument clamp in the neck look (Behavior 23A).** The neck-look step passes the dot product to the arc-cosine without a clamp (confirmed), but whether the shared checked arc-cosine routine clamps its argument itself is not known. A rewrite should clamp. **[Open]**
219. **Movable-block pair relation forms (Behavior 11B; recorded 2026-11-09).** The spec lists four test forms: one-sided forward-Z (found), centred band forward-Z (found), and an axis-swapped forward-X form with a one-sided Z interval (not found). The code read shows a centred band forward-X routine instead; whether a one-sided forward-X routine also exists, or the spec's form (2) mislabels the centred forward-X test, is open (SPEC-0048 Second clearance test lists which form each push direction uses and should be re-checked against the three routines). **[Open]**
218. **Slot 6 and slot 7 of the motion record (Behavior 14A).** The friction step is virtual slot 7 by its signature; the full semantics of slot 6 (it receives the projected scalar and returns a float) are open. **[Open]**
220. **Hero state 37 phase 2 on tilted edges (recorded 2026-11-09; see SPEC-0033 Behavior 51A, state 37 phase 2).** The edge height formula is exact for tilted top planes, but the phase finishes only when the hero's heading has a dot product above 0.99 with the face's inward normal. For a horizontal unit heading this fails once the face normal is tilted about 8.1 degrees off horizontal (|n.y| about 0.141 or more), while edge acceptance admits |n.y| up to 0.2, so the climb may never finish for 0.141 <= |n.y| <= 0.2. The readings also disagree on whether the sink lowers by a fixed 0.2 per tick (the earlier reading) or snaps to the target. Needs an in-game check. **[Medium; open]**
221. **Hero lock-on list (Behavior 20; recorded 2026-11-09).** The refresh uses horizontal distance and direction, an unfound candidate is left unchanged, and the in-range counter counts only refreshed entries; the earlier text said 3D distance and removal of missing candidates. The latest reading governs. **[High; the earlier text is withdrawn]**
222. **Where the 2026-11-09 readings disagree.** The first overlap-test reading called the shape capsule-like in the parallel case (Medium); the full reading found flat ends throughout (High), and this spec follows the later one. The petpet frame-record step's second bypass was read as active before and is dead after (SPEC-0018). **[Recorded]**
